# Broken State — State Map Prototype

A browser prototype of the design doc, built to answer one question: *is running a hub, sending teams across a dangerous state and dealing with other groups fun?* Scavenging and hub building are abstracted; fights play out as XCOM-style tactical battles.

## Run it

```bash
node server.cjs
```

Then open http://localhost:5173. No install step and no dependencies; only Node.js is needed.

Headless tools (no browser):

```bash
node tools/simtest.js 120 42 8
```

Runs 120 in-game days on seed 42 with 8 AI groups and prints a summary (for balance checks). Add `size=small`, `size=large` or `size=huge` for another world size (Normal by default); `autotest.js`, `timelapsetest.js` and `maptest.js` take the same argument, `balancesim.js` takes `--size huge`.

```bash
node tools/cardcount.js
```

Counts which event cards fire across five 90-day runs, then lists the hub events (`src/sim/hubevents.js`) by id, recruit cards by source and warnings before desertion.

```bash
node tools/pacing.js 365 all
node tools/pacing.js log 120 balanced 3
```

Pacing and decision quality (findings in `review/pacing.md`; or `node tools/pacing.js [days] [cautious|balanced|aggressive|all] [seeds...] [--workers N]`, seeds 1–14 by default, about 8 minutes for 42 full years one after another; the campaigns run side by side in worker threads, one per core (`tools/pool.js`), so a fraction of that on a multi-core machine): plays autopilot campaigns and catches every card as it is added, whatever module adds it (the module, the hour, its choices and the autopilot's answer). Reports cards per in-game day (mean, p90, max, per 30-day bucket; all cards and decisions only), cards and clock stops per real minute at 1× and 3× (an hour is 3 s at 1×; every card pauses the clock), other pausing screens (search, trade, battle), bursts (most cards in 6 and 24 hours), quiet stretches, crises stacking (raid, horde at the walls, illness, a team in trouble within 3 days), team cards against base cards, the low-stakes share and the rate without it, big moments from the log next to the cards that warn of them (raids, hordes at the walls, groups turning hostile, hives, short rations), the mix by module, the top 25 cards (repeats within a week, the autopilot's answers) and the decisions with one answer 90%+ of the time. Fails on a crash or a card with no choice that can be taken; flags duplicate cards and cards answered after their subject was gone. `log` prints one run's full log day by day (the log panel keeps only 200 lines).

```bash
node tools/perfprofile.js 120 3 --stages --slow 10
node tools/perfgolden.js --save before.json
node tools/perfgolden.js --check before.json
node tools/perfcpu.js /tmp/prof/CPU.*.cpuprofile --callees factionsDaily --depth 3
NODE_PATH=$(npm root -g) node tools/refreshsplittest.cjs
```

Performance (`docs/prompts/performance.md`). `perfprofile.js [days=120] [seed=3] [size] [--groups 8] [--autopilot balanced|aggressive|cautious|off] [--stages] [--slow N] [--mode hardcore]` plays one campaign and times every hour: the average and p50/p90/p99/worst of an hour, the hours over 60 ms by hour of the day (00:00 is the daily tick), the first midnight on its own (the cold costs), `createWorld`; `--stages` times each call of the hour (`world.js hourOfWorld`) from a copy of `src/` in a temp folder (the game's files are not touched), `--slow N` names the stages behind the N slowest hours. `perfgolden.js [--days 60] [--seeds 42,7,1234] [--save f.json] [--check f.json]` runs `dettest.js --digest` for each golden seed in its own process, side by side: a performance change records the hashes on its base before touching code and checks them after; any difference means it is not a pure speedup (the hashes change whenever the game does, so they are never kept in the repo). `perfcpu.js <file.cpuprofile> [--top 40] [--callers fn] [--callees fn] [--depth 2] [--min 50]` reads a V8 CPU profile (`node --cpu-prof --cpu-prof-dir=/tmp/prof tools/perfprofile.js ...`, or the browser's DevTools): self and inclusive time per function, who calls a function and what it calls. The browser's frame times: `node perf/browser-bench.js --speed=5 --reveal=off --autopilot=on --dev-panel=off` in `desktop/`, or `npm run perf` there with the same options prefixed `--perf-` (`--perf-speed=5`); `perf/driver.js` says what each does. `refreshsplittest.cjs [--ffwd=25] [--seconds=8]` (Playwright and Chromium, as `mapshot.cjs`) checks the panel refresh split (`main.js`: everything on a change of the world or a click, only what counts the minutes on a new minute alone, `refreshClock`): it plays at 5× and, on every frame that showed a new minute, runs a full refresh; any panel it changes was left stale (each left-panel tab, a team in the right panel, a tile selected, the Ledger window).

```bash
node tools/traytest.js 60 1 2 3
```

Pause rules, the card tray and 5× (`src/sim/cardrules.js`, `src/ui/pacing.js`; or `node tools/traytest.js [days=60] [seeds…=1 2 3]`, about 2–3 minutes): the switches and their defaults, keys 1–5; each known card in its kind and with its default answer (never greyed out, never one that needs the map, never a fight; Orders → Hold here, Storm → Shelter, a perk → Decide later), auto-answers with their log line, a choice that throws or a stale card dropped harmlessly; a team holding while its card waits; a 6-day browser-like loop with every switch off (cards wait, answer themselves within 8 hours, nothing stays stuck); 3× and 5× dropping to 1×, deaths, first contacts, groups turning hostile and hordes near a base stopping the clock; every card of the autopilot runs with a kind and a safe default, and the clock stops per day before (every card) and after (the default rules); the time one in-game hour takes on a Huge map.

```bash
node tools/combattest.js 300 5
node tools/combattest.js 300 5 weapons
```

Runs thousands of tactical battles with the AI playing both sides and reports win rates per matchup. The last six matchups put a hive's special dead among 12 of the dead (4 runners, 2 brutes, 3 spitters, a screamer, 3 crawlers, one of each elite: `zombietypes.js`). With `weapons`, the weapon balance instead: teams of three (Shooting 5–11) each armed with one weapon (knife, baton, bat, crowbar, machete, nail bat, fire axe, sledgehammer, scrap crossbow, 9mm, .357 revolver, compound bow, hunting crossbow, shotgun, hunting rifle, lever-action rifle, police carbine, assault rifle) against 4 raiders (power 5) and 12 of the dead (power 2), and pistols with two Molotovs / pipe bombs / flares / smoke grenades each against 16 of the dead.

```bash
node tools/raidhumveetest.js 7
```

A raid's Humvee in the fight (`src/sim/combat.js` `raidCarTurn`, `RAID_CAR`; or `node tools/raidhumveetest.js [seed=7]`, about 5 s): the M2 gunner rides it, a driver and up to 2 riders inside (only while 3+ come on foot); it drives forward with the attack each enemy turn by the same rules as your vehicle (`carSteps` / `driveTo`, open ground only, at most 8 cells, never nearer your people than 6 nor more than 2 ahead of their front), stops where the gunner has someone in range and sight, and drops its riders near the line; a downed gunner's place is taken by a rider; worn by blasts past 10 (the same wear as yours) or with its gun unmanned or the raid broken it pulls back and drives off the map with everyone inside (the group keeps it, worn); worn to 20 it stalls; the driver down, it stays; a save mid-battle; off the walls it is parked outside. Then: hull wear by the round (`HULL`: past penetration 35, × 0.6 plated, a turret burst × its 3 rounds: the .50 6.3 a hit on plate, light rounds none); a caravan's armoured escort (Humvee or MRAP) manned and driven by the same AI; your turreted vehicle crewed and driven when the battle AI plays your side (`botCar`); shooting the cab (`fireCab`: the hull wears, half the hits go in at the windscreen, × the glass, at the driver; a driver down stops it) and the AI doing it to yours; the gunners' duel (a miss at a gunner hits the vehicle 35% of the time; worn to 20, yours will not drive either); pulling back, it waits up to 2 turns for their people running within 8 and they climb in; a plated one with no ram rams a weak gate (100 hp or less: 30–45 a blow, 2.4 wear to it) or drives through a barricade, never walls or people; an escort that drove off is not yours to take.

```bash
node tools/sizetest.js 10 3
```

Battle map sizes by context (`src/sim/battlesize.js`; or `node tools/sizetest.js [battles per matchup=10] [seed=3] [--quick] [--workers N]`, about 10 s for the checks and 30 s with the measurement on 4 cores: its matchups run in worker threads on half the cores, since the enemy turn is timed): the size table (Small 24 × 18, Medium 32 × 24, Large 40 × 30, Extra-large 48 × 34) and what maps to each (sites by location type, fights in the open by the people and the dead on the map and the terrain, a hive, a group's base by its people, your hub and outposts by their buildings); every context built twice the same, valid (everyone on a free walkable cell your people can reach, nobody out of reach but one of the dead shut in a locked room), your people in the first 5 columns and the enemy 14–21 tiles off (an assaulted base's defenders behind their wall), cover within 3 tiles of most of the open ground, the first shot or blow by turn 3 (the AI on both sides); a raid with more rooms, containers and dead on a bigger site; the anti-slog ends (two far-off unaware stragglers: the player is told and can call it, the AI ends it; a raider who runs out of sight gets away). Then battles by size class played by the AI on both sides: turns, the share past the 60-turn limit (fails over 5%), ends called "clear enough", the enemy turn's time (fails unless 99% are under 40 ms).

```bash
node tools/loottest.js
```

Loot audit (`src/sim/loot.js`; or `node tools/loottest.js --quiet` for the checks only): every item has at least one source (site gear lists, small finds per site and per raid container, books, enemy bodies and their pockets, the dead's bags, group and merchant stock, travellers, recruits, crafting, road cards, blueprints); everything a site type (`SITE_TAGS`) or raid area (`AREA_TAGS`) can hold shares a theme tag with it (`ITEM_TAGS`: no military radio in a university lab, a hunting lodge has rifles, bows, camo and game calls, a police station batons, riot gear, cuffs and a carbine…); every item has a trade category, a weight, a hidden value and a description; 28+ valuables in every valuable family, light for their worth (6+ value a kg) and good for nothing but trade; every weapon has battle numbers, bows are silent and shoot arrows (never ammo), every throwable is in the throw table; the packs (sling bag 3 slots, school backpack 6, assault pack 8, hiking pack 10, frame rucksack 14); the small-finds rolls only give what their table holds, about one in ten search hours at full loot and fewer at a picked-over site; about 8% of the dead wear a bag with 1–3 things inside; a raider's pockets hold something one time in five. Then a table of every item: category, worth tier, kg and where it comes from.

```bash
node tools/retreattest.js 40 7 all
```

Sounding the retreat (`combat.js` `soundRetreat`; or `node tools/retreattest.js [battles per matchup] [seed] [cautious|balanced|aggressive|all]`, 0 battles for the checks only): offered on the player's turn of a battle with an evac strip, refused in the enemy turn, with no way out and in a raid, the strip read through `inEvac` / `evacAt`; escape odds by distance, each enemy next to them (−25), a shooter in sight (−6), wounds, armor, Scouting, night and carrying, and the chance a caught unit goes down; the plan has the one next to a downed teammate carry them and can leave them; sounding it (the one on the strip always gets out, a stabilized teammate carried out lives, a dropped one is lost, a wounded unit next to the dead is often caught and sometimes dragged down, the outcome reaches the handler as "retreat"); a downed teammate on the strip goes with the last one out by the per-unit Retreat; the AI retreats against six elite soldiers, not against four of the dead, Cautious before Aggressive with one down and one hurt, Aggressive's numbers for a base defence, never where there is no way out; the same battle plays out the same; the panel's confirm (the odds, Leave / Carry, Esc, Shift+R, Space does nothing, Enter gives the order, the result's summary). Then the measurement: nine matchups (even raiders, an enemy ambush, 3 vs 4 raiders at power 5, soldiers at 7, 3 vs 6 raiders at 6, 12 and 16 of the dead, mixed, a hive with its Broodmother) on maps with an evac strip, each battle played twice from the same start (same team, map and dice), once with the AI never retreating and once in the given style; per matchup and style the wins, deaths and team wipes both ways, how often and on which turn the AI retreated, and how many of its retreats were from a fight the other run won. Fails if a battle it did not retreat from plays differently, if the retreat costs lives or wipes, if over a quarter of its retreats are from fights it would have won, or if it gives up more than 5% / 3% / 2% (Cautious / Balanced / Aggressive) of the wins. Seed 7, 40 a matchup: deaths 411 → 104 (Cautious), 414 → 182 (Balanced), 403 → 251 (Aggressive); team wipes 79 → 2, 75 → 3, 76 → 10; wins 218 → 205, 215 → 209, 218 → 217. In autopilot campaigns the only battles it plays on the map with a way out are hive assaults: over 16 three-year runs (Balanced and Aggressive, seeds 1–8; `state.autoRetreat = false` switches the AI's retreat off for a comparison) it stormed 36 hives, won every one and never retreated, and the runs ended identically with and without it.

The same tool checks the retreat by the numbers (`src/sim/breakoff.js`): the four policies; the numbers' escape check is the tactical formula (the same odds and chance to go down as `escapeOdds` in 40 placed battles), with the car (+10), bikes (+5), a stealthy stance (+10), night, carrying and an enemy on them; the policies' rules (`wantsOut`); 3000 model fights per policy: Never never breaks off, a fight not broken off plays out exactly as with Never and a broken-off one took the same wounds up to its round, the more cautious the policy the more it pulls out, If losing pulls out of a fight it would have won at most a quarter of the time, no policy costs more deaths than fighting it out (also against 16–40 of the dead), and they give up wins; 260 real fights by a team (`autoResolveCombat`): no loot on a retreat, the enemy loses part of its people, everyone is home, dead or a prisoner, the rounds not fought keep their ammo, the downed carried out come home barely alive, the left behind are taken or killed by a group's people and lost to the dead, and the carried pull through at 50% + 2% a point of Medicine; the conflict card (the fight choice carries its fight, the four policies with Never marked for a player's team, the forecast without touching the game's dice, choosing If losing, At first blood breaking off a real "Fight back" with nothing looted, a won fight looted as before, the group's losses) and the road's horde fight (broken off between wounds, the horde thinned less, the policy on its card); the autopilot by style (Cautious and Balanced If losing, Aggressive When most are down), a card's policy over the style, a crew by its role (style or its own Retreat setting, also the AI style of a hive assault it plays headless), base defences, raids and fights with no way out never broken off, `breakOff` (the helper for abandoning a base by the numbers); the same fights play out the same, and a team's policy survives a save and load with the fights after it unchanged. The measurement then also resolves every battle by the numbers (fought out, and with the style's policy) on the same team and enemy and compares the two modes: deaths, wipes and wins by the numbers, each runner's escape odds per enemy kind, runners who got out of every break-off (also one nobody got out of) and the team home alive after a retreat, and deaths a battle fought out; fails if the policy costs lives or wipes, if over a quarter of its retreats are from fights it would have won, if a runner's odds per enemy kind differ by more than 15 points between Cautious on the map and If losing by the numbers (both pull out early), if runners getting out differ by more than 15 points, if a retreat by the numbers brings fewer home than the map's (−10 points), or if Cautious retreats less than Aggressive. Seed 7, 10 a matchup: by the numbers, deaths 12 → 2 (If losing, Cautious), 7 → 1 (Balanced), 11 → 8 (When most are down, Aggressive); escape odds (Cautious on the map vs If losing) against raiders 87% vs 80%, soldiers 86% vs 85%, the dead 73% vs 70%; runners out 97% vs 99%. By the numbers is a much gentler fight than the map before any retreat (about 0.1 deaths a battle fought out against 1.2 on the map), so a retreat by the numbers brings nearly everyone home (99%, the map's 60–88%).

```bash
node tools/conflicttest.js 120
node tools/conflicttest_cover.js 30
```

Conflict balance (six 120-day campaigns with a random-choice auto-player: conflict types, outcomes, conflicts per trip, captives) and coverage (forces every conflict type and option, rescue missions, and a 60-day captive soak). Every team goes out with a "Retreat if it goes badly" policy (`breakoff.js`, the four in turn); it counts the fight choices (each must carry a usable fight for the card's forecast) and the fights broken off by policy, and fails if a team set to Never broke one off or no team ever did.

```bash
node tools/fighttest.js 7
```

Fight and living-world rules: other groups field humans only; a hit that leaves a sleeper alive wakes its group; no horde below 5 stays on the map and wandering hordes that meet merge up to 600 (the rest stays a horde of its own); surrender takes what its text says; gear wear (only the units that fought, only what they fielded, bandage kit per treatment, surgical kit needs the skill); ammo found on the way counts in fights by the numbers; a queued battle whose team is gone is dropped; no double loot after a fight on the map and bonus gear needs carry space; failed payments, the trade-visit queue and the hourly check that no team stays halted; a collapsed group's land and parties; pacts torn up by hostile groups and the raid threshold; tribute and help-call amounts worked out when chosen; tribute paid to a raid already at the walls ends it (no break-in after); a war raid beaten in person scores for your side; raids on you (sized 0.6–1.0× your prepared defence and never above the whole group, escalation +25% a raid beaten up to 2× and reset by a break-in, the siege shortened by a strong defence, damage growing with the hours and the outcome and hitting the defences first, a damaged wall worth 2, a wrecked bunkhouse no beds, no building on alert, the result card with what was taken and wrecked and its "Start the repairs", tribute goodwill fading 0.5 a day, a provoked peaceful group raiding only you, a collapse handing bases and people to the winner, a new gang when fewer than two hostile aggressive groups are left); unknown groups not named; the downed carried out of raids; defending in person (attackers match the raid's strength, no second raid after a loss); trade-route terms and exchanges at barter prices; a route with no escort pausing itself.

```bash
node tools/supplytest.js 70
```

Supply runs: founds test outposts, runs repeat / one-off / hold / disband / recreate jobs and blocked cases for 70 days on three seeds (the groups do not raid in it: raids sized to a base's defence would take the test outposts while their crews are on the road), and checks stock moves and schedules.

```bash
node tools/nightmarchtest.js
```

Night on the road (`src/sim/nightmarch.js`; or `node tools/nightmarchtest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): a team still on its way at 22:00 gets one "Night falls" card a night and holds while it waits; Make camp until dawn rests it on the spot until 06:00 with its trip (and the mission at the end of it) back on its queue, and it moves on by itself at first light; Push through the night keeps it moving with road events ×1.8 and run-ins ×1.6 (cap 0.45) and 4 hours of sleep owed an hour on the move (Tired by dawn); resting in camp sleeps it off by day; the "every night this trip" answers skip the card; never asked under the governor, a team about to arrive (1.5 h) or near dawn; the tray's default is to make camp; the team panel's At night toggle (Ask each night / Make camp / Push through, `setNightPlan`) answers it for the trip, and set at night it camps or marches at once.

```bash
node tools/wrecktest.js
```

Wrecked cars and finds: a restore costs 2 car parts (2 machine parts stand in for each) and 6 fuel; a find another team already took is marked emptied when a team finds it cleaned out, and an older sighting never puts it back on the map; the governor goes to a find once (restoring a wreck only when the hub can pay, else stripping it) and waits 20 days before trying the same find again.

```bash
node tools/medtest.js 30 42
```

Medical: hospital tiers through the build queue (one step at a time, upgrades replace the old tier), medic staffing, passive healing and bed caps per tier, treating at a base (meds used, heal per tier), field treatment with a team's carried meds, infected wounds, and a short campaign with a staffed clinic; treatment limits: one wound treatment per patient per day at a base and in the field (20 clicks a day for 5 days = 5 treatments), medic capacity (3 + Medicine/4 a day, 2 untrained, surgery takes 3 from one medic, a plain treatment when no medic is free for it, illnesses use the same time, resets at midnight), automatic care worst off first (3 meds for 69/69/69/12 go to the 12 first; pneumonia first; the two worst when medic time runs short), the meds reserve kept by every automatic dose over 5 nights at reserves 0/3/6 (also for pneumonia; a hand treatment may use it; automatic care off uses nothing); the admit line at 30/50/70/90/100 (beds for the right people, `s.inBed` matching `patients()`, everyone out of bed fit to work and building), worst first when beds run out; an understaffed upgrade (a Clinic with 1 medic runs as a Field hospital and logs a warning; a Trauma hospital with 2/1/0 medics runs as Clinic/Field hospital/none); the Medical tab's rate against the real change over a day (12 cases, looked at midnight and 10:00); and 200 days with a clinic, an admit line and a reserve with no NaN or negative meds.

```bash
node tools/clinictest.js 7                      # seconds
node tools/clinictest.js --measure --seeds 1,2,3  # a few minutes: Medicine after 180 days: Medic without and with lessons, Trainee
```

Training medics (`src/sim/training.js`): ward rounds (medics on duty earn Medicine from the patients: 2 a bed, 0.5 on their feet, +2 severe, +2 infected, +1 an illness, × the tier's teach factor, shared, capped a day at 4 / 6 / 8 / 10 by tier in their own bucket; an empty ward gives nothing), the doctor (the best lesson at the base: a medic on duty with Medicine 6+, else the best off duty at half) and the Medic trainees (the lesson rate, quiet ward × 0.75, places 1 / 1 / 2 / 3 by tier (to trainees who can learn first, a place left over to a junior medic on duty), lessons stop 2 below the doctor, ward work 4 a day, Leadership for the doctor), trainees as half a medic toward staffing (beside one full medic, not in a bed, only those with a place), the governor's trainee (the most Medicine below the cap, kept, none with enough skilled medics or short food), the Trainee work type in the default Medic role, lessons stop 1 below the doctor (a trauma hospital: at the doctor's own level), the AI groups' medics (start by type, growth with and without a sick bay, faster healing), the groups building their sick bays (`groupworks.js`: the infirmary first with wounded and quarters up, the upgrades after the workshop, a clinic only with a Medicine 6 doctor and a trauma hospital with a 10, shown on the Groups card, faster healing), the doctor kept on Medic duty for trainees on a quiet ward (governor and work priorities), the doctor's career (Leadership and Speech a day of teaching, Professor of Medicine after 60 days at Medicine 10+, lessons × 1.25, an earned perk outside the milestone choices), graduation at Medicine 6 (logged once, a card, Make medic, not for someone already past it, the governor moving a graduate to Medic while the hospital is short), save and load, the Medical tab's Training card, the Overview's count and the Help topic, and 40 autopilot days without NaN.

```bash
node tools/medstest.js            # sources by site type, bodies, trade, meds spent only on need
node tools/medstest.js sample 20  # the autopilot over a year: meds stock, meds spent, deaths
```

Meds: where they come from by site type (an hour of searching and a raid by the bot at each kind of site; pharmacies, clinics, hospitals and medevac wrecks many times the rest, ordinary places 1–2 at a time), bodies, the trade price, a base where everyone is well spending none, a hurt survivor treated from the base stock and a team in the field treating a bad wound and pneumonia from its own meds.

```bash
node tools/sicktest.js            # illness: causes, contagion, courses of meds, untreated, team doses, alerts, saves, the upset stomach's one-dose cure
node tools/sickresttest.js        # sick people rest (sickrest.js): any illness by its kind, Manual/pinned jobs kept and given back, the Work tab setting, the doctor's exception, emergencies, the watch, trips, the governor, the AI groups' ill
```

Illness: each cause raises a person's risk (no heating fuel in the cold, hunger and thirst below 25, building, a long march overloaded, dirty water, spoiled food, a fireless camp, crowding, a sick neighbour) and each protection lowers it (a filter, full gauges, a staffed clinic, a lit camp fire, a treated case barely spreading); flu spreads at a crowded base and on a team; a course costs 1, 2 or 3 meds by severity (chest cold 25, dysentery 55, pneumonia 95) and then mends; untreated fevers turn into pneumonia and cost health and mood; a team doses from its own meds by its best at Medicine, once a day; no meds at a base or on a team is logged and alerted; falling ill and spreading are alerted; the course, the news, the stats and the week's meds survive a save, and an old save's illnesses still run.

Crafting benches (`src/sim/buildings.js` BENCH: a crafting bench 12 scrap + 1 part, 12 h, no skill (the first bench a bare base builds: see *Bare start*); a workbench 16 scrap + 1 part, 18 h, Mechanics 2; a master workbench 28 scrap + 3 parts, 30 h, Mechanics 5; one utility plot, each tier upgrading the one below in place; about 3 s): a bench at a shelter, an outpost and the hub on a utility plot (none without a free one), hand crafting at a base needing one for all but the Field recipes (station recipes and re-packed filters aside; a workshop does for one in old saves), crafters 25% / 40% / 60% faster and +5% / +8% / +12% condition (at most 100%; field crafting gets nothing), the workbench and up counting as a workshop and being what an ammo bench needs, the new hub starting with no bench at all (barestart), and the governor building a bench where someone crafts, then the workbench and the ammo bench at the hub.

```bash
node tools/invtest.js 60 11
```

Team inventory (`src/sim/teaminv.js`, `src/ui/teaminv.js`; `node tools/teaminvtest.js [seed]`, seconds): the team panel's **🎒 Team inventory** opens the team's paper dolls and its cargo side by side. It scales from one person on foot to a full team with a fleet: a strip over the dolls lists every member (portrait, name, load) and opens or folds each one (a team of up to 3 starts with everyone open, a bigger one with the member who receives; *Show all* / *Show one*), and under the cargo's header a fleet of several vehicles is listed vehicle by vehicle with each payload, a team on foot with what its hands carry. The paper dolls and the cargo side by side (move things between them as in the one-person inventory). While the team stands on one of your bases' tiles, **Drop off at <base>…** and **Load from <base>…** (also on the base's Armory tab under *Teams here*, and on the tile view's *Your teams here* next to *Enter base*) open a transfer view: the team's people and cargo on the left, the base's armory and stores on the right; click items and supplies to pick them (how many of each supply), then Drop off picked / Load picked (items onto the member marked ✓ or into the cargo; supplies into the packed supplies), or *Drop off all cargo* / *Drop off all supplies*. Items move as themselves (condition, a magazine's load, a box's rounds, a pack's contents, a carrier's plates); supplies by key (each calibre and type of round its own). Nothing loaded takes the team over its carry limit or a person over their own max load; a worn pack or rig the load needs stays. Each member's share of the team's supplies (`src/sim/carry.js`: pockets, pack, arms; the vehicle's) shows under their paper doll and in the Drop off view, with *Drop off what <name> holds*; supplies loaded are placed again at once (who carries what); a bag carried in the arms is the *In arms* slot and goes in the armory whole. The tool checks the listing, drop off and load by item and count, fresh game and the worm's eggs, bodies in the car, the refusals, only at your own base, a save and load round trip and the window's views.

Rounds onto a person, and kit moved in a fight (`src/sim/supitems.js` roundsOnto / roundRoom, `src/sim/teaminv.js` loadFromBase / dropRounds, `src/sim/teammeet.js` giveGoods, `src/sim/teamform.js` formTeam, `src/sim/combat.js` combatAct "inv" / refitKit, `src/ui/inventory.js` peekModal; `node tools/invfighttest.js [seed]`, seconds): wherever supplies change hands (Form a team's supplies tab, Load from a base, Give goods between teams on a tile) the rows of rounds by calibre have a **Rounds go to** line: the team's supplies as before, or a named member's *pockets*, *pack* or *rig* (its admin pouches), *boxed* or *loose*; the row says how many fit there now and what the person wears against their max load. They become that person's own rounds (their gun draws on them first in a fight) and go back into the stores with Drop off, "fewer" while editing a team, or when the team comes home. In a fight the battle bar's **Inventory** (Shift+B) is the selected survivor's to sort on their turn: with an action left, moving a thing between a pocket, the pack, a rig pouch, the hands and the off hand (click it, then where it goes, or drag it) costs **1 action** a move, as do Split, Open box and Box on a stack of rounds, and what moved counts at once (a gun taken in hand, a magazine moved to a pocket reloads in 1 action, boxes of rounds at hand); armour, the helmet, the rig and the pack stay as worn; with no action left, or on the enemy's turn, the window is look only and says why. The tool checks the picks at Load, at team form and at Give goods (counts conserved, boxed and loose, the stacks' limits, the admin pouch's weight, the refusals), the rounds going back home, and in a fight the cost and refusals of a move, the refit of the boxes and magazines, a gun swapped into the hands, and a save and load in the middle.

Supplies onto a person's pockets, rig or pack, and the rig's pockets (`src/sim/supitems.js` suppliesOnto / supRoom / placeSupplies / suppliesHeld / SUP_PLACES, `src/sim/teaminv.js` loadFromBase / dropSupplies, `src/sim/teammeet.js` giveGoods, `src/sim/teamform.js` formPreview opts.onto, `src/sim/data-kit.js` POUCH_NAMES.pocket / SUPPLY_POUCHES, `src/sim/inventory.js` pouchReason / stow / quickMove, `src/ui/inventory.js` INV_STACK_DESTS; `node tools/supplyplacestest.js [seed]`, seconds): the 2026-10-18 requests ("move x loose ammo or any other item in the stores into the pockets, rig, pack"; "add pocket for rigs that can be any item that pocket can hold and larger rigs can have more than one slot"). The **Rounds go to** line of the day before is **Supplies go to**: in Form a team's supplies tab (a row at the top of the table), Load from a base and Give goods, any supply with an item form (rounds by calibre boxed or loose, water, meds, fuel, a food by kind, parts, scrap…) goes onto the member picked as items in their *pockets*, their *rig* (its admin and pocket pouches) or their *pack*, where it fits (a bottle of water is too big for a pocket, and says so); days of food and any-calibre rounds still go to the team's supplies. In the **inventory window** a stack in the stores (or a team's found supplies) opens the mover with a destination for each place the person has (pockets, rig, pack, the off hand), the fit counted for each, with *boxed* / *loose* chips under the panel for rounds; a stack dropped on one of their slots opens the mover on that place. Every rig has **pocket pouches** (`pouches` kind `pocket`: a chest rig and a tactical vest two, a makeshift rig and a plate carrier one) that take anything a pocket takes, with no weight cap of their own; `stow` and a double-click use them once the person's own pockets are full, the starting-gear editor places a small thing in a pocket before one, and an old save's rig grows them at the end of its pouches (`ensureRig`). The tool checks the pouch kind (fit reasons, moveItem, stow, quickMove, carrySpec / firstFit, a save from before), the room and placement of water, meds, rounds and a food (its shelf kept) in each place by each window's sim call, counts conserved against the stores, the refusals, the drop back and the way home, and the inventory window's destinations.

Reload sources (`src/sim/mags.js` roundSplit / roundSource / sourceWord / reloadPlan, `src/sim/ammo.js` boxesOf / takeBattleRounds, `src/sim/combat.js` reloadRefusal / aiReload, `src/ui/battlehud.js` weaponInfo; `node tools/reloadsourcetest.js [seed]`, seconds): where the rounds are decides what a Reload costs. Loose rounds or a box in a **pocket or a rig pouch** (`u.handBoxes`, the rig's pouches included in `boxesOf` right after the pockets') load for **1 action**: a top-up of the magazine in the gun, an internal magazine's Reload (a revolver, a lever-action, a bolt rifle), a shotgun's shells at hand. Rounds only in the **pack** load for **both actions** (a shotgun's shells too: the old refusal is gone, a Reload digs them straight out of the pack; *Get shells*, 1 action, still brings a handful to hand for 1-action Reloads after); with one action left the Reload is refused ("both actions to reload from the pack"). A teammate's boxes and the team's bags cost both actions too, a top-up or an internal magazine's Reload alike (only what is at hand is quick); spare magazines keep theirs (rig 1, pocket 1, pack 2). A gun spends its rounds in that order (at hand, the pack, a teammate's, the pools). The AI and the raid bot prefer Get shells to a both-actions Reload. The Reload button's cost and tip name the source ("from a pocket", "from the rig", "the pack, both actions"), and the weapon panel splits the reserve rounds into at hand, in the pack and the team's for every gun. The tool checks a revolver's 1-action Reload from a pocket and the 2-action one (refused at 1) from the pack, a rig admin pouch feeding a 1-action top-up, a pump with shells only in the pack (both actions, or Get shells then 1), the magazine costs unchanged, the AI's and the raid bot's choices, the order of depletion, the weapon panel and the Reload order, and a save and load in the middle.

Hand over in a fight (`src/sim/combat.js` handoverTargets / handoverUnfaced / handoverRefusal / combatAct "handover" / HANDOVER_AP, `src/sim/inventory.js` moveItem's `across`, `src/ui/combat.js` the Hand over order and its chooser, `src/ui/inventory.js` handModal; `node tools/handovertest.js [seed]`, seconds): the battle bar's **🤲 Hand over** (Alt+H) trades with the teammate **right next to** the selected survivor (Chebyshev 1, the same floor) **in their view cone**: both paper dolls open side by side, and a thing given to the mate or taken from them (a pocket, a pack slot, a rig pouch, the hands, the off hand; click it, then where it goes, or drag it) costs the one acting **1 action** a move, either way; the mate spends nothing, and a mate with no actions left still takes things. A full slot swaps when the thing there fits back. Both units count what moved at once (`refitKit`): a gun handed into the other's hands is theirs to shoot (with the rounds it held), rounds and magazines sit where they are now. The acting survivor's own kit moves in the same window (the Inventory act, 1 action); the mate's own kit does not; armour, the helmet, the rig and the pack stay as worn on both. A mate next to them but out of the cone: the button reads **Face <name>** (the free Face act toward them) and Hand over then opens the window; more than one in reach: a chooser over the orders, or click the mate on the map; nobody in reach or no action left: greyed with why. A raid's search window keeps its free hand-over between neighbours (that is part of searching). The tool checks the targets by adjacency, floor and cone (facing away refuses, the free Face then works; an enemy, a downed mate never), a pocket → the mate's pocket move costing the giver 1 action and the mate nothing with both units' boxes refit, a stack merging and a full pocket swapping, a shotgun handed into the mate's hands (their pistol back with its rounds, the mate refit to the shotgun) and the giver's own gun given and taken back with its rounds and magazines, the refusals (the mate's own kit, the giver's own, armour, a third person, not adjacent, an empty slot, the enemy's turn, no action left), `moveItem` across places, a save and load in the middle, and the bar (the button, its key, cost and reasons, Face <name>, the chooser with two mates, the two-doll window's click-then-click, drag, look-only state and Own kit).

Home from a trip (`src/sim/parties.js` returnHome, `src/sim/teaminv.js` noteUnpack / unpackHome, `src/ui/teaminv.js` unpackModal, `src/sim/jobtrips.js` checkOutTool; `node tools/homeinvtest.js [seed]`, seconds): a team's supplies and finds go into the stores once, and the log says what reached them (or that a search found nothing; a fishing trip's catch against what came home). If its people still carry things on them (the pack, a bag in the arms, pockets), the **Home from a trip** window lists them: the pack, the bag, boxes of rounds and a job's tool ticked, pockets not; *Put away* moves the ticked ones into the armory (rounds into the stores, counted once), *Keep everything* leaves them. The Fish and Cut wood jobs carry their tools: a fisher takes a rod, net or spear out of the armory for the trip (no tool, no trip; one tool, one fisher), a woodcutter the armory's best axe if they have none, and it goes back on the shelf when they are home. The Needs food row is in rations with the days they last (1d under a day's food), and the gauges have a Sleep bar (the hours of sleep owed).

All ammo by calibre (2026-10-09; `src/sim/ammo.js` ammoBreakdown, calibrateDeal, payRoundsOf, capRounds; `src/sim/trade.js` dealRoundKey; `node tools/ammocaltest.js [days] [seed]`, seconds): the top bar's **Ammo** is one figure, every round of every calibre and type (launcher rounds too) and every arrow and bolt at the hub (its people's boxes included; a hardcore team on the road: the team's), with the breakdown by calibre and type, most first, on hover. Everything you get or give names calibres or settles into them at once: rewards and finds (the rounds the giver's kind keeps, or the common rounds of your guns), deals (the peddler, radio offers and deals, the council, war aid, tributes, ransoms, volunteers' offers, jobs' rewards: what you pay is the rounds your guns need least), trade routes (a calibre each), custom backgrounds and the new-run vehicle back (rows by calibre and type; an old generic number becomes the common rounds of the guns there, 9mm and 12 gauge with none), hardcore grabs and trunks (rounds for the guns they got). Generic ammo left in a holder of yours is settled twice an hour; old saves migrate (save.js ammoCal 2). The AI groups' own stores still keep one ammo count (f.stock.ammo): their side of every exchange is priced and spared out of it.

The arsenal balance (2026-10-10; `src/sim/ladder.js`, `src/sim/data-ammo.js` CAL_DMG, LADDER, ROUND_ECON, THEME_CALS, THEME_TYPES; `src/sim/data-guns.js` ARSENAL_GEAR, GUN_RARITY, ARSENAL_FINDS; `src/sim/gunfinds.js`; `node tools/arsenalbalancetest.js`, about a second; the tables in docs/weapons-overhaul-plan.md "Balance ladder 2026-10-10"): the damage ladder on an unarmoured target (.22 < 9mm < .45 < .357 < 20 ga < 5.56 < 7.62×39 < 12 ga buckshot < .30-30 < .308 < 12 ga slug < .50; 5.56 closer to .22 than to .308; no .22 round beats another calibre's base round; a 12 ga slug beats every .308 round, the .50 every round; no variant leaps a calibre 20% or more above its own), on a soft vest and a steel plate (.22 lowest, .50 highest, AP over the base round on the armour it is made for); every gun's damage within 85–115% of its calibre's, every 5.56 gun under every .308 gun, the M2 over every slug; every calibre has a gun and a magazine or an internal one, 20 gauge with buckshot, slugs and birdshot; over-unders and doubles load both barrels a Reload, single-shots one; every gun is found somewhere and has a value, weight, durability and rarity; every round has a tier and a value, and in all the places together a rarer round of a calibre turns up less than a commoner one (5.56, .308, .22 and 12 ga among the five found most, .50 least); the arsenal's finds (site searches, raid boxes, shelves, AI scavengers, gun safes) on their own dice, never the world's. Prints the ladder table.

Forming a team (`src/sim/teamform.js`, `src/ui/teamform.js`; `node tools/teamformtest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): **Form a team…** (the base screen, the Teams tab, the tile panel's base card, or right-click your base) opens a window with three tabs (keys 1 2 3): *People and vehicle* (everyone at the base with role, key skills, health, sleep, kit and kit weight, filtered and sorted; ↑ ↓ Space F S on the keyboard; on foot, the base's bikes one each, or up to four vehicles (cars and the base's bikes) with their seats, payload, fuel and condition), *Gear* (each picked person's paper doll and the armory: the inventory's own clicks and drags, spare magazines and a light from the armory) and *Supplies and cargo* (food, water, meds, fuel, rounds for their guns or by calibre, anything else in the stores, auto-fill for N days trimmed to what they can carry, armory items picked for the cargo). The weight against what they can carry, pace and the car's fill, and warnings (hurt, exhausted, low fuel, more people than seats) are on top. **Form team** sends them through `launchExpedition` to the base's own tile: they leave the roster and stand there as a *Field team* waiting for orders (no mission), its panel open. **Edit team…** (the team panel, the Teams tab, the base tile's team card, right-click) opens the same window for a team waiting on one of your bases: add people from that base or send some back in, swap the vehicle (the old one parks), change gear (between a member and the armory through `teaminv.js` load / drop off) and set supplies and cargo, all at once and refused when the team could not carry its load. The tool counts every item object, supply, person, car and bike before and after forming, editing and disbanding; checks the refusals (nobody, too heavy, an empty tank, not at the base, too few bikes, short stores), bikes taken and given back one each, Enter base, a save and load round trip of a waiting formed team, and the window's tabs and buttons. Several vehicles (`src/sim/vehicles.js` fleets, `MAX_VEHICLES` 4): `p.vehicleIds` lists a team's vehicles and `p.vehicleId` stays the lead one (old saves get the list on load, `state.fleetV`); seats, payload and tanks add up, the slowest car sets the pace, bikes among cars hold the convoy to the bikes' road pace, each car burns its own fuel (an empty tank is fed from the cans, then the other cars), every vehicle wears on the road and a breakdown hits one (left behind alone when the others seat everyone), a lost team leaves them all marked, Enter base parks them all, a battle map shows them all (the lead driven, the others parked: blasts wear and blow them up), found cars and recovered vehicles join when there is a driver; the other groups' raids bring up to four turreted vehicles (coarse). Closing the window after moving gear asks *Keep gear changes* / *Undo changes* (`gearSnapshot` / `restoreGear`), and *Undo changes* works while it stays open. The trip and move-out planners use the same picker (`src/ui/fleetpick.js`). Every vehicle needs its own person (a driver, a rider), checked in every picker, at launch and when a found or recovered vehicle joins; a team that loses people leaves the vehicles nobody can drive on its tile, marked, spare bikes first, then the worst car (`src/sim/walkers.js` shedSurplusVehicles, checked every hour). A vehicle lost on the way that leaves fewer seats than people makes the rest walk (`p.walkers`): the team goes at walking pace (the right-click and queue hours too) and a card offers to leave the walkers to follow on foot as their own team or stay together; a vehicle picked up seats them again. The tool also covers the trip planner's picker and a launch with three vehicles, one person per vehicle everywhere, surplus vehicles after deaths, walkers (pace, the split, seats again, save and load), 1–4 vehicles and a 5th refused, seats and cargo summed, the slowest pace, fuel per car, a breakdown leaving one behind, battle placement, Enter base, an old single-vehicle save, and keep / undo.

Roster bulk actions, the Watching panel and map notes (about 20 s): Equip best (only upgrades, items conserved, nobody unticked touched, the weight rule, packs keep their contents, people away skipped), Send to the hospital and Move to base (the convoy leaves and arrives after the road's hours, no road food means nobody leaves and no job is left behind), the roster's buttons and report, every kind of watched row and its status, gone rows, a horde out of sight shown where it was last seen, notes added, edited, deleted, hidden, drawn and in the tooltip, both saved and loaded (and older saves without them).

Bodies and the ground on the tactical map (`src/sim/ground.js`, `src/ui/ground.js`, `tools/tacticaltest.js`): in a field battle a body is searched where it fell (Search body, Shift+S: 1 action the first time, free after; the dead of any side, and of the walking dead only those that carried something), rolled once with exactly what the battle's end would roll for it, and opens the search window (the raid's, `src/sim/raidloot.js`, now on a field battle's loot layer too) to take its gun, rounds, magazines, armour, rig and pack contents; Ground (Shift+X) puts things down where a survivor stands (free) and picks them up from that cell or the next (hauling time). Piles are drawn on both views and worn by blasts; an enemy with no gun snatches one up. After a win the piles and what was left on searched bodies are bodies of the loot screen; after a retreat they stay at a death site; what your people carry loose comes home (team cargo or base). Nothing in two places, nothing rolled twice. The enemy's downed (knocked out, surrendered, tied, wounded; a third side's too) are searched the same way, in a field battle or a raid: what they carry goes into the search window and they are left bare-handed; a prisoner after a win keeps what you left on them (one loot entry, not rolled again), one killed after is the body already searched. Fire burns what lies on a burning cell once a turn (`launchers.js fireStuff`: paper, cloth, packs, food, meds, firewood, fuel and powder burn, the rest is worn by the heat, rounds cook off), in raids too; grenades, pipe bombs, Molotovs, smoke grenades, flashbangs, road flares, 40 mm rounds and rockets in a burning pile (inside a bag set down there too) or on a burning body go off where they lie with their own effect, as if thrown or fired there (blast, fire, smoke, flash, light: `launchers.js FIRE_COOK`, `cookFrom`, `cookBody`, `fireCookOffs`; two a burn tick, a log line each, the map's effect and its sound), for either side. The rounds in a gun (`src/sim/gunload.js`): every gun a fighter carries starts the fight loaded; a gun that changes hands keeps exactly what was in it (`it.loaded`: a gun put down takes its rounds out of the team's, a gun taken up puts them back; an enemy's gun off a body or thrown down by one who gave up has what was left in it; a gun found in a container is empty), for your people and the AI alike; a gun that comes home with rounds in it is unloaded into the stores. Vehicles in the iso view stand head high on a survivor (`VEH_TOP` in `src/ui/isoview.js`).

Raid loot by hand (`src/sim/raidloot.js`): each survivor carries their own share and finds up to their max load, the first look in a container costs the action and a second is free, supplies move as far as they fit (the rest stays inside; any part of a stack, 1 to all), gear goes in hands, pockets and the pack (a pack picked up raises the max load, a gun picked up is fought with at once), found ammo counts at once, a car's trunk and the bikes' racks are opened at the vehicle (a car also has to take home what everyone carries), the extracted bring what they carry into the team's cargo and the lost lose it, a raid saved mid-search loads, and hauling time (8 kg a turn free, then 12 kg an action; one heavy lift; putting back free; Take all stops when time runs out), handing things to a teammate next to you (free, their max load holds, a downed one too, a gun into their hands is fought with at once), the pile someone who bleeds out drops (searchable, not counted twice, lost if left), tools that count at once (flashlight, night vision, med kit), and headless raids (bots carry, walk back to the truck to unload when full, extract) never overload anyone.

Searching (`src/sim/search.js`, `tools/searchtest.js`): splitting a stack moves exactly n and leaves the rest (to a teammate too), never more than fits (and `supplyFit` says so beforehand), hauling time paid on what moved; a full search costs 1 action by day and in the dark and hides nothing; a quick rummage costs no action, shows 30–50% of the things (each one and over 160 containers), adds 5 Heat, once a turn, not on an opened or locked container; it pulls an unaware one of the dead and a guard within 7 tiles (not one beyond, not the dead already after you; 9 tiles fumbling in the dark without a light), and indoors not one behind a wall that puts it past the radius; the full search after it shows the rest; a locked container is 1 action a pick attempt at 55% + 4% a Stealth point − 15% a step, then 1 action to search; a raid saved mid-search keeps what is hidden; the search window's number to move (1, −, typed, +, half, all, Shift+click, Ctrl/Shift-drop) and its full-search button; the raid bot rummages at Heat 45 and searches in full at Heat 5; a rummage cuts a hand 6% of the time by day with no skill, 4.5% at Scavenging 10, 3% at Stealth 20 (the better of the two counts, up to 20), 9% in the dark without a light (6% with a flashlight), never 10%, and over 4000 tries each the cuts come at those rates; a cut costs 2–5 health, never down, and puts a cut (severity 12–25) on the survivor with a line in the battle log; the raid bot cut on every rummage still rummages once a turn and gets on with its turn.

Inventory: migration of old gear (backpack out of the tool slot, arm guards to the arms, old football pads + helmet split), equip / swap / unequip rules, pockets and packs (a pack carries its contents along), armor summed across worn pieces, carry limits by weight (40 kg each with nothing on; a pack adds its kg and weighs its own and what is inside; moving a thing between a person and the cargo weighs nothing extra, taking a pack off when the team is full is refused, a bicycle only goes in storage), looting bodies by hand and headless, leftovers at death sites, captives stripped, battles with and without extra armor, and a render smoke test of the inventory and loot screens.

Travel-time check: launches teams to varied tiles (on foot / by truck, each stance, full and low fuel, with what they pack: the load sets the pace, `src/sim/load.js`), redirects them, runs queued orders (move → camp → treat → move), and boat crossings (on foot, truck on a raft, truck left on the shore, then a move on the far side); compares the estimated hours with the real ones. Trips without road-event delays must match to the hour (a card that changes what the team carries or who is in it counts as an event), and the map's estimated position must reach the destination on the arrival hour.

Weight and load (`src/sim/load.js`, `vehicles.js`, `parties.js`; or `node tools/loadtest.js [seed] [autopilot days]`): every item and supply has a weight (pistol 1, assault rifle 4, plate 8, vest 4 kg; food 0.5, water 1, fuel 1, meds 0.1, ammo 0.02 a unit; a pack weighs what is inside); the band edges (Light ≤ 18, Medium ≤ 28, Heavy ≤ 38, Overloaded above) and their pace and rations (×1 / 0.9 / 0.75 / 0.55, ×1 / 1.15 / 1.35 / 1.6); Scouting 10 and 20 taking a quarter and half of it off; Bad Back (max 28 kg, loads feel 20% heavier), the assault pack (+10 kg, 12% lighter), Packrat (+8 kg); the pool shared so everyone is equally burdened, the team at its slowest member's pace, capacity = max loads less what is worn, `poolAtBand`; on foot a Heavy team is slower and hungrier and the trip takes exactly the estimate, water drunk on the way is the estimate's rate, resting is one ration a day; in summer the trip needs 1.3× the rations in water and drinks exactly that, and a trip into a heat wave drinks each hour at its day's thirst (at least 1.6 on a hot day), as the estimate counts it; on the move a lighter load does not speed the team up but a heavier one slows it at once; cars: full pace and normal rations for the riders, fuel ×1 to 60% full, ×1.3 at 100%, ×1.6 at 125%, overloaded 15% slower and breaking down more, and a light, a full and an overloaded truck each take exactly the estimated hours and burn exactly the estimated fuel; bikes: one stack per base, racks of 15 kg, 55% of the effect taken away, faster on roads, a bike each (refused without), the trip matches its estimate, no fuel, left and picked up again without fuel, a boat takes them for 1 scrap, home again they merge, a bicycle item joins them, they break down less, saved and loaded mid-trip they play on the same; plan rations by load; the same bike trip twice; and the autopilot over [days] in each style (how teams went: car, bikes, on foot by band; none leaves Overloaded on foot unless it must).

Moving on the State Map (`src/sim/parties.js` glideAt, hourCourse, arrivalsDue, moveEstimate, `src/ui/render.js`, `src/ui/pacing.js`; or `node tools/mapmovetest.js [seed]`, about 15 seconds): runs the main loop's clock frame by frame at 0.25×, 0.5×, 1×, 2× and 3× while a team crosses several tiles on foot (normal, stealthy, rushed), by truck on a road and off it, on bikes, with road blockages and while held by a card in the tray, and checks the map never draws it going back (no snap back to the tile it left when it gets somewhere before the hour is out or when the hour ticks), never jumps ahead and ends on the destination; then that the ETA is the arrival: a move ordered from the right-click menu at any minute of the hour (on foot, rushed, by truck on and off the road, on bikes; one tile and several; at 0.25×, 1× and 3×) gets there at the minute the menu said, the team panel's time left says the same all the way, the glide reaches the centre of the destination as the move ends (the team arrives at that minute, not when the hour ticks, so it never stands there waiting for the clock), a car ordered at :55 included (the report: 3 h shown, 2 h taken, the car there after 1 h and then standing); a stance changed mid-hour eases onto the new pace (no jump, no step back) and a clock speed changed mid-move changes nothing but the pace of the clock; a trip launched from the planner arrives at the minute its log line said; a road blockage met on the way shows its wait on the map with the minutes left ("Clearing road 25m"); a timed job ordered part-way through an hour lasts its hours from that minute; a convoy arrives at its minute; out of contact the map's estimate glides by the minute and arrives with the team; a team redirected on the move turns from where it is drawn (back the way it came or off to the side, on foot and by truck) on the ETA it was given; queued orders start and end at the minutes the queue said; 0.25× is a speed (key 0, the top bar); then that a plain move that got there raises no card in radio contact (a log line instead), still does under the autopilot and for a recon, and out of contact raises its orders card in the tray (no pop-up), which opens by itself if left 8 hours.

```bash
node tools/carrytest.js 7
```

Carrying the team's supplies (`src/sim/carry.js`, `src/ui/carry.js`; or `node tools/carrytest.js [seed]`, about 20 s): the pool stays one pool, placed person by person (`p.hold`): packs (2 water, 4 rations or 20 firewood a free slot), pockets (one ration or bottle each, small things only), the arms (up to 25 kg, the weakest fighter first) or the vehicle (a car takes it all, bikes' racks 15 kg); drinking and eating lighten the arms first, an item put in the pack pushes supplies out, fresh game is its own 1 kg stack; hands full in a tactical fight (shoot, melee, reload refused; carrying a body blocks melee too), Drop carried free (the load and a bag become a ground pile on the cell, `src/sim/ground.js`: out of the pool at once, so fire, the search window and the enemy act on it like any pile; a gun in the bag leaves loaded, `gunload.js`), Pick up load 1 action (the team's own loads from a pile on or next to the unit), Carry in arms in the battle's search window (a bag, a rig or a case off the ground or a searched body); the team's supplies taken off a pile with the Ground window go into the taker's arms too (hands full); fresh game set down stays fresh game (on the pile, picked up again, on the tile's ground after a retreat, spoiling there); a fight the team starts (a raid, an assault on a base, a hive or a growth, a rescue) begins with free hands: the loads go into the vehicle as far as it has room and the rest is set down where each one starts, picked up again after unless the team is beaten; the battle AI drops before it fights; won, what is left of the loads is picked up again before the piles become loot, retreated (on the map or by the numbers) what was set down stays on the tile's ground; the ground on the State Map ("Cargo left behind" with its contents on the marker, food spoiling, another team picking part of it up, Pick up all, what does not fit staying); a bag carried in the arms (a looted pack moved into the In arms slot weighs on its carrier with what is in it, hands full the same way, Drop carried sets it down whole, the AI drops it, won it comes back, retreated it stays on the ground, picked up into another's arms, put on and off, saved, unloaded into the armory at home); how much to collect (the box's numbers, Fetch water stopping at 12, Cut wood capped at 30 firewood, no quantity = the most that fits); save and load; 20 days on autopilot with every team's supplies always placed.

Team rules on the State Map (scripted cases): the carry limit (by weight: a walker with nothing on carries 40 kg, an assault pack 48 more), crew and mission checks at launch; a team with nobody left always ends as a wiped team (cargo at a death site, vehicle abandoned, both marked) and is swept every hour; cards never outlive their team or the person they are about, and at most one event card per team per hour; an interrupted trip goes back to the front of the queue and queued jobs keep their tile; team names and "Done" titles; gathering only on a shore and running out; boats planned again when the vehicle changes; a vehicle restarted from the fuel in its tank and the cargo left inside it; carry space for recovered items and markers cleared when nothing is there; no camp healing without food; old cards that cannot take stock below zero; one search at a time; supply-run edits and thirsty held convoys; town and group names.

Moving people between bases (`src/sim/moveout.js`; or `node tools/moveouttest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): the Overcrowded card (none while everyone has a bed, within the hour of one person too many, not again the next day, again at once when 2 more are over the beds, again after 5 days, never two open for one base, none while the autopilot plays); a team moved out (from that base's people and stores, the hub with a bed to spare after, waiting outside with no card in the way of the map pick, named *Moving out team*; sent to a free tile it gets the orders card and founds an outpost with the scrap it carried; a crowded outpost sends one too, only its own people, from its own stock); the Enter card (a team sent to an outpost is asked *Enter …?* instead of the orders card: yes, its people live there and its supplies and cargo are in the outpost's stores, "moved into" in the log; a team from the outpost asked at the hub, no: still a team waiting at the hub's tile with its orders card; a team at its own base is not asked); the autopilot (the Overcrowded card: *Leave it for now*; the Enter card: no at a full outpost, yes once a bunkhouse gives them beds, yes for a team sent there to move in).

```bash
node tools/basetiletest.js
```

Teams that end on one of your bases (`src/sim/staging.js` makeCamp, `parties.js` settleInto / returnHome, `moveout.js` enterOption / joinBase; or `node tools/basetiletest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): establishing a base disbands the team into it (the staging camp is not a base: when it becomes the shelter every team on its tile moves in, its people residents, its vehicle parked, its cargo and finds in the stores and armory, nothing left pointing at the team, saves mid-camp and after); a finished camp takes in a team that comes back with a plain Move order; the *Enter …?* card for a Move that ends on the team's own home too (Yes: home and disbanded, the autopilot's answer; No: it waits there with Enter base still offered; a team that moved out with no destination waits outside unasked); **Enter base** (an order: the team panel, the orders card, the Teams tab and the base tile's team card) for a team standing on any of your bases, its home included (one waiting outside after moving out, one that said No on the Enter card, one that ended a recon there, a queued Enter after a move), its prisoners to the cells there, the log line ("is home" or "moved into"), save / load with the team on the tile and after; the governor's hardcore start founding its shelter with the team disbanded into it. A team that founds a base, moves in or takes a base by assault unloads the same way as one coming home (`parties.js` unloadAt).

**Camps** (2026-10-09, `src/sim/staging.js`, `buildings.js` CAMP / isCamp, the base window's Camp tab in `ui/ui.js`; `node tools/campmenutest.js [seed]`, under a second): a camp is the barest base tier, a tier-0 base with `camp: true` (everything a shelter is: no well, no wall strength, the shelter's Heat and fire; barer: 6 bedrolls, one crop plot, no building plot and two utility plots, only a barricade, a rain catcher, crates or a barrel, a crafting bench, a water boiler, a cook fire or a drying rack, and its step up). **Set up camp** (any team of yours, anywhere it may stand, from the orders card, the team panel, the right-click menu or the planner): an hour, nothing carried; the team moves in (its people residents, its vehicles parked, its supplies and cargo in the stores and armory) and the base window opens on its **Camp** tab (its stage and what it can become, the people, Form a team, the teams at the camp with Edit team and Enter, the stores and vehicles, water, Heat, fire, **Strike camp**). Teams come and go as at any base (Form a team, the team inventory, Enter base, Return home). **Establish base** (no base at all: hardcore's first home, the exiles) is the same camp with its step up queued (`camp_up`: 20 scrap, 2 building materials, 20 work-hours, 10 scrap less on ruins) and becomes the shelter, then the base (`hub_up`); **Build outpost** is the same camp beside a hub with `outpost_up` queued. A camp's builders make the building materials its step up waits for from the scrap in its stores. **Strike camp**: everyone forms one team with the running vehicles (drivers and seats permitting) and what they can carry (water and food first), the rest is left as a cache, the camp is gone. A camp holds only its own tile, counts for no coalition land or victory, draws raids at 0.35× (looted, never kept or burned), its people heal twice a base's rate without a hospital; empty for 10 days it may be looted, empty and bare for 20 it is gone. The camp that is your hub gives way to a real base you get back (it becomes an outpost camp). Old saves: the staging camp (state.staging) loads as the camp base that is your hub, its work and materials its step up's, the team there moved in; tier-0 outposts are camps. The timed Camp order left the panels: "camp" is now only the governor's and the plans' internal rest task.

**The base window, construction and crops** (2026-10-12, `buildings.js` placeProblem, `farming.js` cropProgress, `woodcutting.js` woodNeed, `ui/ui.js` upgradeCard and the Construction tab, `ui/farming.js` cropsCard; `node tools/campbasetest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): one rule for where a building may stand (`placeProblem`: a camp's, a shelter's, an outpost's or the main base's), shared by `canBuild` and the Construction tab; the trauma hospital at any base (after the clinic, with Medicine 10 and Mechanics 4 and the manual); the Construction tab's Buildings, Utilities and Walls lists as folds with their own scroll box, ready first, and what this kind of base never takes folded at the bottom with no Build button; the Overview's upgrades (a shelter's or a camp's step up at the top, a base's next tiers with Upgrade buttons); a Crops section (Overview, and a camp's Camp tab) with what grows and how far, counted each midnight; a camp's or shelter's fire on bare ground and its heating with the fire out wanted from the woodpile (the Cut wood job opens there), and a boiler beside a lit fire burning nothing. Plots by stage (user numbers 2026-10-12): a camp 2 utility plots and 1 crop plot; a shelter 1 building plot (a bunkhouse, root cellar, infirmary or hunting cabin), 3 utility plots, 2 crop plots and up to two makeshift cots (6 scrap, no plot, +2 beds each); an outpost 4 crop plots; the main base 8 (one table, CROP_PLOTS, for the Construction tab and the farm). The governor builds a camp's and a shelter's yard (a rain catcher away from water, a cook fire, a drying rack, a crafting bench, makeshift cots, then a bunkhouse, when a shelter is crowded) before its step up takes the scrap. Base hunters hunt 4 hours a day (CABIN.huntHours), are picked by their hunting weapon, and take a hunting weapon for the day from the armory or from someone at the base who is not hunting or on guard (huntLoans).

A selected team's right-click menu (`src/ui/teamctx.js`, `src/ui/teamsel.js`; or `node tools/teamctxtest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): with a team selected (picked on the map, its panel open, or in move mode: `selectedTeam`), right-clicking a tile leads with that team (on foot or its vehicle, what it is doing, hours away) and its actions for that tile, grouped (Go, Base, Site, Land, Camp, Team), each with travel + job hours: move, look around, survey 3 tiles, boat across, scavenge or the city crawl, trade, rescue, assault, cull, hive, growth, find, vehicle, bodies, outpost or establish; forage, hunt, snares, fish, cut wood, fetch water; camp 8 h / 24 h / until told and a camp fire; field repair, listen in, claim, well; on your base enter, drop off, load, resupply; go home. Only what the tile and your knowledge offer (no fishing on dry land, no well on water); what cannot be done now is greyed with the reason in its tip (no rod, no route, no scrap, out of radio contact). A plain right-click replaces the team's plan (the confirmation box first when that drops orders under way); Shift+right-click or the menu's toggle adds to its queue (move there, then the job). Every item is ordinary team orders (`sim/orders.js`). The other teams and the hub's missions are folded below; with no team selected the menu is as before. The tool checks the selection, the lists on a forest, a river bank, open water, a town site, a known group's base, a horde and your base, the reasons, replace / append / asking first, and the HTML. On the team's own tile the Team group also has 🎒 Team inventory… (its panel's window, on its team tab); Shift+B on the map opens it for the selected team.


Team orders, movement and teams together (`src/sim/teammeet.js`, `src/ui/teammeet.js`; or `node tools/team3test.js [seed]`, `VERBOSE=1` lists every check, a few seconds): orders reach a team anywhere (no radio-range limit), and while `TEAM_INTEL_EVERYWHERE` (`src/sim/parties.js` `reportsLive`) is on its sightings reach the map live wherever it is (off: only in radio coverage); a picked-over site can be scavenged / raided again (it yields little); a team on the move is picked on the map, its panel shows where it is and takes orders, the selection follows it, and its right-click menu gives its own actions for the tile; any number of your teams on one tile can hand goods over (packed stays packed, found stays found, only what fits), move people and vehicles across (a vehicle with its driver, or alone to a team with someone free to drive it; every vehicle keeps a person on its team to drive it), split some off as a new team (with their share of the packed food and water) or merge, each step conserving people, items, food and supplies, keeping drivers and seats, and leaving no stray ids (`teamCheck`); the Teams together window, its ways in (tile panel, team panel, right-click) and its buttons. Vehicle-side extraction in a raid (any cell around the vehicle) is checked in `tools/evactest.js`.
