```bash
node tools/tripstest.js
```

Order templates, Repeat trip and undo (`src/sim/trips.js`, `src/ui/trips.js`, `src/ui/undo.js`): templates saved, updated by name, renamed, deleted and capped; a setup applied to another target (fit people kept, stand-ins by job for the hurt, the dead and the away, the vehicle by kind, Move steps of the old target dropped, supplies capped at the stock and flagged, a mission that cannot go replaced); a planner team remembers its setup and its retreat policy, lands in the recent trips when it gets home (other launches do not) and raises the home alert for a day; save and load keep it all, an older world loads with none; undo in the planner (steps, crew, a template applied, at most 20 steps), the build queue (queue, cancel, reorder; refused once started, finished or the refund used) and the Work tab (a cell, a deleted role), Ctrl+Z only on those screens and never in a battle; clean HTML with a handler for every button.

```bash
node tools/fieldtest.js
```

Field rules (scripted cases): zombie speed in battle (3 tiles an action, 4 at power 5+, survivors 5); Heat for teams that stand still (gain by activity, place, hive land, team size, night and stance; 8 an hour off on the move, none at a base; the panel text), trouble by Heat and danger (Monte Carlo on two seeds: a quiet night under 5%, three days in a city nearly always and more than once; the planner's look-ahead agrees; trouble is a conflict card and the team goes back to its camp afterwards); plan presets run in order and come home, nearby sites richest first; ETA and rations for whole plans (12 plans on foot and by vehicle: home to the hour when no road card delayed them, the team panel's queue estimate equal to the planner's); in summer, plans that run into a heat wave carry more water than food and the team drinks exactly the planned water, hour by hour at each day's thirst; Fetch water (a plan to a river or lake shore counts its 2 hours, fills the containers and brings the water home; away from water the order fails with a reason); "if … then come home" conditions (hurt, food, cargo; the report card can resume the plan; no rule, no return); the radio rule (no orders out of contact, orders while its card is open or in the hour you answered it, any time in radio range; a held team checks in again after 12 hours, an open-ended camp once a day); cards on finds (horde, rich site with *Search it next*, supplies short of home); the battle's end-turn confirmation.

```bash
node tools/autotest.js 120 all 1 2 3 4 5
```

Autopilot: plays 120 days on seeds 1–5 with the AI governor in charge (`src/sim/autopilot.js`; style `cautious`, `balanced`, `aggressive` or `all`), next to simtest's simple auto-player on the same seeds. Per run: days survived, population (now and peak), deaths, desertions, captives, buildings, outposts, trips launched, cards resolved, raids held off and hub stock, plus what the governor did (builds, crafts, gear handed out, treatments, search hours, supply runs, outposts, trades, role changes). Then a takeover check on the browser code paths: switch on while a raid battle is open, play 10 days the way the game loop does, switch off (auto-combat restored, teams untouched). Then a relay check: with the hub's station on the air, an outpost 9–11 tiles out and a known group past it (beyond the station's 12 + 6), the governor raises a radio relay at the outpost and the group comes into reach; never one beside the hub's station, nor at an outpost the station does not reach (it would not link). Flags crashes, cards left open after a tick and teams stuck with nothing to resolve.

```bash
node tools/balancesim.js 730
```

Balance census: 100 autopilot campaigns (seeds 1–34 × the three styles, trimmed to 100; or `node tools/balancesim.js [days] [--runs N] [--seeds 1-34|1,2,5] [--styles all|cautious,balanced] [--workers N] [--json path] [--quiet]`), each up to 730 days or until the holdout falls or wins, spread over worker threads (about 3 minutes on 12). It only watches the world between ticks (new log lines, cards as they are added, the memorial wall, survivor flags) and changes no rule, so every run plays out as it would without it. Prints Markdown tables per style: outcomes (fell / hive victory / alliance victory / survived) with the day spread and a survival curve; deaths by cause, desertions, what took the last people and the last 3 days before a fall; population over time; pressure per 30-day phase (group and outsider raids, horde attacks and warnings, deaths, desertions, serious illness, cards and decisions a day, overlap days, hungry or thirsty days); first death / raid / horde / break-in / overrun / desertion; days between big threats; cards a day (mean, p90, busiest day, distribution, most common titles); longest stretches with no card and with no threat; "overlap" (days with two or more of raid, horde attack, sickness, death, hunger or thirst within 3 days); hives, hordes and the dead on the map over time, horde attacks by result and hive assaults; raids by result and attacker; wars, collapses and the coalition; stocks over time and days out of food or water. Flags crashes, NaN, cards left open and stuck teams. `--json` writes every run's raw data (daily counters, samples every 30 days, each raid and horde attack). Findings: `review/balance-census.md`.

```bash
node tools/vehiclewear.js check
```

Vehicle wear and repairs (vehicle-wear pass 2026-10-10; `src/sim/vehicles.js`, `autopilot.js` VEH_REPAIR / tripRepair / stripDeadCars, `groupcare.js` CARE.VEHICLE, `trade.js` POOLED_KINDS; about 5 seconds): the governor's repair before a trip (car parts first, machine parts only for a car under 35% and past the machines' reorder level, no top-up past 50%, repaired up to the 30% a drive needs), a called-off trip giving back exactly what the repair and the refuelling took and nothing the trip had packed, the garage keeping its 2 best-kept cars out of the frost, AI groups' repairs and upgrades paying car parts first (2 machine parts each when short) and their hauls bringing car parts home, spare car parts packed for a drive of 5+ tiles, a car worth less than its parts stripped (never the last running one, always a burnt-out wreck). `node tools/vehiclewear.js census [days=730] [styles] [seeds] --json out.json` plays autopilot campaigns and puts every change to a vehicle's condition and fuel and to the parts in the stores down to the code that made it (no rule changed): condition lost and restored by cause, parts by purpose (gross, and what a called-off trip gave back), the repairs, why each trip went without a car, cars abandoned and recovered and why, trips by car / bikes / on foot, fuel; `report a.json[,b.json] [c.json]` prints two censuses side by side (findings: the vehicle-wear report, 2026-10-10).

```bash
node tools/poitest.js 42
```

Points of interest (`src/sim/pois.js`; or `node tools/poitest.js [seed] [--nocorpus]`, about a minute with the save corpus): the same seed makes the same lists on every tile (and the map's own dice are untouched, so old seeds build the same map); every eligible tile's count is within its terrain's table, bare and with a site, never over 4, and water, city and town centres and hive tiles hold none; every POI kind is a `LOCATIONS` type with theme tags, a raid area set, its buildings (or the street map) and a map size, the pizza place the diner archetype under its own name, a lone house or shed below a school, a gas station or a farm; the top POI is index 0 (the site on a site tile, no bare tile gains a `tile.loc`, else the richest, no extra out-ranking its site); hidden by the zone (the known zone is roads, their neighbours and the city and town footprints: there the top POI is known and at most 2 others hidden; in the unknown zone every POI of a bare tile; a site keeps its discovery class); the pools (a site POI's is `tile.loot` and `poiTake` lowers it, an extra's its own from its kind's richness, stripped at the floor, the container ledger made once); the town suburb rings (radius 1 or 2 per town, both kinds, land only, the centre and countryside sites untouched, the town's shops on them, the suburbs' loot); what the player knows (a team's survey finds reach the hub with its report, a snapshot lists the known POIs, stamps only move forward, the dev reveal shows everything, a hive rising or falling clears the tile); saves (only the changed POIs are written, loaded the same, a save from before POIs gets the seed's lists with `tile.loot` as its sites' pools); then the save corpus (`savecorpustest.js`, 2 days).

The State Map's moving pieces as low-poly figures (`src/ui/mapfigures.js`, drawn by `render.js` and `workermap.js`; or `node tools/mapfigurestest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): the heading maths (`yawOf`: east 315, west 135, down the screen 45, up 225, the hex roads' six ways; `dirOf` back again); every piece built on a stub canvas (your teams in every kind and with a vehicle's upgrades, the groups' parties by type and kind and their parties of one, the outsiders by kind, sub and escort, hordes of 2–7, herds, the workers in every job with every load, a team of one in every gait with a light, a cyclist), each painting pixels, none a NaN, its grid fixed by its kind at every heading and frame, a frame of the cycle apart from standing still and the heading changing the picture, the same look back from the cache; `drawPiece` putting the ground point where asked; and a frame of the renderer with a team on the road drawn as a piece at its tile facing the next one and a horde as a piece at its tile.

The inventory's figure turned by hand (`src/ui/figures.js` figureSpinImg, handOf, holdFigure, dragFigure, wheelFigure, turnFigure, spinFigure, spinFrame, frameOfYaw; or `node tools/figurespintest.js [seed]`, a few seconds, on a stub canvas without a DOM): a figure starts on the automatic spin (the clock's frame); a drag of 60 px turns it four steps of 15° (a degree a pixel, the remainder kept between moves) and makes it manual; the ticker's frame for a manual figure is its own yaw's and the clock leaves it alone; a wheel notch turns one step either way; a hold stops it where the spin had it; ↻ Spin (spinFigure) puts it back on the clock; the frame URL for a yaw is `figureCanvas`'s at that yaw rounded to STEP; every figure has its own state and the markup carries the img, the button (hidden until manual) and the tip; the module imports cleanly without `window` and the inventory, the battle's peek and the loot screen all show the figure.

Points of interest on the map and in the tile panel (`src/ui/mapsprites.js`, `render.js`, `poiview.js`, `ui.js`; or `node tools/poispritetest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): every POI kind and every `LOCATIONS` type has a map sprite painter of its own (not the generic fallback), the painters draw without a DOM (null canvases) and on a stub canvas, the eleven new kinds at their zone's size (town 20 × 17, countryside 18 × 15) and fade when picked over, cached by band; a POI's loot band follows its pool (the site POI's is `lootBand`'s) and only falls; the real renderer on a stub context: a known-zone bare tile's top POI is drawn and named once known and a surveyed unknown-zone tile's afterwards, not before; the tile panel is headed by the top POI's name, its Places rows and the hover text list the known places with their bands, say "POIs unknown" in the unknown zone and "possibly more unknown" with a hidden remainder, never a count; a site tile keeps its name and lists its extras; the dev reveal shows everything; two teams on one tile are drawn left and right of the hex's centre, one alone in the middle.

```bash
node tools/maptest.js 60
```

Map generation over 60 seeds for every world size (or `node tools/maptest.js [seeds] [firstSeed] size=huge` for one): the map has the size's dimensions; every map has its National Guard bases (off-road, away from the hub; two on a Huge map), every town and city has its own police station and auto shop (cities at least two police stations), the size's number of cities and about its share of towns, site names are unique (no numbered towns), every town and city is on the road network, the hub is a small town at least 6 tiles from any hive, hive density stays near a Normal map's (about 2 per 1,000 tiles), the most AI groups the size allows all get placed on their own sites with their own color and name, every site type has raid buildings, valid gear and a `known` rule (hidden sites, `discovery.js`: every site classified by its rule and the road, no town, city or landmark hidden, no hidden site known on day 1 outside the hub's view), and the same seed and size rebuild the same map (a Normal map is the same as one made without a size, as before sizes existed). Prints sites per zone and per type, hidden sites (by type, near the hub, in view on day 1), cities, towns, hives, group spacing and the generation time per size, then a table of the sizes.


The custom setup (`src/sim/worldsize.js`, new-run screen): the custom formula gives the presets' counts at the presets' sizes, sizes, groups and hives are clamped to their ranges, a custom 64 × 42 map is the Normal map, a set hive count of 0–20 is exact on Small, Normal and Huge maps, and every corner (32 × 24 to 200 × 140, 0 to 50 groups, 0 to 20 hives, Normal and Hardcore) builds, shows on the new-run screen, plays 20 days with the autopilot, saves and loads as the same size. Prints the build time and the time a day takes for each.

```bash
node tools/startgeartest.js
```

Starting gear (2026-10-08, user requests; `src/sim/startgear.js`, `src/ui/startgear.js`; or `node tools/startgeartest.js`, a few seconds): with *Customize* ticked on the new-run screen (not with Ironman) each crew card shows the colonist's load (kg of their own kit against what they can carry, the band), what their kit costs in points and a *🎒 Gear…* button. Its window picks every worn and held slot (hands, off hand, body, head, arms, legs, rig, bag) from the whole catalogue (grouped by category with the weight and the points, the description on hover) or *Nothing*, and what they carry is placed by hand (`s.startGear.carry`: a cell for each of the 4 pockets, the pouches of the rig or of a vest or carrier with pouches of its own, by kind, and the bag's slots): click a thing and then where it goes (a swap when both fit), *Add a thing…* puts a new one in the spot picked or where it first fits (a pouch that takes it first), *×* takes it out, *+ 2 spare magazines* for the gun in their hands; a thing that does not go there is refused with the reason (too big for a pocket, the wrong pouch, a pack in a pack, no bag), and a smaller bag or a rig taken off moves what no longer fits or takes it out and says so. The vehicle's back (`s.startVehicle`, on whoever pays): Hardcore's car the team flees in (whoever pays owns it, its kind by who they were; its room is the payload less the riders and what they wear) or a normal start's pickup at the hub (unloaded into the armory and stores on day 1), things and supplies up to `START_TRUNK`, *The dice's back*, *Empty*, *Paid by*. Picked things are new (a carrier with steel plates); a gun in the hands brings a full load and two more of its rounds (a launcher 2 rounds, a bow 12 arrows) to the stock; a colonist built on points (`s.startPoints`) gets nothing of the dice's kit on top (no replaced kit to the armory, no starter magazines or ammo box overflowing to the hub). Outside Customize the sim still takes picks with slots left *as rolled* (what a pick replaces to the hub's armory, Hardcore's replaced grab not grabbed, the start kit's two magazines with a picked gun). The picks ride every rebuild (crewSnapshot/applyCrew, both modes) and count as customized (Ironman reverts, Start refuses); Start leaves ordinary gear and nothing of the screen.

```bash
node tools/custompointstest.js
```

Customization points (2026-10-08, user request; `src/sim/custompoints.js`, every price in `CUSTOM_POINTS`; or `node tools/custompointstest.js [-v]`, about 10 s): with *Customize* ticked every colonist starts blank (skills 0, no traits, nothing worn or carried) and everything costs points from their max (*Points each*, 80 by default: the median rolled colonist and their start kit by this table, 70 in a normal start and 85 in Hardcore; the test prints it): a skill level 1 point up to 10, 2 up to 15, 3 up to 20; a trait by its tier (good ones cost, bad ones give back, at most a third of the max all told; at most 5 traits, never two that clash); a thing worn, held, carried or put in the vehicle's back what a new one is worth ÷ 6, plus half its power for what is worn or held in a fight, at least 1, a gun in the hands with its rounds; supplies their worth ÷ 6; a custom background's own supplies (and at a normal start its own car) to whoever has it. *Start from the dice* loads what was rolled (and shows what it costs, maybe over), *Clear* goes back to blank; a background picked loads what it gives, priced. Each card has a points bar (red when over, the bill in its hover text) and Start is refused while anyone is over (who, by how much). Unticking puts the crew back as rolled (the build kept for ticking it again). The test checks the price table, the calibration, the blank start in both modes (nothing of the dice's kit anywhere), Start from the dice and Clear, the over-budget refusal and the give-back cap, the trait picker's clashes, cap and tiers, the window's placing by hand (cells, Add, moves, swaps, refusals, a smaller bag), the vehicle's back (normal and Hardcore, its room, the dice's back, who pays and owns the car), a custom background under points, every rebuild keeping the picks and the points, Ironman, the seed's map and groups staying put, the screen and the trait colours everywhere, a save and load after Start and four days on the autopilot.

```bash
node tools/stacktest.js
node tools/ammoformtest.js
```

Stacks in a slot and ammo in its forms, 2026-10-10 (user requests and decisions; `src/sim/data-stacks.js` has every number, `src/sim/itemstack.js` the rules; about a second each). *Consumables* stack in one pocket, pouch or pack slot (`item.n`): small things 10 (cigarettes, chocolate, batteries, lighters, dewormers…), medium 5 (coffee, honey, candles…), bottles 3; grenades and throwables never stack, nor anything that wears down (bandage kits, tablets, toiletries). A stack weighs, is worth and is named by what is in it ("Chocolate bar ×3"), merges with the same thing (stowed, moved onto it, put in an armory, picked up in a raid; the rest stays), splits (*Split*: half into a spot of its own) and is used one at a time (Use, the evening treats, the armory's treats, a dewormer, the festival's bottles). *Rounds* in a slot are loose (up to 80 of one type) or boxed (up to five boxes of one kind); a box holds what a box of that load holds off a US store shelf (12 gauge slugs 5, buckshot 10, birdshot 25; 9mm 50, defensive 20; .22 LR 100; rifle 20; .50 BMG 10; a calibre added later by its class); *Open box* turns boxes into loose rounds (never back). *Bulk containers*: an Ammo can (one type, 500, goes in a pack: its rounds are the pack's in a fight, a shotgun's need Get shells), an Ammo carton (one calibre, 1000, bulky), an Ammo bag (any, 2000, bulky), weighed and priced by their rounds, a handful out at a time, opened into the stock at a base or in cargo like boxes. *Finds*: a raid container's, a gun safe's or an Army truck's rounds come partly boxed, the rest loose; now and then a bulk container (gun places far more than homes; common rounds far more than special ones, by the finds' own mix: `ammo.js commonness`). Stock records keep counting rounds as before. The new-run ×N adds one stack (a consumable's, five boxes). Old saves read as they were (an old 30-round 5.56 box is two of the 20-round boxes). `stacktest` checks the consumables, `ammoformtest` the box table, the slot limits, opening, the containers (in a fight too), the finds and saves.

```bash
node tools/newrunpointstest.js
```

The new-run screen's points, 2026-10-09 (user requests; `src/sim/custompoints.js` carPool / backgroundPoints, `src/ui/startgear.js`, `src/ui/backgrounds.js`; or `node tools/newrunpointstest.js [-v]`, about a second): the gear window's *×* boxes put N of a thing in at once (what they carry: each in a spot of its own, at most what fits and what their points pay for; the vehicle's back: at most its 16 things, its room and what its payer and the car pool pay for), priced N times; the backgrounds editor's *Total* (skill bumps priced from 0, traits, kit, supplies, the car at a normal start; the car's back shown apart), live; and the car pool: *Give leftover to the car pool* on a crew card gives what that colonist has left to the vehicle's back, which is paid from the pool first (only what it needs, shared by what each gives; the rest stays theirs) and by its payer after; a giver who spends more gives less and is never over; the payer is over (Start refused, the back named) when the back costs more than the pool and they can pay. Guns (the same day): every gun in the pickers shows its calibre and rounds ("5.56 NATO · 30 rds"); the gun in the hands takes parts in the gear window (a select a slot open on it, only parts that fit, "As it comes" or the factory part off), each adding what it is worth to the gun's points (a bigger magazine brings more rounds too); a gun carried or put in the vehicle's back comes with its magazine loaded (on the item, priced as rounds). A carried gun picked up in the window, or a gun in the back with its ⚙, takes parts the same way (kept as `{ key, mods }`; its loaded magazine follows a fitted one). At a normal start a custom background's car's back (to the hub with its car) is on the vehicle's back's bill, the car pool first. The test checks all of it, the pool through rebuilds, both modes and Start, Random back, Build, Randomize all and the dice, and the guns' parts and loads in both modes.

```bash
node tools/timelapsetest.js 240 42 8
```

Timelapse recording: plays 240 days on seed 42 with 8 groups (simtest auto-play), then kills one survivor a day until the holdout falls. Checks a frame for every day, memory (estimate and measured heap, scaled to 365 days), captions, decoding (sequential vs random access, and exact match with the live world sampled weekly), that the same seed without recording ends the same, renders every day's frame state with the real map renderer on a stub canvas (and, zoomed in, every 10th day with the terrain detail: decals drawn, none with *Map detail: Flat*, the same decals as the game's map), and builds the viewer's overlay HTML; ruins of razed bases match the live world in every sampled frame, and a ruin made, built over and made again shows in the replay (and is drawn) only on the days it stood. The memory budget is 2 MB on a Normal map and grows with the tile count on Large and Huge ones (`node tools/timelapsetest.js 200 42 15 size=huge`: about 0.8 MB for a year).

```bash
node tools/historytest.js 40 42
```

The Ledger's history and the run summary (`src/sim/history.js`, `src/ui/ledger.js`; or `node tools/historytest.js [days] [seed]`, about a minute and a half): a Balanced autopilot run with a test outpost writes one colony row a day and one per base; each base's stock change every day is its in − out + moved, the colony's stocks are the bases' sum and its moved column nets out; deaths, arrivals and population match the world; the outposts' daily surplus run shows as moved. The same seed with the history thrown away every hour plays out the same (dice, log, stocks), and reading it (rows, the Ledger, the summary) leaves the dice alone; the recorder's cost a day. Compaction (daily → weekly past 120 days, weekly → 28 days past 104 weeks: spans, averages, counters), a save made mid-day loading and playing on with the same history, an older save without one starting its history the day it is loaded; causes of death, the worst day, notable events; the Ledger for every base and range, the summary tab, the game-over and victory screens with the summary, the top bar's arrows and sparklines, the E key; no undefined / NaN / null, balanced tags, every data-action handled; the Help topic and this README.

```bash
node tools/wintest.js 1095 30 all
node tools/wintest.js 1095 12 balanced --tune HIVE.SITE_DAYS=9999:9999 --keep --log 40
node tools/wintest.js --force hive
```

Win conditions: plays long autopilot campaigns (days, then seeds as a count, a range `1-30` or a list `1,2,5`, then styles `all` or a list) in parallel worker threads and counts how each ends: hive victory, coalition victory (its own path: over half the land with every group still standing in the coalition), fallen or still going. Per victory: the day, a timeline every 90 days (population, hives standing and being built, hives destroyed, coalition members and land) and the days hives fell and groups joined; per style: deaths by cause and, for runs still going, what keeps each group out of the coalition. `--keep` plays on after a hive victory (as the *Keep playing* button) to see whether the coalition wins too; `--log N` prints the key events and last N log lines of one run per outcome; `--tune` tries balance numbers for that run only (`HIVE`, `DEFENCE`, `ALLIANCE`, `HORDE_SIZES`, e.g. `HIVE.BOSS_DAY=240`; nothing in the game changes). Per style it also counts the conquest (assaults, bases taken and taken back, groups broken) and what the groups took from you (bases, the hub, retreats, times with no base left), and the world: groups collapsed, new gangs, raider bands that settled, bases razed (yours, between groups). `--force hive|alliance|conquest|keep|loss` is a rigged scenario on seed 1 (every hive but one removed and a strong squad sent at the last; for alliance every group put in the coalition and outposts placed until it holds over half the land, with the hives still standing; for conquest every group but two broken by taking their bases, the two left in the coalition, outposts to over half) that checks each ending end to end: the game state, the log line, the timelapse caption and the end screen.

```bash
node tools/eratest.js
node tools/calibrate.js
node tools/balancesim.js 1666 --runs 10 --workers 8
```

Eras (`src/sim/eras.js`, `outbreak.js`; or `node tools/eratest.js [days]`, about two minutes): the calendar (112-day years, matching the season display), the eras (early years 1–4, mid from year 5, late from year 10, since the progression census of 2026-10-09), nothing growing in years 1–2 and every lever growing from year 3 to its cap (a new roaming horde bigger in year 6, a guard or a split part not; an aggressive group's strength, a peaceful one's unchanged; a new gang with the year's arms), the coalition's conditions met on day 30 but no victory (the Accord's clock idle until year 10, then its decay, no victory before year 10, the coalition's age), the hives all destroyed in year 1 and a mass of the dead coming over the border to build a new one with no hive victory, the hive victory only after the burn-out year and a season clear; then one autopilot campaign per style to the end of year 6 (day 658): no victory before year 10, hives and aggressive groups still in the state at the start of year 5, no crash, NaN or stuck team. `calibrate.js [fights=48] [seed=5] [--raw path]` (about 30 seconds): 27 matchups of field fights (people against raiders, soldiers, the dead, a mix, a hive's brood; ambushes both ways) each played on the battle map by the AI on both sides, fought out, and resolved by the numbers from the same start; prints both modes' wins and deaths a fight by matchup and fails when, over the grid, the numbers are more than 8 points off the map's wins or 25% off its deaths. `balancesim.js` runs over 730 days report by game year (`--tune ERAS.ACCORD.DAYS=56` and the like for what-ifs, as wintest.js).

```bash

node tools/progpickstest.js 42 --long
```

The progression picks (`tools/progpickstest.js [seed] [--long]`, a few seconds; `--long` adds two year-long Turtle runs, about five minutes): a member's land counts toward the coalition's share only after a year in the coalition and while it is at war with nobody (the Goals card's rows, the share once every member at peace has its year, the victory, a war with a group or with you, an old save without join days); the animal pen (its food by season, walls and keeper, the pasture it takes, the station, the winter outlook) and the AI groups' pens; the Turtle governor building walls, then pens, and staffing them; a walled Turtle hub with no trips living a year on its own land (`--long`); and the chapel, the Master workbench, storage crates, the radio station and the relay each wished for and queued in a scenario that calls for it.

```bash
node tools/conquertest.js 42
```

Conquest and the coalition victory (`src/sim/conquest.js`, `alliance.js`; or `node tools/conquertest.js [seed]`, about a minute): taking a group's base by hand (defenders, walls, your outpost with its land and stores, the stronghold radius) and with the Assault their base mission end to end (the card with its odds, the battle by the numbers, who stays to hold it, a hopeless assault that fails); a group's last base breaking it (its parties gone, no longer counted); goodwill with its pact partners, friends and enemies, infamy (what peaceful groups want before joining, their drift, the fade), attacking a coalition member (out, no pact); the coalition victory with only members left and the hives standing (and after Keep playing the hive victory still comes once; with nobody left you alone count); late gangs (a raider band without the goods to start a base does not settle, one with people and loot digs in and becomes a hostile gang, the log says so; gangs from the border keep coming with no cap; a gang that settles first has to be beaten, then the coalition wins at that moment); a group breaking at 3 people (also when war raids grind it down); razing (never by peaceful types, more with a grudge and far from home, a razed outpost of yours: gone, a ruin with a little loot, 5 scrap to rebuild, the tile panel, save/load; the razed hub; razing between groups; the warning card); raids that keep bases (between groups by keepChance, your overrun outpost kept, its people leaving as a team, the garrison, the card and the Goals line); retreat before a raid (the card's choice, the walk to the hub, the raiders taking it); losing the hub with another base and with none (the exiles: 20 days with every screen drawn, Establish base instead of Build outpost, the exiles' staging camp with its needs and work becoming a shelter with the stores as stock and then built up into the hub, the autopilot with no base for 30 days); a lost base's walls weak at first and patched up 60 days later, the tile panel, save/load of a base they hold and of a captured or retaken one, taking it back (same base, buildings as they stand), ruins coming back when the holders are gone; base battles' way out on the side away from the attack, and pulling out of a raid fight (the base is theirs) and of a horde fight (the base stays yours); an Aggressive autopilot year that storms and takes bases.

Occupy (`src/sim/occupy.js`, `groupworld.js`; or `node tools/occupytest.js [seed]`, about 20 seconds): an outpost of yours that is lost stands empty with its own buildings (wear and damage) and half its stores (no water), the log and the map say so; the right-click menu (Occupy, not Build outpost), the tile panel (what stands, the stores, who is inside) and the planner (who is inside, the crew's odds, no plan after it); the mission end to end (the card with its odds, the fight by the numbers, the base back under its old name with its buildings, the broken bunkhouse still broken, the stores, the team as its staff), save/load of the base moved into; a group's base taken over with its works as buildings (wall points to sections, fields, a well, a workbench) within the plots and battered by the months it stood empty; who it angers (the group whose base it was, a group whose claim round it you take; between groups, a claim taken costs relations); what is inside (the dead drifting in week by week, squatters some weeks, those killed in a failed attempt staying dead a week, a horde or a group's base on it blocking it); the groups' side (moving in costs people by what is inside, the old owner minds), the rot of an empty base month by month; save/load of an empty base; the governor's candidate (scored above founding an outpost, not one the map does not show, not a pact partner's old base) and its moving in by itself; Build outpost taking the groups' claims round it (the orders card and the planner say whom it angers, goodwill and a growth grievance a claim, the same reach as a group's new base); Abandon outpost (not the hub, not your only base, not under attack; the confirm card, the walk home with what they carry, the empty base with your old record, no "why was it lost", Reoccupy on the right-click menu, the rot); the governor giving up a doomed outpost in winter (and not while its stores or the hub's could carry it), a group spread too thin giving up its farthest base; the map drawing the empty bases you know of (in view or in your last report, not one you never saw) with their tooltip.

Hive birth you can see (`src/sim/hivebirth.js`; or `node tools/hivebirthtest.js [seed]`): the rumour near your land and none far from it, the massing card once the gathering is known (the planner aimed at it, Mark it the default, ignore), the marker's estimate from a fresh and an old look, the alert, the fight smaller than a hive's brood, breaking up a gathering before the mound (given up, the next site pushed back) and under way (set back, unfed: no hive sends to it, no wandering horde drawn in, fed again after the pause), a Cull horde team breaking one up end to end (the card's Break up choice, the battle played headless), the card when a hive you knew of rises, the autopilot's answer and its trip (strong, weak, out of reach, one at a time), save/load, and a year of Aggressive autopilot without an error. The harvest festival (`src/sim/festival.js`; or `node tools/festivaltest.js [seed]`, about a second): the morning of autumn day 22 once a year, holding it (food, bonfire, drink, the day off but for the guard, the roles back at midnight, mood, the afterglow, bonds, the map's fires), the small one and skipping it, put off when under attack or starving and dropped for the year, no hub, the autopilot by its food, stories over 40 festivals, save/load.

```bash
node tools/hivetest.js 24
```

Hives and hordes (`src/sim/hives.js`, `hordes.js`, `defences.js`; numbers in `hiveconst.js`): resource income by cluster size, horde costs and sizes and the horde score, 5–6 hives (over 8 seeds) with no hordes and 5 resources each at the start, hordes moving at most 2 tiles a day, merging and splitting (parts of at least 10), a build site every 120–180 days (hives and hordes alone, 570 days), the 30-score / 1,000-dead rule for a new hive with at most 40 of the dead going in a day (25+ days to grow), the Wallbreaker for 40 and the next one from that hive only 90 days later, hordes from the border, hold limits by wall tier and with defences, a horde held at the walls falling back 4 tiles and leaving that base be for 10 days, a break-in played out as a battle, toughness on a hive's land, infection fading after a hive dies, the win condition, determinism, and two 365-day autopilot runs without NaN. The hive tech tree (`hivetech.js`, `zombietypes.js`): growths placed within 2 tiles of their hive (never on water, a site or a base, one a tile), their costs and build times, income from pits and feeding grounds, the brood chamber's guard, the special dead a grown spawner unlocks (and the guard it buys carrying them), Wallbreakers only from a den, tiers by day and age (a late hive starts at 0), the hive's choices (young: a pit; attacked: a brood chamber; day 700: its spawners; day 1600: screamers, crawlers or the den; its guard first; saving for what it cannot afford), 300 days of growths, burning (one fire leaves 40 of 100, regrowth, the second fire, Molotovs, fighting its guard with a strong team), decay after the hive dies, the horde mix (merging, splitting, battle counts, the walls' weight, crawlers slipping past and the watchtower and floodlights stopping them, border hordes by era), determinism and save/load with growths; the special dead in battle (stats, an unseen crawler, acid through full cover, a brute smashing a barricade, a screamer calling the dead, one battle twice the same). Then the battles calibrated by the AI playing both sides (the argument is battles per row): storming a hive with a well-armed, a medium and a poorly armed team, and break-ins at a tier-2 hub by horde size, with and without a Wallbreaker.

The hive crawl (`src/sim/hivecrawl.js`, `src/ui/hivecrawl.js`, `src/ui/hivecrawlsfx.js`; or `node tools/hivecrawltest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): the den the same for a seed and a hive, another for another seed; the shortest way 2, 3, 5, 7, 9 moves by tier and more chambers the higher the tier; two routes that share no chamber in every den from tier 2 (12 seeds: no single chamber cuts the mouth from the core); dead ends, loops and the kinds; warmth rising at every step to 100 in the core; the brood stronger (count and power) the deeper and worst next to the core; a hive's depth by its tier over the years; the team's crawl (the mouth's brood in front, the answers, no moving on past live brood, a fight by the numbers clearing a chamber and thinning the guard, hours and the Alert, drafts and their misreading by Scouting, the way back); a save mid-crawl keeping the crawl, the den and the hive's memory and crawling on the same; burning eggs, the bone pit, tending the wounded, lying still, the brood converging and filling chambers behind the team at a high Alert, a chamber filling again with time, a refilled mouth in the way out; the core battle (the Hivemind rooted and reachable, never moving under the AI on both sides, tearing brood from the walls; its death kills its brood, wins the battle and destroys the hive); the autopilot's crawl step by step destroying young hives, a deep hive crawled partway with its cleared chambers left behind, the governor's own hive mission entering and destroying a hive; the AI groups' crawl (driven off partway, the chambers they cleared staying clear, a strong party burning the hive, a deep hive costing more); the window, its warmth tint, the sound's levels and the mixer group, the game's modal, the Help topic.

The hive crawl's follow-ups (`node tools/hiveplustest.js [seed] [trials=16]`, a few seconds): lights (on by default with a light, the drafts misread half as often in them over 40 visits, a move +1 Alert and creeping past 1 harder with them on; the cave battles dark (`b.dark`): 12 tiles with a headlamp, 9 without, the brood's sight untouched; the brood × 1.075 by the numbers with half the team in the dark); road flares (the recipe, the chamber beyond lit exactly, Alert +5, no time, the lit passage's draft true whatever the dice, none left); listening (a good listener mostly exact, a poor one mostly guessing, the wrong kind now and then and fumbling; what a good listener makes out is right; the same listen in a loaded game, an hour, once a passage a visit, the window's odds; a fumble raising the Alert and the brood beyond waiting, spotted); the Hivemind's second phase (below half its health: the line, 3–5 pillars down, never moving, its sprite, the hive pulse hearing it, faster and louder) and the core still winnable (the rifle squad with headlamps at tier 2 and tier 4, and without lights); the hoard (the same for a hive and the world's dice untouched, worn items, an old fat hive's richer, the team's cargo and the rest left at the hive marked on the map, the log; a group's arsenal and stores); the crawl's record (none before, deepest, mapped, the Goals card's line, the autopilot listening with a good scout, save and load, an old save, the core found, the State Map's hive tile, a war party's depth); the window (Listen, Flare, the lights and flares, a flare's light and what was heard on the passages and the map, dousing), the autopilot throwing flares without a listener, the Help topic; the Hivemind's weak spots (flames next to it burn it and keep its brood in the walls; a flashbang stuns it: no pulse, no lash, no brood, its flesh open; the battle AI steps into reach and throws Molotovs and flashbangs at it; the rifle squad at tier 4 against a guard of 120 with and without 2 Molotovs and 2 flashbangs (38% → 94% over 16 battles, five prepared 88%); the AI's outlook counting the fire and bangs in hand and the Hivemind burning, what is thrown out of hand, the Broodmother dazed by a flashbang and losing her actions, the brood it called shying from a Molotov, the autopilot queueing Molotovs and flares at the hub only while short, a group making Molotovs from its fuel (none without), the hardcore military's radio speaking of a strike; the core's odds by the numbers dark, lit, lit and prepared; a fight by the numbers spending them; a war party's arsenal paying less at the core and using them); only the Hivemind's death kills a hive (destroyHive called only by the core and a war party that paid for the core, no fire on the hive card or the road's hive, an old save with a fire campaign and a fire trip loading and playing two days); the autopilot's gate (the core's odds by the numbers against the map within 20 points on average over five squads, lights counting) and its hive kit from the armory; the mouth's M on the crawl map. `node tools/hivecorecalib.js [trials=24] [lights=1|0] [tiers=2,4]` (or `--fit [trials]` with `FIT_SAVE=file`, `--refit file [numbersK]`) plays the core battle with the rifle squad (6, assault rifles + plate, Shooting 10, guard 80) by tier (`HP_P2=0`: without the second phase, `HP_DARK=0`: without the dark): 100 battles each, before (no dark, no second phase) tier 2 98%, tier 4 73%; now with headlamps 99% and 72%, without lights 84% and 51%.

Volunteers (`src/sim/volunteers.js`, `src/ui/volunteers.js`; or `node tools/volunteertest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): the answer by points (a friend with a trade pact says yes, more people and longer is a bigger ask, an assault's danger, a no from a cool group turned into a yes by an offer of food or a favour owed, the refusal remembered for 3 days, below goodwill 30 and hostile refused outright, a group keeping 8 at home, a horde at their base weighing against it); a yes (goodwill −2 with nothing offered, their people away), armed from their arsenal, walking to the team and joining it, found by id but not in your roster, carrying and eating, the food and their own guns' rounds they bring, going home at the span's end with their kit back in the arsenal; the battle map (volunteer units on your side, your move and shot orders refused, ending your turn they act on their own, the AI playing your side still works, they fought beside you, they pull out when your people are all out); the odds by the numbers counting them; deaths (in a fight −3, of thirst in your care −10, remembered, not on your memorial wall); going home when goodwill falls, when none of your people is left (the team is done) and when the team comes home; a save and load with volunteers on a team and your people lent out, playing on the same; a group's ask (the card's choices, lending, their strength counting your people, back after the span with a share, declining costs goodwill, coarse fights hurting some over six loans); groups lending each other (a friend sends fighters to a group with a horde at its gates, the lender's people away, home after the span); the hive crawl (a coalition member stops thinning the brood from outside once its volunteers are in the crawl, volunteer units in the core battle, volunteers fighting the crawl's fights); the governor (Diplomat sends people, Turtle declines; a weak squad for a hive asks allies).


A team on a hive's mound after the card (`src/sim/hives.js` hiveArrive, `conflicts.js` contactOn; about 2 minutes): backed off and given new orders, a team still on the mound's tile is not ambushed by that hive's own guard (a team that came on a plain move is), the mark drops off the tile and survives save/load; the governor's hive squad comes home (or the hive dies) instead of searching the hive's land (the Aggressive autopilot's first hive trip on seeds 1, 4, 8, 2, 3 within 90 days).

```bash
node tools/hordecensus.js 365 all 1-20
```

Horde census (the pacing of `hiveconst.js`): autopilot campaigns (days, then cautious / balanced / aggressive / all, then seeds as a list or a range; default 365 days, all styles, seeds 1–20; the runs are spread over worker threads). Per style and for all runs together: the hives at the start and at the end, build sites picked and hives built / destroyed, hordes bought by hives and from the border, Wallbreakers, groups left; then per 30 days (only runs still going): hives standing, hordes bought, border hordes, the dead on the map, horde attacks on your bases (sent by a hive / wandered in, held at the walls, break-in battles won, overrun), people killed in them, the mean horde size against the mean hold limit, attacks with a Wallbreaker, sites and hives built. Then the first horde at a base and at the hub (median and quartiles), the nearest hive to the hub at the start, deaths by cause, repeat hits (the same horde again within 10 days), days between attacks on the hub, how many build sites you saw before they were done, and the build-site timings (picked → building → hive standing: the window to strike). Tuned for: first horde at the gates around day 25–35 (medium, held by the starting walls), one about every three weeks until day 120, two a month by day 150, about weekly by day 330, a new hive every ~150 days if the hives are left alone (one build site at a time; about four weeks from the first gathering to the hive standing), Wallbreakers from day 200. The slower spread (was 50–80 days, two sites, Wallbreakers from day 120) came from `tools/wintest.js`: with it the autopilot wins the hive war in some runs (1 of 20 Balanced, 4 of 20 Aggressive in three years) instead of none.

```bash
node tools/stealthtest.js 42
```

Gun attachments (`src/sim/gunmods.js`, the table of numbers in its header; or `node tools/gunmodtest.js`, about 12 s): sights (red dot +4 aim, holographic +5 and +3 up close, a 4× scope +3 tiles of range and less fall-off but −8 up close, a laser +6 up close), muzzle devices (a 9mm or rifle suppressor: shots 35% / 40% as loud at ×0.95 damage and a tile of range; the oil-filter suppressor, now a muzzle attachment: half, 12 shots of wear; a muzzle brake +3 aim and a pistol compensator +2, both louder; a shotgun choke: +2 range, −4 up close), magazines (an extended 9mm magazine 24 rounds, a drum ×2 for the carbine and the assault rifle at −2 aim, a shotgun tube +2) and under the barrel a laser, a foregrip or a bipod; one per mount, only on the guns each fits (no suppressor on a revolver or a shotgun; bows and the pipe shotgun take none). Fitted in the inventory (select a gun: Fit / Remove; select a part: Fit to the gun in hand), they count in battle (`shotOdds`: aim, up close, range, fall-off, damage), in a shot's noise (earshot, alerts, raid Heat, the map's gunfire Heat) and on a hunt (its Heat, the base's hunting noise, the chance the dead turn toward the shots), add their weight and worth to the gun and are sold and looted with it. Found at gun stores, police stations, the Guard, prisons, hunting lodges, cabins and ranches, army wrecks, malls (searches and raid containers, their own dice), on bodies, on traders' and merchants' tables; enemies' guns carry them by kit (soldiers 65%: holo and red dot sights, scopes, suppressors, a drum now and then; guards lasers; militia and fighters scopes and bipods; raiders and survivors cheap or makeshift parts) with the same effects, shown in their weapon's name and looted with the gun; starting guns come with 0–2 cheap parts (by the run's seed); the autopilot fits loose parts from the armory. A carried oil filter (older saves) still halves a 9mm or rifle shot in battle.

Long guns, bipods and shooting on the move (`node tools/longrangetest.js`, a few seconds): a 5.56, 7.62×39 or .22 round drops past its reach (10 / 9 / 9 tiles; a 9mm, .45 or .357 past 8: −2 aim a tile and ×0.85 damage, scope or not, and no part pushes the best range past it: `gunmods.js ROUND_REACH`), so the hunting rifle, the marksman and sniper rifles beat a scoped AR-15, AK or carbine at 15 and 20 tiles; a scope fitted to a gun helps a hunt (`hunting.js SCOPE_HUNT`, ×1.15 big game; the hunting rifle's own scope is already in its numbers); a bipod (fitted, or built into the machine gun and the .50) gives +8 only when the gun rests on half cover (or a wall shot over: a palisade from its walkway, a barricade) next to the shooter in the shot's line (`combat.js restCell`, both sides; the AI scores posts the same way); shots from a vehicle that drove or turned this turn lose aim (`combat.js RIDE`: the turret −10, its overwatch in the other side's turn right after too, whoever drove −25, a rider −15).

Gun builder (`node tools/gunbuildertest.js`, a few seconds): every gun has a platform and a drawing (`src/ui/gunart.js`), every slot's anchor sits on the drawing, the window has one box and one leader line per slot (nested slots with their parent), fitting and taking off a part moves it between the armory and the gun and back, and the stats shown are `gunStats`'.

Item right-click menu (`src/ui/itemmenu.js`, its rules `src/sim/itemacts.js`; `node tools/itemmenutest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): right-click an item in any inventory view (the inventory, the team inventory, the loot screen, the base's Armory tab, the search window and the kit seen from a battle) for what can be done with it there. Out of a fight: a magazine Unloaded stays empty (its badge, a fight's spares) until Loaded again (with a round of its own), a gun's Reload with sets the round it takes the field with and Unload puts the rounds kept on it into the stores, eating and drinking from the stores and from the pack of food and water (raw food and un-purified water refused), and the rows for a gun (Reload with, Modify, Repair, Salvage), a magazine (Unload, Load with, Fit to), a treat, a food stack and the pack. In a fight: each row with its cost in actions (greyed out with the reason in the enemy turn, with too few actions, for someone else's fighter); a treat used (1 action from a pocket or the rig, 2 from the pack), a bite from the pack giving back the aim hunger cost, Reload, Use meds, and a gun part fitted and taken off in the gun builder over the battle (an action each, from what the fighter carries; the gun's numbers count at once).

Gun art (`node tools/gunarttest.js`, under a second): every gun has its own art spec (`src/ui/gunspecs.js`; the ones without one are listed, and drawn as a look-alike by what they are), every spec (the pre-written ones for guns not in the game yet too) renders in all three forms (the big side view, held in battle, dropped by a body) in real colours, no two guns draw alike in any form, every slot's anchor sits on the drawing and a part fitted there draws at it, a fitted stock or magazine replaces the gun's own, and the drawings are cached; the parts fitted show on the gun held in battle (an enemy's kit parts too), a new part is drawn by its kind from its slot and name (`src/ui/gunparts.js`), every gun item has a thumbnail (its tile, sized in rem), every gun's muzzle is on the side it faces in all four view rotations (a suppressor moves it out), and every finish of every gun renders, chosen on the item's own dice (an old save's gun keeps its first).

Item icons (`node tools/itemarttest.js [--family name]`, a second or two): every item that is not a gun and every supply has its own 16 × 16 pixel drawing (`src/ui/itemart.js`, drawn in `src/ui/itemart/*.js`: food and base supplies, ammo boxes and loose rounds, gun parts and magazines, melee, armour and bags, tools and books, valuables), each renders in real colours and is big enough to read, no two items are within 6 pixels of each other, and the inventory tiles, the armory cards and rows and the supply icons show them. To look at them: `node server.cjs`, then http://localhost:5173/tools/itemartpreview.html (`?family=food` for one family), or a screenshot with `NODE_PATH=$(npm root -g) node tools/itemartshot.cjs [family] [out.png]`.
Rescue (`src/sim/rescueraid.js`, `ransom.js`; `node tools/rescuetest.js [seed]`): getting your captives back. The tactical rescue raid on the group's base map (the cell by jail tier with its locked doors, the jailer with the keys, their armory from their arsenal or kit, captives tied up and unarmed inside); captives escaping on their own (the ropes, a pin found in the cell at about 30%, picking with and without a tool, downing the jailer bare-handed for his keys and freeing the others); weapons off a dead guard (armed at once, the body left empty) and out of the armory (gone from their stock, an arsenal shrinking, kept after extraction); the guards (a downed armed captive taken back, stripped and re-tied in the cell, the gun back on their rack; the armory retaken and a guard posted; a hurt captive alone among three guards giving up); whole raids played by the AI on both sides (the captive freed and home), a failed raid leaving them captive, the by-the-numbers path and the card's choices with and without the battle screen; escapes over time likelier with the keys; ransoms by radio with raiders (the price by skill and hostility, haggling once, a prisoner exchange, a meeting point, the handover team and the exchange, a friendly group bringing them home, zealots refusing below goodwill), the betrayal rate (about 15% with an even team, lower with a strong one, over 120 handovers) and the ambush on the road (on the map and by the numbers); the governor (ransom when rich, a raid when poor but strong, waiting otherwise; agreeing and sending the handover team); a save round-trip with a deal and a rescue raid mid-battle.

Stealth kit, prisoners and the deploy step (`src/sim/stealth.js`, `prisoners.js`, `deploy.js`): takedown odds (30 + 2.5 × Stealth, a club +20, from behind +15, from cover +10, soldiers −15, an aware but staggering target −15; a non-lethal blow on the aware), Subdue knocking out where Melee kills, the knocked out waking in 4–6 turns unless tied up; a won fight's prisoners walking home with the team (10% slower each on foot) and into the cells, a fight at a base sending them straight there, *Leave them* (goodwill +2); cell costs (1 food and 1 water a day) and escapes by the day (guarded cell, unguarded, shed: Monte Carlo against 0.4% / 3% / 8%), starving; questioning (their bases onto the map, the group known, people and stock, once every 3 days, souring them), ransom (paid or refused over 60 asks, stock and goodwill), trade back and release (goodwill, their head count), execute (mood −6, Brave −2, goodwill with them and every known group); recruiting (the rate, months to a recruit card, taken in; zealots far slower); rescue raids (the daily chance and its modifiers, a Monte Carlo against the expected count, the warning card's three choices, beaten off at the walls or freeing them); throwables (carried counts, 1 action, a bottle pulling the near guard but not the far one, the guard walking over to look, too far refused, firecrackers pulling the dead); the suppressor (half the noise, a far guard not woken, wear per shot, gone when worn out, no suppressor on a shotgun); night raids (enemy sight 7 and 5, your sight 7 / 9 with a flashlight / 13 with night vision, detection by day, at night and with a lit flashlight, half range from behind, searching in the dark); a body seen raising the alarm, the same body behind a wall not found, Drag (3 tiles for 1 action, the body behind the dragger); the deploy step (the attack side from the State Map in all four directions, the turned map, valid default posts, the dead on that edge, the gate holder and the towers' shooters, no placing outside the walls, *Hold the gate* swapping places, *Default positions*, floodlights aimed at a third of the approach, headless battles skipping the step, the open yard without walls); the new recipes; the autopilot's rules (no prisoners without a cell, Aggressive subduing for intel up to its free cells, dealing with them by style); determinism of a night raid with the kit and a deployed break-in; and 120 days with prisoners on each autopilot style without NaN or negative stock.

Choices before a raid (`src/sim/raidplan.js`, the window `src/ui/raidplan.js`; `node tools/raidapproachtest.js [worlds]`, default 40, about a minute): every side a raid map offers puts the team and its vehicle on that edge of the same map; going in from a side (enterFrom) places the team on that edge and closes the plan; Leave (callOff) ends the battle at once with nobody hurt and nothing searched (a raid as called off: the team awaits orders and no more hours pass; the vehicle comes back); the scout's odds are the formula (checked by hand for Stealth 4 / Scouting 4 by day, the dead only, at night, with binoculars, with night vision) and over 160 scouts the share that come back unseen matches the odds shown; a success marks every enemy within reach (ghosts) and the fog's memory, a failure alerts every person of theirs and adds Heat (and sometimes hurts the scout); the scout takes an hour; waiting for night runs the clock to 20:00 and the same map is a night battle with Heat 0; the battle screen's AI playing your side scouts with a good scout and the raid plays out, the battle AI elsewhere goes in as things are; a battle without the plan has none; a save keeps the open plan and the scout's marks; the hour-by-hour search's scout and night.

Jails (`src/sim/buildings.js` family "jail", `prisoners.js`, `groupjails.js`, `groupworks.js`; or `node tools/jailtest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): the three tiers and their costs, the upgrade path (plots, Mechanics 2), no jail no prisoners (a card each: release, execute, ask to join, hold at a base with room; walked there by an escort), the autopilot's own pick, a team's prisoners booked into a base with room, escapes strictly ordered by tier (guarded, unguarded, at work, with a smuggled pick), a rescue by tier, a jailhouse guard post, mood and work (×0.6–1.15, the escape bump under 25, sabotage), the tier's mood and winning over, Cut wood and Haul and sort scrap, older saves' shed prisoners kept, the governor's jail wishes and build; the groups' jails (built after capturing, raiders early), capacity and the no-room fates by type, your captive's escape by their tier, the no-room cards (let go, killed, a ransom), the rescue map's cell block, their labour, exchange, ransom back, a sale to the military who free them, your captive sold on (the card, the group and tile follow), and save round-trips.

Non-lethal weapons, taking people alive and surrender (`src/sim/nonlethal.js`, `data-nonlethal.js`, `ui/nonlethal.js`; about 6 s): the taser (odds falling off with distance, nothing beyond 4 tiles, a healthy target stunned for its next 2 actions then −20 aim, one under 40% knocked out, 2 cartridges then a reload from a cartridge pack, the cartridges written back after the battle), the stun baton (stuns 1, never kills the living), pepper spray (the cone 3 tiles out and about 90° wide, the two in front blinded and the one to the side not, −40 aim, overwatch dropped, a spray used, the preview), the dead unaffected (taser, spray; a non-lethal blow kills one of the dead), beanbag and rubber rounds (`AMMO_TYPES[type].nonlethal`, by the hit or the shooter's loaded type); your people knocked out (not bleeding, waking in 2–4 turns at 15% with one action, tied up they do not, the enemy holding the field takes them captive or, raiders, finishes some); each group type's wish to take people alive winning / even / losing (police and guards about 87%, the military 64%, communes 75%, raiders 16% and 64% for a skilled target, zealots never, everyone lethal when losing), guards with tasers using them, your AI with Take them alive; surrender (a hurt guard alone near your people giving up, never zealots, the weapon dropped, Tie (take prisoner) and into the cells, walking off untied after 3 turns, Execute and its mood cost, your own surrender taken captive by soldiers and refused by zealots and the dead); loot through itemcond (police stations, armouries, prisons, gun stores, guards' bodies); by the numbers (the share of a beaten group taken alive in AI raids by the winners' type, held people going home, joining or moving on over 60 days through the groups' jails (groupjails.js), a decisive win of yours now and then taking one or two prisoner); a battle saved mid-fight and played out the same from two loads.

```bash
node tools/enemyaitest.js 20 7
```

The enemy's nerve and its retreat (`src/sim/nonlethal.js` SURRENDER, `src/sim/siege.js` coverSmoke, `throwables.js` FLASH_KIT, `arsenal.js` ARSENAL.NONLETHAL; or `node tools/enemyaitest.js [battles per setup=20] [seed=7]`, about 20 s): giving up only when the side has lost half its people and is outnumbered two to one (2 left against 3 of yours: nobody in 40 turns; against 4: the badly hurt may), militia holding out longer than guards, zealots never; by the numbers a decisive win over 6 militia takes about 0.3 prisoners a win (was 0.9) and AI raids take fewer alive from groups whose kit gives up less (communes, zealots); auto-played battles against militia, guards, raiders and soldiers with surrenders a battle next to what they were before (militia 0.6 → about 0.13 at their will of 0.65); smoke grenades in their kit by kind and in the groups' starting arsenals; the turn the enemy breaks, one of them pops smoke between them and your people (a log line), once, not while they fight on, remembered by a saved battle; a third-side group running from the dead does the same; your side under the AI pops smoke before it sounds the retreat. Three-way fights: a beaten group of travellers gives up to the raiders (2 of 4 left against 4), not while it stands nor 2 against 3; with the raiders still standing at the end they are led off, with them beaten your win frees them (they take up their weapons again); an enemy beaten by you and a squad of soldiers together gives up to the nearest, the soldiers, who keep it unless you tie it first; after the fight the soldiers taken go into the enemy group's jail, the freed earn goodwill with their group and the soldiers' prisoners go into theirs. Behind a smoke cloud a fleeing enemy is gone at 5 tiles instead of 9 out of sight, unless you chase into the cloud. Between groups: a military group restocks smoke while short (a day at a time, SMOKE_RESTOCK) and a side beaten by the numbers with smoke spares one person a grenade, at most 2 (smokeCover: AI raids and war raids).

Base defence in three tiers on two maps, verticality and the battle fog of war (`src/sim/deploy.js` the wall map, `src/sim/interior.js` the yard and buildings map, `combat.js` heights, sight and battering, `defences.js` the tiers by the numbers; or `node tools/defencetest.js [seed]`, about 10 s): the yard map generated from a base's real buildings (the same base lays out the same map; its main house, bunkhouses, infirmary, workshop, storehouse and water tank are on it; upper floors with stairs, flat roofs with ladders, barred doors and windows with hit points; barricades, sandbags and buildings added show up; every door and the wall reachable from the back gate); the wall map (wall sections and the gate with hit points and battered by a horde that broke through, the walkway at height 1 with ladders, towers at height 2, shooters posted on them; a section battered down is a breach; the dead cannot pass the barred gate, your people can); the breach (4 of the dead inside the wall at the start of your turn: on to the yard map, the same people with their health, the downed still down where they lay, the base's ammo, the attackers inside brought along and the rest waiting outside to come in through the openings, a redeploy away from them, people in the buildings, fought to the end); falling back by order (the attackers move next; nowhere further to fall back to); the buildings (a barred door keeps the dead out until battered in, full cover inside against the yard, windows, the battle AI taking people indoors for the last stand); verticality (+2 sight a level, +5 aim down a level up to +10, half cover against shooters below, a watchtower's full cover, sight over a wall in front but not into the dead ground behind one near the target, climbing only by a ladder at 1 a level and 2 for the dead, a blow struck up −10); the fog of war (an enemy behind a wall unseen, its cell fogged, not targetable, a last-seen ghost; a manned watchtower seeing over a wall; at night plain sight 7, night vision and a thermal 13, the dead 5; binoculars +3 by day; floodlit ground seen at night); the tiers by the numbers (walls, a tower and the gate raise the wall tier, barricades and sandbags the yard, buildings the last stand; holdCheck's yard and buildings limits; raids held at the wall, pushed back from the yard or overrun; an auto-resolved base battle reports its tier); the AI falling back with half the team down on the wall and a break-in played out headless on both maps; saving and loading on each map playing on the same; determinism; a base without walls opening on the yard map.

Flashbangs (`src/sim/throwables.js` FLASH; or `node tools/flashtest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): the stun by range (2 actions within a tile, 1 out to 2 tiles, nothing at 3), aim −30 and no overwatch, facing away dazed, the thrower dazed at most, a riot helmet's visor and ear protection, the actions taken off at the start of the unit's next turn and the stun gone when that turn ends (either side's flashbang, in either side's turn); no effect through a wall, 1 tile through a doorway, a flashbang tossed through a doorway into a room nobody sees into; the dead lured (the unaware go to look, the aware turn to it), never stunned, and the people within 12 tiles woken; soldiers and guards carrying them by their own dice, throwing at two or more of yours in cover and never with their own in reach (30 enemy turns played, none of their own stunned), the autopilot and the raid bot throwing theirs (not into a room of sleepers), 8 autopilot fights with flashbangs on both sides ending without NaN; every loot source (search hours, armoury crates of 2–4, barracks, wrecks, cell blocks, armoury safes, Army trucks, the armoured dead, an unthrown one on a guard's body) through itemcond.js; a save mid-battle keeping the stun and the bang's lure; the throw preview, the flash and the STN / DZ tags drawn in both views, the panel's lines.

Siege equipment, fire, decoys and the Shepherd (`src/sim/siege.js`, `throwables.js`, `decoys.js`, `zombietypes.js`; or `node tools/siegetest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): grenades over walls (no line of sight to a cell a teammate sees, refused where nobody does and nothing used, a tile off by aim: 45% less 0.8 a point over 40, +15% blind, a pipe bomb more; 20–30 to everyone within a tile and nobody further, crates and barricades blown to rubble, a pipe bomb at the gate 40–60); ladders (the carrier a tile slower, set up from the foot in one action, the climb onto the walkway, no cover and +10 to be hit on it, the AI shooting climbers first, pushed off by a defender on the walkway at 50% (35% with a climber) with the climber falling and losing the turn, raised again, on the map as overlay data); the ram (a crew of three at 3 tiles an action that cannot shoot and has no cover, 3 × 16–24 a swing, the reinforced gate down in 3–4 swings, set down and taken up again, the crew keeping up with the lead, dropped with one left, rocks from the walkway hitting about 70% and glancing onto the crew); fire (a Molotov's 3 × 3 patch, 3 turns in the middle and 2–3 round it, thick smoke on every burning cell blocking sight through it, spreading a round by fuel: hay 45%, barricades 25%, a field 20%, grass 12%, never road, dirt, walls or water; a barricade burning out to rubble; one of the dead that has seen you walking into the flames instead and burning, most dead within two turns, a brute burning less; the raiders' AI and your AI not walking through a wall of fire, your own order can); decoys by sense (the table of senses per kind; an alarm clock pulls the ordinary dead, runners, brutes and spitters, a heat pack crawlers, a flare the ordinary dead, spitters and crawlers; screamers and Shepherds nothing; an aware runner walks to the ringing; an unaware guard looks where an alarm clock was thrown from, ignores a heat pack, still goes to look at a bottle); the Shepherd (tougher, the dead within 4 tiles herded and one further off not, a decoy and a fire pulling none of its pack, it will not walk into fire, shot first, the aura gone when it dies, the fold at tier 3 from day 995 (year 10) unlocking it, the horde warning "led by something that isn't fooled", decoys turning its horde 30% as often, by the numbers decoys worth nothing against its pack); raiders' kit by preparedness (300 raids a year on a walled hub: none in year 1, about 63% in year 3, 86% year 5, 98% year 8, all by year 10, outsiders less often; none without walls; the same raid the same kit; the warning card's "They carry 2 ladders and a battering ram, and 2 pipe bombs to throw over the walls."; defended in person, the battle hands out the ram, the ladders and the pipe bombs); by the numbers (the wall tier −0.25 against 2 ladders, a ram and 2 grenades, by the raids' numbers and autoResolveCombat; an assault taking the hub's ram, a ladder and grenades along, weighed, their walls counting 43% less; decoys and a Molotov ×1.04 against the dead, ×1.00 against a Shepherd's pack); the AI (in three siege battles the raiders set ladders up, swing the ram and throw pipe bombs, your AI pushes ladders and drops rocks; an alarm clock thrown away from your people when the dead are on them; a Molotov on two of the dead at a breach, not on two in the open); determinism and a save mid-battle with burning cells, ladders and a ram that loads with the same overlays and ends the same; the State Map (no fuel for a decoy fire, "Set out decoys" uses 3 from the armory, the heat pack only for crawlers; ladders and rams crafted and bulky).

```bash
node tools/carriertest.js
```

Medics and the downed (`src/sim/medics.js`; `node tools/medictest.js`, a few seconds): a med kit in the off hand, a pocket or the pack makes a medic (a red cross on the map and in the roster); Heal (Shift+H: 25 + 2 a point of Medicine + 4 a point of the kit, 1 action, 1 meds, a kit use) and Revive (Shift+V: both actions, 40% + 3 a point of Medicine + 5 a point of the kit, +15 stabilized, at most 85%; up at 25–40%, acting next turn, shaken −10 aim; a failed try stabilizes; only with a kit and meds, the button greyed with the reason otherwise); anyone stabilizes (sure with meds, by hand without: 50% + 4 Medicine, at most 90%, tried again) and drags a downed teammate (Shift+D, the same Drag as bodies: half speed, no shooting, put down in cover, on the strip or in the vehicle, one still bleeding stabilized on the way into the vehicle: 1 action, sure with meds, by hand without, the button warning ⚠ then); Pull out (Shift+U, 2 actions from next to a vehicle, either side: someone down inside it, a rider, the gunner who dropped in, the gunner on the enemy's Humvee, laid on a free cell next to you to be treated, dragged, tied or finished); enemy people go down too, bleed out in 3 turns, are dragged to cover, stabilized and revived by their own (a medic in about one group in five of soldiers, guards, militia and fighters), and die for good only by Finish (Shift+K: a blow, or one round at 90% out to 6 tiles; the knocked out, surrendered and tied are Execute's, nonlethal.js) or bleeding out; one model of the downed with the non-lethal kit (bleeding, stabilized, knocked out, surrendered, tied: stealth.js heldDown / prisonerOf, one rule for prisoners after a win); raiders and zealots finish your downed they can see (a quarter of the time they could, when nothing more urgent is on, and only with none of yours standing within 4 tiles of them), and take one alive as often as nonlethal.js NL_TAKE says for their group (raiders 25%, zealots never: dragged off as a prisoner of their group, conflicts.js takeCaptive, or tied up where they lie); an enemy medic's kit is body loot; a medic seen treating is shot first; the AI on both sides (AI plays my side, the raid bot) rescues only when the way is not open ground under fire, drags a downed ally in the open to a quiet cell and names a medic who revives them there (usually the next turn), and spends meds on the standing only above a reserve kept for a revive (with a medic on the side: 1 for every 3 people, at least 1; an enemy medic's kit uses); `node tools/revivecount.js` counts revives and where the meds went in AI-played battles; by the numbers a medic with a kit saves some of the fallen; a save mid-battle keeps it all; 16 whole battles and a raid played by the AI.
Stabilizing oneself (`src/sim/medics.js` `MEDIC.SELF`, `selfStabilize`; `node tools/selfstabtest.js`, under a second): someone down and bleeding with a tourniquet (one use, 0.1 kg: small finds at hospitals, clinics, pharmacies, police and fire stations, the Guard base and Army wrecks and trucks, medicine safes, dead soldiers, traders and the Army's stall; craftable in the field from 1 scrap at Medicine 1) or a field trauma kit within reach lying down (the off hand, a pocket or a rig pouch; not the pack) can try once a turn to stop their own bleeding: a tourniquet 35% + 5 a point of Medicine (at most 85%), a trauma kit 60% + 5 (at most 95%). Stopped: stabilized, still down, the use spent; failed: nothing spent, tried again next turn. The downed one's panel (picked in the roster) has the button ("Tourniquet: stabilize yourself (45%)", greyed with the reason), the roster says who can; one who has not tried by the end of their side's turn tries by themselves. The enemy does the same (soldiers 60% carry one, contractors 50%, guards 30%, escorts 25%, militia and fighters 15%, raiders 10%, survivors 8%, zealots 3%, out of their group's arsenal when it has one; their medic's trauma kit counts), waiting while an ally is coming with time left; after a won fight their self-stabilized are prisoners. The test covers the item and its sources, the odds over many tries, the trauma kit against the tourniquet, the pack out of reach, the end-of-turn try, the enemy's carriers and their try, the outcomes after the battle, the panel, the sound cue and a save.

The Carrier, its worm swarm and the human dead (`src/sim/carriers.js`, `worm.js`; every number in `hiveconst.js` `CARRIER`; a few seconds): the worm cyst is the first special dead a hive breeds (tier 0, after its first pit) and its hordes carry Carriers; a human body within 2 tiles of a Carrier gets up at once, with no warning (at the start of the enemy turn, or on the Carrier's own turn, acting at once; one of the dead never does, a body farther off does not; the map marks bodies in danger); Crush skull works on any body at any time and Double tap shoots one from range (a round, the noise of a shot), the loot stays, and the battle AI crushes a body in danger; fire destroys a body and everything on it; the swarm: a single bullet or bolt does 15%, a shotgun all of it, a blow more, fire 2.5× as hot; its bite leaves the worm in the living 35% of the time (one of yours keeps it: `catchWorm`, saved and loaded) and a kill always does; whoever has the worm gets up when they die, in a battle or on the State Map (others hurt and the body put down; alone on the road they walk off with their things); the risen wears what the person wore; no item is duplicated or lost; walking loot (a risen still walking at the end takes its things into `state.walkingLoot`, the death site says the body walked; a battle made near the pool has that person among its dead, in their armour, and its body is looted; still walking it goes back in; put down in a lost fight the things lie on the tile; entries drift a tile a day); carrying a body (slower, no shooting, put down, laid in the team's car; a body in the car cannot rise, comes home and is buried or burned; one with the worm left unburied gets up at the base); a raid's body containers; whole battles and a raid with Carriers end, and a fight by the numbers weighs them.
