```bash
node tools/radiotradetest.js 42
```

Trading over the radio (`src/sim/radiotrade.js`, the trade window in radio mode in `src/ui/tradewindow.js`; a few seconds): Groups tab → Radio… → *Open the trade window…* trades a base's stores and armory (pick the base) against what the group can spare and its wares; agreeing books the deal and a caravan carries it. *We fetch it*: their side is set aside (out of their stock), nothing leaves your base, the trade caravan planner opens from that base aimed at their base with your side loaded (items included), and at their gates the stop card's *Complete the radio deal* swaps the goods. *They bring it*: the delivery fee (6 + 0.4 a road hour, traders a quarter less, a pact a fifth less) goes on their side; they say no when too few of them, the dead at their walls, at war (goodwill under 40), no trust (goodwill under 0, not traders) or too far (48 h + goodwill, +24 traders, +24 pact); when they agree their caravan's trade window honours the deal, items too. The fee is theirs or your own offer (*Your offer*): under their lowest (their fee less goodwill/200, traders 10% more off, never under 60%) they stay home and name it; over theirs earns a little goodwill. One pending deal per group; called off −3 goodwill, not fetched by its day (10 days or more for a far base) −5, goods back either way. A group's call may ask you to bring your side (always when it cannot send a caravan, else 30%), adding the fee to what it pays: agree at their fee (books the deal and opens the planner), *Ask more for the trip* (half their fee again: yes with goodwill 25+ or when they are short of what you bring, else the call comes back at their fee), or decline. The governor says yes at their fee when the base is not short of the goods (food and ammo within what it trades away), can feed the trip and keep 3 days for those at home, and has 2-4 fit people beyond its share kept home (never the leader or a medic) who can carry their side home (a car when one has the fuel); the caravan sets out at once, deals already waiting are sent by its daily check, and a caravan back without the deal calls it off. A save and load keeps a deal; every screen's HTML has handlers and no NaN.

```bash
node tools/leadertest.js
```

Base leaders (`src/sim/steward.js`, `autopilot.js` stewardSafety / stewardSupplies, the base's Overview tab *Base leader* card; about a minute): handing a base over picks the best there by Leadership (Speech counts half) and names them in the log; you can name another; someone living elsewhere cannot lead it, a person leads one base, and a base that runs itself cannot be left without one; while the leader is away (an errand) the base waits (no hours run, one line each way) and runs again when they are back; a dead leader is replaced by the next best (the line says so), and with nobody left the base is yours again; the night watch roster (two watches, pinned people off it) and a defence plan by role, but never over a roster or a plan of yours, and no roster with Defence handed back; an outpost out of water sends an emergency haul from itself with the leader kept home and the line under the leader's name, none with Emergency supplies handed back; the leader through a save and load. Then: away, the next best stands in as deputy (the line names them, the base keeps running, the leader stays leader), and with nobody to stand in the base waits; Leadership (a named leader lifts the base's mood by 0.15 a day a point, shown as "Base leader" in the mood breakdown, nothing while they are elsewhere; Leadership 0 acts in about half the hours, 5+ every hour); trading (a base out of meds makes a small radio deal with a group whose caravan comes to it, the caravan's deal kept at the gates, the window closed, no second deal within 6 days); a led hub sending water to a thirsty outpost with the leader home; a base short of food sending a hunting or fishing trip with the leader home; a raid warning answered (Prepare or a tribute, never leaving the base), and left for you with Defence handed back. `V=1` prints the passing checks too.

Heat and noise per method through the real combat action; a locked container cannot be searched until it is open; picking gives Stealth XP and wears the lockpicks until they snap), locked rooms (every door a locked door cell that blocks movement and sight, nothing inside reachable, the key opens every door of the room, then it can be walked into), locked loot (a gun cage against a plain box: more supplies, guns, blueprints, keys for lockable sites; keys lost when nobody gets out, on the key ring when someone does), the raid bot opening locks (24 raids, no crash or timeout, mostly by picking) and the hour-by-hour search's locked finds; blueprints (early buildings never locked, each locked building and craft names its manual, a second preserving kitchen, the "Needs: … Look in: …" line, studying on the Read role with the skill (6 h a day, study XP, the days it takes, then the building can be queued), finds only at fitting sites and never one you know, deterministic rolls, traders' stock, the autopilot studying one); crash events (the first between days 8 and 14 and then every 10–20 days, each burning out 7–10 days later and gone from the map; the circle always holds the wreck at spawn and after every narrowing, a look within reach finds it, the wreck is not on the map until found; groups racing for it and stripping it to 30%; a horde drawn by the noise; a search team sent from the card finds and searches it); breakdowns (wear by use, age and frost, Grease Monkey, never broken below 60 wear and always at 100 with a card, a broken generator does not run, a repair paid up front and worked only 08:00–20:00, jury-rigging and its faster wear, cancelling refunds, a broken well, walls and radio station and the radio back after repair, horde attacks wearing the walls and gate, the storm well-pump hub event as a breakdown, a steward repairing by itself, parked vehicles in the frost); and long runs (Balanced and Cautious autopilot and hands-off) with no NaN or negative stock, printing crashes, breakdowns, keys and blueprints.

Human-raid census (`src/sim/factions.js`, `diplomacy.js`, `radio.js`, `prisoners.js`, `visitors.js`; or `node tools/raidcensus.js [days] [cautious|balanced|aggressive|all] [seeds...] [--runs]`, default 365 days, all three styles, seeds 1–20: 60 runs, about 5 minutes): autopilot campaigns watched from outside (new raid parties each hour, each hour's log lines and the autopilot's *card → choice* lines; nothing in the sim is changed). Per style: the groups' raids launched per run, on you and on other groups, by source (ordinary, war, grudge) and target, by group type and attitude at launch, raids on outposts; raids on you per run (median, range, runs with none, the day of the first) and per 30 days over the runs still going; raid strength against the target's defence at launch and after your answer, the raiding group's strength and the hub's defence on days 30 / 90 / 180 / 365; how many were warned, how many only at the walls, the hours from the warning to the end, the autopilot's choice (prepare, pay, defend in person, hold); outcomes (held by the numbers, won in person, break-in, overrun, paid, base gone, faded on the road); the cost of a raid that reached you (stock value lost, deaths, health lost) and of paying; the damage per raid by outcome (buildings wrecked, damaged and destroyed, the repair bill in supply value and work-hours, the hours of the siege). The same for raiders from outside the state (warned, choice, outcome, losses) and rescue raids for prisoners. The gates: aggressive groups hostile at the start / ever / within 20 tiles, the days each could raid you (hostile, in reach, 8+ people), how many stopped being hostile, groups gone by day 90 / 180 / 365 / 730; peaceful groups that ever turned hostile, when, and their raids on you; groups alive, aggressive (their people and bases) and hostile (within 30 tiles) on days 365 and 730, newcomers among them, takeovers of a broken group's land and new gangs; wars between groups, war raids, pacts and peace. `--runs` adds a line per run. Exits 1 if a run crashed.

```bash
node tools/diplotest.js 42 365 1,2,3,4,5,6
```

Diplomacy (`src/sim/jobs.js`, `diplomacy.js`, `alliance.js`, market lists in `trade.js`): every job type made, accepted, done (horde cut to 40% with your team there, escort alongside half the road, a delivery handed over or fetched by their caravan, a rescue paid out, a hive stormed, a region scouted, a package carried home), failed at the deadline (−2 / −4 goodwill), called off with no cost, offers that expire or are declined (−1), and the rewards (supplies, gear, a blueprint, a recruit card, intel, an outpost handed over); the relations matrix (one number per pair, drift back to rest, pacts at 28+); wars (war raids, a base taken and its land, the floor of 8 people, peace by land, tribute or a white peace, captives home); brokering (failure −3, success ends the war, +8 and a thank-you, once a week, never with a hostile side); joining a war (enemy hostile, its raids come for you, your share of land or tribute); trade at war (+35% ammo and meds, +30% weapons, less to spare, buying back still loses); the coalition (each invitation condition in order, the council's vote, shared sightings, help against raids and hordes, allies at a hive, the three council requests, the seasonal meeting, a member leaving, a group asking to join); the alliance victory (false unless land > 50% and every living group a member; a hive standing or being built does not stop it; the reason on the victory); wants and surplus against each group's stock, weekly refresh and price arrows, radio offers and caravans from the lists, the market board's order; the autopilot's choices; determinism (two processes, 90 days); a 365-day autopilot run without NaN; and the yearly group collapse rate with and without wars between groups (each year in its own process, on the collapse seeds given; wars may add at most +0.15 a group a year plus 1.5 standard errors of the gap measured from the run's own seeds, ~+0.23–0.26 at twelve: twelve seeds' gap swings by ±0.05, and on seeds 1–48 it is about +0.07 to +0.11).

Blocs and wars with you (`src/sim/blocs.js`, `playerwars.js`; or `node tools/diplo2test.js [seed]`): the type lines and the new `GROUP_PRIORITIES` weights; a bloc forming with a shared threat read from their knowledge, standing against it, members never raiding each other, intel shared, a vehicle theft found out breaking it as a betrayal; a raider gang and a commune never allying; a falling-out and a bloc whose danger has passed ending; help for a raided member (only what it can spare), a joint raid, a war on a member bringing the bloc in; a bloc's invitation to you, its fighters at your hub, striking at a member ending it; a bloc voting on your invitation (together with every member, without the one that broke off, the invited group alone, nobody when their foe sits in your coalition; Invite the whole bloc, the previews) and two AI blocs merging by the same vote (or voting it down); a war declared on you with its goal from history (revenge for raids and an execution, loot from a spy's report, territory for an outpost nearby, conversion for zealots), one at a time, its card and news, a war raid (one at a time), the score and weariness; the same tribute taken by a commune, less by raiders, all but never by zealots at war for the faith, accepted and refused offers, one offer every few days, their terms handing an outpost over without a "has fallen" card; your declaration (hostile, pact gone, their bloc joining, infamy), weariness ending a war in a ceasefire; the sue-for-peace card and the autopilot; a save round-trip; the Groups tab and World affairs. `node tools/diplo2test.js --pressure [days] [seeds...]` (default 240 days, seeds 1–5, child processes 3 at a time) runs the Balanced autopilot with and without blocs and wars on you: hubs standing, people, bases, raids on you, wars declared on you (goal, days, how they ended) and blocs formed.

Splinter bands (`src/sim/splinters.js`, numbers in `SPLINTER`, by type in `GROUP_PRIORITIES` splinter / splinterAs; `node tools/splintertest.js [seed]`): strife growing with the desertion pressure, every deserter, the dead of a fight (and a leader lost in one) and a lost war, fading when things are quiet; a raider gang torn up inside splitting into a new raider band on a free site near its old base, the people, stores and kit shared out, feuding with its old crew; zealots splitting as a new sect; communes, traders and a fortress never splitting; never beyond the world's group limit, not again for a while; the log line when you would hear of it; a save round-trip. `node tools/splintertest.js rate [days] [seeds...]` counts the splits per world-year on the Balanced autopilot.

Groups fleeing the dead (`src/sim/groupflight.js`, numbers in `FLIGHT`, by type in `GROUP_PRIORITIES` flee; `node tools/flighttest.js [seed]`): a horde coming at a base its group cannot hold: they settle a new site farther from the dead, losing a few people and the stores they cannot carry, the old base left abandoned for anyone to take; with another base they fall back to it; with nowhere to go an ally takes them in; a horde they can hold: they stay; zealots stand far more often than communes; overrun by a horde walking in (hordes.js): they leave; a save round-trip. `node tools/flighttest.js rate [days] [seeds...]` counts the flights per world-year.

```bash
node tools/dettest.js 60
```

Determinism across worlds in one process (`world.js` `newId` is one counter for the whole process): for seeds 42, 7 and 1234 (or `node tools/dettest.js [days] [seeds...] [--workers N]`; each check's worlds run in one worker thread, the checks side by side) world A runs 60 days on the Balanced autopilot, then world B on the same seed in the same process; a digest (stocks, buildings, wear and repairs, survivors' health / skills / XP / perks / mood / place, parties (with scavenging parties' hauls), hordes, hives, factions (and when they next scavenge), every site's loot and the loot you know there, the base's picked-over surroundings, diplomacy, jobs, the coalition, prisoners, crashes, tech, relationships, stats and every log line) must be identical, ids compared by their offset from the world's first id (each world here starts its ids at the next million, so anything hashed on an id shows up). Then each seed again with a world on another seed and style run in between, a pair after another world, and the same run in a fresh Node process. Last, a tactical battle played alone and with another world's battle (on a map turned to 13 × 18) set up while it is under way must play out the same (`combat.js` points its grid helpers at each battle's own size).

```bash
node tools/groupdigest.js 240 42 7 1234
```

The groups' outcomes as one hash, to prove a refactor of the groups' tuning (`groupcare.js` `GROUP_PRIORITIES`) changes nothing: for each seed a normal and a hardcore world play N days on the Balanced autopilot; the dettest digest plus every group's whole record (people, wounded, stock, bases, works, arsenal, vehicles, knowledge), diplomacy, blocs, hordes, wanderers and the wars with you are hashed (ids by their offset from the world's first id). Run it before and after on the same seeds (a `git archive` export of the old commit for the before): the hashes must match.

```bash
node tools/sameseedtest.js 120
```

Same seed, same game, over a long run (`node tools/sameseedtest.js [days] [seeds] [styles] [normal|hardcore] [--workers N]`; each pair of worlds, and each enemy kind's pair of battles, in a worker thread of its own, side by side): for seeds 42, 7, 1234 and 99 and each autopilot style (Balanced, Aggressive, Cautious), world A plays 120 days, then world B on the same seed in the same process (its ids a million higher); survivors alive, every base's stocks, each group's strength, population and stores, a hash of every log line and the whole dettest digest must match. Then, for each seed and each kind of enemy (raiders, soldiers, guards, fighters, militia, zealots, the dead), a tactical battle at the hub on day 10 is fought in both worlds and must go the same way, unit by unit and line by line (soldiers' plates, launchers, rigs, medics, staggers and bleeds). Anything that feeds a process-wide id (`world.js` `newId`) to the dice instead of a world-local key (`localId`, a serial, a battle's own unit numbers, a tile) shows up here.

```bash
node tools/savetest.js 120
```

Saving and loading (`src/sim/save.js`, `src/ui/saves.js`, `src/ui/settings.js`): for seeds 42, 7 and 1234 (or `node tools/savetest.js [days] [seeds...]`) the Balanced autopilot plays 120 days; on day 60 the world is saved (through a JSON string) and loaded again after another world has run in the same process; the original and the loaded world both play to day 120 and must end identical, by the dettest digest (`tools/digest.js`) and by the whole saved state; then one seed again in a fresh Node process. Saves made mid-way through a team's plan, with prisoners in the cells and mid-battle (a fight at the hub, a raid, a break-in on the deploy step, a hive assault) must play on the same after loading; an open card cannot be saved (the message names it) and can once answered. A Small and a Huge world are saved, played on and loaded the same way (they keep their size; the save list shows it), and a save without a size loads as a Normal map (a 100 × 66 one as Huge). It reports the size at day 365 (JSON, gzip and what browser storage holds) and the times, checks the storage layer (slots, index, compression, a full storage, newer and unknown versions refused, damaged and cut-off saves refused, the values JSON loses) and renders the settings view for every tab, Help with several topics included (no undefined / NaN, every button handled).

```
node tools/titletest.js
```

The title screen (`src/ui/title.js`, `title.css`): the game opens on it (the desktop app fullscreen the first time, then as you left it; `?notitle` in the URL skips it). Continue (the latest save), New game (the new-run screen), Load game, Settings and How to play (the menu at its tab, over the title), Quit in the desktop app; Exit and Save and exit in the menu come back to it. Behind the spray-painted logo four animated backgrounds drawn in code take turns, each held 45 s and then cross-faded into the next: a ruined skyline at dusk (the dead on a cracked street, fires, a helicopter, blasts), a campfire at night (survivors round the fire, a tent and a pickup, the dead along the treeline) and a holdout's wall in a storm (a sentry, the watchtower's spotlight, rain, lightning showing the horde in the field) and a hive (the Broodmother breathing among the ruins, pores, egg sacs and veins pulsing with its heartbeat, worms, the dead swaying before it, a scout's flashlight on it). The test checks the menu with and without saves, the buttons, the four-scene cycle and the hooks in main.js, ui.js and settings.js.

```bash
node tools/savehealthtest.js
```

Rotating autosaves, the save list by campaign and the error screen (`src/ui/saves.js` writeAutosave, `src/ui/savelist.js`, `src/sim/savesummary.js`, `src/ui/crash.js`; or `node tools/savehealthtest.js`, about 1 s, on a stand-in browser storage). The summary line (day, people, season, then the run's end, a war you joined, a hostile group or a Hardcore band on the road); each campaign keeping its last 3 autosaves (the oldest written over, named by the summary), another campaign keeping its own, an older build's one-slot autosave replaced by its own campaign's first rotating one, none in Ironman, a full storage clearing the slot written over and then older autosaves (the newest stays); the menu's list with one head line per campaign (the game being played first), the summary under a manual save and the storage warning; the error screen: a crash stops the clock and writes the crash slot, errors while it is up are counted, a crash right after Try to keep playing comes back with a warning and keeps the first crash save, the report's contents, Load the crash save, Back to title, Ironman's crash copy beside its slot (removed when the game saves itself again), no crash save on the title screen.

```bash
node tools/savecorpustest.js
node tools/makesave.js --note "why this save is in the corpus"
```

The save corpus (`tools/saves/`, listed in `tools/saves/corpus.json`; or `node tools/savecorpustest.js [days] [file filter]`, about 5 s): real saves made by older builds, each loaded by the current code and checked (it loads; survivors as many as the save's list line says, each at a base or team that exists; a hub in a Normal game; known building types and countable plots; the mode, world size and migration marks; no NaN or negative amounts in any stock, team supplies or cargo; firewood from before firewood by weight ×30 once, saved and loaded again unchanged; Ironman kept), then played 8 in-game days on the Balanced autopilot without an exception, still sane, and saved and loaded again. The corpus: the first save format (38cccac), before woodcutting (d32694b, Normal and Hardcore), before purified water (3af87c5), before construction plots (ae6e77b, Normal and Hardcore), before firewood by weight (29af708, Normal and Hardcore), before the title screen (7353bc3), before battle QoL and Ironman (62479e4), and dev at suggestions-4 (c63a2d5: Normal, Hardcore, Ironman); seed 7, a Small map (Normal 64 × 42 for the first, which had no sizes), 12 days, 80–110 KB each gzipped. **Adding a build's save is one command**: `node tools/makesave.js` makes one with the code as it is (`--days 12 --seed 7 --size small --mode normal|hardcore --ironman --note "…"`) and adds its line to `corpus.json`; for an older build export it first (`git archive <commit> prototype | tar -x -C <scratch>`) and pass `--root <scratch>/prototype --commit <commit>`. Add one whenever a change needs a migration or adds state an old world lacks.

```bash
node tools/ironmantest.js
```

The battle-start save and Ironman (`src/ui/settings.js` autosaveCheck, `src/ui/saves.js`; or `node tools/ironmantest.js`, about 10 s, on a stand-in browser storage). **Battle start**: as each battle opens (before anything is done in it) the game is saved to its own slot (`battle`, the latest battle's; open cards are left out of it, as a card's choices cannot be saved), and the battle panel and its result screen offer *↺ Restart battle*, which loads it after a confirm: turn 1, everyone as they were. A later battle takes the slot, and the earlier battle's Restart is refused then; none with *Save as each battle starts* off (Options), on autopilot or in Ironman. **Ironman** (a box on the new-run screen, with Normal or Hardcore): the game is saved to one slot of its own (`iron…`) at the start, every dawn, when a battle starts and when it ends, at the end of the run, when the window is hidden and as the page closes (written at once, uncompressed); never to the autosave slot. A card waiting for an answer holds the save. Saving, loading, save files, exporting it and Exit without saving are refused; the menu offers Save and exit (and Continue on the new-run screen picks it up again), and a new campaign or quitting saves it first. The tool checks each of these, the menu and the new-run box.

```bash
node tools/battlebartest.js
```

The battle's bottom bar, XCOM-style (`src/ui/battlebar.js`; the orders from `src/ui/combat.js` unitOrders, written into `#cbbar` by `renderCombatBar`; or `node tools/battlebartest.js`, about 15 s, on stub canvases). Along the bottom of the battle map, in place of the panel's old order grid and footer (no control is in both). **Left**: the selected survivor's card (their sprite, name and number key, health, the actions left as diamonds, and chips for what they are doing: down, bleeding, pinned, overwatch, hunkered, sneaking, which way they face, the light and night vision on or off, hands full, tired, with the survivor's mood, hunger and wounds as on the map; 🎒 Inventory and 📐 view cones as two small tabs on its top edge, since they only look) and a tab for each of yours (click or 1–9 selects and centres on them; greyed once they are done for the turn, faded when out). **Middle**: an icon button for each order the selected one has (the same list the panel had: move, face, sneak, shoot, overwatch, reload, fire mode, melee, subdue, throw, hunker, meds and the medic's orders, search and the ground, drag, the doors, the room's lights, the flashlight, night vision, the visor, the vehicle and its trunk, the rescue and siege kit …), grouped (moving, fighting, the wounded, loot, doors and lights, the vehicle, the rest). **Ready only** by default: only the orders that can be given now (or are switched on, like sneaking or the light) are shown; the slim ⋯ button at the end (with how many are hidden) switches to **All**, which adds the greyed ones and the key table's other battle orders, each saying why or when it applies (remembered in the browser). **Trays**: the everyday orders stand on the bar (`PRIMARY`: move, sneak, shoot, overwatch, reload, melee, throw, hunker, meds, search, and the ones that only appear when they matter: unjam, get shells, launch, heal, revive, the retreat, extract, getting in, out and driving); each group's others sit behind one button (🗺️ Moving, ⚔️ Fight, 🩺 Wounded, 🗃️ Loot, 🏠 Doors & lights, 🚗 Vehicle, 🧰 Other, with how many can be given now) that opens them, named, in a tray over the bar; a group with only one other order shows it on the bar instead, and a short list (under 9 orders) has no trays at all. Esc, the button again or an order given from the tray closes it; every order keeps its key. Ready only reaches the lock and car panels over the orders too: their options that cannot be taken now hide (a car next to you shows *Get in*, not six greyed buttons). Hovering an order turns the diamonds on the card it would spend amber (the last one for a move, both for a shot or anything that ends the turn), and an order that needs doing now pulses red (Unjam, an empty gun's Reload, Get shells from the pack, Revive). Each shows its key as bound now (Menu → Controls: a rebound key shows at once), its cost (free, 1, 2, *all*, or *end*: it ends the unit's turn) and, greyed, why it cannot be given now in its tip: the sim's own refusals (`fireRefusal`, `reloadRefusal`, `facesBox` / `faceRefusal` for loot, `quickRefusal`, `evacRefusal`, `selfStabRefusal` …), no light or goggles carried, no actions left. With All on, the core orders (move, face, sneak, shoot, overwatch, reload, melee, hunker, throw, meds, search, vehicle, light, night vision; the room's lights on a raid) are always there, greyed when they cannot be given (a greyed order that only matters now and then, such as Unjam or Revive, waits in its tray). A button runs `combatAction("cb-act", { v })`, the code its key runs, so a click and a key press cannot differ. A lock, a car, the reload chooser or the Execute / Surrender question next to the selected one opens over the orders. **Right**: the turn, how many of yours are still ready, the selected one's weapon (magazine pips, the calibre and load, spares, the jam risk), *End turn* and the other turn controls (*Fall back to the yard*, *Sound the retreat*, *Area clear: finish*, *AI plays my side*, *Auto-resolve*, *Restart battle*, with their keys); their questions (the end turn's, the retreat's odds, the fall back) open over them. The enemy's turn, the deploy step and the AI playing your side say so in place of the orders. **Sizes**: the bar is a CSS size container (its steps in rem, so Options → Display's UI scale moves them: a 4K TV at scale 2 looks as 1080p does): from about 1400 px of battle map down the order buttons fold to their icons (a tray keeps the names), from about 1200 px the turn controls but End turn fold to their icons (the names are in the tips); under 760 px the orders take a row of their own; from 2300 px (a 4K screen at scale 1) everything grows a third; at 1280×720 it fits with no sideways scroll (the battle panel is 20rem wide up to 1400 px), the ready orders in one row from 1280×720 to 1920×1080 (up to 11 of them and the ⋯ at 1280×720; a 12th wraps). The compass, the edge arrows, the squad strip and the target card keep clear of it (`iso.bottomInset`, `--cbbar-h`). The tool checks, in a field battle, a raid and a building raid, that ready only shows no greyed order (on the bar or in a tray) and the ⋯ button counts the hidden ones; that with All every battle order of the key table has a button (on the bar or in a tray), the core ones always, every greyed one saying why, the keys as bound and a rebound one; that no tray holds just one order or a ready primary, core or look-only one, and the bar itself keeps under half the buttons; that a short list (no actions left) has no trays; what a hovered order spends (Hunker all, Move 1, Face none) and the card's diamonds numbered for it; a jammed gun's Unjam pulsing; the reasons against the sim's (a full magazine, an empty gun with no rounds, nobody next to them, no light, no actions left, a body not faced, on foot) and the costs; that eleven orders pressed as a button and as their key leave two identical battles the same; the card's chips, the tabs (marked, greyed when done, selecting), the turn controls and their questions, the enemy's turn and the AI playing, All on and off, a tray opening and closing (the button again, Esc, an order given from it), and that the side panel has no order or turn button left.

```bash
node tools/battleuitest.js
```

Battle screen conveniences (`src/ui/combat.js`, `src/ui/isoview.js`; or `node tools/battleuitest.js`, about 15 s, on stub canvases): **AI plays my side** (the bottom bar, Shift+A) hands your turns to the battle AI (`autoPlayerTurn`, the same as the autopilot's: moves, shots, searches, the retreat from a fight it judges lost) at the enemy turn's pace until you take back control; End turn is greyed while it plays and each battle starts in your hands. **The threat preview**: hovering an empty tile or one of your people lists the enemies you see that could shoot it (each with the odds its shot would have against the selected unit standing there, cover from that side included) or walk one action and strike it, and draws a line from each (solid red with ⚠ odds for a gun, dashed amber for a blow); an enemy you do not see is never counted. **Big maps**: the ground is painted once per battle in map space (16 × 16 texels a tile, the texels the ground colours use) and stretched onto the diamonds with one transform, so turning the view no longer repaints it, and the fog of war is a one-pixel-a-tile mask stretched the same way: on a 72 × 72 fort a move redraws in about 10 ms instead of about 100, opening the battle takes about 200 ms instead of 300, turning the view about 40 ms (upper floors of a building with storeys keep the old pictures). The tool checks the toggle, every threat against shotOdds and sight over thousands of tiles, every layout drawn in all four turns of the view with no NaN, and the Restart battle button (only with a battle-start save, never in Ironman). **The selected survivor's kit**: 🎒 Inventory on the bottom bar (or Shift+B) opens their paper doll over the battle, look only (things move in a fight only by orders), with a tab for each of yours in the fight; never an enemy's.

```bash
node tools/vaulttest.js
```

Vaulting half cover (`src/sim/combat.js` VAULT, `vaultable`, `canVault`, `reachable`, `distField`, `moveAlong`, `moveMob`; or `node tools/vaulttest.js [seed]`, about 3 s): which cells a person goes over (a fence, crates, a car, sandbags, hay, a rock, a table, a counter, barrels; not a window, a wall, a tree, cell bars, a railing, a well, a trap or a plated vehicle), who (a survivor or a human enemy on their feet with their arms free; never the dead, nor carrying or dragging someone); a fence line down the whole map is crossed by a survivor's reach and a human enemy's at 1 + VAULT.COST a step, never ending on the fence and never round its corner, not with a bag or supplies in the arms, by a walker only at the dearer crawl (`src/sim/barricade.js` CRAWL), and by a Climber its own way; the human AI's field (`distField`) goes over it at the same cost, a walker's at the crawl's; a move over it lands past it for one action, leaves the unit winded (VAULT.COST tiles less of movement for the rest of the turn: `moveMob`, `dashRange`, the reach bands), raises the raid's noise by VAULT.NOISE, is a scuff heard nearby (silent sneaking) and a log line; a mid-fight save and load keeps it all; the next turn clears it; the enemy turn completes in milliseconds on a map of fences down every other column, with the enemy coming on over them and nobody standing on one.

```bash
node tools/windowtest.js
```

Windows and the kinds of door (`src/sim/windows.js` WINDOW, `windowState`, `windowOpen`, `insideOf`, `windowsBy`, `windowAct`; `src/sim/buildingmaps.js` DOOR_KINDS, `doorStyleOf`, `doorSees`, `throwBlocks`; `src/sim/combat.js` `vaultBreaks`, `vaultCost`, `clambers`, `los`; `src/sim/locks.js` `openOdds`; or `node tools/windowtest.js [seed]`, about 10 s): every window on a building's map carries a state from the map's own dice (unlocked, locked or fixed; a house's mostly unlocked, a grocery's mostly fixed; the same map twice has the same states and the same containers after them); checking one is free (with no actions left too) and reveals it; an unlocked one opens from either side for 1 action, quietly, and closes again; a locked one refuses from outside with the reason (the try reveals it, free) and opens from inside; a fixed one never; breaking one takes 1 action and is loud (the raid's noise +2, the unaware within 8 tiles wake, in a raid Heat +6); sight and shots pass a window closed, open or broken, the wall beside it not; the sill is half cover in every state and nobody stands in a broken one; a throw is stopped by a closed window and passes an open or a broken one; a survivor's reach goes through an open or broken window at 1 + VAULT.COST a step, never ending on the sill, and through a closed one SMASH_COST dearer, and the move crashes through it (broken, 1–3 of cuts whatever the armour, the glass's noise and the vault's, the unaware wake, winded, one action), through an open one quietly; with the arms full the window is a wall; a walker clambers through an open or broken window at the vault's cost (its field too), never a closed one; doors are placed by archetype (a house's front door of 45, a grocery's glass door of 20 seen through when shut, a police station's security door of 90, screen or patio doors at the backs of houses, inner doors wood, roller doors rollers, every door cell with a kind and hit points, a locked door's lock carrying its kind); sight passes a closed glass or screen door and not a wooden one; a security door refuses force and is harder to pick, a glass door's lock is easier; a glass door goes down in a few blows and breaks with a crash; a mid-fight save and load keeps the states, the known flag, the kinds and the locks' kinds, and a window from an old save is a closed, unlocked one; on a building's map Break window raises the Heat and the rooms say which side is inside, and Climb window through a broken one is quiet; the headless raid bot finishes a raid on a house and on a shop with nobody left standing on a sill.

```bash
node tools/barricadetest.js
```

Barricades (`src/sim/barricade.js` SHOVE, CRAWL, BOARD, FURNITURE, `shoveable`, `canShove`, `shoveTargets`, `shoveDests`, `shoveAct`, `crawls`, `crawlable`, `overCost`, `boardsOf`, `boardTargets`, `boardAct`, `tearTargets`, `tearAct`, `breakBoarded`; `src/sim/combat.js` CELL boarded, `reachable`, `distField`, `moveAlong`, `breakCell`, `structureName`, combatAct "shove" / "boardup" / "teardown"; `src/sim/data.js` GEAR.boards; `src/sim/crafting.js` CRAFTS.boards; or `node tools/barricadetest.js [seed]`, about 3 s; the user's request of 2026-10-18): shelves, a cabinet, a counter, a table, a bed, crates, tall shelving, lockers, barrels, hay and sandbags can be shoved (never a car, a gun safe, a window, a door), by a person with free arms (not the dead, not with a bag in the arms); the piece right next to the unit (not diagonally) goes one tile for both actions, pushed onto a free open cell beside it or pulled onto the unit's own cell with the unit stepping back (no pull without room behind them; a cell with someone or something on it is no destination); the shoved piece carries its hit points (FURNITURE[k].hp, given when first moved) and its raid container with it, the cell it left is open floor, the unit faces it, the scrape is the raid's noise +SHOVE.NOISE and a log line; lockers shoved into a gap in a wall stop a walker on foot, its siege field goes through them at the battering cost, it batters them and at 0 they are rubble; a walker crawls over what a person vaults (a fence, a table, a shelf; never a closed window, tall shelving, lockers or a wall) at 1 + CRAWL.COST a step (more than a person's VAULT.COST), never ending on it, its path over the cell, its field over a fence line at the crawl's cost while the field of nobody in particular treats it as a wall, a line of closed windows stops it and an open one is clambered at the vault's cost as before, and a walker next to a table spends both actions getting past it; GEAR.boards is a bulky pack item and CRAFTS.boards makes it from 30 firewood and a scrap by anyone, a team too; Board up refuses without a bundle (saying what to craft) and with one action left, boards the window next to the unit for both actions and the bundle: the cell is "boarded" with BOARD.HP hit points, the window kept under it, sight through the open window blocked, nobody stands or climbs there, full cover, nobody's side, loud (the raid's noise +BOARD.NOISE, a log line), no way through for your people or a walker on foot while a walker's siege field goes through it at the battering cost and it batters the boards; a second and a third bundle add a layer each, never more than BOARD.MAX; a mid-fight save and load keeps the boards, the layers and the window under them; Tear down from outside gives back a closed, unlocked window for both actions, quieter, the bundles spent for good; a closed door boarded keeps its own hit points under the boards' and nobody braces it any more, your own people cannot pass it until the boards come down, and a walker batters through it in a few blows to a broken door it comes in by; a broken door's frame boarded holds the boards' hit points alone and is a doorway again when torn down; a boarded window battered through ends broken; the enemy turn completes on a map of fence lines and a shoved table with the dead coming on over them and none standing on one. The picks of the same day (all seven): a piece goes into a broken or open door's frame too (the frame kept under it, c.frame, and back when the piece is shoved on or smashed: `isFrame`, `breakPiece`); two people shove for SHOVE.HELP_AP each (a teammate right next to the piece with free arms and an action: `shoveHelpers`, `shovePlan`), a tall piece (lockers, tall shelving: `tallPiece`) taking two or one with a Strong back or Melee SHOVE.STRONG_MELEE+ (`strongEnough`), refused alone otherwise with the reason; tearing boards down gives a bundle back BOARD.TEAR.BACK of the time a layer by hash dice (`tearBack`: about half over many tries), into the kit or on the ground at the unit's feet when there is no room; a bundle in the team's vehicle counts from next to it (`boardsOf` through raidloot.js `trunkVehicle`), spent out of the trunk; a human of the AI holding a building with nothing in sight to shoot shoves a half-cover piece into a door frame beside it or nails its bundle (u.boards by kit: BOARD.KIT, `kitBoards`; guards about 40%, raiders never) over an outer window or door (`aiBarricade`; never out in the yard, never your own people); a group's base you assault starts with its outer windows and doors nearest your side boarded, as many bundles as its stock pays for at the player's recipe (BOARD.AI: 30 firewood and a scrap each, at most 6; `preBoardBase` in fort.js fortAssault; a window between two rooms is not an opening); and the dead's crawl, when one of yours sees it, puts a CRAWL tag on the cell and a log line.

```bash
node tools/barkstest.js
```

Combat barks (`src/ui/barks.js`, hooked into `src/ui/combat.js` drawCombat; or `node tools/barkstest.js`, about 5 s, on stub canvases): short speech bubbles over heads in battle ("Reloading!", "I'm hit!", "Man down! Ana's hit!"), about 1.8 s each, following the unit's walk, sized by the zoom and the UI size, at most 3 on screen, one a unit and about 3 s between one unit's barks. They come from what changed since the last frame (the units' health, down, jams, magazines, overwatch, hunker, surrender, fleeing, the retreat sounded) and the new battle events (shots, throws, heals): reload, empty, out of ammo, jam and unjam, hit, hit bad, crit, low health, going down and the teammate's man down (always shown), kills (the dead or people), frag / Molotov / flashbang / smoke, overwatch shot and going on watch, suppress, hunker, a revive and getting up, an enemy giving up, the first special dead seen (and its groan), the retreat; the rest about 40% of the time. Lines (under 24 characters) by trait (Brave bold, Coward nervous) and background (veteran, cop, hunter, prepper terse; teacher, office worker, student, nurse chatty); raiders taunt, soldiers talk like a radio, zealots preach; the dead say nothing. An enemy barks only while your people see it. Each line is picked by a hash of the event's seq, the unit and the trigger: no world RNG, no battle dice. Options → Play → Combat barks: On, Important only (just going down and man down, with a short chirp, the Combat bark sound), Off (remembered in this browser). The tool checks a scripted run of events (every trigger), the cooldown and the cap, the lines (length, words, the same line for the same seq, the trait and background pools, enemies by kind, the dead silent), the fog, the option, the same seeded battle played with and without barks ending the same (log, units, dice), and the hook drawing them in both views with no NaN.


The battle map's look (`src/ui/battleedge.js`, `src/ui/battlelights.js`, `src/ui/battlemarks.js`; hooks in `src/ui/isoview.js` and `src/ui/combat.js`; or `node tools/battlelooktest.js`, about 6 s, on stub canvases). **The edge**: the map no longer ends in black: the ground runs on 5 tiles past every edge (each edge tile's ground continued, the roads run off the map, grass tufts, the odd stump, rubble in towns) and fades into a fog the colour of the sky (a grey-green haze on a clear day, greyer overcast, blue-grey in rain, pale in fog, white over snow, a deep blue-black at night), with a soft vignette of the same fog; scenery only (never hovered or clicked), painted once per battle in map space and stretched with the ground's transform, so it is right in every turn of the view and at every zoom (about 30 ms on a 64 × 64 base map, once). **Lights at night**: everyone with a light shines it: a headlamp's, flashlight's or spotlight's cone the way they face (from `LIGHTS`: 6, 6.75 and 12 tiles, as wide as its cone), a torch's or lantern's warm flickering pool; fires, flares and floodlit cells light the ground too, and the unlit scene is darker than before. Fog-of-war honest: only your people's lights and those of enemies you see, every light cut out of the cells you do not see; no sim rule changed (a light's `seen` is what the beam shows). Painted at half resolution into one reused canvas, used as a glow under the units and cut out of the darkness over them. **Marks that stay**: blood where someone was hit or fell (a spitter's acid green, none for a miss), spent brass under shooters (a few for a burst or a stream, red shells for shotguns, none for bows, launchers or throws), scorch where grenades, Molotovs, rockets and fire went off, and where something in the flames cooked off with a bang (sized from `FRAG`, `BLAST`, `FIRE`, `GL_HE`, `ROCKET`; ash where it burned; a launcher's dud leaves none), glass where a window is smashed or a car hit; read off the battle's fx events and unit and cell states on the UI side, at most 600 (the oldest brass first), cleared by a new battle, a restart or a map swap, drawn once your people have seen the cell; baked into one map-space canvas repainted only when a mark is added or seen (about 1 ms with 600), pools of blood spreading for 2 s first. Bodies of either side stay where they fell once seen, drawn under the living. **Display**: ⚙ Menu → Options → Display → *Battle ground marks* and *Battle map edge* (Full, Light, Off) for slower machines. A frame of the battle view costs the same as before (median 0.8 ms by day, about 2 ms at night on a 64 × 64 map, on a 4268 × 2970 canvas). The tool checks the skirt on every layout (outside the bounds, never picked in any turn of the view, a road running on, the fog by night and weather, the Display levels), the marks from a scripted run of fx (kinds, places and counts, the cap, a restart, the fog of war, bodies kept), a battle played by the AI leaving brass and blood, the beams (direction from the facing, length and width from `LIGHTS`, only seen enemies, the light cut out of every unseen cell, no unseen enemy drawn) and that the sim plays the same with the modules drawing, off, or not loaded.

```bash
node tools/picturestest.js
```

Lost pictures drawn again (`src/ui/pictures.js`; hooks in `src/ui/combat.js` and `src/ui/render.js`, every module with a picture cache; `desktop/main.js` notes a GPU process gone; or `node tools/picturestest.js`, about 3 s, on stub canvases). Every picture drawn once and kept (the sprites and figures, the battle map's ground, fog, edge skirt, light masks and marks, the State Map's chunks and night shade, the minimap's land, the weather's clouds, the gun art) lives in an offscreen canvas or ImageBitmap the browser keeps on the GPU. When the GPU context is lost (a driver reset, the GPU process crashing, a laptop switching graphics cards, a screen changing mode; the screen flashes as it happens) every one of them comes back blank while the page runs on: the game's own canvases are drawn again each frame and recover, but nothing used to redraw a kept picture, so a battle went on with the units as rings with no bodies, the ground a flat colour and the walls outlines, for good (a night raid with a light on is where it was reported: the beam, painted every frame, still showed; everything kept did not). Now each module registers how to forget its pictures (`onForgetPictures`), the game's two canvases are watched for the browser's `contextlost` / `contextrestored` events (Chromium 99+, so the desktop app; a browser without them is as before), and a restore drops every kept picture once (a burst of restores, one per canvas, counts as one) with a console warning; the next frame draws them all again from the sim's state. A browser may also blank the kept pictures without a word to the game's canvases (the small canvases taken off the GPU, a reset the page is not told about; the State Map then shows its hexes with no trees, places or figures once its chunks are drawn again), so the frame loop checks a sentinel once a second (`checkPictures`: a small canvas of the sprites' kind, painted once and kept, drawn onto a fresh probe and its pixel read back): found blank, every kept picture is forgotten and the sentinel made again; blank five times running the check gives up with one warning (the browser keeps no picture at all; forgetting every second would only cost frames). `forgetPictures()`, `readSentinel()` and `pictureStats` are there for the console. The tool checks that a frame that changes nothing makes no canvas, that a forget makes the ground, fog, scene and sprites again, the canvas's own report (lost counted, a burst of restores one forget, a later one another, one warning each), the sentinel (intact forgets nothing; blank forgets and is made again; blank five times running gives up with a word; a canvas that cannot be read or a hidden page is no verdict), a stub canvas left alone, and the sim untouched.

Night on the State Map and the screen-edge warnings (`src/ui/nightfx.js`, `src/ui/edgepulse.js`, `src/ui/attackwatch.js`, `src/ui/edgefx.js`; or `node tools/mapfxtest.js`, about 5 s, headless): the night curve (0 at noon, 1 at midnight, darker all through dusk and lighter all through dawn, each fade 2 hours, the sim's 20:00 and 06:00 at their middle), Night darkness (Off none, Light, Dark 55–65% at full night, an unknown setting counting as Dark), the shade drawn at night and never by day or in the timelapse, the renderer drawing it after the cached ground and before the bases, teams and labels with the hour kept out of the chunk cache key; the lights (the hub with people home lit, a base with nobody home dark, floodlights only with fuel or power, a team's lantern where the map shows the team and not when it is out of contact); where a point off screen shows on the edge (straight out on each side, toward a point from the middle of the screen, kept off the corners, nothing for a point on screen or without a position) and badges on one edge pushed apart; the attack sources from a test world (a besieged base, a team whose battle waits, an ambush card, a raid seen coming at an outpost, a horde coming that pulses only once the siege is over, worst first, one per place, nothing for a siege between other groups); the pulse tracker (at most 4, the most urgent first, one per source, fading, the 20 s cooldown, back while fading kept, a source on screen kept quiet and not started again); a battle's shots and blows at your people (your own shots and moves ignored, held 3.5 s, nothing for the dead, nothing replayed from before the battle was first seen); the Display options (defaults, stored, junk ignored, the rows and handlers, main.js hooks); and the same seed playing 72 hours to the same save with all of it running every hour and without it.

```bash
node tools/gunsfxtest.js
```

Gun, blast and handling sounds (`src/ui/gunsfx.js` the gun voices and the battle's sound space, `src/ui/blastsfx.js` explosions and grenades, `src/ui/battlesfx.js` the router, `src/ui/sfxdsp.js` the synthesis kit, `src/ui/sfxlab.js` the sound test; planned in `src/ui/soundcues.js`; or `node tools/gunsfxtest.js`, about 5 s, on a stub AudioContext). Every voice is synthesised in layers once into buffers (no audio files): a revolver's hammer, the supersonic crack (none for subsonic rounds or the .45), the muzzle blast through a waveshaper, a bandpassed body with a low sweep, the boom, the action slamming home, the outdoor tail, a pump, bolt or lever worked after, brass tinkling down. 17 voices: .22, 9mm, .45, .357, the SMG, 5.56, 7.62×39, .30-30, .308, .50, the M2, the LMG, 12 gauge, sawn-off, pipe gun, 40 mm, rocket; suppressed forms (a thud, the crack kept for supersonic rounds). Bursts and full auto play the fx's rounds at the gun's cyclic rate with one shared tail. Space: pan by screen x, distance (level, highs, delay; the crack ahead of the bang far away), enemy shots out of sight muffled, a generated reverb by place (indoors, town, forest, field); a close blast ducks the battle, deafness muffles it; a limiter on the effects bus (in a normal firefight it takes off about 0 dB on average, at most 1–2 dB on a close blast). The guns sit about 4 dB over the old pistol (a 9mm), the handling well under them, the blasts over them; **Options → Sound**, the mixer's Weapons groups (Gunfire, Explosions and grenades, Reloads and handling, each 0–150%, saved with the other volumes) scale guns, handling and blasts only. Blasts in layers (crack, saturated sub thump, roar, rumble, debris; frag whizzes, pipe shards, a vehicle's fireball and cook-off, a flashbang's ring), merged per frame, timed to the throw's flight and fuse; the pin and spoon, the clatter, a Molotov's smash, whoomph and fire. Handling from the units' state between frames: reloads by platform, the slide locking on the last round, jams and the rack, a dud's click, launchers. The tool checks every gun and launcher has its voice, the suppressor and subsonic routing, bursts' timing and the throttle, the handling from a scripted sequence, grenade and launcher timing, merging, pan and distance, rooms, the deafened muffle and ducking,, the levels and the Gunfire gain (and, when `sfx-preview/stats.json` is there, the rendered loudness old against new, no clipping and peaks under −1 dBFS in the mixes at default and full sliders and at Gunfire 150%, the limiter's reduction), and that a battle plays out the same with and without sound. **Hear them**: Options → Sound → *Gun and blast sounds* (every gun and mode, suppressed, near / far / out of sight, each place, handling, every blast and sequence, old then new). **WAV previews**: `../desktop/node_modules/electron/dist/electron.exe tools/sfxpreview/main.cjs ../sfx-preview` renders every voice old and new, the mixes (a firefight, a grenade fight, at default and full sliders) through the game's chain with a real OfflineAudioContext and prints peak, loudness, clipping, centroid and node counts (`sfx-preview/` is not committed).

```bash
node tools/zombiesfxtest.js
```

The dead's voices (`src/ui/zombiesfx.js`; or `node tools/zombiesfxtest.js`, about 1 s, no speakers): every kind of the dead has a voice set (formant synthesis: a torn glottal pulse, rasp, fry, throat distortion, sliding formants, gurgles), each unit a stable voice seed from its id; a scripted battle hears the aggro, lunge, bite, hurt, death, smash, spit and sizzle, the screamer's two-call shriek (in-breath, a cracking first call of 1.6–2.2 s, a pause, a lower second call, some breaking into a sob-shriek) and the Nightmare's, a crawler's strike, a Carrier bursting, a Flinger, a Sleeper; the dead at rest moan in irregular clusters with real silences (the crowd bed dips through them) and now and then snarl out of one, about half of them snarl as they start for you (always the first, and any within 2 tiles), all seeded by the battle; the nearest two of the dead within 2 tiles of your people breathe, quiet, wet and ragged in their own voices (not a hidden crawler unless right next to you, never over their own voice); the crowd bed follows the count and nearness; the throttle and the volley cap hold for a horde; the map's horde moaning by distance, size and night (several hordes adding up softly), the hive's heartbeat, the mixer's Zombies groups; the voices placed and played through gun-sfx's spatial chain (`spatial` on each cue, `spatialOut` in `zRoute` at the zombie voice gain × the event's mixer group, never the Weapons ones); every voice built on a stub context; a battle plays out the same with and without the sound layer. To listen: `..\..\..\..\desktop\node_modules\.bin\electron.cmd tools/zombiesfxpreview.cjs` (from `prototype/`, any Electron will do) renders every voice, old and new, and the mixes to WAV in `sfx-preview/` beside `prototype/` (not committed) with a measurement table (peak, RMS, loudness range, spectral centroid, flatness, periodicity, how alike the seeds are, what the limiters took off: the effects bus's, shared with the guns, and the zombie bus's on the crowd bed).

The sound mixer (`src/ui/soundgroups.js`, Settings → Sound; or `node tools/soundmixtest.js`, under a second, no speakers): every sound belongs to a named group with a volume (0–150%, squared like the other sliders) and an On switch, a part's gain its own × its group's. **Weapons**: Gunfire, Explosions and grenades, Reloads and handling, Melee hits and swings. **Zombies**: Moans and idle, Attacks bites and death cries, Shrieks and roars, Special dead, Crowd murmur (battle), Map hordes, Hive drone. **People**: Voices, Footsteps, Gear and small sounds, Deaths and downed cues. **World**: Weather, Wildlife, Heartbeat. **Interface**: Clicks and windows, Card chimes, Alerts and stings. **Music**: the music's volume (0–100%, 100% its usual level), with a *Stings and phrases* part; the *Music* button is its On switch. They sit under the Master, Effects and Ambience buses. The panel shows the buses and the top groups; *Advanced mixer…* every part with its On box and ▶ to hear it; *Mix* presets (Default, Quieter zombies, Focus on combat, Atmosphere, Hush the interface) and *Reset to defaults*. One-shots read `groupGain(id)` as they are made (a group off: not even built); beds that keep playing (wind and rain, the crowd, the map's hordes and hive, the base murmur) go through `groupBus` nodes that ramp on a change. A module puts its sounds in a group with one line, `assignGroup(names, "people.voices")`, or a voice's own `group` field. An old save's Gunfire, Zombies, Map hordes and Footsteps sliders become groups at the same loudness. The tool checks the hierarchy's maths, that every name the sound modules register lands in a group, the migration (old and new gains computed and compared), the presets, mute, live ramps, reset, save and load, and that the guns, blasts, reloads, the dead's and the people's voices play at their group's gain.

Music (`src/ui/music.js` the engine and the hook, `src/ui/musicdirector.js` what plays when, `src/ui/musicscore.js` the states and the composer, `src/ui/musicsynth.js` the instruments; or `node tools/musictest.js`, about 5 s, on a stub AudioContext): generative and adaptive, made on the spot like the sounds (no audio files). A director reads the game every half second (never changes it; no world RNG): the title; the map by day (plucked guitar, D dorian, 76 bpm) or night (a low drone and distant bells, 60 bpm), colder and sparser in winter, warmer in summer, a banjo waltz on the harvest festival day; **tension** (a horde you can see near a base or a team, a raid party seen or a raid warning, a hive newly seen, a base out of food or water: uneasy pulses, a phrygian cluster, ticking); **crisis** (a base on alert or under siege, an ambush: low toms, a driving bass ostinato, stabs, anvil hits); in **battle** no contact yet (a heartbeat pulse), enemies in sight or aware (rising toms), a firefight for 9 s after any shot, blow or throw (full drums, ostinato, stabs), the mode turning brighter when you are winning and darker when losing, a swell and a few bars of heartbeat when one of yours goes down, a resolving sting when the battle is won and a sombre one when it is lost or fled; a short elegy (the main motif's head on a bell) when a colonist dies, then a quieter stretch; a tolling sting when a base is lost; a full sad theme at game over and a bittersweet major one at a victory. Hysteresis: up after about a second, down only after 8–16 s of calm, sideways (day, night, festival) after 10 s, never sooner than 6 s after the last change; the title, a battle and the end change at once. The engine schedules each note 120 ms ahead (1.4 s while the window is hidden) from its own timer and the frames; a change lands on the next bar line, the six layers (pad, bass, keys, pluck, drums, texture) cross-fade over a bar (two beats into a battle) and the tempo glides. Melodies are seeded random walks over the mode with the main motif (5 4 3 1 · 2 ♭7 1) and remembered cells coming back; the title, the calm map and the victory theme share the motif. Instruments are baked once into buffers (Karplus-Strong guitar and banjo, FM electric piano and bell, detuned-saw pads looped without a seam and voice-led so common tones are held, a sub bass, a noise kit, an anvil), a slow tape wobble and a faint hiss on the whole; at most 40 nodes at once (the busiest battle about 37). The music dips 3–6 dB under a big blast, a screamer's shriek, the alarm and the crisis chime, back within about 1.5 s, and never reaches the effects' limiter (its own compressor into the mixer's Music group). Settings → Sound: *Music* on / off, *Music intensity follows the action* (off: calm and night music only, no battle or crisis music or battle stings), the Music group's slider, and *Music test* to hear every state and sting (the music follows the game again when the menu closes). Muted or in the background with *Mute when the window is not focused*, the music pauses. Levels at the default sliders: calm about −19.5 dB RMS, tension −19, crisis and battle about −18, under the gunfire. **WAV previews**: `C:/Dev/BrokenState/desktop/node_modules/electron/dist/electron.exe tools/musicpreview/main.cjs ../music-preview` renders each state, the battle's arc, a death, the game's ends and a day turning into a horde attack through the game's chain, with loudness and node counts (`music-preview/` is not committed). The tool checks the director against made-up and real worlds and battles, the hysteresis, the bar-line changes, the lookahead under jitter and stalls, the node budget, the ducking, the pause, the settings and their migration, and that a world and a battle play out the same with and without the music reading them.

```bash
node tools/humansfxtest.js
```

The people's voices (`src/ui/humansfx.js`; or `node tools/humansfxtest.js`, under 1 s, no speakers): non-verbal, made like the dead's (a cleaner throat): a voice per person from their id, looks (a beard low, a heavy build darker) and traits (Loud harsher, Coward breathier, Tough gruffer), a seeded register and age; enemy people by kit (raiders rough, with a harsh laugh on a kill; soldiers and guards clipped; zealots ecstatic). In battle: hurt grunts by the damage, a cry on a critical hit (and the bite's victim in their own voice), going down, a last breath, revived, effort on blows and throws; now and then pinned down, tied up, bleeding, sick, hurt and moving; deafened, panic, hands up. At most 2 at once, a cooldown a person. The barks' shout (`src/ui/barks.js`) is the speaker's own; on the map a base you select murmurs (the evening meal loudest, quiet at night, laughs, winter coughs) and someone dying at a base screams. The tool checks voice identity, every trigger, the cap, the barks hook, the murmur by the hour, every kind of enemy, every voice on a stub context and determinism; `tools/zombiesfxpreview.cjs` renders people, kinds, the murmur and a mixed battle too.
```bash
node tools/depletetest.js 42 365 1,2,3,4,5
```

Sites running dry and other groups' scavenging (`src/sim/aiscav.js`; or `node tools/depletetest.js [seed] [days] [curve seeds]`): a group short of supplies sends one party on foot to the best site in reach by its needs, which searches up to 8 hours (loot down by 0.03 × crew an hour), walks home and adds the haul to the group's stock (13 finds per 0.1 loot from a full site before the per-good multipliers); a horde at the site costs a quarter of strength and haul, a party below strength 1 is lost; a contested site (your team there) gives a *Contested site* card once per party and team, nothing is taken while your team searches there, a party driven off drops its haul into your cargo, a friendly group keeps to its side; sites in a group's own land lose 0.3% a day into its stock; the test switch (`state.aiScavOff`) brings back the old economy. Nothing grows back: every assignment to `.loot` in `src/sim` is in mapgen, scavenge, crashes or aiscav and none adds to it, and over a 150-day Balanced run no tile's loot rises except on crash sites (a burned-out wreck leaves its tile at its old loot); the base's surroundings (Scavenge role) do not recover in 20 or 30 days without scavengers and fall 0.4% per scavenger a day to the 20% floor. What you know: a site stripped out of view still shows *last seen …: rich* in the tile panel (with the warning) and *Loot: rich (then)* in the tooltip and still offers a search, a team's look brings it up to date, in view it says *· now*; radio news (*On the radio: … has been stripping …*) with a radio station updates only the loot you know (an older team report does not undo it), and without a radio and out of view there is no news. The autopilot goes past the picked-over ring around the hub (Balanced: range + 5 tiles) to the sites it knows are rich, counted as far trips, and picks a site it believes is rich over the real loot. Determinism (two worlds, one process) and a save / load round trip with parties out. Last, one year on each curve seed (1–12 by default: on five the collapse rate is mostly dice) in a process of its own with scavenging groups on and off (the world goes on after the holdout falls): loot left on days 120 / 240 / 365 (less on every seed with them, falling over the year, at least 25% left on day 365, at most 75% on average) and the group collapse rate per group per year (at most +0.15 with them, diplotest's base tolerance for wars, which adds 1.5 standard errors of its own seeds' spread).


Work priorities and crews (`src/sim/work.js`, `src/sim/crews.js`, `src/ui/work.js`; or `node tools/worktest.js [days] [seeds...] [--dice N]`, seeds 1–10 and 4 rolls each by default, about 3 minutes on 20 threads): priority 1 first, then 2, back to 1 when a job turns up again, rest when nothing has work, and the why line; capacity (two stations, four on Workshop 1: two operate, each its own, the others say Workshop is full; a Manual operator's station never booked twice; a clinic's medic slots, filled while someone is hurt and left when everyone is healed; one lookout; two teachers at 12+ people with no learner in two lessons; one reader per copy); ties by skill (the better shot gets the lookout), a person's own cell over the role's (and back), the role's skill minimum; emergencies (the defences manned: everyone with Guard set, even at 4, people without it keep working; a horde the walls hold anyway pulls nobody, one of 5,000 everyone; a warned raid; health 20 and too ill rest; the watchtower alarm: none without a tower, a warning once for a horde 3 tiles out, Guard 4 moved up to 1 with the old 4 kept and their own cells untouched, Guard 1 or no Guard untouched, more lookouts, a horde of 5000 taking everyone with Guard set, a cell set by hand left alone, save / load mid-alarm, the horde gone: stood down and back to 4 and to the farm, a new warning when it comes back, leaving the base ends it, a horde that was beaten off and one 5 tiles out roaming ignored, one coming 5 out warned and kept a tile past the view, at night a tile of view, the dev autopilot moving nobody, and the governor posting more guards on the alarm (at most half); days off kept; a severe wound with nobody on duty pulls Doctor 5; a broken well pump pulls Repair 5 onto the job); the schedule (22:00 on the night watch guards, 06:00 asleep with the mood, 14:00 works; at midnight someone asleep counts at the day's farming, but not a guard while prisoners are in the cells); mood free time below 25 until 40; Manual and pinned people untouched, a station's fallback leaving people on priorities alone, a steward with Roles moving only Manual people, a fixed job from the drop-down (or the roster) putting someone on Manual; roles (the defaults, unique names, duplicate, delete puts members on Manual, everyone on priorities by what they do, newcomers); save / load (an old save loads with everyone on Manual and stays so, roles, schedules, minimums, members, own cells and tasks survive); crews (paused, a trip within range by its field priority with its come-home rules, a small road card answered the governor's way, a fight with people held for you unless *Handle everything*, the status line, the people at home planned while the crew is out, home again with the trip in its log after an hour-by-hour search, resting before the next; Recover at 1 before Scavenge at 2, nothing within an hour's limit, a member below the hurt line turning the team home, danger at home keeping it in); the Work tab (clean HTML, every action handled, left / right / Shift+click, the number keys and R, the schedule strip, the role editor and crews panel, the role drop-down, the roster); then [days] days on each seed with the Balanced autopilot, once with its own roles and once with everyone on work priorities (its test switch `governor.priorityRoles`: the governor plans trips, builds and crafts as usual but leaves people on priorities alone), comparing the food harvested and the buildings and crafts finished (with the governor's survey trips off, `state.surveyOff`: extra trips taken with spare hands that only add noise to this measure; with them on the priorities side finishes about 1.5% fewer buildings). One run per seed is a coin toss (advancing the world's dice by one draw moves the governor's ten-seed total by about ±30 buildings), so each seed is played on 4 rolls of its dice (advanced 0–3 draws before day 1), both ways, in parallel child processes (seeds 1–10, 120 days, 2026-10-09: governor 54,390 food, 1,059 buildings, 642 crafts; priorities 55,808 / 1,166 / 484; on 8 rolls 1,295 vs 996 crafts: priorities about 23% fewer, the measured gap the check allows plus two standard errors of the paired runs; both modes cap the farm crew while food is comfortable, autopilot.js farmCrewLimits), with no NaN, negative stock or crash; and determinism (two worlds on priorities with a crew in one process).

Watch roster and defence plan (`src/sim/watch.js`, `src/sim/defenceplan.js`, `src/ui/defence.js`, the base's Defence tab; `node tools/watchplantest.js [seed]`): presets (two, three, four watches, night only), watches added, timed and removed by hand, people kept on a matching watch when the preset changes, Fill from the people here; standing the watch (someone on priorities on guard at 23:00 with "On watch: Night", a Manual farmer on guard and back to the farm after, counting at the farm at midnight, the watch in the walls' hold limit, the badly hurt, the away and the pinned left off, the coverage strip and its gaps, through the real hourly tick); the drill (the base's battle map with only your people, valid default posts, Start and the AI refused, a defender placed by hand, Save as plan, Close leaves no battle); the plan taken up at the deploy step of a real attack on the fort map (the planned cell, valid posts, the log line, everyone else at theirs), Default positions and Planned posts; on the yard map of a base without walls and a bridge barricade's wall map, both turned to face the attack (a plan made facing east stands at the same post when the attack comes from the north or the west), changed in the deploy step and saved back, a base that grew a wall (another map: the old plan not used), clearing; save / load mid-drill and an auto-played battle starting from the plan. Unwatched nights (`watch.js` surpriseAt, lookoutsSpot): caught asleep at night with nobody on guard, not by day, not with someone awake on guard, an exhausted lone watcher dozing off about half the nights, the alert and a tripwire alarm waking everyone; a raid by the numbers (everyone but the guards 0.5×, the walls not counted) and a horde (the defence buildings half); a raid party reaching the walls unseen at 01:00 (the card and the report say nobody was on watch); the watch's early warning (nothing seen at night unwatched, 3 tiles out in the dark and 2 tiles further by day with a watch, the cards saying the watch saw them early; a horde warning only with someone on watch); a horde at night (the log says why); a surprise battle (their volley, the deploy step, 1 action each) played out on a walled and an open base. The governor's night watch (`autopilot.js` govWatch): two watches at a base of 3+, the first on guard at 23:00 with their job kept for the day's work at midnight, others the next night, nobody Tired over eight days, no roster at a base of two.
Watch roster and defence plans (`src/sim/watch.js`, `src/sim/defenceplan.js`, `src/ui/defence.js`, the base's Defence tab; `node tools/watchplantest.js [seed]`): presets (two, three, four watches, night only), watches added, timed and removed by hand, people kept on a matching watch when the preset changes, Fill from the people here; standing the watch (someone on priorities on guard at 23:00 with "On watch: Night", a Manual farmer on guard and back to the farm after, counting at the farm at midnight, the watch in the walls' hold limit, the badly hurt, the away and the pinned left off, the coverage strip and its gaps, through the real hourly tick); the drill (the base's battle map with only your people, valid default posts, Start and the AI refused, a defender placed by hand, Save as plan, Close leaves no battle); the plan taken up at the deploy step of a real attack on the fort map (the planned cell, valid posts, the log line, everyone else at theirs), Default positions and Planned posts; on the yard map of a base without walls and a bridge barricade's wall map, both turned to face the attack (a plan made facing east stands at the same post when the attack comes from the north or the west), changed in the deploy step and saved back, a base that grew a wall (another map: the old plan not used), clearing; save / load mid-drill and an auto-played battle starting from the plan. Several plans: the first is "Default", New plan from where everyone stands, renamed and tagged (the dead, at night), the drill switching plans, the attack taking up the plan that fits (a horde at night: the night horde plan; by day and raiders: Default; a plan for another attacker never), the deploy step's picker and "no plan", plans made only in a drill, a drill opened on a given plan, Delete, at most `PLAN.MAX`, save / load, an old save's one plan per map loading as "Default".
