camping only above the threshold and only while camped; the stages (hidden, gut: work, food and health; brain), spotting (a medic sooner than nobody; a team not before the gut stage's 7th day, then the card with its choices, the dewormer choice when one is carried; the brain-stage card with the purge disabled), the cures at a base (dewormer, failed and working purges, once a day, surgery only with a staffed Clinic, the night's care using a dewormer first then a purge, nobody treating what nobody has spotted), turning (at a base, locked away, the base card, on the road), the old random card gone, the autopilot building a water boiler, the boiler's upkeep (0.2 firewood a head a day, firewood then fuel, unlit and unsafe when the base cannot pay, counted in the firewood plan, an outpost's covered from the hub, nothing burned with a filter), searching infected sites (scaled by the eggs, nothing below 10% infection, rolled when the day turns or at home, washed by a lit fire or safe water at home, the planner's warning), crops and forage (a harvest on infected ground carries eggs, dry grain does not, forage at the base and in a team's cargo, eaten after the clean food and before the raw meat, catching it, cooked by a cook fire and a camp fire, dried after the meat), the other groups (the loss rate and the type multipliers, the boiler flag after the first loss, the news and the chronicle, never below 4, clean ground, a party on a herd's land) and save / load of the new state (a carrier's worm, the base's meat, crops and forage with eggs, the cold boiler, herds, stats, a team's unboiled water, meat, forage and hands, a group's losses and boilers). Since the week-long course (2026-10-06): the 7-day ramp (3, 6, 10, 15, 20, 24, 30 health, 2 … 8 mood a day; full health dies on day 7, hurt sooner; no healing while infected), a dewormer on days 1–4, surgery only from day 5, spotting from day 1, clean-ground un-purified water at `RAW_BASE` (about 0.9 cases per 10 people per 30 days), prisoners, guests, holding crews and your people held by groups catching it, every death-and-rise place (the base, locked away, the road card's put down / leave / crush the skull, alone, an errand, a holding crew, a crawl block's "One of you turned", a battle unit becoming one of the dead on its cell and floor, a battle hour's damage, a group's base held or visiting), the tablet order (on the move, a lit fed camp fire, a camp with nothing to burn, a filter first; at a base only with the boiler cold for want of firewood and no filter), the upset stomach (its odds, its own name, no health, it passes, meds shorten it), save / load mid-course and an old save's stages moved onto the new count. Since the half-healing change (2026-10-06): half the healing while infected from each source (rest at a base, a hospital bed, a treatment, camp rest, field treatment, a prisoner's rations; a holding convoy and a battle's patch-up by their code), the death day at full health (day 8 resting at a base, day 9 in a Clinic's bed with a medic, day 10 at best), prisoners and guests spotted with a log line and listed, a guest's dewormer (+8 goodwill) and failed purge (+3, once a day), surgery needing a Clinic, a prisoner's purge (+12 opinion) and release (+3 goodwill more), the night's care leaving them alone until *Treat prisoners and guests too* is on, the autopilot turning it on for guests; the boiler's burn order (firewood, then the allowed junk with its log line, then fuel; cold with nothing: the tablets, the filter first, else un-purified), the marks and the toggle (a weapon only when marked, *Keep* never, the toggle off), the wood plan keeping the boiler's firewood, breaking down (an armory item, metal refused, the bulk button sparing *Keep*, worn things refused, a team's book feeding its camp fire on bare ground) and firewood in the loot (a cabin's search hours, a barn's shelves, never a hospital). Since the huge-map census (2026-10-07): the autopilot gets the sick to a cure (an outpost with no dewormer and no meds sends them walking to the hub, cured there by its dewormer; with no food for the road the hub sends meds and the outpost purges it; nothing once the last gut night has passed, nor from a base that can cure it) and puts a queued boiler first with a builder at an outpost drinking un-purified water. `node tools/deathcounttest.js` checks the census tools count a worm death once (its rise line is not a second death).

```bash
node tools/discoverytest.js 42 365 3
```

Hidden sites (`src/sim/discovery.js`; or `node tools/discoverytest.js [seed] [autopilot days] [runs per style]`): every LOCATIONS type has a rule (public / road / secret), the road rule (a countryside site by a road is public, one more than a tile from any road hidden), towns, cities, landmarks and Guard bases public, no hive named after a hidden site; before it is found a hidden site is not drawn by the real map renderer (none of the unknown hidden sites' names drawn with the map centered on each, while a public site nearby is), the tooltip and the tile panel show bare ground, no *Scavenge / raid* in the right-click actions, it is not among the planner's nearby sites, tileLabel names the ground and the autopilot's and crews' site score refuses it; Dev Reveal draws it and hides it again; a team that has seen it knows it (and its nearby-sites step may pick it) before the hub does. Each discovery route puts it on the map with exactly one log line and it stays there: seen from a base, a team's sight radioed in, a team's sight brought home (not on the hub's map before), radio news, an explorer's map, intel paid for, a prisoner questioned (their camp on a hidden site), the coalition's reports, a retrieve job's pick-up place. Save and load keep the found sites and announce nothing again (an old save without `foundSites` announces nothing on load); the same seed finds the same sites in the same order with the same log. Then [runs] autopilot campaigns of [days] days per style: no team is ever sent to scavenge a hidden site the player has not found (raids on found ones are counted), and how many hidden sites are found by day 30, 90 and the end, near the hub (within 12 tiles) and on the whole map. Seed 42, 365 days, 3 seeds a style (Normal map, about 34 hidden sites each, 5–6 of them within 12 tiles of the hub): near the hub 41% found by day 30, 71% by day 90, 98% by day 365; on the whole map 14%, 32% and 58%; none raided before it was found. Before the Survey mission (`src/sim/survey.js`): 35%, 71% and 86% near the hub, 9%, 28% and 55% on the whole map.

node tools/poiraidtest.js 42 120
```

The raid flow per point of interest and the POI surveys (`src/sim/poiraid.js`, `src/sim/scavenge.js`, `src/sim/survey.js`, `src/sim/pois.js` threat and visits; or `node tools/poiraidtest.js [seed] [autopilot days]`, about 2 minutes at 120 days): the dialogue (a scavenge on a tile with places raises a card with a choice per known place only, "Not now", and the crawl on a bare city block; picking one starts the search in it; with no place known the order is refused with "survey first"; never a count of the hidden); the headless path (the best known place by value × what is left / the perceived threat, its own pool falling and the others' not, the site POI's pool `tile.loot`, a visit recorded with a threat stamp, a place picked clean sending the search on to the next known one and the last saying so, the raid map each kind opens); threat (the true value, the bands and skulls, "threat unknown" before any look, a closer look replacing a rougher one the same hour, the error growing with the days and "stale" after two weeks); the Survey (the odds by day and night and by ground, the preview, binoculars adding the ring, an 8-hour task with Field Heat, the progress text, the "surveyed" stamp and the survey threat stamps, the finds reaching the hub, the same seed finding the same) and the Quick look (2 hours, "looked", no threat stamp, half the odds, a recon's look too); the autopilot's survey target and candidate; a place on another group's base tile (their guards, their goodwill); a save keeping the stamps, the visits and a team's pick; [days] days of the autopilot without exceptions, with POI surveys sent and places searched; on screen the right-click actions and the dialogue's screen.

node tools/poiheattest.js 42 40
```

Tile heat (`src/sim/pois.js` `TILE_HEAT`, `tileHeat` / `addTileHeat` / `tileHeatDaily` / `tileHeatEstimate`; `src/sim/scavenge.js` `raidEnemies`, `heatSurge`, the search's going in and coming out; or `node tools/poiheattest.js [seed] [autopilot days]`, about a minute): a tile starts cold, heat adds up to its cap and is a record of its own (not Field Heat, not a search's Heat); going into a place adds a little, coming out of the hour-by-hour search the approach's heat (sneak < normal < loud) and the shots fired, a raid on a map by how hot it ran and its shots, another group's raid a little, your own crews' abstract take nothing extra; it falls 10% a day and is dropped below the floor (also on the world's own clock); every place's true threat on the tile rises with it, a raid's roster gets more of the dead (state.rng untouched) and a hot tile a horde or a wandering band about the expected share of hours (never below the threshold); the player sees it only as a stamp made by a team there ("unknown" before, faded and stale after two weeks, new heat since not seen), in the tile panel's row and the place dialogue's line; determinism, save / load, and [days] days of the autopilot with heat within range.

node tools/poicontainertest.js 42
```

Containers that persist per point of interest (`src/sim/poicont.js`; `src/sim/combat.js` `createRaid` / `searchBox`, `src/sim/scavenge.js` `afterRaid` / `searchHour`, `src/sim/locks.js`; or `node tools/poicontainertest.js [seed]`, about a minute): the same POI gives the same containers (ids, kinds, places) with or without a vehicle, from another side and hours later (a building, a camp's street map, a site's street map and one of its buildings); a POI container's contents come from its own dice: the same at another hour and in another world on the same seed, nothing drawn from `state.rng` (a raid that is not on a POI still draws from it); a raid that walks out saves every opened container (what was taken, put in, swapped, left hidden by a rummage) and none of the unopened, the pool falls by the opened ones' share, the dialogue's note says so, and the revisit shows them as left with an unopened one still holding its original things; a wipe and a retreat save too; things you leave never raise the pool or the band; another group's abstract raid marks the fresh containers looted in the seeded order (the fresh share follows the pool), they spawn empty, open and ransacked, it takes what you left (marked looted by them, "looted by others since your visit") and adds tile heat; a raid before any visit only lowers the pool and the containers follow at the first visit; your own headless search takes through the ledger and carries off what you left; save / load keeps the ledger; the same raids give the same ledger; the raid bot through a raid and a revisit.

AI groups survey and raid points of interest (`src/sim/grouppois.js`; `src/sim/aiscav.js`, `src/sim/visitors.js`, `src/sim/hardcore-groups.js`, `src/sim/hardcore-loot.js`; or `node tools/poiaitest.js [seed]`, about a minute): a group knows the known zone's places within 10 tiles of its bases and nothing hidden; a hidden place only after a survey of its own (the player's Survey odds with the group's Scouting, better scouts better odds, the same dice in two worlds, kept with the day, not surveyed again for 120 days, unknown to the other groups, through a save, dropped with a dead group); what a survey may turn up is guessed from the terrain and zone only; its targets are places it knows and survey stops; a raid lowers the pool to the floor at most, marks the containers looted by the group, takes what your people left in an opened container (with a log line when seen), heats the tile, brings a haul of the place's kind and strips it; a party sent for one place stands in it (the dialogue's "being raided now"), lowers its pool and goes home with the haul, a survey stop stands 8 hours on the tile before the roll; on a tile shared with your team, different places: no card and both get on with it; the same place: they wait while your team searches it, then the contest card; a group at war with you contests whatever place; raiders from outside survey a tile first and raid a place there through its ledger; the hardcore start doubles every place's pool and the surplus wears off to exactly the start, and the bands raid their camp's places.

```

Nomad bands (`src/sim/nomads.js`; `src/sim/grouppois.js`, `src/sim/poicont.js` underneath; or `node tools/nomadtest.js [seed] [--quick] [--years N] [--sizes normal,huge]`, about 25 seconds with `--quick`, several minutes without): the same seed gives the same bands, camps, stocks and tallies, another seed other ones; the count fills up to half the size's default group count on all four sizes and never passes it; a hardcore start fills to twice that on Normal and Huge, then the respawn keeps it at the target; looters only in year 1 (and early in hardcore), survivalists from year 2, preppers from year 4, every band within its tier's size range, a fed band recruiting never past its cap, the three kits real weapons and armour (`combat.js` `KITS.looters` / `survivalists` / `preppers`, `arsenal.js` `KIT_OF` `nomad1`–`nomad3` and `starterKit`; the preppers' armour class 3 and up, the looters' none); a band without food or water starves and breaks up and the respawn brings the count back; a band leaves when its stay (one to two game years) is up, and by year 5 every unlocked tier has come; neutral wanderers and refugees from outside join, hostile ones never, nobody with an empty larder; a camp beside tiles it does not know surveys them first and then knows their hidden places; a raid lowers the pool, names the band in the container ledger and takes what your people left in a container; never in `state.factions`, no land, the coalition's land share the same, refused by the coalition and by pacts; looters stop a weak team, the fight resolved by the numbers, survivalists talk and trade (the trade window on the band), a camp in view marked and attackable (mission `nomads`), preppers stronger with a vehicle, 20 days of the Balanced autopilot with bands round the hub; save and load round trip. Without `--quick` it then runs N (6) game years (112 days each, `eras.js` `YEAR_DAYS`) on Small, Normal, Large and Huge in parallel processes with `simtest.js`'s auto-player and prints the bands alive every 28 days by tier, the people, what came and went, recruits, surveys, raids, meetings: no exceptions, the count near the target, no cap passed, nobody in `state.factions` or holding land.

```bash
node tools/surveytest.js 90 2
```

The Sweep area mission (the radius survey, "Survey" in the texts below), rumours and optics, and the two POI surveys (`src/sim/survey.js`, `src/sim/optics.js`; or `node tools/surveytest.js [autopilot days] [seeds per style] [first seed]`, about 3 minutes at 90 days): the POI surveys (the Survey of a tile is an 8-hour task on the spot and the Quick look a 2-hour one, by the orders' estimates and the tasks run; the Survey's one roll on the own tile and its ring of six only with binoculars or a scope (never further, never for a Quick look); success finds every hidden place, failure says "not sure you found everything" and may leave some; the stamps "surveyed" and "looked"; the threat stamps a Survey leaves and a Quick look does not; no text with a count of the hidden); the hourly odds of spotting a hidden place and how each part moves them (Scouting 2 / 10 / 18, three people ×1.16, farmland against forest, a tile away against two and four, night ×0.35 and a flashlight ×0.5, fog ×0.5 and a thermal's ×0.9, a car ×0.6 and bikes ×0.8, stealthy against rushed, binoculars ×1.25 with a tile more reach, the best optic in full and a quarter of the next, a spotting scope only while still, the cap, a rumour ×3 for its place only with the survey centred in its circle) and the planner's word (three scouts on 2 tiles for 24 hours "good", one novice by car at night on 4 tiles for 6 hours "poor", with the reasons); the route (the survey's hours long, every spot inside the circle, one tile a step, back at the centre on the last hour, the same twice, stealthy covers fewer tiles); a survey in the field (the team panel's "Surveying 3 tiles around …: 14 of 30 hours, 1 place found", a hidden site found and logged with *Survey team (…)*, still on the map after a save, a load and two days, a team out of radio range knowing it before the hub); Heat building on a survey, road encounters on the tiles it walks into (against camps of the same hours) and hive land drawing the dead; 30 survey trips (on foot, on bikes and by car, every stance, 2–4 tiles, 8–36 hours) against the planner: home on the estimated hour, the estimated food and water and fuel when no road card delayed them or changed their load (a card during the survey costs survey time, not trip time), and the team panel's queue estimate while it surveys; rumours (the circle holds the place off its centre, the vague "somewhere north of …", fading after 20 days, struck off when the place is found, none without a source, more with travellers at the gate than a radio alone, travellers' news about half the time, the world's random numbers untouched); optics (five items with weight, value, description, effect and their sources; the military and raiders value them; binoculars +1 sight tile and seeing the other side first more often, a thermal more at night, a thermal in the off hand sees like night vision in a night battle, a telescope in the armory widens a base's view, a survey packs the hub's optics and other trips a pair of binoculars); crews with Survey at priority 1 (out with the hub's binoculars, home with the trip in their log), the autopilot's surveys, survey targets that are the same with every unknown hidden site removed (no peeking) and no team sent to scavenge a site nobody has found; a save mid-survey that plays on the same, the same survey twice in one process, and a right-click survey for a team out there. Then how fast the autopilot finds the hidden sites within 12 tiles of the hub with and without surveys (`state.surveyOff`), Cautious, Balanced and Aggressive. Seeds 52–53, 365 days (36 sites near the hub): without surveys 39% by day 30, 61% by day 90, 97% by day 365; with them 44%, 61% and 97% (25 surveys). The autopilot's hunting, fishing and water trips already roam the land near home, and its surveys wait for spare hands above a defensive floor (without the floor: 58%, 75%, 97%, but Aggressive lost its hub early on two seeds of four). Before those trips existed: 44%, 58%, 83% without surveys and 69%, 78%, 97% with.

## Controls

| Input | Action |
| --- | --- |
| Left-click | Inspect a tile (a tile with one of your teams in contact opens its team panel) |
| Right-click | Actions for a tile, or move a waiting team there; also 📌 Pin the base, group, hordes or teams on it, and 📝 Add note / Edit note |
| Right-click with a team selected | That team's actions for the tile, with their hours (a plain pick replaces its plan); **Shift+right-click** adds them to the end of its queue. Other teams and the hub folded below |
| 📌 (survivor screen, team panel, Groups and Bases tabs, tile panel) | Pin it to the Watching panel at the top left of the map (again to unpin); click a row there to go to it |
| Note editor | Pick an icon, type the text; Enter saves, Esc closes |
| Hover | Description of any button or option |
| Drag / wheel | Pan / zoom |
| W A S D or arrow keys | Pan the map |
| Space | Pause / run. A new game starts paused (a banner at the top of the map says so; when a pause rule stopped the clock, it says why); orders given while paused start when you unpause |
| 0 | Speed 0.25× (an in-game hour takes 80 seconds, a day 32 minutes): to watch a move or a fight up close |
| 1–5 | Speed 0.5× / 1× / 2× / 3× / 5× (an in-game hour takes 40 / 20 / 10 / 7 / 4 seconds; a day 16 / 8 / 4 / 2.7 / 1.6 minutes). At 3× and 5× the clock drops back to 1× by itself when a card (one that stops the clock or one for the tray) or a new red alert arrives (Options: *Drop to 1× when something happens*, on by default) |
| H | Center on hub |
| I | Infection overlay |
| B / P / G / T | The Bases / People / Groups / Teams tab on the left |
| Tab or N | Next team waiting for orders: centers on it and opens its team panel (Shift+Tab goes back) |
| F1–F9 | Jump to your bases (F1 the hub, then the outposts in the order they were founded) |
| Ctrl+1–9 (or Alt+1–9) | Save the camera view as spot 1–9 (Alt for browsers that keep Ctrl+1–9 for their own tabs) |
| Shift+1–9 | Go back to a saved camera spot (1–5 alone are the speeds) |
| J | Diary: every day of the run (also the 📖 Diary button in the top bar and the log); J or Esc closes it |
| R | Roster: everyone in one table (also the 👥 Roster button); R or Esc closes it |
| C | Codex: every kind of the dead, item, building, illness and group you have met, with its numbers and how to deal with it (also the 📚 Codex button); C or Esc closes it |
| Ctrl+K or / | Search (also 🔍 in the top bar): a box in the middle of the screen that finds people, bases, teams, groups, known sites, the buildings you can build at the selected base, the menu, Help topics and codex entries by a few letters; ↑ ↓ pick, Enter goes there, Esc closes. Empty, it shows your last picks |
| E | Ledger: production and use of each resource, stock, people, mood and Heat, per base or for all, over 30 / 90 / 365 days or the run, and the run summary (also the 📈 Ledger button); E or Esc closes it |
| M | Minimap on / off (also its – button, and ▣ Minimap in the same corner when it is off; remembered). Click or drag on the minimap to look there |
| Enter / Esc | In the question an order that cannot be undone asks first (and the menu's questions): yes / cancel |
| [ / ] | Fold or show the left / right panel (or click the slim bar between it and the map; drag that bar to make the panel wider or narrower) |
| L | Fold or show the log (folded, its strip shows the latest line; drag its bar to make it taller) |
| Alt+[ / Alt+] / Alt+L | Close the left panel / right panel / log completely, strip and all, so the map takes the room; again to bring it back |
| ✕ on a folded strip, or ◧ ◨ ⬓ in the top bar | The same close, by click; the top-bar button (dark while the panel is closed), the slim tab at that edge of the map (⟩ ⟨ ▴), Alt + the panel's key, the panel's plain key, or picking something it shows opens it again. Remembered like the folds |
| Shift+[ / Shift+] | Fold or show the running orders / the alerts on the map (folded alerts are one button with the count, red when one is urgent) |
| \ (backslash) or ▣ in the top bar | Map only: fold every panel and box over the map; again to put them back as they were |
| Window edges, corners, title | Drag to resize or move a big window (base screen and its Work tab, survivor, trade, radio, caravan, inventory and loot, planner, route, diplomacy, memorial, the menu with Help, diary, roster, new run); double-click the title for its usual size and place. The timelapse's two boxes resize from their corner |
| ` (backquote) or Dev button | With *Show dev tools* on (⚙ Menu → Options → Display; off by default, and the Dev button is hidden until then): dev tools: reveal the map (no fog of war; display only, your intel is unchanged), infection overlay, 10× / 50× / 200× fast forward (Space goes back to your last normal speed, never to a dev speed), *Show FPS* (frame rate, frame time and simulation ticks per second in the map's corner), and a tracker of each group's population, bases and land plus zombie hotzones, hives (count, resources and income, Wallbreakers waiting), hordes, the hive build sites (dead gone in of 1,000, gathered mass, score), the next build-site and border-horde days and the hive record (built, destroyed, attacks held, break-ins beaten, bases overrun), with the change over the last 7 days |
| Shift+P (or Dev tools → Autopilot) | Autopilot on / off, with *Show dev tools* on (P alone is the People tab): an AI governor runs your holdout (pick Cautious / Balanced / Aggressive in the dev panel). Pairs well with Reveal map and fast forward |
| Esc | Close the top window, one layer per press (inventory, then the survivor screen, then the base); then move mode, then the team panel; with nothing open, the menu |
| ⚙ Menu (top bar, or the battle panel) | The menu: Save, Load, Save and exit, Exit without saving, New campaign, Quit to desktop (desktop app), save files (Export, Save to file, Load from file), Options (autosave and how often, confirmations, pause when the window loses focus, sound volumes and mutes, *Pause for…* (the pause rules and the drop to 1×), the battle's end-turn question; Display: UI size, map detail and animations, zombie look, colour-blind-safe colours, larger map text, reduce motion, the minimap and its size, show dev tools; Confirmations: the kinds of order you stopped being asked about, with Ask again; what the mouse does), Controls (every key, rebindable) and About. The game waits while it is open. On the new-run screen, *Continue* loads the latest save and *Load game…* opens the list |
| Battle | Click a unit, then a tile to move or an enemy to shoot; Tab or 1–9 select units; F shoot, I reload (1 action: fills the gun's magazine from the team's ammo), V melee, O overwatch, H hunker, M meds, U crush the skull of a body next to the unit (any human body, any time: a Carrier raises the human dead near it at once; the loot stays), J double tap a body in sight (1 action, 1 round, the noise of a shot), P pick up or put down a body (3 tiles an action and no shooting while carrying; a carried body cannot get up), Z lay a carried body in the team's car (a raid's vehicle zone, a field battle's evac strip), R retreat (one unit, from the evac strip), Shift+R or *Sound the retreat* (everyone at once: a confirm over the bottom bar's turn controls shows each unit's odds, who carries whom and a *Leave* / *Carry* button per downed teammate; Enter gives the order, Esc keeps fighting); Enter ends the turn; Space (or the End turn button) ends it too, but while units still have actions the first press asks over the bottom bar's turn controls ("2 units still have actions — press Space again or Enter") and the second ends it (*Don't ask again* or the checkbox under the panel switches this off, remembered in the browser); Esc or right-click cancels. Shift+C or *Area clear: finish* ends a fight with only a couple of far-off unaware stragglers left, as a win. On the result screen Enter or Space is *Take all and continue*. Stealth kit: C subdue (a takedown on the unaware or staggering, else a non-lethal blow), T tie up someone knocked out, G throw a bottle / noise maker / firecrackers (then click a tile), Shift+D drag a body, someone knocked out or a downed teammate (click it, then where to). Search, quick search and drag are on Shift (Shift+S, Shift+Q, Shift+D) because W A S D pan the view. Before a break-in (deploy step): click a defender, then a green cell; *Hold the gate*, *Default positions*, floodlight aim; Enter or *Start the fight* begins it |
| Battle view | Always isometric (there is no top-down battle view). Drag the map (any button), hold W A S D or the arrow keys, or push the mouse to the edge to pan; wheel or + / − zoom (9 steps); Q and E turn the view a quarter (, and . too); Home centers on the selected unit; B roofs on / off; in a building with several storeys PageUp / PageDown (or the view bar's Floor ▲ ▼) show the floor above / below, selecting a unit shows its floor, clicking the ▲ / ▼ marker of someone on another floor jumps there. A click is made on release (a press that moves pans instead); right click cancels |
| Raid | As in battle, plus Shift+S search the container next to the unit (1 action, everything shows; opens the search window: drag what to take), Shift+Q a quick search (a rummage: no action, once a turn, 30–50% of it turns up, loud: Heat +5 and the unaware within 7 tiles come to look; 6% chance of cutting a hand), N open the vehicle when standing at it, and X extract from a green extraction zone (R explains this instead of retreating); inside a building K peeks through the door or doorway next to the unit (free, once a turn), Shift+E closes the open door next to it, Y braces a closed door, L climbs through the window next to it (with several doors next to the unit, click the one you mean). Next to a locked container or door (padlock marker, brown door) the panel shows *Key* / *Pick N%* / *Force N%* (or *Smash*) buttons with the odds (*Pick N% a try*: 1 action an attempt; hover for how Stealth and the difficulty make the odds). In the search window click a supply stack to pick how many to move (1, −, the number, +, ½, All, What fits), then click where they go; Shift+click sends all that fit straight across; drag moves all that fit (Shift on the drop: half, Ctrl: one) |
| Ctrl+Z | Undo the last change, one step at a time (up to 20), in the trip planner, a base's Construction tab (the build queue) and its Work tab; the ↶ Undo button there does the same. Nothing else (never in a battle) |
| Work tab (a base screen) | Priority cells: left-click counts up (blank → 1 … 5 → blank), right-click counts down, Shift+click changes the role's default for everyone in it; with the mouse over a cell 1–5 set it, 0 / Backspace / Delete clear it, R puts it back to the role's default. The schedule strip: click Work → Guard → Free → Sleep, right-click back |
| Timelapse (game over or dev tools) | Space play / pause, ← / → one day, Home / End, 1–4 speed 1 / 2 / 5 / 10 days per second, I infection, T territory, F fit the map, drag / wheel / WASD pan and zoom, click a caption to go there, Esc close |

Every key above (but Esc and the Work tab's number keys) can be changed in **Menu → Controls**: the keys by group (Time, Map, Panels, Windows, Teams, Bases, Camera spots, Battle, Battle view, Timelapse…); click a key and press the new one (Esc cancels), + adds a second key, ✕ takes one off, Reset / Reset all. Two actions on the same key where both work (the map, a battle, the timelapse) are flagged in red, and only the first answers until one gets another key. When a later build gives an action a new default key (Q / E turn the battle view, V melee, Shift+E close a door, Home center: `keymap.js` NEW_DEFAULTS) that your own saved binding already gives another action, yours keeps it and the new default steps aside (the action keeps its other keys, or has none until you pick one). The keys are kept in the browser with the other options, not in the save. **Help → Keys** is the same list as a key sheet, with a 🖨 Print button that prints only the sheet. The table above lists the default keys.

The top bar keeps the clock and weather, the stock (the Needs button) with the 7-day threat forecast joined to it, the head count, 📖 Diary, 👥 Roster, 📈 Ledger, 📚 Codex, 🔍 Search, Help and ⚙ Menu (and the panel buttons); UI size (75–200%), the zombie look and the readability options are in ⚙ Menu → Options → Display. Map shortcuts do nothing while a window is open (a card, the planner, a base, Help) or on the new-run screen; only J, R, C and E work in the diary, roster, codex and Ledger (they close it, or switch from one to another), and the search box (Ctrl+K) opens over any window but a card. Help (top bar) opens the menu's Help tab (`src/ui/help.js`): the game as it is now, one topic at a time (the help's paragraphs plus Map layers and Map legend), with ◂ / ▸ to step through. Small ? buttons open it at their topic: the Layers box (Map layers), the paused banner (Time) and the right panel with nothing selected (Map).

Windows and panels (`src/ui/layout.js` rules, `src/ui/windows.js` on the page): a folded panel gives the map its room (the canvas is measured again and the camera kept on the same land; one redraw). A folded panel opens by itself when you pick something it shows: a tile, site, base or team on the map, a team or tile from a list, an alert or a log link open the right panel; a tab key (B P G T) the left one; a new urgent (red) alert opens folded alerts, except in map-only mode. The log never opens for a new line. *Auto-open panels on select* in Options switches this off. Folded panels, panel widths and each kind of window's size and place are remembered in this browser (Options → *Reset window sizes* puts them back); window sizes are kept in rem, so they follow the UI size, and are clamped to the screen. Help → *Windows and panels* lists all of this.

## Hardcore mode

Chosen on the new-run screen (**Mode**: Normal or Hardcore; `src/sim/hardcore.js`, which dispatches to one module per part). A normal game is unchanged: the same seed builds the same world and plays the same run. A save remembers its mode (the save list and the menu's *This game* line say which); a save without one is Normal.

- **The timeline:** Hardcore day 1 is the day the State of Calder falls; the normal game begins 6–12 months later (game months: a game year is 112 days), when people have built their hubs. Each hardcore world sets that point by its seed: `state.hardcore.eraOffset`, days 56–112.
- **You** (`src/sim/hardcore-start.js`, numbers in `HC_START`): three survivors on the road, no hub, one car between them. **Starting people** (new-run screen, Hardcore only; `hardcore.js START_PEOPLE`, `createWorld({ startPeople })`, kept as `state.startPeople` and in the save; a hardcore save from before it loads as three): 1–4 flee together, 3 by default (the start below; the same seed with 3 builds the same run as before). Each one rolls their own grab, so the team's own food and water scale with the head count (1–3 days each, `TEAM_MIN`..`TEAM_MAX` per person; food by background, below); the car, what is in its back and a prepper's bag stay one of each (one car fled, one bag was packed), so one or two have more of those per head, four less, and fewer hands carry, scavenge, build the camp (the same materials) and fight slower. **One or two** (`HC_START.SMALL`, 2026-10-07): so a solo or a pair is not several times deadlier than three, for each seat short of three (two alone, one for a pair) everyone on the team gets +1–2 in Scouting, Stealth and Medicine (finding food, slipping past trouble, patching yourself up: +2–4 alone) and the car's empty seats were loaded too, 3–6 food, 3–6 water and 1 meds a seat on top of the trunk (alone: 6–12 food, 6–12 water, 2 meds); the orders card and day 1's log say so. Three or four draw no dice for it, so a default start is the same start. On the road a small team also meets fewer armed strangers, and fewer of them (wanderers.js `HC_SMALL`, under People on the move). A normal start is a full hub of six and does not take it (the select is greyed out with the reason). They spawn on open land (no site on the tile), away from the outbreak's origin and hives (14+ and 8+ tiles), a city centre (3+ tiles; its blocks can be close by), the military sites and the other groups, out of the map's outer fifth, with a town, a city or any other site within 5 tiles (somewhere to drive to on day 1; 8 relaxed), and a river or lake within 6 tiles (a camp has no spring; 10 relaxed) (relaxed a step when no tile qualifies). New-run crew edits apply to the three (the leader too; a leader picked from the normal crew's last three falls back to the first). What each grabbed as they fled is rolled: half fled with a bag (a school backpack, a sling bag, now and then a hiking pack) holding 2–5 things (a flashlight, a bandage kit, chocolate, a novel, toilet paper, batteries, a bottle, now and then binoculars or a water filter); the others with only their clothes and 0–4 small things in their pockets (a phone, lighters, family photos, a flashlight, candy, cigarettes). A weapon from the house for about 65% with a bag and 40% without (mostly a kitchen knife or a bat; a crowbar, a pistol, a machete, now and then a revolver or a hunting rifle), a gun with a few rounds (3–12). Food and water: 1–3 days each (3–9 of each for three), a bag holds more; **food by background** (`ARCHETYPES` `food`, 2026-10-07): a farmer grabbed 2–5 with a bag (1–2 in pockets), a hunter 2–4 (1–2), a trucker 1–4 (1), and they lift the team's food cap to 5, 4 and 4 for their head; a veteran 1–2 (0–1), a nurse, a mechanic or a cop 0–2 (0–1), who ran with ammo, a vest, meds or tools instead; the others the common 1–3 (0–1). Weighted over the backgrounds a head averages about the common grab (+0.1 food), so a team of three carries about what it did and a fourth brings food by who they were; maybe a bottle of pills; no fuel, scrap or parts beyond what is in the car. **Who they were** (`HC_START.ARCHETYPES`, 2026-10-06): each of the three is drawn, no two alike, from twelve backgrounds (prepper, veteran, college student, office worker, blue-collar worker, nurse, farmer, hunter, mechanic, police officer, trucker, teacher; weighted). A background bumps two to four skills (a nurse +4–7 Medicine, a veteran +3–5 Shooting, a mechanic +4–7 Mechanics...), pushes a fitting trait 60% of the time when a slot allows (a veteran Brave or Tough, a hunter Keen Eye, a nurse Former Medic), and shapes what they grabbed. **Balance** (2026-10-07): every background's skill bumps average 6–8.5 points (before: 5 for an office worker, a blue-collar worker or a trucker, 9 for a hunter), and the ones that bring the most kit get the fewest: a veteran (+3–5 Shooting, +1–2 Leadership and Scouting, a vest 35%, a hunting rifle less often, 1.5× rounds) and a cop (+2–4 Shooting, a vest 50%, 1.5× rounds) carry less than they did; an office worker (+1–2 Scavenging, Keen Eye likelier than Bad Back, a first-aid kit and maybe pills), a teacher (+1–2 Medicine, a school first-aid kit, maybe pills), a trucker (+3–5 Scouting, +0–2 Shooting for the revolver in the cab, Night Owl likelier), a blue-collar worker (+3–5 Mechanics, a tool to hand 70% of the time), a student (+1–2 Scouting) and a farmer (+0–2 Shooting for the rifle) more; the prepper's packed bag turns up less often (weight 0.9, was 1.2; car odds 1.3). It shapes what they grabbed: the bag (a veteran an assault pack or rucksack, a student almost always a school backpack, an office worker a sling bag half the time), the weapon (a cop a pistol with double the rounds and a vest, a hunter a rifle or a bow, a tradesman a crowbar or a fire axe), and the small things (a student's laptop and candy, a trucker's pocket radio and coffee, a nurse's bandage kits). The prepper keeps the packed duffle bag above. `s.background` holds it; the People tab shows it as a chip. **Customize and custom backgrounds** (2026-10-07, user request; not with Ironman): the new-run screen's *Customize* box sets any skill level 0–20 with no point budget and picks traits (up to 3, good and bad in two rows, never two that clash: `data.js` `TRAIT_CLASH`), and in Hardcore a *Background* select on each colonist picks who they were: the dice (what was drawn), a preset, or a custom background. Picking one builds the world again on the same seed (the first pick gives the crew dice of their own, so the map and groups stay put from then on; the others keep who they were); that colonist's skills and traits start over from it. *Backgrounds…* opens the editor (`src/ui/backgrounds.js`, kept in this browser under `brokenstate.customBackgrounds`, at most 24): name and blurb, skill bumps (0–10 each), traits (up to 3, always given and first), what they wear and hold (hands, off hand, body, head, arms, legs, bag), up to 12 carried things (a tool to the off hand, small things to the four pockets, the rest to the bag; it warns what would be left behind and shows the kg), the supplies on them (food and water on top of the team's clamped 1–3 days, like a prepper's bag; ammo, arrows, meds), the car (odds 0–10 that it is theirs, 0 never; its kind; fuel, food, water, ammo, meds, scrap and parts and up to 10 things in the back). *From a preset…* starts from a preset's dice made exact (the middle of each roll, its likeliest bag, weapon and things). A custom background draws no dice (`src/sim/custombg.js` makes it safe; `hardcore-start.js` `archetypeOf`, `pickArchetypes`, `rollCustom`); the world keeps its own copy (`state.customBackgrounds`, saved), the chip is marked ✎. **Ironman**: the crew starts as rolled. Ticking Ironman turns Customize off, greys out Backgrounds… and every skill button (moving points too), and if the crew was customized (`s.startCustom`, `s.bgPicked`) builds the world again with only the names, looks and leader kept (the crew's original dice); Start refuses an Ironman run with a customized crew. A run started customized has `state.customStart`. **Normal starts** (2026-10-07, user request): the six at the hub have a background too (the chip on each, the select with Customize), drawn as a hardcore crew is (no two alike) but with dice of their own (`hardcore-start.js` `normalStartBackgrounds`, `NORMAL_BG.SALT`), so the map, the groups and the rest of the run are what the seed made before. A preset reshapes their skills and keeps the total (`keepSkillTotal`: its bumps go on, as many points come off their other skills, more often the higher ones), so a normal start is as strong as it was, and pushes a fitting trait as in Hardcore; what they grabbed and the car are a hardcore start's (six years on, the hub's kit is theirs). A custom background gives exactly what it says (`normalStartKit`): its bumps and traits, its kit worn and held (what the hub had handed them goes to the armory), its supplies and the car's back to the hub's stores and armory, and the car parked at the hub when it names a kind and its odds are above 0. `NORMAL_BG.ON = false` starts the six without (the tool compares against it). `node tools/custombgtest.js` checks it all (about 30 s). **The car** (`HC_START.CAR`, `rollCar`): one of the three fled in their car, whose by their background's odds (truckers, preppers and tradesmen likeliest, students least): preppers and blue-collar workers mostly pickup trucks, a prepper now and then a Hummer, students, office workers, nurses and teachers old sedans or SUVs, veterans trucks or Hummers, cops SUVs, truckers and mechanics work vans (`vehicles.js`: the SUV, the Hummer and the work van are new, drawn and priced as a truck where nothing says otherwise). Its tank is 50–100% full, its condition 55–90%, and what the owner kept in the back is the team's cargo: a farmer's harvest and jars, a trucker's food and jerry cans, a tradesman's tools and lumber (scrap and parts), a prepper's gear and rounds, a nurse's meds, a student's laptop, an office worker's lunch. The team sets off in it and it parks at the first hub when the camp is founded. The run opens on the team's orders card, which says who each was, what they grabbed, and whose car it is and what is in the back. You are with the team: it always takes orders and you see live around it (as if in radio contact), and the map centres on it. People met on the road can join it.
- **The first home: camp → shelter → base** (`src/sim/staging.js`, `buildings.js` SHELTER and hub_up): your home grows in three stages. With no base, a team on a land tile gets the order **Establish base** (it replaces the old instant founding). It pitches a **staging camp** there (one at a time; not on water, a hive or another group's land): a site on the map in your color (a tarp shelter, crates and a fire; then the first stakes and the posts of a shack; the shack half boarded and the barricade on both sides; the shack roofed and the barricade all but closed, with a flag), with a bar of the work done. The camp lists what the shelter takes: **15 scrap, 2 parts, 10 food and 10 water** (10 scrap less on the ruins of a burned base): the old cost of the hub (30 scrap, 4 parts, 15 food and 15 water, 108 person-hours) is split over the two steps. The team unloads into the camp's stores (the same order at the camp: *Unload and work on the camp*): all its scrap and parts, and its food and water beyond two days of rations (down to one day while the stores are short); it can leave to scavenge and come back as often as it likes. Then the **work**: 60 person-hours of daylight work (07:00–19:00), each worker 1 an hour + 3% a Mechanics point (× health and the Handy / Master Builder perks, as base construction): **under 2 days for three people**, about 5 for one alone, 1 for six (more than six add nothing). The work goes on as far as the scrap and parts in the stores allow (half of them: half the work) and stops there with a report; the shelter stands once the work is done and the stores hold everything. Nobody at the camp, no progress; at night the workers rest (the hurt heal as in a camp); they eat their own rations and from the camp's stores only once theirs run out, and a team at the camp takes food and water for the road (up to 2 days) from what the stores hold beyond the needs. Food and water go in up to twice the need (the rest stays with the team and joins the shelter's stock when it moves in). **Water must be found:** the camp has no spring. Beside a river or lake the people there drink from it and haul 10 a day into the stores (nothing while nobody is there); anywhere else every drop is carried in (*Fetch water* at a shore, water found searching), and a worker team with no water left and dry stores stops and says where the nearest water is. The order's text, the camp's log line, its card and the tile panel say *Water: river 1 tile away* (or 2–3 tiles, or *No water source nearby*). **Camp danger** (`fieldheat.js` CAMP_HEAT): everyone at the camp shares its Heat, made of what the camp does, per hour: camped there 0.3, 0.12 a person, the **fire** 0.5 by day and 1.4 at night (its light carries; a smokeless stove ×0.6), 0.5 a car and 0.7 the pickup parked there (and +0.12 danger each: raiders want them), **shooting** at or next to the camp lately 0.1 a shot (every fight's shots go on the map; a neighbour tile counts half; it halves every 18 hours, at most +3), and 0.3 while the building work goes on; × the place (city, suburbs, infection, hive land), stealthy and the Ranger perk, and the camp kit. The Heat **settles**: 3% of it fades each hour, so it levels off near 33 × the hourly gain (three working with the fire lit day and night near 60; the fire out at night near 35) instead of climbing to 100; one roll for trouble an hour for the whole camp (the dead, a horde close by, raiders). The camp's card shows it as *Fire at night: +1.4, 4 people: +0.48, Truck: +0.7, Shots yesterday: +0.6*, where it settles and the Heat now. **The fire** is a toggle on the camp's card (and in the team panel): lit, it keeps the cold off (cold sickness ×0.35), cooks the food and boils the water (no stomach bugs from raw food: 1.5% a person a day without it) and lifts spirits (+0.3 mood an hour resting by it, +0.1 without); new camps start with it lit. **Neglect:** nobody there for 10 days and scavengers may come (35% a day): 30–60% of each store and a tenth of the work gone each time; empty and unworked for 20 days it is gone. A team that dies there leaves the camp standing for the next. *Strike the camp* (at the camp) packs what the team can carry and leaves the rest as a cache on the tile. **Done:** the camp becomes your **shelter** (tier 0, "… Holdout"), bare: no buildings, no truck, the scrap and parts used up, the rest of the stores and everything the teams there carry its stock, everyone at the camp its people, starting jobs by skill as a normal start; a log line (a timelapse caption) and a card mark the day. **A shelter** is a real base you live at (the hub: stores, rest and healing, the armory, people with jobs, the base panel), but small: tarps and a shack behind a scrap barricade (its own map sprite, labelled *(shelter)*), **no wall strength**, beds for 4, 2 crop plots (a garden patch, as an outpost), **1 tile of vision**, **no well** (beside a river or lake its people haul 10 water a day; else rain catchers and what teams bring in; water holds 40), and room only for **2 rain catchers, a drying rack, a barricade and one farm plot** (no walls, towers, wells, bunkhouses or anything else). In a base battle it is the base defence with only its bunk shack behind the barricade on a small yard map, so it holds back far fewer of the dead. Its Heat is the base's (people, workshops) with its fire (1 by day, 3 at night), its parked vehicles (1.5 a car, 2 the pickup) and shooting nearby (0.25 a weighted shot, at most 6) in place of the hub's flat 3; its fire is a toggle in the base panel (lit: warmth indoors, boiled water, +0.5 mood a day; out: the tarps are as cold as outside, dirty-water sickness doubles, and cold nights cost 1 mood). The base panel shows *Camp → [Shelter] → Base*, what the next step takes and the button: **Build up into a base** (Construction; shelters only): **18 scrap, 2 parts and 96 work-hours** by the shelter's builders (about 2 days for two). Done, it is the hub as it always was (a main house, the dug well's 12 water a day, the cellar, wall strength 3, 3 tiles of vision, beds for 4 and every building). A camp from a save made before the tiers becomes the full hub it was promised. Losing that hub later is the exiles' camp below; later outposts keep *Build outpost* (15 scrap). Before the hub exists the top bar shows the team's food, water, scrap, parts, ammo and meds and the camp (built %, what it needs); the Bases tab has a *Your first hub* card (with no camp: what it takes and the best spots within 4 tiles; with one: its stores against its needs, built %, the materials' limit, workers and days left, days alone, threats, the stage and what the next one takes, its water, its danger by cause and the fire toggle); the Goals card starts with *First: found your hub* and the camp's state; the alerts warn when the team's water or food runs under a day and a half, keep the camp's missing materials up, and flag a horde within 2 tiles, Heat 60+ or raiders close (camp in danger), nobody there for 3+ days, and everything in with nobody at work; the camp's tile panel has its card and the map tooltip its line; the tile panel measures the walk from the team and rates the tile as a home (sites around, and *Water: river 1 tile away* or *No water source nearby*); the planner says there is no hub to send from.
- **Autopilot** (`src/sim/autopilot.js` roadAct, campAct): on the road it pitches a staging camp at the best spot within 4 tiles (water decides most: beside a river or lake +6, 2 tiles away +1.5, none within 3 −2; then sites around, few of the dead; with no spot beside water within 4 it looks out to 9 tiles for one) once it carries 30% of the scrap or from day 3, then ferries: it searches the best known sites for what the camp still lacks (pulled toward the camp; water and food first when under two days), hunts or fishes when food is what is missing and the land promises 4+ rations in 8 hours, fetches water at a river or lake, rests when someone is badly hurt, leaves behind what the camp does not need, takes in people it meets while its food lasts, and goes back to unload when it carries everything missing, half the missing scrap and parts within 6 tiles, a full load, or when the camp has materials and nobody at work; there it works until the materials run out. While the hub has four or fewer people one person minds it, the leader goes out too, and a trip leaves the hub a day of food each and takes the rest. At the camp a team short of food or water takes what the stores hold beyond the needs. A 20-game sample (`node tools/hcstarttest.js 20 60`: seeds 9000–9247, Balanced, the hardcore outbreak) pitched the camp on days 1–3, all 20 beside a river or lake, had the shelter in 19 of 20 on days 3–12 (median 5, the hub of old was median 6), after 1–4 deliveries, and built it up into a base in 18 (days 7–33, median 17); 18 of 20 still had people alive on day 60, none died of thirst (the one that never finished its camp starved on the road, short of parts and food). At a shelter it queues Build up into a base first (saving for it), takes water runs with whoever it can spare, and puts the fires out on mild nights. Exiles (8 normal worlds after day 20 with every base lost to a garrison too strong to retake) pitched at once and had their new hub 1–10 days later, all of them alive. The governor gathers fast (it knows the loot tables and never pauses); a careful player will likely take longer.
- **The outbreak** (`src/sim/hardcore-outbreak.js`):
  - **Day 1:** one hive, in a city: the first hive rises among the blocks of the city nearest mapgen's outbreak origin (open ground within 5 of its centre, city and suburb blocks first; 25 more resources than a young hive, so it has a guard within days), and the infection starts there. Every hive mapgen placed is taken off the map (their tiles are where the first year's hives rise). Your three never start within 14 tiles of that city (10 relaxed). The military base's hive comes later (below). With no military group, a second hive starts at another hot spot half the time. Infection is 0 everywhere but within about 4 tiles of the city's hive.
  - **The first year** (until the era offset, numbers in `HC`): each tile keeps the infection a normal start would give it as its goal (`t.infGoal`) and climbs toward it: a front widening out from the hives, the cities and towns filling first (each on its own day, sooner near the outbreak, cities before towns; the dead pack them at 0.55 / 0.4 or more early on, settling back to their normal level), open country slowest; tiles below their level rise up to 0.03 a day faster. By the normal start every tile is at its normal level. New hives rise at the spots a normal start has them, each as a build site on its own day (the origin's cluster first, at 12–50% of the year; the others at 30–80%), standing in 12–20 days, until the state holds a normal start's count. Sample seeds: land infection 0.01–0.02 on day 1, 0.08–0.15 on day 30, 0.19–0.29 on day 60, normal (0.20–0.29) by day 90–112; hives 1–2 → 5 by day 112.
  - **The eras** (`eras.js rampDay`): every ramp keyed to the year or the game day (the tech tree's tiers and special dead, bigger hordes, the outbreak beyond the border and its floor, the year victories open) counts from the normal start, so hardcore day N ramps like normal day N − offset. The hives' own new-site cadence begins after the offset and border hordes at half of it.
  - **The military base's hive:** 1–3 days after the military's strike force leaves for the city (below), a mass of the dead gathers 3–6 tiles from the base (a radio call: "The dead are massing…") and builds a hive in 8–14 days while the garrison is thin; while the mass is short of what the hive still needs, the dead of the surroundings drift in (up to 40 a day), so the garrison's shelling delays it but does not stop it. Once it stands it grows faster (+3 a day) and sends hordes at the base 2–4 days later, then every 6 days down to every 3, each sized from what would break the thinned garrison (0.3× at first, 1× at 70% of the way to the overrun day, 1.4× by it). The overrun day is set by the seed at days 24–48, never sooner than 10 days after the hive stands. Destroying the hive (or its mass) stops it. The Goals card shows "The fall · the outbreak is spreading (N days to go)".
- **The loot** (`src/sim/hardcore-loot.js`, `HC_LOOT`): the buildings are only half picked over: every site starts with twice what a normal start leaves (`tile.loot` × 2, the normal level kept in `tile.lootNormal`), and the surplus wears off by the same share a day (the panic looting nobody tracks) to a tenth by the day a normal game would start (`state.hardcore.eraOffset`), and on toward nothing; searching a place takes from it as always. The raid map's richness cap is 2 in hardcore (1.2 in a normal game). More people are on the move in the first weeks (People on the move, below).
- **The other groups** (`src/sim/hardcore-groups.js`, numbers in `HC_GROUPS` and `HC_MILITARY`):
  - **Bands:** every group but the military starts as a band on the road with no base, 14–34 tiles from you near a site its type likes. It is already what it will become: traders, a commune, a fortress, zealots or expansionists (never raiders). It carries more than you, by type: traders fuel, meds, parts and preserved food; a commune about 110 food, tools and jars; a fortress 12–17 people and about 150 ammo; zealots 16–22 people; expansionists about 170 ammo. Bands walk in daylight, scavenge a site on the way, eat and forage, lose people to hunger and hordes, and found their hub at a site their type prefers (factions.js `PREFERRED_SITES`) on a day between 8 and 29 by the seed (one still on the road 10 days later settles where it stands). Founding makes it a normal group with the same name, colour, people, stores and goodwill. A band is not in `state.factions` until then (it lives in `state.hardcore.groups.bands`), so diplomacy, radio, trade, wars, the coalition's land and conquest never see a group without a base. A band you see is drawn as a party; a team of yours within a tile gets a card (trade news, give 5 food, ask a friendly band for help, go on); the Groups tab lists bands met *On the road*.
  - **How the military fell** (the hardcore era's story of it): the military always exists in hardcore: its base on a National Guard site, 12+ tiles from you when possible, 36–46 soldiers with plenty of ammo, known from the start; it neither expands nor grows.
    - *The strike:* on a day from 3 to 12 (by the seed) it sends 50–65% of its soldiers (keeping at least 13) with their share of the ammo against the hive in the city, as a column of trucks drawn on the map like a group's convoy where you can see it (driving by day; the tile panel and the Groups tab say where it is). At the city it fights 2–4 days by the numbers: its guns cut down 5–9 of the dead a soldier a day, the city's own dead first (500–900, more where the city is thick with them, 60–140 more pouring in each day), then the hive's guard (at most 60% of it) and 2–5 of the hive's stores a day; it loses 50–70% of its soldiers in all (more against more of the dead), and the soldiers who fall join the hive's guard as armoured dead. It fails: the hive stands, weakened. The survivors fall back to a site 3–8 tiles from the hive and are cut off: they hold out there while the base's hive stands (60 days at most) and only go home when it is gone. Radio lines through the fight, a timelapse caption when it leaves and when it fails, and a card each time when you are in contact (a radio at your hub, a friendly Guard, or your people within 8 tiles or seeing the column).
    - *The garrison* fights the hive by its base: sorties against the free dead within 6 tiles (30% a day; never a hive's guard or the mass building one) and shelling every 10–16 days (the hive's stores and guard, or 8–16% of the gathering mass before it stands). Down to 85% of the garrison it asks you for help (40 ammo: harder sorties for two weeks; 6 meds: 2 soldiers back on the walls); at 70% it sends you its civilians. It falls when the garrison is down to 65% of what stayed (8 at least), loses the base or is broken: the base becomes a ruin full of the dead (a horde of the fallen in body armour, infection, the armory's loot left behind), a last radio message, a card and a timelapse caption; 10–25% of the last defenders die and 1–3 soldiers reach you as recruits.
    - *The splinters:* the military is gone, and what is left of it becomes groups of its own, each a band on the road that founds its hub 8–20 days later somewhere defensible (its type's preferred sites, 10+ tiles from any hive): the garrison's survivors as the base's **Holdouts** ("Marrow Guard Holdouts", a fortress, with a fifth of the base's stores) and the strike force's stragglers as the **Remnant of the 3rd Battalion** (or "of Bravo Company"; military, with the ammo they have left). Fewer than 5 join the other; a handful in all make for a peaceful group or come to you. Soldiers on the road lose fewer to the dead than other bands and a horde never leaves them too few to found. A splinter counts as desperate when the raiders' day comes, so the Holdouts may turn to raiding. Destroying the hive by the base saves it: the strike force's survivors come home.
    - Sample (16 seeds, the player kept fed, to day 110): the strike leaves on days 3–11 and fails on days 7–19 with 52–71% lost; the base's hive stands on days 17–29; the base falls on days 27–41; two splinter groups on all 16 (Holdouts 5–8, Remnant 6–10 people), both founded their hubs on all 16 (days 36–68); 4 of the Holdouts turned to raiding, one broke up later.
  - **Raiders:** no raider groups, outsider raider bands or new gangs until `state.hardcore.raidersFrom` (0.6–1.0 × the era offset by the seed, about days 35–112). From then struggling groups may turn to raiding (0.4% a day, more when hungry, small or aggressive; the most desperate one within 14 days; at most two this way), and outsider bands and gangs come as in a normal game. Zealots and expansionists do not raid and nobody declares war until 10 days after the last band has its hub (day 60 at the latest).

`node tools/hcoutbreaktest.js` checks the first hive in the city nearest the origin (16 seeds) and your start far from it, infection 0 away from the outbreak, towns and cities ahead of open country, normal-start levels by day 112, the dead massing by the base and their hive standing, its hordes at the base and the fall on days 20–60, the era offset and save/load. `node tools/hcgroupstest.js` checks the bands on day 1 and their stores by type, meeting one, founding by day 30–45, no early raiders, save/load mid-founding, the military's arc on 8 seeds (the strike force leaves in its window with half or more of the soldiers, reaches the city hive, fails with heavy losses while the hive stands, stays cut off; the hive by the base stands; the base falls on days 20–60 on most seeds; ruin and radio message; splinter groups formed and founded; recruits), save/load with the strike force on the road, destroying the base's hive saves the base and brings the strike force home, holding without a hive, raiders after their day, and 45 days of autopilot.

`node tools/hcstarttest.js [games] [days]` checks the start: the spawn rules on 12 seeds, the gear rolls (bags and pockets, valid items in valid places, varied), the low supplies; the staging camp (the order offered and no instant founding, the needs, nothing to build with, one camp at a time, a first trip of half the materials and the work stopping at half, no progress while away, the second trip, the hub it becomes: bare, the materials used, the stores as stock, the finds in the armory, everyone moved in, jobs by skill, the log lines), the work by crew (under 2 days for three, over twice that alone, under 60% with six, built in 1–3 days), Heat while building, the camp looted after 10 days alone, striking it (a cache), a team lost there (the camp stays); the hubless views (top bar, the four tabs, tile panel, team panel, cards, planner, diary, roster, alerts, settings, the new-run screen in both modes) clean before and after two weeks on the road and with a camp (its card, the Goals line, the alert, the map tooltip); the three stages (the camp becomes a shelter: tier 0, small walls, beds, plots and vision, no well, its upkeep per head, the shack-only battle map and lower hold, only a rain catcher, drying rack, barricade and one farm plot, the base panel with the stage, the step-up button, the water source and the fire; Build up into a base by its builders into the full hub); camp danger (the fire lit and out, worse at night, one more person, a truck parked and raiders wanting it, shots at and next to the camp fading over days, the Heat settling, the team panel text, a team's own camp and its fire toggle); water (no spring: a dry camp gets nothing by itself, water carried in goes to the stores, a riverside camp fills them, the tile panel's *Water: …* row, a shelter's river haul and rain catchers); saves before, during (the camp, its workers, its fire and its target come back, and the loaded world finishes it at the same hour) and after founding (the shelter and its fire), and an old save's camp (no tier: it becomes the full hub); and the autopilot pitching a camp (beside water in most games), founding a shelter in most games within four weeks and building it up into a base (default 8 games, 40 days). `node tools/hardcoresmoke.js` creates a normal and a hardcore world and runs five days. `hcstarttest.js` also starts 1, 2, 3 and 4 people (each that many on the team, the food and water 1–3 days each, the default 3 the same run as leaving it out, out-of-range counts clamped, the count through a save) and `uitest.js` checks the select (four choices, greyed out for a normal start). Its section for 2026-10-07 checks the food by background (each in its own ranges over 300 rolls, the food backgrounds above the common grab and the armed or skilled ones below, the weighted mean about the common, the team's food cap, fours with a farmer, hunter or trucker carrying more a head), the small teams (the skills bumped and nothing else, no dice drawn for three or four, the seats loaded within their ranges, the card and log lines, the load, a save) and the road (the small-team rule for one and two only and only in hardcore, the band sizes, the one-stranger card and its fight, 4000 road rolls on city ground: fewer aggressive meetings alone and for a pair, as many people met in all).

## What is in it

- **Minimap** (`src/ui/minimap.js`, M): the whole state in the map's bottom-right corner, as far as you know it (Reveal map in the dev tools shows all): land bright where you see it, dimmer where you have a report, dark where you have none; water and roads; the Territory, Infection and Coalition layers tinted like the map when they are on; your bases (blue squares, the hub bigger) and teams (yellow when waiting for orders, faded at the planned position out of radio contact); the hordes (faded where last reported) and hives you know of; other groups' known bases with the Territory layer; the camera's frame. Click or drag on it to look there. Small / Medium / Large in Display, in rem so it grows with the UI size; remembered. Cheap: a pixel block per tile in one small image, rewritten only where tiles changed when the hour, fog, layers or colours change (at most twice a second), dots gathered twice a second, the canvas redrawn only when something or the camera moved (at most 30 times a second); hidden, one check a frame.
- **Readability** (⚙ Menu → Options → Display; `src/ui/display.js`, `src/ui/palette.js`): *Colour-blind-safe colours* (good / bad as blue / orange instead of green / red everywhere: moods, stock, odds, health, friend and foe through the CSS variables, and the canvas code reads one palette table: battle hit chances, health bars, threat lines, extraction zones, the map's Infection layer and hive land in purple), *Larger map text* (names, badges and crew tags on the State Map ×1.4, apart from the UI size), *Reduce motion* (map animations off, teams jump tile to tile instead of gliding, no pulsing hives or sieges, no CSS animation or transition anywhere). *Show dev tools* brings back the Dev button and the ` key (the Shift+P autopilot too); `window.game.dev()` opens them for a session (tools, the desktop perf driver). Map animations Off is now remembered across reloads (it was lost).
- **One confirmation style** (`src/ui/confirm.js`): every order that cannot be undone asks first in the same box over everything, with the consequence spelled out, Yes / Cancel and Enter / Esc, and *Don't ask again* for that kind of order (remembered; ⚙ Menu → Options → Confirmations lists the kinds stopped, with Ask again): executing or releasing a prisoner, pulling a building down, breaking gear worth more than junk down for firewood, attacking a caravan or robbing merchants, joining or leaving a war, expelling a coalition member, giving up a job, ending a trade route, deleting a supply run or a work role, digging a growing crop under. The menu's own questions (load, exit without saving, new campaign, delete a save, save over…) are the same box; the leaving-with-progress-not-saved one can be stopped there (the option *Confirm before leaving a game*). Battles keep their own safety: "Space again" to end a turn with actions left, and the retreat and fall-back panels with each unit's odds.
- **Procedural State of Calder** (64 × 42 hexes on a Normal map, `src/sim/mapgen.js`): terrain, 3–4 rivers, roads, an outbreak origin with infestation hives (plus a second hot spot in the other city), and about 185 sites in four zones. New map per seed.
  - *World sizes* (`src/sim/worldsize.js`, chosen on the new-run screen; the numbers below are for a Normal map): everything that comes with land is scaled by area: towns, countryside sites, landmarks, highway stops, rivers (by width), the hives (about 2 per 1,000 tiles), how often border hordes arrive and new hive sites are picked (and how many can be built at once), and how far from the hub the groups settle (by the square root). Bigger maps are for longer games: raid reach is the same 30 tiles on every size, only 5 (Large) or 4 (Huge) groups start within it (the rest come into reach as they expand), and the hub is placed at least 28 (Large) or 34 (Huge) tiles from the outbreak origin, so about as many hives start within striking range of it as on a Normal map (5.3 and 5.4 within 45 tiles, Normal 4.9); over two years (30 autopilot runs each) about half the runs fall on Normal, Large and Huge alike (Huge was 63% before). Normal is the original map: its seeds and old saves build exactly what they did. A save remembers its size (one without it is a Normal map).

    | Size | Hexes | Tiles | Cities | Towns | Sites | Hives at the start | AI groups (default) |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | Small | 48 × 32 | 1,536 | 2 | 7 | ~128 | ~4 (3 at the origin) | 3–6 (4) |
    | Normal | 64 × 42 | 2,688 | 2 | 13 | ~186 | 5–6 | 5–10 (7) |
    | Large | 80 × 52 | 4,160 | 3 | 20 | ~282 | ~9 (plus a second outbreak of 3) | 7–14 (10) |
    | Huge | 100 × 66 | 6,600 | 4 | 32 | ~419 | ~14 (5 at the origin, two more outbreaks of 3) | 10–18 (15) |

    Headless, a day takes about 15–20 ms on every size with the autopilot (the cost follows the hordes and groups, not the tiles); a Huge map is generated in about 120 ms. On a Huge map trips are longer (vehicles and outposts matter more), the groups raid you less (they sit farther out) but more hives are within reach of your walls, so more hordes come.

    *Custom size (experimental):* the size list's last entry, *Custom…*, takes any width × height from 32 × 24 to 200 × 140 hexes. Everything the presets set by hand (cities, military bases, extra hot spots and their hives, groups near the hub, how far the hub sits from the outbreak) is worked out from the area so it gives the presets' own numbers at their sizes; a custom 64 × 42 map is the Normal map. The screen warns that custom sizes are not balanced like the presets and that maps bigger than Huge may run slowly (it says how many times Huge it is). Measured headless: 200 × 140 takes 4–6 s to generate (each change of width or height builds it again) and about 25–35 ms a day with 50 groups. Saves remember a custom size (the save list says *Custom map*).
    *AI groups and starting hives* (any size): the AI group count is 0–50 on every size (the tip gives the range that suits the size; a new size still picks its default). Each group needs a site of its own 8 hexes from the others, so on a small map not all of them fit: the screen says how many found room. *Starting hives* is *Default* (what the map makes, shown in brackets) or 0–20: fewer are taken from the outbreak origin outward (the origin keeps its cluster, the lone hot spots go first), more are added one per hot spot in turn. With none, new hives still rise near the border. Hardcore always has the National Guard, and its outbreak still starts at one hive: the count is how many the first year grows to.
  - *Cities:* Calder City in the south (a cluster of about 50 city and suburb tiles) and a smaller second city far from it, packed with urban sites, several of a kind and named by district ("Calder City North Precinct", "Calder City Eastside Auto"): police stations, auto shops, pharmacies, supermarkets, gun stores, hardware stores, gas stations, apartment blocks, offices, schools, fire stations, warehouses, a hospital and a mall each, a university, a clinic, churches. Rich, but danger is high (more of the dead).
  - *Small towns:* 13, spread out. Each has a police station and an auto shop next to its center, 0–2 other shops (grocery, pharmacy, clinic, diner, hardware, gun store, gas, church, school, motel, feed store), and 1–2 satellites a few tiles out (gas station, diner, motel, church, feed store, clinic, farm). Moderate danger, a little poorer than the same shop in a city.
  - *Countryside:* farms, barns, ranches, country churches, cabins, hunting lodges, campgrounds, ranger stations, radio towers, quarries, crash sites, plus truck stops and gas stations on the highways. Quiet and poorer; many sit off the road.
  - *Landmarks:* at least one National Guard base (sometimes an Army depot too) off-road in the hills or woods, away from settlements and the hub; a state prison, a dam, a regional airport near Calder City, factories near towns.
  - Highways join every city and town (a spanning tree plus shortcuts where the roads made a long detour); city and town sites have streets to them. The hub starts in a small town away from the cities, the outbreak and the military bases; AI groups start outside the cities, spread apart (at least 8 tiles), mostly within 30 tiles of the hub (23 on a Small map, 37 on a Large one, 47 on a Huge one).
  - On the map, each site is a pixel sprite of its type on a plate in its zone's color (fading in steps by the loot left as you last saw it: rich, some, little, picked clean; with a row of skulls under its name for its danger when zoomed in; where the name is not shown the skulls sit under the sprite); zoomed out, small sites are dots and only cities, towns, landmarks, group bases and your own bases keep their names (overlapping names give way to the more important one); you can zoom out until the whole state fits. The tile panel and tooltip show the site's zone, danger and likely loot, and the loot left as you know it: *now* in view, else what you last saw (or heard on the radio) with its age, and a warning once it is 3 days old (other groups strip sites too).
- **Customizable start (new-run screen):** in Hardcore, *Starting people* 1–4 (above, under Hardcore mode; greyed out for a normal start); per colonist: rename or random name, move skill points (fixed point total), edit or randomize the look, randomize name / skills / look / background / traits / gear / all (gear: Normal trades the weapon and vest with a crewmate, Hardcore draws what they grabbed again, Customize a kit on their points; all: everything for that person and nobody else's, a normal start's weapon and vest kept; in Hardcore each one's grab and the car are on dice of their own, so one person's dice leave the others' grabs as they were); "Randomize all" for the whole crew (names, skills, traits, gear and looks; in Hardcore new backgrounds, grabs and car on the same map); pick the leader; choose the world size (Small 48 × 32, Normal 64 × 42, Large 80 × 52, Huge 100 × 66) or a custom width × height (experimental), the AI groups (0–50 on any size; 3–6, 5–10, 7–14 and 10–18 suit the presets, and a new size picks its default, 4, 7, 10 or 15) and the starting hives (Default or 0–20). Edited colonists are kept when the seed, size or group count changes, with their traits and unspent skill points ("New map" keeps them on a new seed; "New map and survivors" rerolls them too). No two colonists or recruits share a first name while a free one is left, and renaming someone to a full name already in the group is refused. When the run begins the starting jobs are handed out by skill: the two best farmers farm, the best scavenger scavenges, the best shot guards, the best mechanic builds and the sixth farms; the ammo bench starts without an operator. Next to a skill the screen shows what it counts as in play when a trait changes it ("5 →3" for Stealth with Loud). The seed is a whole positive number. The game starts paused: look around, give the first orders, then press Space.
- **Sprites:** procedural pixel sprites drawn in code (`src/ui/sprites.js`, no image files). Survivors are built from their `appearance` (build, skin, hair style and color, facial hair, top style and color, pants, accessory) plus worn armor and weapon; edit it from the survivor screen ("Customize look"). Zombies come as walkers, runners, bloated and brutes (by strength: weak dead are walkers, badly infected ground brings runners, bloated ones and brutes); enemy humans wear raider / soldier / zealot kits in their group's color. On the State Map everything is a sprite too: the places (bases, sites, camps, markers) flat pixel sprites (`src/ui/mapsprites.js`), the moving pieces low-poly figures (`src/ui/mapfigures.js`, built from the battle figures' limb in the same projection and light, a person 14 px tall) with a heading (the yaw of the way they travel, every 15°, no mirroring: a team walking up the screen shows its backs) and a cycle frame (people walking, a limp, a heavy load, wheels turning, oxen trotting, oars pulling, the dead shuffling) picked by `render.js` while they are on their way (docs/design-doc/sprites/map-figures-3d.md; `tools/mapfigures.html` and `tools/mapshot.cjs` for the sheets, `node tools/mapfigurestest.js` to check them). Your teams: a pickup or sedan (the actual vehicle) for motorized parties, walkers with packs on foot (armed for raids, scouts with binoculars on recon, a relief squad behind a banner), a rowboat or a raft with the vehicle (and a foam wake) while crossing water; trade-route convoys haul crates (lime) and supply runs haul drums (teal), by truck or pulling a handcart; on a ring in the team's status color, facing the way they travel; a faded sprite with a "?" badge marks an estimated position. Status badges are pixel icons too (waiting "!", wrench for work, radio contact, crossed swords for a siege). Other groups' parties look like their group: raiders on bikes and in a spiked pickup, a military humvee and helmeted squads, robed zealots with a banner and torches, an expansionist armored truck and shield troopers, a trader caravan (covered wagon, pack mule), a commune tractor hauling hay and pitchfork militia, fortress spear-and-shield militia; raids stand on a red-tinted ring and a siege pitches a camp (tents, barricade, fire, banner) beside the base. Sites: one sprite per location type (about 40, from the city skyline and the town water tower to the police station's light bar, the pharmacy's green cross, the gun store's rifle sign, the red barn, the radio mast and the smoking crash site), on a plate in the zone's color, faded when picked over, with danger skulls up close.
