Hordes are a crowd of shamblers (2–7 figures by size, in the zombie palette, facing where they drift) with their size on a badge, stale ones as faded ghosts with "N?"; hives are a festering mound over a dark pool with glowing pods, bigger and redder on badly infected ground, pulsing while in view, their land tinted dark red and the ring around them brighter with a red skull on every tile; a hive being built is a raw mound of the dead with bones sticking up and a red bar for the dead gone into it (of 1,000); a Wallbreaker is a grey brute with a slab of concrete for a fist at the head of its horde (`src/ui/hivesprites.js`, which also paints the two bosses for the battle map: the Wallbreaker and the bloated, bone-crowned Broodmother with her egg sacs). Markers: a grave (with the gear still lying there), your abandoned truck or sedan (dusty, a red jerry can when it needs fuel, smoke when badly damaged), a rusted wreck (up on blocks once stripped), a cache of crates and a duffel (opened once emptied), an abandoned camp (collapsed once picked through). Bridges are railed decks along the road. The **Zombies** button (top bar) sets palette, gore, variants, eyes and clothes, saved in the browser.
- **Map animations** (`src/ui/render.js` anim, Settings > Display > Map animations, on by default): the State Map's sprites come alive by motion and small overlays drawn each frame (the sprites themselves stay single cached canvases). Teams and parties on the move bob in step (yours, other groups', outsiders, hardcore bands), a cart a little slower; a vehicle on the move rumbles and raises dust; the crossing boat rocks; live hordes shamble (a sway and a heavy bob, more while they move; last-seen ghosts stand still), with the Wallbreaker at their head; infested herds graze; campfires flicker with sparks and smoke (a team's camp, the staging camp, a shelter's fire pit) and a siege camp smokes; a base with people in it smokes from the hearth (a shelter from its stovepipe); a crash's smoke column keeps rising; a hive build site under way glows; a hive's growths swell; the waiting badge bounces and the radio badge sends out a ring. While anything animated is on screen the map draws about 15 frames a second (otherwise only when something changes, 4 a second, as before; a live hive or a siege still pulses at 40); off, everything stands still. The timelapse never animates.
- **Night on the State Map** (`src/ui/nightfx.js`, Settings → Display → *Night darkness*: Off / Light / Dark, Dark by default): the map darkens with the clock, dusk fading in from 19:00 to 21:00 and dawn out from 05:00 to 07:00 (the sim's night, 20:00 to 06:00, at the middle of each), to a deep blue-black at full night (62% darker at Dark, 38% at Light). Lights cut through it with a warm glow: your bases with somebody home (bigger for the hub and more buildings), floodlights that can be lit (fuel or generator power: wider and cold white), other groups' bases you know of, a festival's bonfires, your teams' lights and camp fires, the staging camp's fire, siege camps' fires and burning crash sites. The shade goes over the ground and the cached sites only: bases, teams, hordes, names, icons and the selection stay at full brightness. One small offscreen canvas (at most 960 px wide) is painted again only when the shade moves by a step, the camera moves or a light comes or goes, and copied over the map with one drawImage a drawn frame; the hour is not in the map's cache key. The timelapse and the minimap skip it. **Screen-edge warnings** (`src/ui/edgefx.js`, `src/ui/edgepulse.js`, `src/ui/attackwatch.js`; Settings → Display → *Screen-edge warnings*, on): when something of yours is attacked off screen a red glow pulses on the map's edge toward it with a small badge ("◀ ⚔ Wexford Holdout under attack"): a base besieged by a raid or by raiders from outside or a battle at it, a team in a fight (its battle open or waiting) or ambushed (a confrontation card), a raid the lookouts saw coming or a horde coming for a base (warned, on the watchtower's watch, or in sight within 3 tiles); in the battle's isometric view, one of your people shot at or hit out of view ("◀ 🩸 Nadia hit", held 3.5 s after the last shot). Click the badge to look there (the map centres on the tile and selects it; the battle camera glides to the unit). One pulse per place, the strong beat for its first 6 seconds, fading when it is over, not again for 20 seconds, at most 4 at once; read from what the game already keeps (no state, no dice). *Heartbeat while a base is attacked* (off by default) plays a soft heartbeat on the ambience bus (the Ambience and master volumes and the mutes apply) while a base of yours is under attack and the map is up. `node tools/mapfxtest.js`.
- **Sound** (`src/ui/audio.js`, cues in `src/ui/soundcues.js`; ⚙ Menu → Options → Sound; Help → *Sound*): every sound is synthesized on the spot in WebAudio (oscillators, filtered noise, envelopes; no audio files). *Effects:* a soft click on buttons; a card chime by urgency (routine: two light rising notes; warning: a falling minor third; crisis: a low tense sawtooth chord with a thud; a raid on a base is the alarm instead); a bell for a new warning or danger in the alerts (an alert is heard once: a new count is not a new alert, one that went for under 12 hours and came back is not heard again, one turning from warning to danger is); in battle a report per gun class (pistol, revolver, rifle with an echo, pump shotgun with its pump, assault-rifle burst of three, suppressed, bow, acid spit), a thud for a hit and a whiz or ricochet for a miss, melee thumps (a crunch on a critical), a whoosh for a melee miss, bites and groans of the dead (more often the more of them are up), screams, a pipe bomb's or grenade's blast, a Molotov's glass and whoosh and crackle, smoke's hiss, a flare; a body falling and a dropping tone when one of yours goes down, a low minor chord when one dies; two drum beats when a battle opens. *The map:* a low grinding sting (a thud and a minor-second brass swell) when a horde you can see is newly within 6 tiles of a base or 3 of a team (once per horde); a hammered alarm bell for a raid warning, a break-in alert or a battle at your own base. *Ambience* (`src/ui/ambience.js` for audio.js; `ambscene.js` decides, `ambweather.js` and `ambwild.js` play, `ambkit.js` is the engine) follows the weather, season, clock and land where the map is looking (the battle's tile and room in a battle; the terrain blended over two rings of tiles; the weather read through `weatherAt` there and two tiles out, so a front's edge thins out): *weather*: wind in three layers (a low rumble, a whoosh, a whistle) with irregular gusts that set off loose metal, hinges and shutters in towns and leaves and branches in forest; rain from drizzle to downpour (a wash plus drop clusters by surface: leaves, tin roofs and gutters in towns and at your bases, puddles) and dripping for a few minutes after; thunder near (a rip and a boom) or rolling far, sized and delayed after its strike, and distant thunder from a storm front coming; the snow's hush, with the world muffled under snow cover; fog still, close and dull; rivers running, lakes lapping, frozen rivers groaning and singing; the night drone. *Wildlife*: songbirds (four kinds of FM song, a dawn chorus about 3.5× at sunrise), crows and ravens (more at fresh death sites and over a battlefield once the shooting stops), owls at night in forest, geese over in the spring and autumn migrations and ducks on the water, a woodpecker, choruses of crickets on warm nights, frogs by water on spring and summer nights, cicadas on hot afternoons, feral dogs in the ruins, rare wolves and coyotes at night in the hills, an elk bugling in the autumn rut, rustling in the brush, flies near a hive. Winter: few birds, no insects. *Danger silences it:* a horde you can see within 3 tiles (4 for a big one) hushes everything in a few seconds (the birds flush once) and it comes back slowly; a battle; gunfire (silent for 8 s, back over a minute); near a known hive it is sparse, pitched down and cut short. Indoors (a building raid) the weather comes through the walls low-passed, the rain on the roof, the wildlife gone; zoomed far out (zoom under 0.8, all of it under 0.45) only the beds remain. Calls are baked once per variant (FM synthesis, `ambkit.js` fmNote) and played as buffers (3–4 nodes each, at most 4 wildlife calls and 4 weather one-shots at once), on Poisson timing from a local generator; beds are built when first heard and let go after 10 s of silence (at most about 21 nodes in a storm). Every sound is named in `WEATHER_SOUNDS` and `WILDLIFE_SOUNDS` (World → Weather and World → Wildlife in the mixer). The title screen has the wind and the drone, and thunder after the storm scene's lightning. `node tools/ambiencetest.js`; WAV previews old and new: `tools/ambiencepreview/main.cjs`. *Options:* sound on / off, Master / Effects / Ambience sliders (squared, so the low half is usable; defaults 70 / 80 / 50%), *Mute when the window is not focused* (on by default), *Hear* any one sound to set the volumes; kept with the other options. Browsers start sound only after the first click or key press; muted or in the background the audio is suspended (no CPU). A volley is cut to 5 sounds a frame (the loudest kinds first, your own shots first) spread 70 ms apart, each sound has its own gap and voice limit (`LIMITS`), and no more than 28 play at once. On autopilot the cards are answered at once and do not chime.
- **Bases (Manage):** the hub and outposts share the same rules: roles (farm, scavenge, operate, build, guard, rest, medic, craft, teach, read), Heat (people, plus 3 per workshop station with an operator at work), defense, beds, healing, morale and desertion. Farmers tend the crop plots (see *Farming, water and food*). Under a week of food left, everyone goes on short rations (60%, costs mood, not health). A base that runs out of food starves: everyone there loses health every day it lasts (6, then 8, 10 … up to 20 a day; armor and Tough do not help) and nobody heals until there is food again, so about a week without food kills. Stock never goes below zero. Trade-route escorts waiting between trips live at the hub: they eat, heal and can be treated there, but do no base work. If the leader dies you pick a new one from the six best free people by Leadership. The run ends when nobody is left alive: deaths, desertion and a captive changing sides all count. Tabs for overview (with a food forecast line: days of food left and the next harvest, and water per day, stock and days left; the Bases list shows the same), farming (see below), workshops (base scavenging, stations with operators and running status), buildings and their bonuses, a construction queue with builders and timers, an armory with everyone's equipment (skill-gap rule shown), and vehicles.
- **Bare start** (`src/sim/world.js` HUB_START and createWorld, `src/sim/groupworks.js`; `node tools/barestarttest.js`): no base starts with buildings, and every construction plot is free. The hub is the town hall and what comes with it (4 beds, 6 crop plots, the well, the cellar, 3 wall strength) and its stores: 80 food, 50 water, 50 scrap and 3 parts (was 25 scrap and 2 parts with two farm plots, a crafting bench, a bunkhouse and a wall section standing), enough for a bunkhouse, a crafting bench, a farm plot and a rain catcher. What goes up first is the player's choice; two of the six start on Build. Six people in four beds are crowded from the first hour (−6 mood a day): the new-run screen and the first-week guide say so, and the Overcrowded card waits its usual 5 days. The crafting bench is the first bench (12 scrap and a part, 12 work-hours: half a day for the two builders, about a quarter of the starting scrap; the workbench and the master workbench unchanged). Hardcore already started bare (staging.js). The governor (`autopilot.js buildWishes`) builds by need: water when it runs short, beds while people sleep crowded, walls and defences when raiders or a horde threaten, farm plots when the farmers run out of plots, a bench when someone crafts or the ammo runs low, storage when the food fills it. **The AI groups** build too (`groupworks.js`: abstract works per base, `f.works[tile]`, one job at a time, paid in scrap or, short of it, salvaged at twice the work): water first, fields while food is short, quarters while people sleep rough, walls when threatened (the dead within 6 tiles, a hive within 10, raided in the last 30 days, an aggressive group within 14), the rest of the fields, a workshop, a storehouse when their stores fill, then walls up to their goal (7 for a fortress or the military, 4 at a main base, 1 elsewhere: the old fixed walls). What it does, kept small: the walls an assault faces and a captured base's walls and bunkhouse are what they built; their base's flat defence against raids and hordes is 8 × (0.5 + 0.5 × walls / goal) (a flat 8 before); without wells and fields their food from the land is 6% lower, sleeping rough cuts their growth by 30%, no workshop makes 85% of their ammo and scrap, no storehouse keeps 85% of the usual caps. The Groups tab lists what a group has built. Old saves and the Hardcore military (an army base from before the fall) count as built up.
- **Construction plots and water barrels** (`src/sim/buildings.js` PLOTS, WATER_BARREL, demolish; `economy.js` waterCapacity / rawRoom / waterLayout; `storage.js` crates; `node tools/plotstest.js`): every building stands on a plot, and the plots grow with the base (building / utility): a shelter (the camp stage) 0 / 2, an outpost 6 / 6, the main hub 16 / 14. Building plots take anything with a roof (bunkhouses and barracks, the storehouse, cellars, the cold store, cisterns, workshops like the machine shop and preserving kitchen, the radio station, garage, cells, greenhouse, common room, chapel, hunting cabin, the hospital: one plot whatever its tier); utility plots the small things in the yard (the water boiler, water barrels, storage crates, the watchtower, the cook fire, rain catchers, drying racks, wells, the ammo bench, med station, generator, still, the radio relay). Walls, defences and farm plots need none. A queued building holds its plot. **Beds** (`BEDS`, `baseBeds`): the hub 4, an outpost 2, a shelter 4, a **bunkhouse** +6 (20 scrap, 30 h, up to 6) and a **barracks** +12 on one building plot (40 scrap + 2 parts, 54 h, up to 4), for a base whose plots run short; a standing bunkhouse can also be **expanded** into a barracks where it stands (On the building plots: *Expand*; 24 scrap + 2 parts, 30 h, no new plot, its 6 beds in use meanwhile; the job counts against the barracks' 4 and the bunkhouse cannot be pulled down while it runs). A broken bunkhouse or barracks sleeps nobody until it is repaired (`breakdowns.js`: raid damage; repaired for 8 scrap, 14 for a barracks). *Demolish* (two clicks) pulls a building down to free its plot and salvages half its scrap and parts. **Water barrels** (up to 4, utility plots): each is upgraded on its own (water barrel 25 → barrel rack 50 → water tank 100) and set to hold purified or un-purified water: un-purified barrels give the well, rain and river water more room; purified barrels keep room only safe water may use (the filters' surplus, bottled water brought home), never crowded out by the well. **Storage crates** (up to 4): +30 dry food room each. The Construction tab shows the plots, the queue, what stands on each kind of plot (a bunkhouse with *Expand*), the barrels (tier, what they hold, Upgrade, Demolish) and the build list split into Buildings, Utilities and Walls, defences and fields. Old saves with more buildings than plots keep them; nothing new goes up on that kind of plot until some come down.
- **Expeditions:** trip planner (crew, vehicle, bikes or on foot, stance, supplies with round-trip estimate). Packed supplies count against what the crew can carry (by weight, see *Weight and load* below): a launch over the limit is refused, as is a crew member who is dead, away or already on a team, or an outpost on water. Food, water and fuel drain hour by hour (water at each day's thirst: 1.3× in summer, at least 1.6× on a hot day, 0.9× in winter; the planner counts each hour at its own day's rate, so a trip into a heat wave packs more); running out hurts (armor and toughness do not help against hunger and thirst). A team keeps the name it left with. If the last member of a team dies or leaves, the team is lost: what it carried lies at a death site on that tile and its vehicle stands abandoned there, both marked on the map; a team that loses people drops what the rest cannot carry on the spot (also recoverable).
- **Order templates and Repeat trip** (`src/sim/trips.js`, `src/ui/trips.js`): the planner's *Templates* box saves the setup as a named template (*Save as template*; the same name again updates it): mission, stance, retreat policy (the box's *Retreat* list, set on the team when it leaves), the crew by id with each person's job and best skills, the vehicle, supplies, tablets and lights, the plan's steps and conditions and the Survey settings. Click a template in the box to apply it to the trip you are planning; the Teams tab lists them (rename, delete with two clicks, *Plan →* the target it was saved at, *Pick target…* for the next trip you plan). Applying one keeps everyone fit and puts in a stand-in for anyone dead, away, hurt (health under 60), in a hospital bed or too ill: the best fit person at the hub with the same job, else the same best skills; a missing vehicle becomes one of its kind (else on foot); *Move to a site* steps planned from another target are dropped; supplies are packed as saved, as far as the hub has them (the shortfall is flagged), and the planner's usual warnings check them against the new trip. Every change is listed in the box. A team sent from the planner remembers its setup: when it gets home an alert (🔁, for a day) and the Teams tab's *Recent trips* (the last 6) offer *Repeat trip*: the planner on the same target with the same mission, plan, supplies and people (stand-ins as above), one click on *Send team* to confirm. Saved with the campaign.
- **Undo in the planners** (`src/ui/undo.js`): ↶ Undo (or Ctrl+Z on that screen) takes back the last change, one step at a time, up to 20: in the trip planner (crew, transport, stance, mission, supplies, tablets, lights, retreat policy, plan steps and conditions, a template applied), a base's build queue (queued, cancelled and moved jobs: ▲ ▼ on each job reorder the queue, and the first job is the one being built) and the Work tab (priorities, roles, schedules, minimums, crews' settings, who is in which role). Each step keeps a copy of just what that screen edits. What the sim has acted on stays: a team sent is gone from the planner, and a build step is refused once its job finished, the builders started on a job it would take back, or the materials it would hand back have been used. Never in a battle; the steps are not saved.
- **Pause rules and the card tray** (`src/sim/cardrules.js` kinds, defaults and staleness, `src/ui/pacing.js` the tray, the rules and the speeds; Options → *Pause for…*): you choose which moments stop the clock. Each card gets a kind when it arrives: ⚔ combat and engagements, 🛡 a raid or horde at a base, ✝ a death, 🤝 first contact with a group, ☠ a hostile act or war, 🧟 a horde sighted near a base, 🐎 trade visitors, ❗ team orders needed, 🃏 other cards (road and hub events, people asking to join, perk points, morale, illness, crashes, job offers, reports). All are on at first except trade visitors and other cards. A card of a kind that is on stops the clock as before; the others go into the **card tray**, a strip at the top of the map (newest first; each chip shows its icon, title and the in-game hours it has left, its tip the text and the answer it will take). The clock keeps running; click a chip to answer it (the clock waits while it is open; Esc or *Back to the tray* puts it back). A team whose card waits holds where it is (it still eats). Left `TRAY_HOURS` = 8 in-game hours (2 min 40 s at 1×, 32 s at 5×), a card takes its default answer by itself with a log and diary line ("Left unanswered for 8 hours, "Storm" (near …) took its default: Shelter and wait (6 hours)."): a choice marked `default: true`, else the first "do nothing" answer (OK, Hold here, Leave it, Not now, Decide later, Turn them away…), else one that steps back from trouble (Shelter, Go the long way, Hide, Return home, Pay…), else the first that is not a gamble (no fight, no odds, no risky word); never a greyed-out choice, one that needs the map or a fight. A card with nothing like that left always stops the clock, as do a card over a battle and one whose answers all need the map; a battle, a search, the trade window and a city crawl always stop it too. A card about a team, person, group, visitor, base or job that is gone leaves the tray with a log line; a choice that throws is dropped with a console line. *✓ all* answers the whole tray with its defaults, ⚙ opens the rules. A death, a first contact, a group turning hostile and a new horde or break-in alert near a base stop the clock even with no card (each by its switch; the paused banner says why, and Space then runs at 1×). The "N cards waiting" alert counts only the cards that stop the clock. On autopilot nothing changes (the governor answers every card at once), and the headless tools never use any of it.
- **Travel by the minute** (`src/sim/parties.js` clockFrac, wayOn, stepToNow, moveEstimate, hourCourse, arrivalsDue, plannedPos; `src/sim/orders.js` tasksDue, queueEstimate): the sim moves teams by whole hours, but the clock shows minutes, and what you are told and shown follows them. A move given part-way through an hour starts that far behind (the tick credits the whole hour), so ordering at :55 no longer buys a free hour on the road; a timed job (rest, wait, look around, hunting, setting up camp…) given then lasts its hours from that minute and ends at its minute (`task.owed`); a team given a new way while on the move first travels the minutes of the hour already gone, for real, and turns from the point the map draws it at: on along the step it is on, on to the next tile, or back to the one it came from, whichever gets it there sooner (drawn walking back; no snap back); a team that gets where it is going part-way through an hour arrives at that minute (the main loop steps its hour early, the same course the tick would take, and the tick then leaves its move alone), so the glide stops on the centre of the tile exactly as the move ends; the ETA in the right-click menu, the planner, the launch log line and the team panel's time left are worked out the same way, to the minute ("2h 05m"). A wait on the way (a road blockage, a repair, a road card's delay) is sat out on the spot with a pill over the team saying what and how long ("Clearing road 25m"); a pace changed mid-hour eases the glide onto the new course. Convoys arrive at their minute the same way. The panels are redrawn each new minute on the clock (`src/main.js` step; a panel whose HTML comes out the same is not touched), so the times left, the ETAs and the running orders' bars (`orderStatus` pct counts the minutes of the hour already gone) count down with the clock at any speed, not only as the hour ticks or at the next click. Out of contact, the map's estimate of where the team should be (plannedPos) glides by the minute, sets off at the minute the way was given and gets there at the team's minute; the panels say when, to the minute. Queued orders are estimated from the minute each one really starts (the one before it ends at its minute), so the menu's "after Xh" and the queue's hours add up to the clock. The headless tools never call arrivalsDue or set the clock's minutes, so their teams arrive and their jobs end as the hour ticks, as before.
- **Speeds** (`src/ui/pacing.js` SPEEDS and SLOWEST_SPEED, keys 0 and 1–5 and the top bar): 0.25× (an hour in 80 s), 0.5×, 1×, 2×, 3× and 5× for quiet stretches (an hour in 4 s, a day in about 1 min 36 s). At 3× and 5× the clock drops back to 1× by itself when a card arrives (pausing or tray) or a new red alert comes up, with a line on the map saying why (*Drop to 1× when something happens*, on by default). The loop still runs at most 12 hours a frame and drops a backlog, and the panels redraw once an hour; on a Huge map an hour takes about 1 ms on average to simulate (6 ms at worst in `tools/traytest.js` after a day; up to about 0.4 s with the machine busy), so 5× (one hour every 4 s) holds the frame rate; any hitch is the same as at 1×, only every 4 s instead of every 20 s.
- **Team orders:** when a team finishes a job it waits for orders: act on that tile, move to any tile (click or right-click), return home, or hold. Teams in radio range can be redirected while moving. Giving an order never changes the speed: an order given while the game is paused is armed and starts when you unpause (the pause banner counts the waiting orders, and the *Running orders* list and the team panel show "starts when unpaused"). When the order is done the team reports in with a "Done" card offering the next steps (an order that could not be done, or was stopped, reports in with a plain orders card saying why; a team cut off by water gets "Cannot go home").
- **Trip plans (`src/sim/plans.js`, planner section in `src/ui/plan.js`):** the planner's PLAN section builds the team's order queue before it leaves: the trip to the target with its mission, then steps (look around, scavenge here, scavenge the best sites nearby (up to N within R tiles, richest by what is known, picked when the step starts so the plan adapts), camp N hours, gather, fetch water (2 h on a tile next to a river or lake: every free container is filled, 1 kg a unit, and carried home; also a team panel order there), wait N hours, move to a known site, return home), reordered or removed freely (nothing after Return home). Presets: *Recon and return*, *Scavenge and return*, *Survey trip* (survey the area for the hours set, then home), *Explore* (recon, then the best 2 sites within 3 tiles, then home), *Camp N days, then return*, *Custom*. Missions that come back start with Return home after them. The planner shows the whole plan's time (every leg stepped hour by hour like the trip; searches count 6 hours, Look around 2, a recon's 2 hours are paid by the next move) and packs and checks food, water and fuel for it (plus the way home if the plan ends away), and a Heat look-ahead for camp and wait steps. *If … then come home*: anyone hurt below N health, food below N days, cargo full; checked every hour, the team turns home by itself and a "Coming home" card says why (continue home, resume the plan, or stop). While a plan runs the team raises a card, once each per trip, on a horde (15+) in sight, a rich site in sight (loot 55%+) that is not on the plan and not known to be rich (with *Search it next*), and food short of the way home; each offers continue, change the plan (team panel) or come home. The Teams tab and team panel show the plan, its steps left and its rule.
- **Radio rule:** a team in radio coverage (stations and linked relays) takes new orders at any time. Out of contact it follows its plan (the team panel and *Running orders* say "following its plan (no radio)") and you cannot give it orders, except through the cards it raises: while its orders or report card is open, and for the rest of the hour you answer one (its panel opens with orders then). A team holding out of contact checks in again with an orders card every 12 hours, an open-ended camp once a day. Your own base tiles count as contact.
- **Heat in the field (`src/sim/fieldheat.js`):** a team that stays on one tile (waiting, holding, camping, gathering, building a boat or the staging camp, looking around, crafting, repairing, treating) builds Heat from noise, smoke and smell, per hour: activity (waiting, looking around, treating 1; gathering, crafting, repairs 1.5; boat building 1.8; a camp, and anyone at the staging camp, by the camp danger model instead: its fire, people, vehicles, shooting lately and work, settling rather than climbing; see the first home above) × place (countryside 1, suburbs 1.4, city 1.8, × 1 + 0.6 × infection) × hive land 1.4 (2 on the ring next to a hive) × team size (0.7 + 0.1 a person) × night (a camp fire 1.3, otherwise 0.8) × stealthy 0.8, capped at 100. It falls 8 an hour on the move and is 0 within a tile of your bases. Each hour on the spot the chance of trouble is 12% × (Heat / 100)^1.5 × danger, where danger = 0.15 + 3 × the road's conflict chance there (groups, AI parties, infection, hordes, traffic) + 0.3 city / 0.15 suburbs + 0.3 hive land / 0.8 next to a hive + 0.2 a horde within 3 tiles (cap 2). Trouble is a road conflict card (the dead, a horde close by drawn in as a bigger surge, or people from the groups around; camp lookouts spot it a little more often) and halves the Heat; after it the team goes back to its camp or work. At most one conflict per 20 hours, as on the road. A quiet countryside camp for a night reaches Heat ~25 with about 1% risk; a night in an infected city is about a coin flip and three days there bring trouble every day or so. The team panel shows a Heat bar with the hourly chance and the reasons, the map a small bar under the team's sprite (hover the tile for the text). The autopilot gives up waiting at a site for another team's search past Heat 60.
- **Order queue and team panel:** click a team (map, Teams tab, or the *Running orders* list in the map's top-left corner, which shows each team's current order and time left) to open its panel: people (health, infection, skills, gear), packed supplies and found cargo against carry space, the vehicle's condition and fuel, and the queue. Actions: move, return home, scavenge, look around (2h), camp (8h / 24h / until broken; the hurt heal slowly, infected wounds need meds), roadside repair (1 part, 3h, better with Mechanics), treat a member with carried meds, field crafting, and boat crossings. A waiting team starts an action now; a team on the move (or with queue mode on) adds it to its queue, which runs by itself (e.g. move, then scavenge). A team stopped on the way with orders queued (after a road fight, say) puts the unfinished trip back at the front of the queue, so *Resume queued orders* carries on to the right tile; a queued job on the spot (a mission, look around, gathering) remembers the tile it was planned for and is dropped with a message if the team ends up elsewhere. A camp with no food or no water heals nobody. Arrival estimates (planner, panels, queue) step through the same hour-by-hour movement as the simulation.
- **Boat crossings:** water blocks travel and routes never cross it, but a team on a shore can build a one-use boat and cross one water tile to the land straight across (team panel, the orders card, or right-click the far tile). Cost: scrap = 4 + 2 per person + 1 per 40 kg of supplies and cargo (+14 scrap and 2 parts to raft a pickup, +9 and 1 for a sedan, +1 for bikes, or leave the vehicle on the shore, marked for recovery); build hours = 2 + 0.5 per person + 1 per 80 kg (+4 / +3 for a raft), cut 5% per Mechanics point (up to 50%), then 1h on the water (2h with a raft) with a small mishap risk (a crate lost, someone bruised, the raft vehicle damaged). Hours and materials are fixed when the order is given, so queue estimates hold. *Gather materials* (3h) turns up scrap, only on a shore with a crossing; a shore holds about 10 scrap and gets 1 back every 3 days. A boat order given before the team's vehicle changed (gained or lost one) is planned again when it starts. Delays on cards and the 2 hours of a recon are real hours at any pace.
- **Bridges, barricades and the cars on the road** (`src/sim/combat.js` layRoad / AREAS.roadcars, `src/sim/locks.js` "car", `src/sim/parties.js` bridgeClosedTo, `tools/bridgetest.js`): a road laid over a water tile is a bridge (`mapgen.js`, drawn as a railed deck on the State Map). On its battle and raid maps the river runs across the map, 12–24 cells wide (about half the map: 20 on the Large map a bridge raid or fight always gets), and the road crosses it on a concrete deck between railings (half cover you see over): the only way from one bank to the other, so fights funnel onto it; the ways in and out of a bridge raid are the two ends. Every road map's road is 8 cells wide (6 on a small field map) with the cars people fled in scattered along it in its lanes. **Cars are 2 × 3 cells (a sedan) or 2 × 4 (a van, a pickup, a truck)**, one object drawn across its cells in both views, with a free ring of cells round each (never against another car or a wall, at most against the bridge's railing), and the road is checked to still run end to end past them (cars come back out if not); cars parked around sites and in the suburbs are the same size (fewer of them). On a raid the road's cars are containers (search from next to any of their cells; the marker sits over the middle) (*Cars on the road*: fuel, a bag of food and bottles, now and then a pistol or binoculars); a bare road or a bridge is searched for them alone, a roadside site gets them outside it too. Some are **locked cars** (40% on the road, 20% in a wreck pile): no site key fits; pick the door (lockpicks), pry it with a crowbar (Heat +6, wakes the dead within 8) or **break the glass** without one (both actions, 90%: Heat +10 and the crash wakes every unaware dead and guard within 11 tiles). Locked cars hold more (×1.5, extras: fuel, food, water, rounds, a flashlight or a pistol). **Barricading a bridge:** an outpost may be founded on a bridge tile (*Barricade the bridge*; still not on open water) and is named "… Bridge Barricade". A base on a bridge, yours or a group's, closes the road over the river to everyone but its owner and their allies (your coalition): routes never pass through it, a team already on its way stops at the foot of the bridge and asks for orders, and groups' raids, convoys and caravans must go round. The barricade itself can still be a destination (raid it, storm it with *Assault their base*, trade at it), hordes walk up to it and attack it like any base, and the river can always be crossed by boat elsewhere. Groups barricade bridges too when they expand (RAIDS.BRIDGE_PICK, more for aggressive groups and for bridges near your bases); the log says so. **A barricade's battle map** (`deploy.js`, `conquest.js`): the wall stands across the deck only, the river is the rest of the line, and two staggered rows of **tank traps** stand on the deck in front of it (DEPLOY.TRAP_HP 25 / CONQUEST.TRAP_HP 30): people walk between them, no vehicle drives through, and they are battered down from next to them (click one next to your unit; Melee at a cell). **Hordes** are stopped by a barricaded bridge: their way never runs through one, and a horde whose way needs that bridge turns on the barricade (role attack) and attacks it when it arrives like any base. **Boats** cross plain water tiles only, never a bridged one.
- **The team's vehicle on the battle map** (`src/sim/combat.js` placeVehicle / driveTo, `raidloot.js` atVehicle, `siege.js` loadRam / unloadRam): the car or truck you bring is a 2 × 3 or 2 × 4 object in the entry zone of a raid (a bike stays one cell) and by the west edge of a field or assault battle. **Drive** (both actions, from next to it): click where it goes, up to 8 cells over open ground with nobody in the way (tank traps, railings, other cars and walls stop it); loud (Heat +5 in a raid, the unaware within 10 tiles hear the engine). The driver steps off beside it. The loot window and *Stash* work next to it wherever it stands, and extracting from a zone it stands at keeps it (drive it back; the raid header says where it is). On an assault, the **battering ram rides in the vehicle** instead of slowing three people: *Unload ram* next to it puts it on the ground for a crew to take up, *Load ram* puts a carried or dropped one back in. **Passengers** (*Get in* / *Get out*, 1 action, SEATS by model: sedan 4, SUV 5, Hummer 4, van 7, pickup 5): aboard you are off the map (not a target, not drawn, no shooting), ride along when it is driven, and *Extract* from inside takes everyone aboard out with it from a zone it stands at; everyone is put out on the ground when a battle ends. *Turn* (1 action, from inside or next to it) swings it a quarter at the same corner when the cells are clear. **Ramming** (RAMMING): driving through people hits anyone in the way, friend or foe (14–28, the dead 20–40) and shoves them aside (crushed for more if there is no room); light obstacles in the way are broken (fences, wire, sandbags, crates, hay, barrels, barricades, rocks, pumps, wells, spike pits, bear traps, tank traps) at a cost to the vehicle's condition (1 a person, 2 a zombie, 2–12 an obstacle; tank traps 12), settled on the vehicle when the battle ends. Walls, trees, other cars, water, railings and trenches still stop it.
- **Evac** (`src/sim/combat.js` evacTurn, the *Evac* button in the battle panel): everyone still standing heads for the way out with the actions they have, the extraction zones of a raid or the evac strip of a battle, or a free seat in the team's vehicle, whichever is nearer; arriving, they extract, slip away or get in, and upstairs they make for the stairs first. The order stands: each of your turns starts with the next leg until everyone is out or you press *Stop evac*. The downed are not carried (*Sound the retreat* carries them). **In the vehicle counts as out** (leaveAboard): when nobody of yours is left on foot, everyone aboard drives off with it; a raid ends with them extracted and the vehicle kept wherever it stands, a battle ends in a retreat with them safe (a won battle puts them back on the field). *Extract* from inside works anywhere on a raid map. A **bike or motorcycle** is a 2 × 1 object with one seat; it rides round people and obstacles instead of ramming them.
- **Hordes from 1 to 4 sides** (`src/sim/fort.js` HORDE_SIDES, `interior.js` interiorUnits): a horde attacking a base comes at it from up to 1 side (under 6 of the dead), 2 (6+), 3 (12+) or 4 (18+), how many by its dice: the side it came from on the State Map first with half of it, then the sides next to it, then the far one. On a base without walls the extra sides are the north and south edges of the yard and its back (never onto the way out). People (raiders) still come from one side, a quarter of them round the next.
- **Their vehicles** (`src/sim/aiscav.js`, `conflicts.js` takeTheirVehicle): a group's scavenging party drives out (half the time) when the group has 6+ fuel in stock, using 2: a pickup, a sedan, a van or an SUV, faster on the roads. Meet it in a fight (on the road or at a contested site) and its vehicle stands on the battle map behind them (cover; `combat.js` setup.enemy.vehicle). Win, on the map or by the numbers, and it is left where you fought with a little fuel and the wear of the fight: an abandoned vehicle marked on the State Map for any team to recover (*Recover vehicle*).
- **Base maps by tier** (`src/sim/battlesize.js` BASE_TIER, `interior.js` yardDims): the yard map is square and grows with the base: a shelter 25 × 25, an outpost 40 × 40 growing to 60 × 60 over its first 10 structures, the main hub 64 × 64 growing to 100 × 100 as it fills. A yard over 5,000 cells uses a sparse line-of-sight cache (combat.js SparseLos) instead of a (W·H)² typed array; a save makes a fresh one on load.
- **The fort map** (`src/sim/fort.js`): a walled base on land fights its whole battle on one map, whether it is yours under attack (`defences.js` "baseDefence") or a group's you storm (`conquest.js` "baseAssault"). The map is the yard with the base's buildings (the same generator as before) inside a walkway ring (▲ height 1) inside the wall ring, with a two-cell gate in the middle of each of the four walls, watchtowers at the corners when the base has one, ladders up to the walkway every 7 cells, and a margin of 8 cells of open ground all round with a little cover on it: the base can be attacked from the north, south, east or west. **Staging:** the AI's attackers come from the side they came from on the State Map (a quarter of them round onto the sides next to it) and you deploy your defenders anywhere inside the walls (walkway, towers, gates, yard, buildings) before the fight; storming a group's base, you stage your people on any side of the margin (the deploy step with a green margin), their defenders hold the gates, the towers and the walkway all round. The battle runs through its tiers on this one map: the wall until enough attackers are inside the ring (or any are and nobody holds the wall), then the yard, then (your own base) the last stand in the buildings; there is no fall back to another map. Floodlights light a third of the attack side's margin. A group's base gets a stand-in base for its layout (an outpost or a hub by the strength of its walls, buildings for its numbers, deterministic for the tile). A bridge barricade keeps the old maps (the deck's wall line, then the yard), as does a base without walls (the yard map); a battle's context `strip: true` forces the old maps (the tests use it). The wall map for a bridge keeps 18 columns and takes the yard's rows.
- **Weight and load** (`src/sim/load.js`, panels in `src/ui/loadview.js`): one system, weight in kg. Every item has a weight (`data.js` GEAR `weight`, last on each line; a pack also weighs what is in it) and so does every supply (`SUPPLIES.weight`: food 0.5, water 1, fuel 1, meds 0.1, ammo 0.02, scrap and parts 2, preserved food 0.35, jars 0.4). A survivor's load = what they wear, hold and keep in pockets and pack + their share of the team's packed supplies and found cargo; the team shares that out so everyone is equally burdened (the same felt load, each up to their max), so whoever carries best takes more. Max load 40 kg (Bad Back 28, Strong Back 48), + the pack's `carry` (improvised backpack +6, assault pack +10), + 8 with Packrat; a team carries at most its max loads together (plus a vehicle). Load bands, the same for everyone, by felt load (× 0.88 with an assault pack: its `ease`; × 1.2 with a Bad Back, × 0.9 with a Strong Back): **Light** ≤ 18 kg (full pace, normal rations), **Medium** ≤ 28 (−10% pace, +15% food and water), **Heavy** ≤ 38 (−25%, +35%), **Overloaded** above (−45%, +60%). Scouting takes 2.5% a point off the slow-down and the extra rations (half at 20). The team moves at its slowest member's pace; the extra rations count only on the move (resting, camping or waiting: one a day). The team is weighed when it sets off, each hour it waits, and when its people or vehicle change; on the move only what makes it heavier counts at once (eating does not speed it up until it stops), so the ETA, rations and fuel the planner shows are what the trip uses. Cars take the whole load off their riders; bikes take part of it (below). The planner shows each person's load, band, pace and rations and warns when anyone is Heavy or Overloaded; the team panel and the inventory screen show each person's load and band, item tiles and tooltips their weight. New packs only need `slots`, `carry` and `ease`.
- **Vehicles:** own fuel tanks and condition; worn vehicles break down more. Cars: payload truck 650 kg, sedan 400 kg, riders (80 kg each) and what they wear included; fuel per tile (road 0.6, off-road 1.5) × 1 up to 60% full, rising to × 1.3 at 100%, and a car can be loaded to 125% (overloaded: up to × 1.6 fuel, 15% slower, 1.5× wear and breakdowns); the planner shows the load % and the fuel multiplier, and its fuel estimate is the fuel the drive burns. **Bicycles** (`type: "bike"`, a stack of `count` bikes per base): one each for a team, no fuel, roads at 0.75 a tile (walking 1.2, cars 0.5), walking cost off-road and over mountains; each rack carries 15 kg of the team's load and riding takes 55% of the rest's slow-down and extra rations away; they break down less (a flat, a chain) and are patched with scrap; a boat takes them along for 1 scrap; left behind they are picked up again without fuel. Found as cargo (`GEAR.bicycle`, 12 kg, bulky: towns, apartments, motels, schools, campgrounds, police stations, auto shops, hardware stores, fire and ranger stations) or built in a workshop (8 scrap, 2 parts); a bicycle brought home joins the base's bikes. Trade routes, supply runs and caravans use cars only. A vehicle left behind (out of fuel, broken down, no road home, on a shore, at a raid site) is marked on the map and can be driven again when its tank plus the fuel the team carries reach 2. Found cargo the people cannot carry on foot stays inside it (logged, noted on its marker) and is back on board when it is recovered; a team that cannot restart it, or has a vehicle already, can still take what it can carry. Wrecked cars can be restored into new vehicles (2 car parts, or 2 machine parts for each, and 6 fuel) or stripped for parts and fuel. **Spare car parts for the road** (`vehicles.js` CAR_SPARES, carSpares; the planner's *Car spares* line, on by default, and every governor or steward trip): a team driving a car 5+ tiles out packs up to 2 car parts from the stores (when there are any and the car has the room), so a breakdown takes *Use a spare part* (+10, an hour) instead of the on-the-spot repair (8 hours and −10 when the Mechanics check fails); unused spares come home with the team. Parked cars in a garage (the 2 best-kept) are out of the frost.
- **Army trucks, the abandoned convoy and found cars** (`src/sim/miltrucks.js`, `src/sim/foundcars.js`, maps in `combat.js` placeMilTrucks / AREAS.miltrucks, UI in `src/ui/carpanel.js`; test: `node tools/miltrucktest.js`): Army cargo trucks, Humvees and MRAPs stand on road tiles (about 1 road tile in 100, up to 45% next to a National Guard base), 3–5 in a base's motor pool, a Humvee at half the army helicopter wrecks, and 2–4 at an *abandoned Army convoy*, an event every 14–28 days (a card, a temporary site on a road that other groups race you for, stripped and burned out after 6–9 days). Each truck is big hard cover with a cargo bed to search (one loot table, `truckLoot`: rifles, ammunition by the crate, armour, grenades, rations, now and then night vision, a thermal sight or a radio; less in a wrecked or burned-out one, less in a base's motor pool), its turned crew of dead soldiers about it and more strapped in the cab who rise when the cargo is opened. Any car or truck on a battle map can be got into (locked: pick, pry or break the glass), its keys found or hotwired (Mechanics), inspected (engine, tyres, battery, fuel: most are dead, a few run, some can be fixed), siphoned and claimed; a claimed one joins the team or waits on the tile, a broken one is repaired in the field (parts, scrap, a Mechanics level, hours on the spot, a check that wastes half the parts when it fails), and a team with a vehicle can send someone home in the found one. A running Army cargo truck carries 2500 kg and seats 12, but is ~1.5× slower than a car (slower off-road), burns twice the fuel and its engine draws hordes. A convoy nobody has stripped yet is guarded (`CONVOY_GUARD`): 2–3 more dead soldiers round each unopened truck on the raid map, raid and search Heat +2 a truck from the start and +4 more when one is forced open, the hour-by-hour search's fights harder (+1.6 threat a guard), a fight with the dead there by the numbers counts them, and another group's salvage party loses people to every soldier about the trucks by the numbers (its type's arms and kit) before it strips it, and takes 70% of the unopened trucks' cargo home (`convoyHaul`: guns, armour and grenades into its arsenal, rounds and supplies into its stock, the rest on its shelf; the trucks keep 30% for whoever comes next); the first group there puts the guard down. `node tools/convoyvalue.js [seeds]` measures a convoy against a National Guard base (what the map holds, the dead, a bot raid, the hour-by-hour search, an AI party's losses).
- **Markers and map layers:** teams roll perception to spot caches, wrecked cars and abandoned camps, marked with how long ago they were seen. Toggle layers (locations, territory, teams, hordes, death sites, vehicles, finds, infection, weather, weather fronts, known area, coalition land) in the Layers box in the map corner: one line each with a color chip and a short hover description; its ? opens Help → Map layers (what each layer shows) and Map legend (every symbol on the map), and ▾ folds it away. **Known area** (off by default): teal hatching on the ground your people looked hard at (`state.searched`, `src/sim/discovery.js`: per tile the day it was last searched, a Uint16Array saved with the game; the spots a Survey covers hour by hour with the team's optics' reach, what a Look around takes in, a site a team searched), brighter with a faint wash for the last 30 days, a thin teal line round it; a team's looks reach the map with its report (radio contact or home), and the tile panel and tooltip say on which day a tile was searched. **Coalition land** (off by default): the land tiles the alliance victory counts (`alliance.js controlledLand`), each in the color of its nearest base's owner (you blue, each member its group color) with a border where another owner's land begins and a thin gold line round the coalition's land as a whole; the layer's line shows the share held against the 50% goal (other groups' land stays on the Territory layer). **Ruins:** a razed base (yours or a group's, `tile.ruin`) is drawn with the Locations as a burned-out compound (charred wall stubs, a lone chimney, rubble; a torn blue rag on yours) named "Ruins of …", on your map once you have seen it since it burned; the tile panel shows who burned it, the salvage left, the danger and the 5 scrap an outpost there needs, founding one replaces it, and the timelapse shows each day's ruins (`chronicle.js` frames). The overlays are drawn into the map's cached chunks and worked out again only when their data changes (a report of searched ground, a day passing, the coalition's bases or members), never per frame.
- **Tactical combat:** fights open an XCOM-style battle on a grid built from the tile (cover, two actions per unit, hit chances, flanking, overwatch, hunker, melee, meds, line of sight, retreat zone, ambush rules, enemy AI, zombies drawn by gunfire). Survivors and human enemies move 5 tiles an action; the dead move 3, the strong (power 5+, drawn as runners) 4 (review C-10's middle ground); the Wallbreaker and the Broodmother 2. *Critical hits* (`combat.js` `CRIT_MULT` = 2; the chances in `CRIT`) do double damage, on both sides: a shot at someone exposed 25%, at one of the dead in the open 10%, at anyone in cover 5% (+15% with Deadeye), a melee blow 8%, an overwatch reaction shot never; a critical also goes through a dead soldier's or riot cop's armour. Injuries and deaths carry back to the map. After a win the dead are bodies to loot (see Inventory). Every group fields its own people, humans only (raiders, fighters, guards, militia, soldiers, zealots); the dead fight beside humans only where both are found, as at a scavenge site. A sleeping enemy that is hit and lives wakes its group, whatever the weapon (silent kills stay silent). Makeshift gear wears when the battle ends: only on the units that took the field, and only the items they took it with. Battles that start in the same hour wait their turn; one whose team is gone by then is dropped.
- **Magazines and reloading** (`combat.js` magazines: `WEAPONS[k].cap`, `magCap`, `reload`; `tools/magtest.js`): every gun holds a magazine (9mm pistol 15, pump shotgun 6, hunting rifle 5, assault rifle 30 = 15 two-round bursts, pipe shotgun 1, .357 revolver 6, lever-action rifle 8, police carbine 30; × `gunMods(item).capMult` + `.cap` from a fitted magazine, `gunmods.js`) and starts a battle loaded. A shot takes `ammo` rounds from it; an empty gun cannot shoot, double tap or go on overwatch. **Reload** (I) fills it: 1 action from a spare magazine, a clip or rounds in a pocket or the rig, so reload-and-shoot fits one turn; both actions from the pack (`mags.js` reloadPlan, `tools/reloadsourcetest.js`). Your side's rounds stay in the team's pools until fired (the magazine is a counter: `ammoUsed`, the raid's pools, auto-resolve and saves are unchanged), and a magazine never shows or fires more than the pools still hold; a reload fills it only as far as they go. The enemy's (and the third side's) guns have magazines too and endless spare ammo: the AI reloads an empty gun before acting, and tops up one under half full when nothing is in reach (your side's AI, the raid bot and auto-played fights the same). A gun swapped in mid-raid comes empty; the same kind of gun keeps its rounds. Bows, crossbows, melee and a spitter's acid have no magazine. Fights by the numbers ignore magazines.
- **Gun builder** (`src/ui/gunbuilder.js`, the drawings in `src/ui/gunart.js`; docs/weapons-overhaul-plan.md section 9): select a gun in the inventory and press **Modify** (or the ⚙ on the gun in someone's hands, on a gun in the armory or team cargo, or on the base's Armory tab). The gun is drawn large (a 64 × 24 pixel drawing of its own for every gun, from its art spec in `src/ui/gunspecs.js`: the over-unders' stacked barrels, the single-shots' hinge and hammer, the AUG's scope-handle and clear magazine behind the grip, the FAL's carry handle, the M14's wooden stock, the M240's belt pouch and bipod, the revolvers' cylinders, the Desert Eagle's slab of a slide...; the same spec draws it held in battle and dropped by a body; a gun added without a spec is drawn as a look-alike). Guns come in finishes where they really do (black, tan, OD green, wood, stainless, blued...: `src/sim/data-finish.js`, shown in the item's tooltip and the builder's header), held in battle with the parts fitted to them, shown on their item tiles, in the trade lists and the start-gear window as small pictures, and a shot's tracer and flash start at the gun's own muzzle (or its suppressor's end) with each fitted part drawn on it, and a leader line runs from each slot to a box above or below (sight, mount, muzzle, magazine, under the barrel, stock, grip, rail, light or laser, bipod, sling: the ones that gun has; a mount's sight or a rail's grip, light and bipod appear with a dotted line once the part that opens them is on). Click a box: every part within reach that fits (on anyone at the same base or on the same team, in a pocket or the pack, or in the armory or the cargo), each with what it changes, **Fit**, and **Remove** for the fitted one. Hover a part: the stats panel shows before → after (aim, up close, best range and fall-off, damage, magazine, noise and earshot, weight, worth, the loaded round's penetration, reliability: "jams 0.5% a shot · ~1 in 200") and the gun's condition as a bar (the maximum lost to repairs a darker band). The **Ammo** list sets the round it is loaded with (`item.load`). No time; never during a battle. A selected loose part offers ⚙ for each gun on that person it fits. The item tooltip keeps a compact version: its slots and what is in them, its load and its reliability.
- **Fire modes, jams, suppressing fire, thrown spears, the reload ammo chooser** (battle panel, `src/ui/combat.js` *gun orders*; the rules are the weapons overhaul's, docs/weapons-overhaul-plan.md section 20): **Fire: single / burst / auto** (Shift+F, free) on a gun with fire modes; **Unjam** (Shift+J) and a JAMMED badge on a jammed gun; **Suppress** (Shift+O), a targeting mode like Shoot with the cone drawn on the map (both views) and who in it keeps their head down; **Throw spear** (Shift+G) with its 5-tile reach drawn; **Reload** opens a chooser when more than one kind of round for the gun is to hand (Shift+I picks the next, I loads it). Each button shows only when the unit can use it. Overwatch reacts to every enemy that moves in range until your next turn or the magazine runs dry. An enemy's line shows its gun and parts, never what it is loaded with.
- **Battle view (`src/ui/isoview.js`):** battles are drawn isometric (2:1 diamonds, pseudo-3D like the old X-COMs and Project Zomboid); there is no top-down battle view (its drawing code is gone). Everything is procedural pixel art in the State Map's gritty palette, painted at 32 × 16 art pixels a tile and scaled up nearest-neighbour: ground textured by surface (grass, fields, dirt, gravel, asphalt with faded paint, floorboards, concrete, rubble, water, mud and blood round the hive's flesh), walls of brick, plaster, wooden siding or block with windows and doors, the base's log palisade and gate, fences, sandbags, barbed wire, barricades, crates, cars and trucks (along the road), trees, rocks, hay, pumps, shelves and lockers, the hive's flesh. Height is real elevation: the wall's walkway and flat roofs one level up, upper floors inside two-storey houses, watchtowers two (the near rail in front of whoever stands there), with ladders and stairs. Units are the battle sprites standing on their tile (shadow, a ring in their side's color, the kind's letter for a hive's special dead, ★ elite), drawn back to front with everything else (u + v, then height); health bars, action pips, status tags and, for every enemy in sight, the selected unit's hit chance float over them. Buildings have roofs (tar, rusted metal, shingles, concrete); a building with one of your people inside, an enemy you see or the mouse inside has its roof cut away and its near walls dropped to stubs (B or *Roofs* in the panel: every building that way), blocks between your people and the camera are faded, and anyone still hidden shows as a silhouette. Fog of war darkens and greys what nobody sees, with ghosts where enemies were last seen; at night the scene is darker and flashlights, fires, flares and floodlit ground light it. Move range, paths, the deploy cells, the evac strip, raid zones and the floodlit area lie on the ground (on a raised floor at its height); cover shields, the line of fire and the hit chance show at the cursor. Camera: drag, W A S D, the arrow keys or the screen edge pan, the wheel or + / − zoom (0.75–5 CSS px an art pixel), Q and E (or , and .) turn it in 90° steps, Home centers on the selected unit (picking one off screen with Tab or a number moves the camera there); a compass shows north. Mouse picking runs the screen point back along its view ray to the frontmost cell it meets (raised floors and blocks; roofs and building walls do not stop it, so pointing inside a building opens it, and neither do trees: under a tree's crown the pick is the ground behind it, the tree's own cell at the foot of its trunk; cars, lockers, walls and gates stay targets in themselves) or the unit sprite under it. A tile hidden behind something that stands up in front of it on the screen (a tree, a car, a wall, a locker, an uncut roof; at least 20 art px taller than its floor: `coverOf`) is drawn with a dashed outline on top when hovered, and so is the tile of anyone standing there (in their ring's colour, the selected one bolder) or a pile of things lying there; the trees in front of the hovered tile are faded like those in front of your people. Speed: the whole map's ground is one small canvas (rebuilt only when a floor changes or the view turns), fog one more (rebuilt when what your people see changes), every block a cached sprite; a frame is two image draws, the overlays and one sprite per visible block and unit. Measured on the biggest maps (hive 32 × 22, raid 34 × 22, a fully built hub's yard 26 × 28) with a 4K-tall battle canvas (2830 × 2162 device pixels, Chromium on an RTX 3080): a frame takes 0.4–0.7 ms (median; 95th percentile 0.8–4.5 ms, worst 5.6 ms), well inside the 8.3 ms of 120 fps; headless Chrome at 3840 × 2160 draws 300–650 frames a second. The first frame of a battle or after turning the view builds the caches (40–90 ms, once); an action or an enemy step rebuilds only the fog and the cells' looks (a millisecond or two). Map sizes (`src/sim/battlesize.js` SIZES, one table; `registerBattleSize` for a layout's own): four classes, Small 24 × 18, Medium 32 × 24, Large 40 × 30, Extra-large 48 × 34 (a scale 0–3, in between in between). Raids by the site (a hospital, university, prison, military base, mall, factory, airport, the warehouse district or a city center Large; a supermarket, school, police or fire station, apartments, offices, a motel, church, farm, ranch, lodge or campground Medium; a gas station, diner, grocery, pharmacy, clinic, cabin, barn or small shop Small–Medium): the street map at that size, a building's own map (`buildingmaps.js`) its archetype's floor plan with the outside grown to at least the site's size. Fights in the open Medium, growing to Large with the people and the dead on the map (each of the dead counts 0.6), +0.2 on plains and by water, −0.15 in forest, hills and mountains, at a site at least its size less half a class. A hive assault Large (the mound at most 28 columns in, more of its land behind), a group's base Medium to Extra-large by the people living there (their wall at most 20 columns in, their grounds behind), your hub's yard map Large to Extra-large and an outpost's Medium to Large by their buildings (`interior.js` yardDims: the buildings by the yard and round the gate's rows as on the old 16-row map, the grounds beyond them grass, fields and a woodlot; the wall map 18 deep and as long as the yard less 3, the attack's front, breaches, towers and ladders within 6 rows of the gate: `deploy.js` FRONT). Content scales with the area: more buildings and blocks of houses, rooms (2–3 on Small to 6–9 on Extra-large) and their containers (one a 7 tiles of floor), the street raid's dead ×0.8–1.4 and its late waves the same, and every map topped up with props (`densify`: rocks and trees, hay, wrecks, crates and barrels by the ground; never closing a way through) until most open ground has cover within 3 tiles (82%, open plains 72%, a city 86%). The sides start 14–21 tiles apart whatever the size (`CONTACT`), so the first shots come on turn 1–2; the noise's reinforcements come in within 18 tiles of your people. Anti-slog: a raider who breaks and runs is gone once out of sight and 9 tiles off; when at most two enemies are left (a quarter of those at the start), none a boss, all unaware or running, 10+ tiles off and out of sight, the area is clear enough (the player is told, *Area clear: finish* or Shift+C ends it as a win; the battle AI ends it). The camera opens on your people and the ground toward the enemy (1.5–2.5 CSS px an art pixel, a raid 1.5–2; the whole map if it fits at 1.5), again when the map changes (the fall back to the yard); red arrows at the edge of the view point at every enemy seen (or last seen) off screen. Battle length (`tools/sizetest.js`, 10 battles each): Small 11 turns, Medium 11, Large 13, Extra-large 10, none past the 60-turn limit; combattest 0 timeouts in 1,080 (1 before). The battle AI: `distField` (every walker's way to its targets) is a binary heap and cached while the map is unchanged (the same field for every one of the dead in a turn), so the enemy turn takes 1–3 ms on average on every size (the worst 16 ms, a hub siege) and combattest runs 2× faster than on the old smaller maps.
  - *Sounding the retreat* (`combat.js` `soundRetreat`, `escapeOdds`, `retreatPlan`; any battle with an evac strip, on your turn): everyone still standing runs for the strip at once. On the strip with no enemy next to them, or anywhere with no enemy next to them, in sight or close: safe. Otherwise an escape check: 100 − 3 a tile of walking distance to the strip − 25 for each enemy next to them − 6 for each armed person who sees them and 4 for each of the dead (or a brawler) within two moves (those two at most 35) − 30 × the share of health lost − 2 a point of armor worn + 1 a point of Scouting + 10 at night (the hour the battle began), between 5 and 95%. Carrying a downed teammate who lies next to them: −10, and 4.5 a tile instead of 3 (each carries one; by default the one with the best odds carries, and the confirm can leave someone instead). Caught: one blow for each enemy next to them (one if none), 18–32 each before armor; at 0 health they go down and are left behind, otherwise they tear free and get out hurt; a caught carrier drops whoever they carried. A downed teammate carried out, or dragged onto the strip before the last one leaves (also with the per-unit Retreat), comes home like the downed after a win (stabilized: lives; else 50% + 2% a point of the best Medicine among those who got out). The outcome is "retreat" (a defeat if nobody got out) and goes to the battle's handler as before; the result screen lists who got out, who was caught and who was carried. The confirm (and the odds over each unit's head on the map) shows each one's chance, the chance of being lost when caught, the expected number out and who is left behind.
  - *The AI retreats* (`autoPlayerTurn`, `fightOutlook`, `RETREAT_AI`): at the start of its turn it weighs the two sides Lanchester-style (total firepower × total health ÷ armor; a gun counts its hit chance, −20 for cover against people, × damage; melee and the dead 40%) and sounds the retreat when the ratio falls below 0.45 (Cautious) / 0.3 (Balanced) / 0.18 (Aggressive), or when a third / half / two thirds of the team is down and the ratio is under 1. The style is the autopilot's (or `setup.aiStyle`, or the caller's); without one, Balanced. Defending a base on a break-in map that has a way out, every style uses the Aggressive numbers (giving up means abandoning the base). Human enemies already break and run when beaten badly (a third of them left and outnumbered 2 to 1; zealots never); the dead never flee.
  - *Retreat by the numbers* (`src/sim/breakoff.js`; `combat.js` `autoResolveCombat` hands every fight resolved by the numbers to `fightByNumbers`): the Auto-resolve button, headless tools, the autopilot and crews. The fight is decided as before (one roll on the odds the card shows; ammo, friends and rivals, fortified walls) and plays out in 3 rounds: a fight being won takes its wounds early, one being lost keeps taking them to the last round. After rounds 1 and 2 a team in the field (`breaksOff`: a team's fight with a way out, not a raid or a base defence) looks at its *Retreat if it goes badly* policy: **Never** (fight it out; the old resolution, the same odds and wounds), **When most are down** (more than half the team down or under 50 health), **If losing** (the fight turns: seen after round 1 in 50% + 40% × the odds against of the fights being lost, 95% by round 2; a false alarm in 25% / 10% × the odds against of the fights that would be won; or anyone is down), **At first blood** (anyone hurt). Breaking off: the blows of the fight land whole, so those still standing first take part of any blow still to come for them (that blow × (the share of the team down)^1.5, never to 0 health: about fresh after an early break-off, about half health when most are down, as on the map); then everyone still standing runs (`escapeChanceByNumbers`, the tactical check with the map's numbers estimated: 2–5 tiles to safety, +2 when they had the drop on you, −1 when you struck first; of the enemy left (at most 16 get at the runners), 0.35 of the dead per runner on them (people 0.05; at most 3), 0.35 of the people shooting, 0.5 of the dead chasing, the dead's share × 0.35 after round 1 and × 0.5 after round 2 as they close in; then −3 a tile, −25 a pursuer on them, −6 a shooter and −4 a chaser (at most 35), up to −30 for wounds, −2 a point of armor, +1 a point of Scouting, +10 at night, and by the numbers +10 with the car close by, +5 bikes, +10 a stealthy stance; 5–95%), each downed teammate carried by the free runner with the best odds (−10, 4.5 a tile); caught: the tactical blows (18–32 per pursuer on them, at least one, before armor), at 0 health dragged down and left behind (a carrier drops whoever they carried). The carried pull through at 50% + 2% a point of Medicine of the best medic who got out (home at 5–15 health); the left behind are lost to the dead, and to a group's people killed or taken prisoner half the time (`conflicts.js` `takeCaptive`). The enemy loses 10–50% (35–75% in a fight you would have won) × the share of rounds fought; nothing is looted (only a won fight is searched), the ammo of the rounds not fought is given back, and the result is "retreat" (a defeat if nobody got out) with `retreatOrder` (who got out, was caught, carried, dropped) and a summary line for the handlers' after-fight text. *The card*: every card with a fight choice (road conflicts, ambushes, a firefight, rescues, outsiders, a group-base assault, a job's rescue, the road's horde) shows the team's policy as four buttons (kept on the team, `p.retreatPolicy`; saved with it) and, under each fight choice, a forecast run on its own dice (300 fights, the game's dice untouched): "If losing: about 64% to win; if it turns, most get away (pulls out 30% of the time). About 0.1 deaths a fight (0.3 fought to the end)". A team without its own policy: a crew's role setting (Work tab, *Retreat*: by its style unless set), the autopilot's style (Cautious and Balanced If losing, Aggressive When most are down), else Never. A set policy is also the AI style of a battle a team plays headless on the map (a hive assault: At first blood Cautious, If losing Balanced, the others Aggressive). The road's horde fight breaks off between its wounds by the same policy: the horde thinned only for the rounds fought, then everyone runs from the dead. `breakOff(state, people, opts)` is the same escape for any other resolver (the downed carried, the caught hurt, the left behind killed or taken): for abandoning a base by the numbers.
- **Lights** (`src/sim/lights.js`, items in `data.js`, finds in `loot.js` LIGHT_FINDS, recipes in `crafting.js`): five, each heavier and brighter: headlamp 0.2 kg (hands free, works from anywhere on the person), flashlight 0.3, torch 0.7 (an open flame; made from firewood anywhere), lantern 1.2 (all around), spotlight 3.5 (a long beam); all but the headlamp in the off hand. At night in raids and base battles they set your people's sight (7 without; 8, 9, 9, 10, 12) and add to how far off the guards notice them (+1, +3, +4, +4, +6) (`stealth.js`); a rummage without one is louder (`search.js`); a survey's night hours keep ×0.42–0.7 of the odds by the best light carried (`survey.js`). Teams take a light each from the armory when they set out (the planner's *Lights* line, on by default; the autopilot's and crews' trip kit, `kit.js`), the lightest that works for each person, packed before the estimate so the pace counts the weight; a failed launch puts them back. Battles draw a beam as long as the light (a glow all around for a lantern or torch); on the State Map a team carrying lights glows at night. `node tools/lighttest.js`.
- **Travel times and road blockages** (`src/sim/data.js` TERRAIN / ROAD_COST, `parties.js` BIKE_ROAD, `src/sim/roadblocks.js`): on foot a tile takes an hour whatever the ground; a car 15 minutes a tile on a road or a bridge and 30 off-road (no mountains); bikes 30 minutes on a road, walking pace off it; stance, load and weather scale these. While the clock runs the top bar's minutes tick by and your teams glide between tiles on the map (`parties.js` glideAt; out of contact, the estimated position glides too): a team that gets there before the hour is out waits on the tile, and a team holding for a card in the tray does not glide. A car or bikes on a road meet a blockage on about 6% of road tiles (stalled cars, debris, a fallen tree, a pile-up, a washed-out dip): 15 minutes to 3 hours lost (bikes 40% of it), told in the log and shown as *Road blocked* over the team while it pays the delay off; the team is held up on the tile where it met the blockage (the rest of the hour's drive comes after the delay). Their own dice (hash01), so the rest of a run draws the same numbers; `state.noRoadblocks` is a test switch.
- **Travel events:** about 18 choice cards with skill checks (hazards, the dead, raiders, traders, discoveries, crew drama), plus forced horde encounters. One card per team per hour; a card is dropped when its team is gone or the person it is about has died or left; costs are checked against what the team carries when the choice is made.
- **Fog of war and recon:** geography is known; current conditions are not. Teams report only when they get home, unless they are in radio coverage (live position and sight).
- **Points of interest** (`src/sim/pois.js`, 2026-10-15; the surveys and the raid flow per POI below, `src/sim/poiraid.js`; sprites, the tile panel, tile heat, containers and the other groups' surveys and raids below; nomads come with the sessions in `docs/prompts/poi-and-nomads.md`): every land tile can hold 0–4 small places (`tile.pois`: a house, a shed, a hunting blind, a campsite, a gas station…), each a `LOCATIONS` kind with a loot pool of its own that never grows back. A tile with a site keeps the site as its top POI (`tile.loc` stays as it is and its pool is still `tile.loot`) and gains 0–3 extras by terrain; a bare tile holds 0–4 (mountains 0–1, hills and forest 0–2, farmland 0–3, suburbs and bare city blocks 1–4; its top POI is the richest, and `tile.loc` is never written for it). City and town centres, water and hive tiles hold none (a hive rising clears the tile, and it stays empty when the hive falls). Towns now sit in a **suburb ring** of radius 1–2 (laid last, so old seeds build the same map under it). The *known zone* (a road tile, a tile next to one, a city footprint or a town footprint) starts with its POIs known except 0–2 hidden ones among the non-top; the unknown zone starts with everything hidden; a site keeps its own discovery class. Hidden POIs are found by surveys (`revealPois`): a team keeps its finds (`p.poiFinds`) until its report reaches the hub (`world.js mergeKnowledge` → `state.poiKnow`, with the day the tile was surveyed or looked at); snapshots carry what is known (`TileSnapshot.pois`). New kinds: house, neighbourhood, garage, corner shop, pizza place, farm stand, shed, hunting blind, campsite, hiker camp, mine shack (a plain building stands in for a kind without a sprite yet). Saves hold only the POIs that changed (`state.poiMods`); a save from before gets its lists from its seed on load. Hash dice from the seed and the tile throughout: the map's own random numbers are untouched (`tools/poitest.js`).
- **Hidden sites** (`src/sim/discovery.js`; the rule is `known` at the end of each `data.js` LOCATIONS line): only what a normal person knew before the fall is on the map from day 1. *Public* types are on it wherever they stand: towns, cities and every shop and service in them (police, schools, hospitals, malls, gas stations…), truck stops, landmarks (dam, prison, university, factory, airport) and the National Guard bases (everyone knew where the base was). *Road* types (farmsteads, barns, ranches, cabins, hunting lodges, campgrounds, ranger stations, radio towers, quarries) are public when they stand on a road or next to one (within 1 tile) and hidden otherwise. *Secret* types (the old crash sites, and the wrecks and supply drops of crash events) are always hidden until found. A hidden site is not drawn (no icon, name or danger skulls), the tooltip and tile panel show bare ground, the right-click menu offers no scavenge, plans, presets ("nearby sites, richest first"), crews and the autopilot never pick it and text calls it by its ground ("forest (12,30)"), until your people have intel on its tile: seen from a base or a watchtower, seen by a team (radioed in when it is in coverage, else brought home; the team itself may use it at once), or reported (radio news, an explorer's or scout's map, intel bought or given as a job reward, a prisoner questioned, the coalition's reports, a group showing you where a retrieve job's pick-up is). Intel is never taken away, so a found site stays on the map and is saved with the game. Finding them on purpose: the **Survey** mission (below). Each find gets one log and diary line: *🧭 Move here team (Cole, June) found a hunting lodge deep in the woods: Pine Hollow Hunting lodge. It is on the map now.* (a team out of contact "brought word of" it when home; *Lookouts at …* from a base; *Word reached you of …* for reports). Hive names only use public sites. The hub knows the public sites everywhere plus what it sees on day 1. The timelapse replay is a god view and shows every site; Dev Reveal shows them too (display only). About 14–19% of the sites are hidden (Small 18 of 128, Normal 33 of 186, Large 54 of 282, Huge 82 of 419, about 5 within 12 tiles of the hub; `tools/maptest.js`).
- **POI surveys and the raid flow per POI** (`src/sim/survey.js` LOOK, `src/sim/poiraid.js`, `src/ui/poipick.js`, 2026-10-15): *Survey this tile* (8 hours on the spot: one roll at the end for the team's own tile with the sweep's odds compounded over the hours; success finds every hidden place there, failure each on its own at 40% of the odds and "not sure you found everything"; binoculars, a scope or a thermal add the ring of six tiles at ×0.6, never further; it also rolls the tile's hidden site and the look-around finds, stamps the tile surveyed and records a threat estimate on every place known there) and *Quick look* (the old Look around, 2 hours: each hidden place at half the Survey's odds, the kind only, stamped looked; a recon's look on arrival is one too). Both build Field Heat. A place's threat is its kind's danger + the infection + the tile's heat + hive land; what you see is an estimate (a word with skulls, the error growing 3% a day, "stale" after two weeks, "threat unknown" with no look). A scavenge / raid on a tile with places goes into one of them: in the browser a card lists the known places (visits, picked-over band, container note, raided now, threat) and you pick; the autopilot, crews and tools take the best known one (value × what is left / perceived threat) and a search that strips it moves on to the next. With no place known the order is refused ("survey first"). The raid map is the place's building (the site keeps its own choice of buildings); each place has its own pool. Raiding a place on another group's base tile is hostile. The autopilot sends POI surveys to tiles that may hide more (`tools/poiraidtest.js`). The radius mission below is now *Sweep area*.
- **Tile heat and containers that stay** (`src/sim/pois.js` TILE_HEAT, `src/sim/poicont.js`, 2026-10-15): every tile keeps a *heat* of its own (`state.tileHeat`, 0–100): going into one of its places adds 3, coming out the approach's 7 / 13 / 22 (a raid on a map by how hot it ran) and half a point a shot fired (at most 15), another group's raid 4; it falls 10% a day. It raises the true threat of every place there (up to +1.5), brings one more of the dead to a raid every 20 heat, and from 50 a chance (up to 20% at 100) of a horde or a wandering band drawn by the noise (hash dice). The player sees it as a team last saw it (the tile panel's Tile heat row, the place dialogue): stale after two weeks, like the threat. A place's raid map is the same on every visit and each container's contents are rolled from its own dice (the seed, the tile, the place, the map, the container), so an unopened one holds the same things whenever it is first opened.
