A base over its beds says so with an **Overcrowded** card (`src/sim/moveout.js`, checked every hour: once when it gets crowded, then after 5 days or sooner when 2 more people are over the beds; not while the autopilot plays): *Move a team out…* opens the **move-out planner** (also under Beds on the base screen, for any base): who goes, transport and supplies from that base's stores (by default the trip plus 3 days of food and water, 10 ammo each and, with no destination, the 15 scrap an outpost needs), and where to: another of your bases (free beds and hours shown) or *Pick a place on the map* (the team waits outside and the map goes to move mode). The *Moving out* team is a team like any other (Build outpost founds a base with it; *Return home* takes it back). A team of yours that ends a move on one of your bases that is not its home, with nothing queued, gets **Enter …?** instead of the orders card: yes, its people live there from now on (supplies, cargo and armory items go into its stores, the vehicle parks there, "moved into" in the log), no, it stays a team and gets the orders card; not for convoys, caravans or crews. `launchExpedition` takes `fromBaseId` (a team from any of your bases). Below 30 mood someone *talks about leaving* (a log line, and a card for the worst case at each base each day), below 20 they are *close to leaving* (log line and card); the card offers *Talk to them* (the leader there, or the best Speech: 30% + 3.5% per point, up to 95%, mood +25, else +5), *Give them 3 days' rest* (the Rest role, +5 now, then back to their role), *A gift from the stores* (4 food, 1 meds: +20 mood, +30 for the Greedy) or *Let them be*. The People tab shows the risk next to the mood (Unhappy, Talks about leaving, Close to leaving, May walk out any night). Desertion: below 15 mood, only after the second warning on an earlier night, 8% a night (+0.6% per point below 15); leaders never desert. A deserter keeps everything they carry (worn, held, pockets and pack) and takes 2–4 food, 2–3 water and maybe 1–2 meds and 5–12 ammo (Greedy twice as much and likelier, a Coward half the food and water; never more than a quarter of a stock); the log line names it all. Between 8 and 90 days later a deserter may turn up at the gate again (0.6% a day) as a recruit card. The run still ends when the last one leaves.
- **Mood that matters** (`src/sim/relations.js` `moodFactors`, `morale.js` `moodWork`): mood sets how much work people do: 70% at 0, 80% at 20, 100% from 45 to 80, 110% at 100 (straight lines between; `health.js` `workFactor` multiplies it with illness, so builders, crafters, farmers, station operators, medics, base scavengers and readers all follow it). New daily factors besides beds, the leader, food, rest and the dog: nothing but preserved food for 7 days −1 (21 days −2), hot meals from a kitchen +1, a **Common room** (22 scrap, 30 work-hours, up to 2, no operator) +1.5 for up to 15 people a room, a **Chapel** (16 scrap, 24 work-hours, no operator) +1 and grief weighs half, books in a pack +0.5 each up to +1 (books on the shelf count up to 2 a person but are shared, each copy going round 5 people, so a big hub needs a bigger shelf; manuals and blueprints from `blueprints.js` are study, not comfort), the **Inspiring** perk +0.5 from each inspiring person at the base (up to +1), cabin fever after 14 days of winter −0.5 (21: −1), three grey days in a row −0.5, friends nearby +0.4 each (up to +1.2), a partner +1.5 (away: −0.5), rivals −0.6 each (up to −1.8), grieving −(0.5 + bond) a day, after a rough trip (a death, someone badly hurt or downed, 10+ days out: −4 at once) +3 a day resting or −1 a day working for 4 days, and high spirits fade by 10% of the mood above 50 a day (a hub with only beds and food settles around 70–80; one with a kitchen, a common room and friends stays near 100, a crowded or grieving one well below). Hover any mood bar (People tab, survivor screen) for every factor with its number, the recent one-off changes and the work it gives. **Breaks:** below 12 mood, 10% + 2% per point below 12 a day (at most one in 5 days): they sit the day out or wander off (no work), binge on 3 food from the stores, or pick a fight (the other loses 3–8 health, their opinion drops); afterwards mood +8; a card (talk to them, 2 days' rest, let them be) for a fight or a second break within 10 days.
- **Mental breaks** (`src/sim/mentalbreaks.js`, every number in `SNAP`; `tools/snaptest.js`): under 10 mood for 3 mornings running at a base, then 12% + 2% a point below 10 a day (10-day cooldown), someone breaks for good: runs off in the night (a deserter), drives off in a car, goes for the person they like least (or the leader) with whatever is in their hand and may kill them, takes their own life, sets fire to a building, wrecks the stores, walks out into the dead (infected ground only) or shuts down in bed for days; leaders never run off. On the road a team's people break the same way (not mid-fight): slip away from the camp with a little of the team's food and water, go for a teammate, walk into the dead, wreck the packs or sit down for half a day; no fires or cars. A death: everyone at the place −10 on top of grief. The morning after an attack, a killing, a fire or the stores, a card: confine them (7 days), cast them out, put them down or forgive them. The log warns on the third morning and the People tab says "May break down".
- **Traits** (`src/sim/data.js` `TRAITS`, the rules' numbers and where each works in `src/sim/traits.js`): one or two at birth, up to three by hand. Good: Brave (+1 fighting strength, +5% to hit), Night Owl, Former Medic (+4 Medicine), Greedy, Tough (−25% from wounds), Keen Eye, Lucky (+1 every skill), and since 2026-10-08 Quick Study (+25% XP), Iron Stomach (an upset stomach half as likely, the worm's eggs from water, raw food, camping and searching shrugged off a third of the time), Quick Healer (wounds heal 30% faster at rest: a base, a hospital bed, a camp, a convoy, a prisoner's cell), Strong Back (max load 48 kg, loads feel 10% lighter), Fleet-Footed (+1 tile a move in battle, +10 on escape checks on the map and by the numbers), Farm-Raised (+3 Farming), Persuasive (+3 Speech: prices, talks, recruiting) and Iron Will (small breaks, mental breaks and walking out half as likely). Bad: Coward, Loud (−3 Stealth), Bad Back (max 28 kg, loads 20% heavier), and Unlucky (−1 every skill), Frail (+25% from wounds), Clumsy (−10% to hit in melee, 50% more cuts and sprains at work and searching), Glutton (a quarter ration more at a base, hunger 25% faster on the road), Sickly (falls ill 50% more often, illnesses clear a point a day slower) and Hothead (−10 opinion with everyone; a break is far likelier a fight, a mental break an attack). Opposites never go together (`TRAIT_CLASH`: Brave/Coward, Lucky/Unlucky, Tough/Frail, Strong Back/Bad Back, Quick Healer/Sickly, Iron Will/Hothead): the birth roll, a child's traits, the new-run screen's picker (the clashing one greyed out with why), custom backgrounds and a start's backgrounds. Rolling: the world's dice draw from the ten older traits exactly as before (`BIRTH_TRAITS`), then each is swapped for a newer one 14 times in 24 (`TRAIT_ROLL.NEW_SHARE`, every trait about as common as any other) with dice of their own keyed by the seed and how many people the world has made, so a seed keeps its map, people and every roll of the world's dice. Everyone with traits plays by them: your people, the autopilot's, recruits, prisoners and children grown up; other groups' people are numbers until they join you or are taken.
- **Friends, rivals and grief** (`src/sim/relations.js`): every pair who share a base, a team, a job or a fight has an opinion (−100..100) that drifts each day toward 100 × compatibility (seeded chemistry, temper: Warm / Easy-going / Prickly, traits: Brave vs Coward −30, Iron Will vs Hothead −10, Loud, Greedy and Hothead −10 each, Glutton and Unlucky −5, Former Medic +10, Lucky +5, Speech) plus shared history (a fight together +6, +10 if someone went down or died; a trip +3, a rough one +6; hungry days; quarrels, arguments, gifts; it fades 1% a day) plus 0.6 × the leader's Leadership where the leader is; 5% of the gap a day at a base, 2.5× on a team, more at the same job. Friends at 40, close friends at 70, rivals at −30. Partners come from the hub event *two survivors grown close* (it now picks the closest friends) and split up below 15. In battle +3 aim per friend alongside (up to +6) and −2 per rival (up to −4); by the numbers a team's strength ×(1 + 3% per friend pair − 3% per rival pair). Rivals make hub quarrels likelier (and are the ones quarrelling 70% of the time) and argue on the road (weight 5 instead of 2); friends on a team tell stories by the fire (+2 team mood, +4 for them). A death: friends and a partner lose 6 + 14 × bond mood at once and grieve for 3 + 7 × bond days (bond = opinion/100, a partner 1), working up to 20% slower; acquaintances −3; friends of someone killed by a group want revenge (+5 aim against them for 60 days, +6 mood when avenged). A friend who deserts costs half as much. On the survivor screen: their mood and its factors, people (partner, friends, rivals with opinion and why; *Talk it out* with the leader or best Speech, or a *Shared meal* for 3 food, once in 10 days), the **service record** (days with the group, trips, sites searched, the dead put down, people killed, people treated, buildings built, food harvested, hives destroyed, times downed, rescues) and **notable moments** ("Held the gate during the 300-horde break-in at Dunmore Holdout, day 147"). The People tab shows a chip (♥ partner, +friends, −rivals; Grieving, On a break, Shaken). `survivorSummary(state, s)` gives all of it for other screens. **Memorial wall** (base panel → *Memorial wall*): everyone who died, with portrait, days with the group, cause and place of death, record highlights, moments and who grieved them; *Hold a memorial service* (1 food per person, once a week): everyone +3 (+2 more in a chapel), the grieving +8 more and 2 days less grief; also a hub event (*Remembering the dead*: a proper service, a few words, or no time for it). The autopilot keeps friends together and rivals apart among near-equal crew picks, rests people low after a rough trip, builds a common room once the hub has 8+ people and spirits are below 85 (saving up for it below 75), or as planned comfort once the hub has 12+ people and the scrap (saving up for it at 15+), and holds a memorial service when people grieve or spirits are low.
- **The harvest festival (`src/sim/festival.js`, every number in `FEST`):** once a year, on the morning of autumn day 22 (campaign day 64 in the first year, then every 112 days), after the harvest and before the hard frosts, the hub (at least 3 people) asks for a festival. *Hold the festival:* a day off for everyone at the hub but the guards and the medics (the Rest role until midnight), 2 food a head (a child at the hub counts half: children have no mood of their own, so the lift does not reach them), a bonfire of 40 firewood if the hub has it and a bottle of liquor from the armory for every 4 people if there is any; everyone at the hub mood +10 (+2 bonfire, +2 drink), then +1 a day for 7 days ("The harvest festival" in the mood breakdown); everyone at the outposts and on the road +4, then +0.5 a day; every pair at the hub a little closer, and a 40% chance of a story (two friends at 50+ becoming a couple, two rivals making peace, or two people hitting it off). *A small festival* (the default answer): an evening supper with no day off, 1 food a head, mood +5 then +0.5 a day for 4 days (+2 elsewhere), a 15% chance of a story. *Skip it:* nothing, unless the hub's mood is already under 40 (then −3). While the hub is under attack (a siege, an alert, a horde heading for it within 4 tiles) or starving (out of food or under 5 days of it) it is put off 3 days, once; still not possible then, there is none that year. No hub (hardcore before the first one), no festival that year. Extra fires burn at the hub on the map on the festival day, and the day is in the diary. The autopilot holds the full festival with 40+ days of food left after it, a small one with 20+, and skips it otherwise. The stories use relations.js's own dice, so the world's are untouched.
- **Children and generations** (`src/sim/family.js`, `src/ui/family.js`): **switched off for now** (`FAMILY.enabled = false` in `family.js`: no couples, pregnancies or births, the new-run box and the Help topic hidden; the code is kept and `true` brings it all back; `tools/familytest.js` switches it on to test it). When on: close friends (opinion 70+) living at the same base may become a couple (4% a day, a diary line); partners from the hub event count too. A couple at home together may expect a child: 3% a day at best, ×0.6 under 30 days of food, ×0.6 with under 3 free beds, ×0.5 while the base is under threat (a raid warning or a horde on its way), ×0.4 in low spirits (×1.3 in good ones), ×0.5 hurt or ill; never with no free bed, under 10 days of food, on short rations, with both very low, or when the base already has one child per 3 adults (at least 2). The survivor screen's **Family** card shows the chance and why. A pregnancy lasts 56 days (two seasons; a year is 112 days), the last 14 at work ×0.85; the birth is at a base (away: when back, or after 7 days overdue on the road, the child brought to a base). A hard birth (10%, halved by a medic (Medicine 5+ or the Medic role), halved again by a hospital, doubled on the road) costs the parent 20 health and a med; rarely the baby does not live (a fifth of hard births with no care, 5% with a medic or a hospital, never with both). The couple then waits 56 days. Children are kept apart from the survivors (`state.children`): no team, battle, guard post, job, prisoner trade, recruit card or head count ever sees them. A child has a name nobody has (a parent's surname), a look between the parents' and their traits; they grow up over a year by the days they are fed: 🍼 baby (to day 28), 🧒 child, 🧑 youngster (from day 84), and at day 112 a survivor with the same id who joins the work at their base (work.js newcomers) with low skills: 0–3, plus a third of the better parent's, +2 Farming for chores and +3 / +2 in the skills of the work they grew up around. They eat a quarter, half and three quarters of a ration (the base's food use lists them; water the same), a baby sleeps with a parent and older children two to a bed (they take beds from the adults' count); with nothing in store the parents go hungry for them. From day 28 they do chores at a base with farm plots or a kitchen (0.25 food a day, 0.5 as a youngster). Mood: expecting +0.5 a day, their own child at the base +1, children about the base +0.5 for everyone, a birth +10 for the parents and +2 for the base. In raids and horde attacks they shelter; a base that falls sends them with its people to your nearest base (8% lost on the way), with no base left they go with the exiles. Losing a child: the parents −25 mood and 12 days of grief, the base −5. People tab and roster list the children (stage icon, age, parents, chores); a chip on a parent's card. Births, couples, expecting and coming of age are diary lines (Base & people), never a card. The new-run screen's **Children** box (on by default) turns it all off. In 2-year Balanced autopilot runs (seeds 1–3, Normal) the first year has no births (couples are still forming in a colony of 6–18), the second 5, 2 and 0 births (with one more expecting in each) at 34, 25 and 18 people. The family has its own dice: the world's are untouched. `birthsOn(state, day)` and `state.family.births` count births for the history.
- **Hub events** (`src/sim/hubevents.js`): decisions for the days at home: a daily chance of 4% + 2.6% per person at the hub (8–40%, up to 30% more when overcrowded), never two days running and never while one is open: about one every 8 days at 4 people, 5 at 10, 4 at 20. Weighted by season, weather, crowding, mood and size: a **fire** in a bunkhouse, workshop, garage, watchtower or rain catcher (fight it with 15 water and every hand: 45% + 4% per person to save it; sand and blankets: 20% + 2.5% per person; or let it burn; someone may be burned for 8–18), a **quarrel** (side with one: +8 / −12, mediate with the leader or best Speech: both +6 or both −6, stay out: both −5 and 30% it comes to blows), a **peddler** (two swaps at fixed amounts), a **lost teenager** (a recruit card: +4 mood to all if taken in, −4 if turned away), a **wandering preacher** (everyone +5 for 2 food, but anyone below 35 mood may follow them away; or feed them and send them on), **food missing** (the food is gone when the card comes; find out who: half back, the thief −10 mood; lock the stores for 4 scrap: far less theft for 30 days; or let it go: likelier for 20 days), a **stray dog** (eats 0.5 food a day, +0.5 mood a day for everyone), **storm damage** after storms or rain (a leaking roof over the stores: patch for 4 scrap, a tarp for 1 that holds 60%, or lose 8% of the food; a broken well pump, now a breakdown (`breakdowns.js`, see *Things break*): repair it (a mechanic, 6 work-hours, 3 scrap and 1 part), jury-rig it (2 h, 1 scrap, breaks again sooner) or leave it on the Repairs list (6 less water a day until it is fixed)), a **feast** (2 food each: +10, 1 each: +5, not now: −2), a **personal errand** (away 3–5 days with 3 food and 3 water: back with +25 mood and maybe a find, 8% never come back; refused: −12) and **two survivors grown close** (a corner of their own for 3 scrap: both +15; a party for 4 food; leave them to it; either way they are partners now), and **remembering the dead** after a death nobody has marked (a memorial service, a few words, no time for it). Quarrels and couples follow relationships (see *Friends, rivals and grief*). The autopilot scores each choice.
- **The Kitchen and its cooks** (2026-10-10, `src/sim/kitchen.js` KITCHEN, `buildings.js` "canteen", `ui/kitchen.js`; `node tools/kitchentest.js [seed]`, about 20 s): a building plot, 20 scrap, a part, 24 work-hours, one a base. The older key `kitchen` is the **Preserving room** (it was the "Preserving kitchen": it jars food, saves keep it). The **Cook** job (economy.js ROLES.cook; the work priorities' Cook type `meals`, the old `cook` type is Preserve, column "Jar"): one cook per 12 people living there. A fixed-role cook (Manual, the governor's) works the full day (up at 05:00 for breakfast, serving at 06:00, the hunters' 08:00 and 20:00, bread and stock between, dinner from 16:00, washing up until 22:00: the People tab's activity); a cook on work priorities is part-time: the Cook type claims only the cooking hours (05:00–06:00, 16:00–20:00) and the rest of the day goes to their other priorities, and having cooked they count as one of the day's cooks for 24 hours (s.cookHour), and everyone fed shows "Eating dinner at the kitchen". With the cooks feeding everyone: the residents eat 10% less a day (upkeep.js foodUse × kitchenEatMult; the best cook's Farming adds 0.5% a point above 4, −13% at most; Farming is the cooking skill), hot meals +1 mood a day (+1.5 with a Farming 6+ cook; the preserving room's stove gives its old +1, never both), a quarter of the day's drinking boiled for the meals (worm.js purifyCap, nothing extra where a boiler or a shelter's fire boils it all); too few cooks: that share of the people. Cook or no cook it cooks the game and greens through like a cook fire (worm.js baseCooks). No kitchen or no cook: the food is eaten as before. The base window: the Kitchen card (Buildings tab and Construction: cooks of wanted, today's hot meals, the food saved, the best cook), a Meals line in Bonuses, the Work now panel's Cook row (meals and food saved a day, output.js). The governor (and a steward with Building and Farming & food) builds one at a base of 8+ people (saved for from 12; at the hub after the first infirmary) and staffs its cooks (planRoles 5c, ahead of the comfortable farm crew). AI groups (groupworks.js KITCHEN): a kitchen at a base of 8+ once their workshop is up; the people it serves eat 10% less (groupEatMult in factionsDaily).
- **Cooking: bills, recipes, food poisoning** (wave 2 cooking 2026-10-11, `src/sim/cooking.js`, `ui/cooking.js`; `node tools/cookingtest.js [seed]`, about 30 s): the Kitchen and a cook fire (a shelter's or camp's own fire too) take RimWorld-style bills (Do N times, Do until you have N, Do forever; an ingredient filter: fresh only, human meat, what spoils first or the cheapest, items left out; pause; the list is the priority) on the Farming tab's *Cooking* section. The Kitchen's cooks work them (a fixed Cook 12 hours, a part-time cook their cooking hours), a cook fire is tended 4 hours a day by the best cook at home (1 firewood an hour; a shelter's fire is free), at 60% speed at Cooking 0 to 140% at 20. Recipes (`COOK_RECIPES`): simple meal (gain 1.25, Cooking 0), stew (1.35, 4 water, Cooking 2), fine (1.4, meat + veg, Cooking 6), lavish (0.8, Cooking 8, the mood), pemmican (keeps a year), survival meals (a powered vacuum sealer), flour (a hand-cranked grain mill), bread, ground meat and smoked sausage (a meat grinder), a team's camp roast; each takes purified water by the recipe (un-purified boiled for the pot at 0.5 firewood a water). A new Kitchen starts with Simple meal until 2 days of eating (the old automatic meals). Cookbooks (four, `blueprints.js` CRAFT_BOOKS `cook`): read to the end, the cooks make their meals below the usual Cooking. Dirt per station (+3 an hour cooked, *Clean first* scrubs it). **Food poisoning** (`health.js` poisoning, an illness: about a day of vomiting): a meal's lot carries `pz` (3% a day of food at Cooking 0 down to 0.06% at 20, × up to 3 for dirt) and its cook (`by`), rolled where it is eaten (the eating contract at the top of cooking.js); raw meat or fish eaten uncooked 5% a day of it; game and greens with the worm's eggs eaten raw give it in place of the upset stomach. AI groups cook coarsely in their kitchens (the same gains, fine meals with a skilled cook, survival meals with a sealer, bread with a mill, sausage with a grinder; poisoning as illness).
- **The food chain** (2026-10-12, the base window's **Food** tab, `src/ui/foodchain.js`; numbers in `src/sim/foodchain.js`, the week's record in `src/sim/foodlog.js`; Help: *The food chain*; Codex: *The food chain*, *Spoilage*, *Fires and firewood*): why food runs out, read left to right. **Sources**: the last 7 days in by where it came from (farms and harvests with a pen's eggs and milk, hunting and snares, fishing, foraging, scavenging and teams home, trade and gifts, your other bases) and the crops ripening with their days and expected food. **Stores**: days of food on hand for everyone fed there (foodIn ÷ heads; at today's eating too), fresh and what keeps, kg and kcal, each food room (its shelf factor today, held / room, the days until its next lot spoils; outside; the dry store) and what spoils within 7 days (and how much of it will not be eaten in time). **Kitchen**: each cooking station, its cook, its bills and whether each can go now (ingredients, purified water, firewood, the cook: cooking.js batchWhy), what went into the pots and came out over the week, the purified water, and the fires: the woodpile, what burns it a day (a camp's or shelter's fire, a cook fire while it cooks, the Kitchen's stove, the water boiler, the heating) and the hours it lasts; "No cook fire, no Kitchen: nothing cooks". **Eating**: people, eaten a day (measured) against the need, the ration level, what kind of food (cooked meals, rations, preserved, cans and produce, raw meat and fish) and the days since hot food. **Losses**: spoiled, eaten raw, sent out with teams, filled into the packs everyone carries (and what came back), shipped to your other bases, used or taken, lost to preserving. Then the verdict: "Food lasts about N days at this rate; biggest drain: X" (the measured net a day, the packs left out; "Food is holding" with the days it would last with nothing coming in) and one or two things to do (cook the venison before day N, build a root cellar for the food outside, bring in firewood or light the fire, more people on Farm, Hunt, Fish or Forage or Lean rations, get a meal cooked). The record: `base.foodLog`, one row a day for the last 8 days, booked at marks between the hour's steps and the steps of the base's day (world.js, economy.js) by the phase and the food's kind, eating (storage.js noteAte) and spoiling booked where they happen; every row closes on the base's days of food. Pure bookkeeping (no dice, nothing changes); saved with the game, an older save starts it on its first hour. `node tools/foodchaintest.js`.
- **Farming, water and food** (`src/sim/farming.js`, `src/sim/economy.js`): crops do not give food every day. Each base has crop plots (buildings.js CROP_PLOTS: the main base 8, an outpost 4, a shelter 2, a camp 1, +2 per Farm plot building up to 10, +2 sheltered plots per Greenhouse). A crop takes its days to ripen, slowed by the weather (`growthFactor`: cold, dry soil, snow; nothing out of season), and is harvested in one go: yield = base yield × care × ground (farmland 1.3, forest 0.9, hills 0.85), where care averages, over the days the crop stood, tending (farmers per planted plot: 0.5 → ×1.0, 1 → ×1.23, 2 → ×1.4) × skill (Farming of the tending farmers: 0 → ×0.75, 7 → ×1.0, 20 → ×1.45). Crops: potatoes 28 days / 65 food per plot / seed 4 / water 0.2 (spring–autumn), beans 18 / 36 / 3 / 0.2 (spring–summer, tender), corn 32 / 80 / 3 / 0.35 (spring–summer, tender, thirsty, storms flatten it), wheat 42 / 72 / 4 / 0.15 (spring–autumn, hardy), squash 24 / 52 / 3 / 0.3 (summer–autumn), greens 12 / 19 / 2 / 0.2 (spring–autumn, wilts in heat), winter roots 30 / 40 / 3 / 0.15 (autumn sowing that overwinters and ripens early in spring), and three winter crops, each with a catch: **kale** 16 / 22 / 2 / 0.15 (frost- and snow-proof, stands ripe for 24 days, but small and it hardly grows in the cold: sow it in the first half of autumn), **winter wheat** 40 / 88 / 6 / 0.1 (sown in autumn only, sleeps under the snow and ripens in late spring: the biggest grain harvest, but it ties up the plot for months, costs the most seed and comes in after the worst of the hungry gap), **dry beans** 32 / 40 / 4 / 0.25 (twice as slow as green beans, thirstier and tender, but the harvest keeps). Wheat, winter wheat and dry beans go into the *preserved* stock when harvested (threshed grain and dry beans keep a year and need no cellar room). Crop climates are registered with weather.js (`registerCrop`). *Planning by the climate:* farmers judge a crop by the usual weather for the time of year (`climateTable`: average growth, kill chance and food need per day of the year over 12 years of other seeds, never the real days ahead or the play RNG): `cropForecast` gives the days to ripen and the chance it lives that long, so tender crops are no longer sown into the first frosts; food that comes in during the hungry gap counts more (1.6× in its first half, 1.25× after), fresh food grown for the winter store counts what will be left of it by midwinter, and each plot already growing the same crop marks it down 15% (a mix beats one big bet). Nothing new is sown while the base cannot water what is in the ground, and seed comes out of the fresh food, then the preserved stock. Frost, hard freezes, blizzards, storms, heat and (on unwatered plots) drought can kill a standing crop (`cropDamage`); wet weather rots a ripe one, and a ripe crop not harvested within its window (3–14 days) is lost. Farmers harvest 3 ripe plots and sow 2 a day each (planting costs seed food, never the last two days' food), replanting by themselves with the best crop for the season, or what you picked for the plot (*Replant automatically* toggle). The Farming tab lists the plots (crop, growth bar, days to harvest, expected yield, weather effect and warnings, Plant / Harvest / Clear, next crop), the food and water outlook (food in store as fresh + preserved, the storage line, spoilage, the **winter outlook**) and a crop guide (with where the harvest goes and *Sow now*: days to ripen and the chance it survives if sown today). **Food storage** (`src/sim/storage.js`, replacing the old flat spoilage): every base has room for fresh food: the hub a small underground cellar (200), an outpost a pantry (40); a Root cellar adds 200 (up to 2), a Storehouse 150 (dry goods; raiders still steal half as much), a Cold store 150 (an ice house). Fresh food fills the best storage of the day first and spoils slowly there: cellar 0.5% a day (0.3% in the cold, more in heat), storehouse 0.8%, cold store 0.1–0.2% in winter, 0.5% in spring and autumn, 1.2% in summer. Food above the room is *stored outside* and spoils at 3% a day (×1.6 in heat, ×1.4 in rain, half in the cold, 0.3× frozen); the log warns when food starts spoiling outside (outposts after three days, as they ship their surplus daily). **Preserved food** (new supply key `preserved`: jarred, dried, canned, grain and dry beans; value 4, weight 0.6) needs no room and loses 0.1% a day; eating takes fresh food first and preserved only once the fresh is gone; teams can pack it as rations (lighter) and eat it after their packed food; it is a good trade good (groups price it like any supply, with a reference of 2 a head). It comes from the **Preserving kitchen** (18 scrap, 1 part; a workshop station: an operator jars 9 food + 3 water + 1 scrap + 4 empty jars into 8 preserved a day, 9 with Farming 8+), **Drying racks** (6 scrap, up to 3, no operator: 4 surplus food into 3 preserved a day, half on wet days, taking food stored outside first, else only food above 15 days of eating), dry crops, loot (supermarkets, groceries, ranches, cabins, lodges, truck stops) and trade. **Jars** (supply key `jars`, value 0.5, weight 0.4, kept out of the top bar) are found in kitchens, shops and farms (towns, farms, feed stores, diners, churches, groceries…, by the box); a jar holds 2 rations and 85% come back when jarred food is eaten at a base. The base panel shows *Food storage*: "in cellar X/Y, outside Z (spoiling N/day)" plus the preserved stock and empty / filled jars; the top bar's food tooltip and the Farming tab show the same. Water: everyone living at a base drinks by the season (`weather.js` `THIRST`: 1 a day in spring and autumn, 1.3 in summer, 0.9 in winter, at least 1.6 on a hot day, a heat wave or 30 °C and up; prisoners in the cells, guests you shelter and a supply convoy holding there too), crops are watered on dry days (a fifth more on a summer day, half again when hot), people first; in summer 5% a day of the water kept above the capacity evaporates. The Farming tab and the base panel say when everyone drinks more (*Summer: everyone drinks 30% more*), and from spring on the Farming tab has a **Summer water** line (`economy.js` `summerWater`: an average summer day with wells and springs at 90% and rain catchers at 0.7 a day, and a heat-wave stretch of up to 9 days at 1.6 a head with wells at 60% and no rain; the stock that stretch needs; what would close a gap: wells, water filters, rain catchers, cisterns). A week before summer every base whose summer water would not hold says so: a log line with the shortfall and what to build, and a *Summer water* card. The weather tooltip's forecast marks hot days (everyone drinks 60% more). The hub's well gives 12 a day, an outpost's spring 3, a base by a river or lake +3 (less in a dry spell, `wellFactor`); a Well building +6, a Rain catcher +2 per unit of rain (`rainWater`), a water filter +3 (a filter clogs after 20 days of use, but only the ones in use wear: one always, more while the tanks have room after the day's wells and rain or the worm's eggs need straining; the rest stand dry and keep. A clogged filter leaves its bucket at the base: *Re-packed water filter*, 1 scrap and 3 hours, makes it a filter again); the well and rain fill a base up to its capacity (hub 80, outpost 40, +150 per Cistern; water brought home is kept). A base that runs dry goes thirsty: everyone loses 10, 14, 18 … up to 25 health a day (scaled by how short it is; armor does not help) and nobody heals. Outposts keep a week of water. Picking around a base (Scavenge role) usually finds nothing: about a 7% chance a day of a small find (better with Scavenging and a crowbar, less as the surroundings get picked over: 0.4% less per scavenger a day down to 20%, and it never comes back); expeditions are where supplies come from. Fuel is scarce: gas stations, truck stops, the airport and military bases are the real sources (little elsewhere, in raid containers, events and other groups' stock); the hub starts with 12. Start stock: 80 food (was 60) plus 10 preserved and 12 jars, 50 water (was 40). **Animal pens** (`farming.js PEN`, building `animal_pen`, station `livestock`; 2026-10-09, the progression picks): hens and a few goats on a building plot (14 scrap, at most 2 a base); a keeper (the Operate role, Farming) gets 3 fresh food a day (eggs and milk; 4 with Farming 6+) for 1 water, in every season (× 0.75 in winter), half without walls standing at the base; each pen grazes 2 of the base's outdoor crop plots (`plotLimit`, `farmSlots`). Steady, winter-proof food for a guarded base against the crops' bigger seasonal harvests; the winter outlook counts the kept pens, and the water plan (`economy.js waterNeed`: wells, catchers, the governor's water planning) counts their water; the governor keeps a keeper on a pen until the water is all but gone (under 3 days). The AI groups get the same, coarse: a pen at a walled base with fields after the fields for everyone, +2 food a day (`groupworks.js PENS`, `penFood`).
- **Upkeep per head** (`src/sim/upkeep.js`): what a base uses up just by people living there is a rate per person × the people fed from its stores, and every base (the hub and each outpost) feeds its own people from its own stock. *Food:* everyone living there (at the hub also trade-route escorts waiting between trips) eats 1 a day × the cold (×1.1 on a day below 0 °C, ×1.2 below −8 °C) × a Quartermaster there (×0.9); prisoners in the cells eat 1 × the cold, guests you shelter 1 × the cold, a supply convoy holding there 1 × the cold each (all fresh food first, then preserved: guests and a holding convoy used to eat only the fresh food); the dog 0.5. *Water:* 1 each × the season's thirst (+1 for each case of dysentery), the same for prisoners, guests and convoy crew. People away from the base eat their team's packed supplies (one ration a person a day, more on the move under a load), not the base's; people on an errand eat neither. Short rations start when the food left covers under a week for everyone fed there (only the people living there ration). Days of food and water (the base panel, the Farming tab, the top bar's tooltips, the alerts, the recruit card) count everyone fed there; an outpost keeps 3 days of food for its heads + 6 and 7 water a head + 5 before shipping its surplus; the winter outlook counts the prisoners; the governor's food and water targets (days × heads) count prisoners, guests and convoy crew. The base panel and the Bases list have an *Upkeep* row ("8 fed here: food 8.5 · water 8 a day", with "6 people × 1.0 = 6.0 + 2 prisoners × 1.0 = 2.0 + the dog 0.5" under it and the rules in its tooltip), and the food and water lines read the same way. Drains that belong to a thing rather than a person stay with the thing: food spoils by what is stored and where, a generator burns 2 fuel a day it runs, a vehicle fuel by the hour it drives, wear goes by building and use, a relay mast 1 part every 10 days, meds by the treatment of someone sick or hurt, ammo per fight (3 a head per horde attack). *Heating* is per head too (below, Needs; firewood first, then fuel: Firewood). There is no passive light or comfort drain.
- **Needs** (`src/sim/needs.js`, bars in `src/ui/needs.js`): every survivor has a **Hunger**, a **Thirst** and a **Mood** gauge (0–100), shown as small bars on the People tab, the roster (a *Needs* column), the team panel and the survivor screen. *Falling:* hunger 40 a day at one ration a day (their load band's ration multiplier on the move; ×1.1 / ×1.2 in the cold for a team without a lit camp fire; below 50 the body gets by on 60%), thirst 60 a day at one water a day × the day's thirst (summer, a heat wave) — a full thirst gauge lasts under two days, hunger about three. *Eating:* out of a base people eat and drink from the **group's inventory**: the team's packed supplies and what it found (fresh game first, packed food, packed preserved, then food from the cargo — what foraging, hunting and looting bring in; water packed then found: no free water). A ration fills 40 hunger, a water 60 thirst, so one of each a day keeps someone level and the trip estimates hold (the planners count the cold too: every hour of the trip at its own day's appetite from the fixed weather ahead, the hours camped by a lit fire, or at the staging camp's, without it, so a winter trip packs 10–20% more food; the team panel's and the plan's "days of food" use today's cold). The team's *Rations* (team panel): **Full** (eat below 70, up to full), **Short** (eat only below 40: about 60% of a ration a day once lean; food lasts longer, people are Hungry) or **None** (keep the food; water is never rationed). *When short:* **the hurt first** (default: the hurt eat and drink first, then the hungriest) or **evenly**. At a base the stores feed everyone once a day (upkeep per head, unchanged); the gauges follow: fed in full they hold and recover 3 an hour, on short rations they sink to the lean level and hold (under rationing the hurt eat first, then the workers, then those resting), with no food or water they fall (the base's hunger and thirst days do the damage). *Effects:* hunger below 50 cuts work (×0.8 at 25, ×0.6 empty) and aim (up to −10), thirst more (×0.75 at 25, ×0.5 empty; aim up to −15); away from a base an empty hunger gauge costs 0.6 health an hour and an empty thirst gauge 1.0, and below 25 both cost mood; the team counts as out of food (no camp healing, illness) when someone's gauge is empty or someone wants to eat and nothing is left. Log lines and alerts: "Ana is starving (hunger 12)", "Sam: parched (thirst 9)". *Starving day after day* (`STARVE`, `starvingDaily`, once a day before the mood factors and the breaks): each morning someone is starving (living at a base that is out of food that day; away from a base, on a team or an errand, hunger below 15) counts a day starving (`s.starveDays`) and costs 2 mood × those days (−2, −4, −6 … up to −20 a day); parched the same for thirst (a base out of water, or thirst below 15 away from one: `s.parchDays`, −3, −6, −9 … up to −24). At a base the stores decide, not the gauge: short rations feed some people in full and others not at all for the day, and that stays the base's −3 a day for short rations. Anywhere (a base, a team, an errand; not captives), on top of the hourly mood below 25; it replaced a base's flat −10 a day out of food and −8 out of water (gentler for the first days, harsher after: −56 over a week without food against −70 before, −110 over ten days against −100). A morning fed and watered again resets it. It shows in the mood breakdown ("Starving: day 3", naming the base when its stores are empty) and logs once on day 3. Left alone it ends at rock bottom: a mental break, walking out, or the empty gauge kills them. *Mood items:* every evening (20:00) each team member below 35 mood uses a treat from the team's inventory (their own pockets and pack, then a teammate's, then the cargo), the gentlest first: chocolate, hard candy, instant coffee, cigarettes, rolling tobacco; wine, vodka or whiskey only while the team rests (a hangover follows); valuables (candy, tobacco, wine, whiskey) only below 20 mood. Keepsakes (cards, a harmonica, a guitar, family photos) are never used up. The survivor screen and each person on the team panel list the treats they can reach with a button to use one by hand. *Errands:* someone leaving on a personal errand takes about 1 food and 1 water a day of it from the base, eats on the way and brings back what is left. *Heating:* on a day below 0 °C (the day's mean) a base needs 0.1 fuel's worth of heat a person present a day (×1.5 below −8 °C): it burns **firewood first** (1 firewood heats as much as 0.2 fuel, so 0.5 firewood a head), then fuel for what the wood does not cover (see Firewood): the Upkeep row adds "Heating: 6 people × 0.1 = 0.6 → 3.0 firewood a day (or 0.6 fuel)". An outpost short of it gets firewood, then fuel, from the hub over its supply line (as its food). Without either everyone there is cold: mood −4 and health −2 a day (× how short it was), illness as if outdoors, an alert. A shelter (tier 0) with its camp fire lit is warmed by the fire and burns no fuel (the fire burns deadfall from around it; on bare ground firewood, see Firewood); a team in the field burns none (camped at a lit fire it does not eat more in the cold). The autopilot cuts the winter's firewood from late summer (Firewood); `heatFuelWant` asks for fuel only for what the wood in store will not cover, makes no fuel runs before the winter while the base can still cut its wood in time, and from the second half of autumn stocks the rest (fuel runs to gas stations and truck stops, trades, no fuel traded away in the cold months) and keeps it from its cars and the generator.
- **Hunting, fishing and snares** (`src/sim/hunting.js`): food from the land. *Hunt* (a team order on any land tile, 2–24 hours; right-click *Hunting trip* or the planner's *Hunting trip* preset: go, hunt 8 hours, come home): every hour rolls for big game (a deer 30 food, a wild hog 16) and small game (2 food), by the land (forest 1, hills 0.8, farmland 0.55, mountains 0.5, suburbs 0.12, a city 0.03; × 1.1 by water, × 0.8 on a site, less with infection, hive land × 0.15, next to a base × 0.6), the season (autumn 1.25, winter 0.95 and × 1.2 with snow on the ground for tracks, summer 0.9, spring 0.8), dawn and dusk (× 1.3; night × 0.35), the weather and the team (its three best hunters count 1, ½ and ¼: weapon × skill (0.5 Shooting + 0.35 Scouting + 0.15 Stealth) × the Hunter perk; hunting rifle 1 for deer, bow or crossbow 0.85 and silent, hunting with arrows (each shot at game loses its arrow half the time: about 0.6 arrows a kill; in a fight they are picked up again), shotgun 0.45 for deer but 1.2 for small game, pistol 0.3; melee nothing: set snares). Two decent hunters with a rifle and a pistol bag about 23 food in 8 hours of autumn forest, about 13 on farmland, 15 in spring. Every gunshot uses 1 ammo and adds 6 Heat (fieldheat.js; a hunt itself builds Heat at 1.2) and turns each wandering horde within 3 tiles toward the shots 25% of the time; bows add none. **Overhunting:** each tile has a game level that every kill lowers (a deer −0.3) and that grows back with a half-life of 20 days. **Carcasses** weigh 1 kg a ration (packed food 0.5): a deer on foot moves a pair of hunters into a heavier band, a car carries it at no cost to the riders, bike racks take part of it; what does not fit is left where it fell. **Fresh game spoils:** in the field 35% a day above 24 °C, 22% above 12 °C, 12% above 4 °C, 6% around freezing, 2% in a freeze; a team eats its fresh game before its packed food; at a base it is eaten first, the drying racks take it first, and on top of the stores' rate it loses 5% a day (10% in the heat, 2% in the cold, almost nothing frozen; × 0.4 with a cold store, × 0.6 with a hunting cabin). **Snares** (team order, 2 hours: a coil of snare wire, which stays with the line and comes back when it is lifted, or 2 scrap of fence wire): a line of 8 catches about a rabbit a day in good country; check it (1 hour) within 2–3 days: caught game rots after 3 days (2 in the heat, 6 in the cold), and from the third day unchecked an 8% chance a day (more by towns, in hive land, with a horde near) that the catch is stolen and 30% of the time the line with it; lines left 30 days are gone. **Fish** (team order on a river bank, with a fishing rod or a net carried; the planner's *Fishing trip* takes the hub's along): 0.6 food an hour a rod, 1.2 a net worked by two, × the water (a deep river bend 1, a river 0.85, a pond 0.6), the season (spring 1.15 … winter 0.75), dawn and dusk 1.4, Scouting and Stealth; a frozen river needs a crowbar, machete or axe to cut the ice and gives half; a fished-out spot comes back in about 10 days. New items: fishing rod, fishing net, snare wire (cabins, lodges, campgrounds, ranger stations, hardware and feed stores, farms). **Hunting cabin** (12 scrap, any base with game land within 2 tiles): the people on the *Hunt* job (or role; no fixed cap: as many as the food the land must bring in wants, the day's eating and the stores refilled less the plots, each on the best ground left) hunt the land within 2 tiles 3 hours a day at dawn and dusk by the base's hunting mix with their own weapons, the base's ammo (never the walls' last 3 a head + 10) and its arrows (snares without a weapon), with the same rolls and depletion: two hunters in autumn forest bring in about 11 food a day; gunshots add the gun's noise / 3 a kill to the base's Heat (hordes.js baseHeat) and each wandering horde within 3 tiles of a hunting ground with gunfire that day turns toward it 25% of the time; the cabin smokes up to 6 fresh game a day into 5 preserved. The *Fish* job: a base on a river with a rod or net in its armory, one fisher each. Work types *Hunt* and *Fish* (work.js), the default *Hunter* role and *Hunting party* crew, and crews' *Hunt* and *Fish* trips (crews.js). The tile panel shows *Game: plenty / some / scarce* with the expected haul of an 8-hour hunt (the team there, or the hub's two best hunters), *Fish* by the water and your snares; the team panel's *Land* block has Hunt 4/8/12h, Fish 4/8h and Set / Check / Lift snares; the planner shows the expected haul of each step and counts it on the way home (its weight sets the pace, as the real team is weighed again when it bags something). The autopilot hunts (and fishes, and sets or checks snares on the way) when the winter outlook falls short, food is under its target, or in autumn to stock up, builds a hunting cabin where the land is good and puts its best shots on the Hunt job when food is not plentiful.
- **The hunting mix and arrows** (`src/sim/hunting.js`; the base's Farming tab, *Hunting*): each base has a setting for how it hunts, the share of the hunting hours with **traps**, **bows** and **guns** in steps of 10% (`base.huntMix`, saved with the base; the default is all guns, every hunter's best weapon, as before), with presets *Guns* (0/0/100), *Bows and guns* (0/50/50), *Quiet* (40/60/0) and *Traps only* (100/0/0). The Hunt job's hunters at the hunting cabin and the teams that hunt from the base (Hunt orders, Hunting trips, crews and the governor's trips) follow it. *Guns:* each hunter's best weapon (a rifle first; a bow if that is all they have): 1 ammo a shot (a second after a miss 40% of the time for big game, 20% for small), Heat (a team +6 a shot × the gun's noise / 3; a base the gun's noise / 3 a kill) and the dead within 3 tiles turn toward the shots 25% of the time. *Bows:* bows and crossbows only, with arrows: silent, no Heat, nearly a rifle's food (a compound bow 0.86 for deer, 0.9 for small game; at the cabin, where the land runs down, about the same as rifles); each shot loses its arrow 50% of the time (about 0.6 arrows a kill). *Traps:* snares and deadfalls worked in those hours, small game only, silent, no ammo or arrows: at a base a trap hour is a third of a day on two snare lines (as the weaponless hunter's work), about half a rifle's food (three hunters through 20 autumn days: 116 food against 246 with rifles); on a team's hunt the lines soak for hours, not a day: half of that, + a quarter a hand past the first (up to two), about a third of a rifle hunt (a rifle and a bow, 8 hours of autumn forest: 7 food against 26). *Fallbacks, never louder than the mix:* a gun hour without a gun or ammo (above the walls' keep at a base) goes to a bow, then to the traps; a bow hour without a bow or arrows to the traps. A team's hours go in turn (the method furthest behind its share next: 50/50 alternates); the cabin deals its hunters' hours out each day (gun hours to those with guns, bow hours to those with bows, the rest on the snare lines, the fraction of an hour carried to the next day). The team panel's Hunt buttons and the tile panel give the expected shots and arrows; a hunt's report gives the hours by method, the traps' catch and the arrows lost; the Hunting card gives yesterday's hunt (food, hours, shots and Heat, arrows, the traps' catch, hordes drawn). The governor sets the mix every day: at a base with a hunting cabin, quiet with wandering dead within 3 tiles or the dead at its gates (bows and traps; half traps and half guns without a bow and 4+ arrows), unless its stores are down to 5 days of food (a base without a cabin hunts only with teams, away from its walls, and keeps its mix); bows only while the ammo above the walls' keep is under 30, bows and guns half and half with bows and arrows, else guns; it crafts arrows (a dozen a bow and a dozen more, with 150+ firewood). **Arrows** (`data.js` SUPPLIES, 0.03 kg, the bows' trade category): crafted 12 at a time from 30 firewood and 1 scrap (Mechanics 1, any base or in the field), found at hunting lodges, gun stores, malls, campgrounds, cabins, ranger stations and farms (`loot.js` `SITE_ARROWS`, `AREA_ARROWS`, their own dice), and packed in the planner; a hunting trip takes up to 12 of the base's arrows along when someone on it has a bow (`plans.js` applyPlan); a hardcore start's bow comes with 12. Groups make their own and keep them (they buy arrows, they do not sell them). **Bows use arrows in fights too** (`world.js` `ARROWS`, `usesArrows`, `armedFactor`, `spendShots`; `combat.js` `WEAPONS[k].arrows`, `arrowsLeft`, `hasShot`): guns use ammo, bows and crossbows (the scrap crossbow, the compound bow, the hunting crossbow) an arrow a shot, never ammo, from the same pools as the ammo (the team's packed and found supplies; a base's stock when it is defended). *On the battle map* a bow with no arrows cannot shoot (Shoot, Overwatch and Double tap say "Out of arrows"): its holder fights in melee, as with a gun out of ammo; the battle panel shows *Arrows* beside *Ammo*, the result the arrows shot and picked up. *After a won fight* about half the arrows shot are picked up again (`ARROWS.BACK` 0.5, rounded: 1 of 1, 2 of 3), into the team's packed supplies or the base's stock; after a loss or a retreat none (a raid: only when someone got out and nothing aware is left standing). *By the numbers* (`autoResolveCombat`, `breakoff.js` `fightByNumbers`, the dead found on a search, the road cards, the killzone, convoys, raids and hordes at your bases, the odds the cards show): up to 6 shots a head as before, the archers' in arrows, everyone else's (melee and unarmed too) in ammo; under 3 a head the guns, or the bows, count 60% (`armedFactor`, power-weighted: with no bow it is exactly the old ammo rule); rounds not fired when a fight is broken off are given back, arrows too. *Packing:* the planner's Auto-fill packs a dozen arrows for each bow on the team (`ARROWS.PACK` 12; ammo stays 15 a head), as do the governor's trips, the standing crews and a help squad; a base defends with its own stock. The governor crafts arrows first thing when its bows have under 6 each (100+ firewood), and up to a dozen a bow and a dozen more as before. *Other groups and wanderers* stay abstract: their fighters on the map never carry bows (`KITS`) and spend neither ammo nor arrows (only your people do), and a group's strength does not count its stock; the arrows a group keeps are a trade good. Also fixed: what a hunter carries in a pack (a bow, a fishing rod, snare wire) counts again (the pack's slots were read wrongly).
- **Foraging** (`src/sim/foraging.js`): wild food anyone can gather: berries, nuts, mushrooms, wild greens, roots, apples from abandoned orchards, now and then a handful of medicinal herbs (1 meds) or a wild hive's honey (2 preserved food). Three ways: the team order *Forage* (any land tile, 2–24 hours like *Hunt*: team panel *Land* 4 / 8 / 12h, right-click *Go and forage here*, a *Forage* plan step with the expected haul), a team in **camp** short of food (under a day of it: its fit people, health 50+, forage around the camp in daylight, 40% of each hour; breaking camp says what they gathered) and the base job **Forage** (work type and role: no fixed cap, as many as the food gap wants (each picks the best ground left, so a crowd strips the land faster), gather within 2 tiles of the base 6 hours a day while food is under 25 days; through `hunting.js huntingDaily`). Rations a forager-hour = 0.3 × the land (forest 1, farmland 0.8, hills 0.6, mountains 0.35, suburbs 0.3: overgrown gardens, a city 0.08; × 1.15 by water, × 1.3 on a farm, ranch or barn: old orchards; × 1.1 at a cabin, lodge or campground; less with infection, hive land × 0.5, next to a hive × 0.25) × what is left of it × the season (spring 0.35 → 0.75: greens, then the first berries; summer 0.95 → 1.1; autumn 1.25 → 0.8: nuts, apples, mushrooms, until the frosts; winter 0.12, half under snow) × the hour (daylight 1, dawn and dusk 0.6, night 0.1; a storm 0.5) × the forager (0.6 + 0.05 × (0.55 Farming + 0.45 Scouting), × 1.25 with the Forager perk, half when badly hurt, less when ill; on one tile the next hands count 0.9, 0.8 … at least 0.4). An average forager (Farming and Scouting 4) per daylight hour on untouched land: forest 0.12 / 0.23 / 0.23 / 0.03 (spring / summer / autumn / winter), farmland 0.10 / 0.18 / 0.19 / 0.02, hills 0.07 / 0.14 / 0.14 / 0.02, suburbs 0.03 / 0.05 / 0.05 / 0.01: a summer day's order is about 2 food a forager, three foragers 4–8. Each tile is picked down (a forest tile gives about 40 rations from full) and grows back with a half-life of 10 days (most of it back within a season, unlike a site's loot); the base job takes the best tiles around first, about 0.8 food a forager a day over a summer. Foraging is time on one spot: Heat builds like any work there (fieldheat.js, × 1), and a bad mushroom (summer and autumn, 0.25% a forager-hour, fewer for the skilled) gives a mild dysentery. Foraged food is plain fresh food (0.5 kg): at a base the drying racks and the kitchen preserve it. **Low food:** an alert under 3 days at a base says where (*food for 1 day: hunt or forage nearby: forest to the east*), a team in the field under 1.5 days gets its own alert with the best food from the land within 2 tiles, the team panel under 2 days lists forage / hunt / fish here and nearby with the expected food and a *Go* button, and hardcore's road team (alert, hub card *Food nearby*) the same. The autopilot puts one or two foragers out first when a base is under 4 days of food (before the farm crew: the plots take days, the berries are there now) and the spare hands under 12 days (`planForage`), and a team short of food (the road team under a day or out of food, a trip without enough for the way home) forages, hunts or fishes here or within 2 tiles, whichever brings the most food an hour with the walk (`landFoodAct`).
- **Firewood** (`src/sim/woodcutting.js`): the winter's heating, cut from the land. A new supply, *Firewood* (3 kg a unit, cheap; stored at bases with no limit, carried by teams, traded: groups cut and burn their own, so it is dear in winter (×1.5, ×1.35 when winter is coming) and cheap in summer (×0.7); fortresses, communes, zealots and travellers prize it, raiders, merchants and the military care little). A base burns it before fuel (1 firewood = 0.2 fuel of heat: 0.5 a head a freezing day), so a winter (about 30 freezing days) takes about 15–17 firewood a head; fuel is kept for the vehicles and the generator. *The land* (per tile, `state.wood`): forest 1, hills 0.6, mountains 0.35 (scrub), farmland 0.25 (hedgerows, windbreaks, fence rails), suburbs 0.2 (fallen trees, fences, furniture), a city 0.05, water nothing; × 1.1 by water, cabins, lodges, campgrounds and ranger stations × 1.3 (woodpiles), farms × 1.2, less with infection, hive land × 0.5 (its ring × 0.25). Each firewood taken thins the tile (a full forest tile gives about 250); it grows back with a half-life of 60 days (a quarter within a season, most within a year). *Yield:* 0.6 firewood an hour × the land × what is left × the woodcutter (0.7 + 0.05 × (0.7 Farming + 0.3 Mechanics); badly hurt half) × their tool (a fire axe ×1.6, a sledgehammer and wedges ×1.3, a machete ×1.25, a saw-blade machete ×1.2, bare hands ×1: deadfall and branches) × daylight only (dawn and dusk half, nothing at night) × winter 0.85 (× 0.75 more in deep snow) × a storm 0.4; on one tile the second counts 0.9, the third 0.8. An average woodcutter with a fire axe brings in about 0.8 an hour in forest (about 5 a day on the base job), 0.5 on hills, 0.2 on farmland, 0.12 in the suburbs; bare-handed about 60% of that. *Two ways:* the base job **Cut wood** (Work tab column *Wood*, role *Cut wood*; within 2 tiles of the base 6 hours a day, each on the best tile left, no fixed cap: as many as it takes to have the pile in within three weeks, each one past the first 5% less (at least half), open while the woodpile is short of what the coming cold needs (urgent in the winter or with it two weeks off), and only where a woodcutter would bring in 0.4 a day; their own axe or one from the armory) and the team order **Cut wood** (any land tile, 2–24 hours like *Forage*: team panel *Land* 4 / 8h with the expected yield as far as the team can carry it, right-click *Go and cut wood here*, a *Cut wood* plan step with the expected firewood; a team's spare axes in the cargo are lent to those without). *Risks:* chopping is loud: a team order builds field Heat ×1.3 (the axes carry), and at a base each woodcutter adds to its Heat (0.8 with an axe, 0.3 by hand); axes slip: a team rolls 0.15% a person-hour for a deep cut or a sprain (half without a tool), the base job is a work accident risk like building (1.2% a day). *Break down for firewood:* anything made of wood, paper or cloth (GEAR `burn`: the spiked and nail bats 1.5, the siege ladder 3, a guitar 2, the skill books 0.6, novels, comics, magazines and crossword books 0.3–0.4, rag boots and the magazine arm guards 0.5, the ghillie wrap 1, the cloth bags 0.3–0.8, toilet paper 0.3, candles 0.5; metal and plastic things nothing) in an armory, a team's cargo or a pocket or pack: the inventory's *Break down for firewood (+N)* (a second click for anything worth more than junk), and the armory's *Break down junk for firewood (+N)* for all its unmarked junk. No time: at a base it goes into the stores, on a team into its cargo, where the camp fire burns it like any firewood carried. The armory's items also carry the boiler's mark (*Boiler may burn it* / *Keep from the fire*). *Found:* woodpiles and log baskets turn up when searching farms, barns, ranches, cabins, lodges, campgrounds, ranger stations, hardware and feed stores, towns, churches and motels (`loot.js` `SITE_WOOD`, 1–6 a find, a roll each search hour × the loot left, its own dice), and in raid and building containers (`AREA_WOOD`: houses' living rooms, farmhouses, barns, hunting cabins, campsites, garages, workshops, chapels; wooden cabinets, shelves and counters twice as often), 1–5 a find. *Fires:* a camp fire burns deadfall picked up around it for free where there is any (wood 0.15+ on the tile or next to it); on bare ground (a city centre) a shelter's lit fire burns 0.6 firewood a day from its store (none: it does not warm the shelter) and a team's camp fire 0.3 a day of what it carries (none: it goes out). *Panels:* the tile panel's *Wood* row (the land, what is left, ~firewood in 8h for the team or the hub's best and their axes), the top bar's *Firewood* (days of heating left in the cold, else what is in store against the coming cold's need; its tooltip says how much a woodcutter brings in here), the Upkeep row's *Heating* (people × 0.1 → firewood a day), and an alert from autumn when the winter is coming (or here) with less firewood and fuel in store than the cold ahead needs (firewood N of M, with what a woodcutter brings in here or, on bare land, to send a team). *The autopilot:* from late summer a base short of the coming cold's firewood (everyone fed there and the teams coming home × 0.2 × the freezing days ahead × 1.05 for the bitter days, a fifth to spare) works out how many woodcutters it takes to have it in by the winter: from autumn that many go before the builders and stations (at most the cap and a quarter of the people), in late summer one, and its spare hands cut wood until it is stocked; when the land around the hub gives little (under 2.5 a woodcutter a day) and the winter is within a month it sends a team of 2–3 with the armory's axes (and the car: firewood is bulky) to the best wood land within reach for 8 hours of daylight cutting (it stays the night there when it arrives late, rather than cutting in the dark), scored by the share of the shortfall a trip covers; when the Cut wood job at its cap cannot have the pile in by the cold (a big hub on farmland: three cutters bring in 2 a day against 100+ wanted) the trips are the only way to stock it, so they score above ordinary scavenging by the share of the shortfall the job will leave (`WOOD_TRIP`) and start with the stocking season; it buys firewood only when the cold is near and the pile under 30% of the need, and never sells it; outpost sites score a little for the wood land around them. Crews have a *Cut wood* field job (the same trips). Old saves start with no firewood and the work roles get *Cut wood* at their *Forage* priority.
- **Hand crafting and makeshift gear:** the base's Crafting tab has a craft queue like construction: materials are paid when queued, people on the Craft role put in work-hours (1 + 3% per skill point each) on the first job they are skilled enough for, and finished gear lands in the armory (equip it in the Armory tab). No station needed; sturdier recipes (scrap crossbow, pipe shotgun, scrap plate vest) need a workshop (ammo bench or machine shop) at that base. Makeshift weapons (spiked bat, pipe spear, saw-blade machete, silent crossbow (bolts: arrows), pipe shotgun), armor (magazine arm guards, duct-tape shin guards, tire-rubber vest, scrap plate vest; football pads and the football helmet are found, not made) and tools (bandage and splint kit: +2 Medicine when treating, at a base, in the field or in battle; crowbar and lockpicks: +2 Scavenging at a base; charcoal water filter: +3 water a day at its base; improvised backpack; rag earplugs, `ragplugs`: a scrap, an hour, no skill, in the field too, makeshift ear protection that works from a pocket under any helmet like foam earplugs (`hearing.js` isPlugsKey / settleEars: 2 off a bang's loudness where foam takes 3, −3 tiles of hearing, a pair a fight for about 4 fights, foam used first when both are carried, never mended; `tools/heartest.js` section 12)) are weaker than manufactured gear and wear out: each fight, treatment or day of work uses one point of durability and the item breaks at 0. Boiled-rag dressings make meds by hand. Condition comes from the best crafter's skill (+10% for workshop recipes). A few makeshift items show up as loot (houses, garages, campsites, factories, towns, raiders). Teams can make the simplest recipes in the field (`fieldCraftOptions` / `fieldCraft` in `crafting.js`).
- **Production orders (`src/sim/production.js`, controls in `src/ui/production.js`):** every workshop station (Workshops tab, *Order* column) and the drying racks take a standing order: *Forever* (the default, as before), *Until stock reaches N* (stops once the base holds N of its product, starts again when it falls below N − 10%, at least 1, so the operator does not flip every day), *Keep at least N of an input* (pick the input: the station stops when a day's work would take it below N) or *Off*. While an order holds a station its operator idles there, or does the fallback picked on the order (*When it stops*: farm, scavenge, build, guard, craft, rest, medic, or run another free station) and comes back by itself when the order wants work again; pinned people idle. An idle station makes no workshop Heat. The fallback is a hand rule: where the autopilot or a steward (Roles) sets roles, it staffs only the stations whose orders want work. **Standing craft orders** (Crafting tab): *keep N of an item in the armory, at a minimum quality*; every hour an order short of N queues one job (materials paid then; one at a time per order). Quality: only items at the minimum or better count, a job is only queued when someone at the base would make it at the minimum (expected condition from skill, `craftQuality`), and the job only moves while a crafter on the Craft role reaches it, so a low-skill crafter never makes items that would not count. Supplies (dressings) count the stock.
- **Power (`src/sim/power.js`):** a **Generator** (20 scrap, 4 parts, 30 h, Mechanics 3+ to build, up to 2) runs by itself, no operator (machines-2 2026-10-10, the user's pick: "generators do not need operators just people to repair and fuel it"): 6 power for 2 fuel a day of running, only while something draws power (as many generators run as the demand needs), burned by the hour (1/12 an hour) from its own tank of 3 fuel (a day and a half's running). Every morning from 06:00 the least busy able hand at the base (not in a bed, not resting sick) refuels it from the fuel above the base's reserve (the Power card's *Keep N fuel*; the governor and stewards keep their fuel floor): half an hour of their day. With nobody able at the base it runs its tank dry: a day and a half of power at most. A running generator adds 4 Heat to the base; a breakdown needs a Mechanics 3+ hand and machine parts. Powered (need, served in this order while the supply lasts; each, the generators too, can be switched off in the Workshops tab's Power card): Walk-in freezer 2, Radio station 1 (+2 tiles of coverage), Floodlights 1 (no fuel burned when a horde attacks), Machine shop 2 (2 parts a day from 9 scrap instead of 1 from 6), Preserving room 1 (half as much again: 14 food + 5 water + 2 scrap + 6 jars → 12 preserved), Vacuum sealer 1. The cold store is an ice house (machines-2: no power). Powered stations draw only while their operator works them. The **Ethanol still** (15 scrap, 2 parts, 24 h) is a station too: an operator turns 8 food + 2 water into 3 fuel a day, up to 4 of the food being yesterday's spoiled food (the day's spoilage is kept as mash); give it a Keep-at-least-N-food order. The Workshops tab's Power card and the Buildings tab show supply / demand, generators running, fuel a day and Heat. The governor builds a still (hub of 9+ with surplus food) and a generator (hub of 10+ with a still or 40 fuel, and a machine shop, a freezer, or the Refrigeration Schematics and 15 days of food), runs the still on the surplus only and fills the generators' tanks only above its fuel floor (10 and the winter's heating, Cautious: its fuel reserve).
- **Stewards (`src/sim/steward.js`, Overview tab):** *Hand to a steward* gives one base to the governor's logic (`autopilot.js` `governBase` / `governRoles`, the same per-base code the autopilot runs every hour) in a style you pick (Cautious / Balanced / Aggressive), for the areas you tick (all on by default): *Roles*, *Building*, *Crafting & production orders* (armory gear, crafts, the stations' orders: your own orders stand), *Medical* (treatment with base meds, medics, the meds reserve), *Farming & food* (farmers, plots, stores, water) and *Defence* (guards, lookout, walls and defences). With an area off, the steward leaves its roles as they are (farm = Farming & food, medic = Medical, guard = Defence, operate and craft = Crafting & production, build = Building) and does not build its buildings. **Pin** someone (Overview people list) and no steward (nor the autopilot) changes their role, station or gear. You keep the teams, trips, cards and the other bases. Each steward writes one 🧭 log line a day at 20:00 (role changes, buildings and crafts queued, treatments, gear handed out, orders set, the roles now). *Take over* switches it off and nothing is undone. While the dev autopilot is on it runs the whole holdout and stewards wait.
- **Inventory (paper doll):** every survivor has Hands (main weapon), Off hand (a tool in use: med kit, radio, goggles, crowbar), Head, Body, Arms, Legs and Backpack slots, 4 pockets for small things (knives, pistols, kits, radios, goggles) and, with a pack on, pack slots (sling bag 3, school backpack 6, assault pack 8, hiking pack 10, frame rucksack 14; packs also add to the wearer's max load, +3 / +6 / +10 / +14 / +18 kg, and the assault pack, hiking pack and rucksack make their load count 12% / 8% / 20% lighter; a hiking pack's clinking buckles add 5% to a search's Heat). Armor from every worn piece adds up (cap 5.5; plate carrier alone is 4) and scales with the piece's condition (at 60% it gives 80% of its armor): Kevlar vest or plate on the body, motorcycle / riot / football helmet on the head, magazine arm guards, knee guards or duct-tape shin guards. Items in pockets and packs do nothing until moved to a hand. Everything weighs what it weighs wherever it is (on a person or in the team's cargo; *Weight and load*): pockets and pack slots are where things are kept, weight is the limit. Bulky things (a bicycle) only go in storage. Supplies (food, ammo, meds, scrap…) stay pooled per team or base, not per person. Open it from the survivor screen (People tab), the base Armory tab, or a member card in the team panel: click an item then a slot (or drag it), click it twice to send it across, a full slot swaps. At a base items move between the people there and the armory; on a team between teammates and the cargo. `s.gear.weapon/armor/tool` keep their meaning, so combat, medical and crafting read them as before (`src/sim/inventory.js`, `src/ui/inventory.js`).
- **Searching by hand in a raid** (`src/sim/raidloot.js`, `src/sim/search.js`, `ui/inventory.js` search window): Search (Shift+S, or click a container next to you) costs 1 action, by day or in the dark, and opens a window with the survivor (hands, off hand, armor, pockets, pack and what they carry loose, with their kg against their max load) beside the container (supplies and items, each with its weight). Drag what to take; click a thing twice to send it across; Take all. Any part of a supply stack can be taken, 1 to all: click the stack and the bar below picks how many (1, −, type it, +, ½, All, What fits, with how many fit where it would go), then click where they go; Shift+click sends all that fit straight across; drag moves all that fit (Shift on the drop: half, Ctrl: one). Only what fits moves, and hauling time is paid on what moved. **Quick search** (Shift+Q, or the panel button): a rummage that costs no action (the survivor needs one left, once a turn), so it fits between two moves; only 30–50% of what is inside turns up (rolled each time, whole things: an item or a supply stack) and the rest stays hidden until someone searches it in full (1 action: Shift+S, or *Search it in full* in its window). It is loud: Heat +5, and the unaware dead and guards within 7 tiles walk over to look (`stealth.js` noiseAt; indoors each wall or closed door between counts 3 tiles more; 2 tiles further, 9, fumbling in the dark without a light). Hands going fast can slip: a 6% chance a rummage of cutting a hand on broken glass, a rusty can or a needle (2–5 health, never down, and a cut on the survivor that needs dressing with meds and can get infected untreated, `health.js`); less with Scavenging or Stealth (the better of the two: −2.5% of the chance a point, half at 20), 3 points more in the dark without a light, never 10%. The Quick search button's tooltip, the line in the raid panel (*Quick search: shows 30–50%, loud, 6% chance of cutting yourself*, when someone stands next to something unopened) and a container's hover show the survivor's own chance. A rummaged container has a blue … marker on the map (both views) and its window says a full search may turn up more; clicking it opens what turned up for free. A locked container opens first (key, 1 action a pick attempt at the odds shown, force), then searching it is 1 action. The raid bot searches in full, and rummages when pressed (Heat 40+, an aware enemy within 6 tiles, on its way out), grabbing only what is free to haul. A raid saved mid-search keeps what is hidden. Each survivor carries their own finds. Hauling takes time: each turn the first 8 kg out of a container or the vehicle (or into it) are free, then every 12 kg costs one of the searcher's actions (one heavy thing can always be lifted with what is left of the turn; putting things back is free). Teammates standing next to the searcher (a downed one too) show as tabs: drag things between them to hand them over, free, within each one's max load. Someone who bleeds out drops what they carried where they fall, a pile the others can search; whatever is still on the map at the end is lost. A weapon, armor, flashlight, night vision, scope, suppressor or med kit put on mid-raid counts at once. With a vehicle, walk to it and press N (or click it) to open its trunk: put what you carry in it to free your hands, or take things out (the team's packed supplies and cargo ride in it). On the State Map the team's cargo box shows the vehicle's name.
- **Looting bodies:** after a won battle, dead enemies carry what they fought with (in worn condition), their armor, ammo, sometimes food, meds, scrap, a pack or a spare piece of kit; zombies now and then have something in a pocket; your fallen keep their own things. The result screen offers *Loot the bodies* (bodies list, each body's paper doll next to your survivor and the team's cargo; take items one by one, supply stacks with a click (or 1 / ½ of one with the small buttons under it), *Take all* per body or *Take everything*, limited by carry space), *Take all and continue* (Enter) or *Leave the rest and continue*. Whatever is left stays where they fell: a death site on that tile that scavengers strip over the days (a recovery mission can fetch it); your fallen's things are at their death site anyway, and taking them from the body takes them off the site. Base defenses put everything in the armory. Headless and auto-combat battles take everything that fits straight away. After a fight on the map the after-fight card adds nothing of its own: it lists what was taken from the bodies. A fight resolved by the numbers has no bodies, so its haul is rolled on the card, and a bonus item only comes along if there is carry space. A person's body has one small thing in its pockets one time in five (`combat.js` `KITS` finds: raiders cigarettes, liquor and gold, soldiers chocolate, coffee and smoke grenades, zealots candles…); about 8% of the dead wear a sling bag or a school backpack with 1–3 things inside.
- **Loot that fits the place** (`src/sim/loot.js`; check with `tools/loottest.js`): every site type has its equipment list (`data.js` LOCATIONS `gear`, the search's gear roll) and a table of small finds (`SITE_FINDS`: valuables, comforts, smokes and drink, throwables; its own dice, about one find in ten search hours at full loot); every raid area has its gear chances (`combat.js` AREAS) and small finds per container (`AREA_FINDS`). Theme tags keep them sensible: a hunting lodge has rifles, bows, crossbows, camo netting, ghillie wraps, bear traps, game calls and scent masks; a police station batons, riot helmets and guards, pistols, revolvers, handcuffs, smoke grenades and a police carbine; a hardware store crowbars, sledgehammers, fire axes, barbed wire, bear traps and sandbags; a school bats, sports gear, bags and books; houses and apartments kitchen knives, jewelry, keepsakes, gadgets and comforts; gas stations and truck stops cigarettes, lighters, sweets and road flares; malls jewelry, electronics, bows and hiking packs; the military rucksacks, camo netting, sandbags, wire and carbines. Odd ones went (no military radio in a university or a news helicopter, no school bag in an office, no Kevlar at a car crash).
  - *Weapons, each with a role* (battle numbers in `combat.js` WEAPONS; `combattest.js … weapons`): baseball bat (a club: takedowns +20), nail bat (crafted from a bat and 1 scrap), police baton (pocket-sized club), crowbar, fire axe and sledgehammer (hit harder, heavier; the sledgehammer is −10 to hit; all three force locks in a raid like the crowbar and lockpicks), compound bow and hunting crossbow (silent; arrows, not ammo: about half picked up again after a won fight), .357 revolver (harder and louder than a 9mm, no suppressor), lever-action rifle (a handier hunting rifle), police carbine (a semi-automatic rifle, one round a shot).
  - *Throwables* (`stealth.js` THROWABLES, effects in `src/sim/throwables.js`): a pipe bomb (22–34 to everyone within a tile, wakes everything within 14), a Molotov (12–18 and a 3 × 3 patch burns 2–3 turns, 8–14 to people and 16–24 to the dead who stand or walk in it, the dead drawn into it, spreading by fuel: see Siege, fire and decoys; three set a hive on fire instead of 6 fuel), a road flare (a lure that lights 5 tiles at night: your people see and aim +10 there), a smoke grenade (a cloud for 3 turns: −30 to shots through it, +20 to get away when the retreat is sounded), a flashbang (see *Flashbangs* below), a deer call (blown where you stand: a reusable lure for a takedown) and handcuffs (tying up takes no action). The battle AI throws a pipe bomb or a Molotov on three or more enemies bunched up with none of yours close, and a smoke grenade before it sounds the retreat.
  - *Base defences from the armory* (`defences.js` `armoryDefence`): barbed wire (+5% hold a roll, up to 3; one torn up each attack; on the break-in map it stops the dead like the trench), sandbags (+4% a bundle, up to 4; cover on the map), bear traps (3 of the dead each before the walls, up to 4, a quarter break; traps on the map) and a tripwire alarm (everyone counts half on the walls without a warning; carried by a team, trouble a fifth less likely).
  - *Camp kit against Heat* (`fieldheat.js` `kitMult`, shown in the team's Heat text): camo netting ×0.75 while the team stays put, a ghillie wrap ×0.85 (and ×0.9 searching), rag-wrapped boots ×0.85 searching (×0.95 on the spot), a smokeless stove ×0.6 while camping, a gore scent mask ×0.6 on the spot and ×0.75 searching for 24 hours (one used up when the team starts to gain Heat or starts a search). Each kind counts once; together at least ×0.35.
  - *Comforts and treats* (`src/sim/comforts.js`, lines in the mood breakdown): a deck of cards and a harmonica +0.4 mood a day and a guitar +1 for everyone at the base or on the team that has one (together at most +1.5), family photos +0.6 a day to whoever carries them; used up with the inventory's *Use* button: a chocolate bar +6, instant coffee +4 and 10% more work for the day, a pack of cigarettes +5 and −2 health, cheap vodka +10 and then a day hungover (work −15%, −10 aim, −2 mood); magazines and a crossword book are mood reading (`skills.js` books).
