  - *Toiletries* (`comforts.js` `groomKit` / `groomDaily`, 2026-10-06): a stick of deodorant +0.5 mood a day, toothbrush and toothpaste +0.4, shampoo +0.3, wet wipes +0.3, lip balm +0.2, a makeup bag +0.4, perfume +0.5, bars of soap +0.4, razor blades +0.3 (soap and razors were trade-only household valuables until 2026-10-06; each kind once, all together at most +1.2: a "Washed and groomed" line in the mood breakdown). Each holds days of use (deodorant 30, soap 40, razors 60, wet wipes 10, perfume 60; the tooltip shows what is left). Carried, it is the carrier's own; in a base's armory or a team's cargo it is a shared shelf for everyone there without their own, used up a day per person (a stick on the hub's shelf for ten people lasts three days). Empty, it is thrown away. Their worth follows the mood they give over their life (about what a chocolate bar's +6 is worth, 4): deodorant, toothpaste, soap and razors 10, shampoo 6, wet wipes 3, lip balm 5, makeup 12, perfume 20, less as they are used up. They turn up in pharmacies, supermarkets, houses and apartments, locker rooms, barracks, motels, malls (perfume, makeup), airports, truck stops and gas stations, on survivors', soldiers' and militia bodies and the dead, and traders and communes stock them (trade category *toiletries*: communes ×1.3, traders and explorers ×1.2, raiders ×0.7). Adding them raised each table's find chance by their weight, so every older find keeps its rate. `node tools/groomtest.js`.
  - *Valuables* (good for nothing but trade, `valuation.js`): 30 of them in seven families: jewelry (gold ring and chain, wristwatch, diamond ring, pearls, silver cutlery), smokes (cigarette cartons, cigars, rolling tobacco), liquor (whiskey, red wine, single malt), treats (coffee, tea, hard candy, honey), gadgets (smartphone, handheld console, camera, laptop), collectibles (silver coins, trading cards, vinyl, stamps) and household goods (batteries, lighters, toilet paper, soap, razor blades, candles). Jewelry in houses, malls, churches and on raiders and the dead; cigarettes at gas stations, truck stops, prisons and on raider bodies; electronics in offices, malls and universities. Merchants bring a few to sell.
  - *Packs*: sling bag, school backpack, assault pack, hiking pack, frame rucksack (see *Inventory*); the dead sometimes wear one, a road card finds a dead hiker's pack with things inside, a crashed Humvee may hold a rucksack.
  - *Crafting* (`crafting.js`; `gearIn` = items used up from the armory, or on the road from the team): nail bat (a bat + 1 scrap), Molotov (an empty bottle + 1 fuel), pipe bomb (2 scrap, 1 part, 15 rounds, a workshop, Mechanics 5), tripwire alarm, sling bag, ghillie wrap, rag-wrapped boots and gore scent mask (Stealth).
  - *The autopilot*: hands out the best weapon, armor and pack by power and carry as before (the new ones included); packs a trip's kit from the armory into pockets and packs (`kit.js`: scent mask, boots, ghillie, tripwire, stove, camo netting on a search trip, a harmonica or cards, a few throwables; up to 1.5 kg a person); gives the glum (mood under 35) a treat in the evening; makes boots, a ghillie wrap and, with hordes about, Molotovs; firebombs a hive with Molotovs before it burns fuel; sells valuables as before.
- **Outposts:** found satellites with 15 scrap; a new outpost is named after its site or the nearest known place ("North of Dunmore Outpost") and any base can be renamed on its screen (click the name); production by terrain; supply line strength and backup ETA from distance, hostile land and hordes. Each night an outpost ships its surplus to the hub and keeps a reserve (30 scrap to save for a building; a camp its fire's few days of firewood); the hub covers its food shortfall. An outpost with no land route to the hub is cut off: nothing moves either way and it lives off its own stock (logged each day). When an outpost is lost, its people flee to the hub, vehicles parked there stay on the tile as abandoned vehicles (marked on the map), supply runs to or from it are paused, and the log lists what went with it.
- **People on the move** (`src/sim/wanderers.js`, numbers in `WANDER`; the third side in battle in `src/sim/thirdside.js`; `node tools/wanderertest.js [games] [days]`): survivors on their own, loose bands of armed survivors and military squads are met on the road (a chance per tile entered: 3% × the tile's density, city 1, suburbs 0.55, farmland 0.15, forest and hills 0.1, + 0.3 a town or urban site, + 0.06 a road; × 0.6 at night; at most 10%; then nobody new for 10 hours) and at the places you search or raid (one roll per site per 3 days: 25% × density, at most 70%; whoever is there is gone once met). Normal games keep a steady low rate; in hardcore there are many more in the first weeks (people fleeing) and fewer as the months go by (× 1 + 2.2 × e^(−day / 35): ×3.2 on day 1, ×2 by day 30, ×1.1 by a normal start). Who: neutral 58%, aggressive 30% (×1.5 in a city), a squad 15% × closeness to a military base (within 24 tiles; any living military group, or a fortress group that came out of it). **Neutral survivors** (1–4, a mood: wary, friendly, desperate): *Talk to them* (20 / 45 / 35% by mood + 2.5% a point of your best talker's Speech or Leadership − 2, +15% having met before, Charming +5%), *Share food and water* (2 + 2 each; +30% to the chance, mood +2 for the team) or *Leave them be*; a good talk brings their recruit card (recruits.js: take them along, send them to a base, part ways or turn them away). Left alone they live on lightly (once a day, no dice of the world's): they wander, may die (0.8% a day + 4% × infection, +6% next to a horde), join a band within 2 tiles (25% a day) or a group with a base within 3 (4%), settle for good after 25 days (2% a day), and can be met again near where they were. **Aggressive survivors** (2–4, one more in a city, never more than one over your team; in hardcore a team of one or two (`HC_SMALL`, 2026-10-07): half as often alone and only one of them, three quarters as often for a pair and never more than two, the other meetings going to neutrals and squads; not a raider group, from day 1 in both modes, nothing to do with the raiders' day): *Hand over* about 30% of the team's supplies, *Talk them down* (Speech DC 10; 15% one of them asks to join), *Run for it* (Stealth DC 9), or *Fight*; seen first: slip around them, wait 3 hours, or ambush them; now and then they are robbing travellers: help them (the travellers fight beside you and ask to join after) or slip away. **Military squads** (3–5 soldiers, that group's goodwill; a hostile one is a checkpoint): share news (+2, they mark their base and the hives within 20 tiles), keep your distance, or open fire (−30); 45% are met fighting the dead: help them (they fight beside you: +8 and 15–30 ammo), watch from cover (they hold by the numbers, or lose 1–3), or slip away. On a battle map neutrals and soldiers are a **third side** (yellow and green rings): neutrals stay put until the dead or armed strangers come near, then defend themselves when armed, healthy and not outnumbered (or cornered) or run for the edge; soldiers fight the dead and armed people who are up and fighting; your AI never targets them and attacking one takes a second click: a soldier hit turns every soldier on you (goodwill −30), a neutral hit turns its own group. Your people can squeeze past them while they are not hostile (as over the downed: through, not stopping there), so a squad standing in a corridor never shuts your team in. In a raid or a building's interior they are there with the rest (survivors hiding in a room, a squad clearing the place, squatters as guards); the survivors you found can be talked to after the raid. Rolls use their own dice (hash01), so a normal game plays exactly as before until someone is met.
- **Conflicts on the road:** near hostile land, busy roads and infected ground a team can run into people (or a surge of the dead). A perception roll (Scouting, Keen Eye, stealthy stance) decides who sees whom first. *Forced* conflicts (ambush, roadblock, zealot patrol, checkpoint, challenge, a contested site, surge): run, hide, talk, bribe or pay the toll, invoke a pact, surrender, or fight. *Observed* conflicts (a camp, a robbery, two groups fighting, survivors swarmed by the dead, prisoners on the move): watch for intel, slip away, steal, ambush, talk, or take a side. The approach panel shows who they are, estimated numbers, your team and odds for every option. Fights go through the tactical combat entry point (`combat.js`). Surrender costs what its terms say (traders and communes take a fifth of the supplies and leave gear alone). A payment the team can no longer afford brings the card back. A halted team always has something that will release it (a card, a battle, a search, a trade visit); if not, it checks in for orders within two hours, in or out of radio range. Roughly 0–2 conflicts on a 3–5 day trip.
- **Captives:** surrender, lost fights and failed rescues can leave people held at a group's base (People tab: "Held by …"). Get them back with a Rescue mission (sneak in, storm the base, negotiate, ransom), a ransom during a trade visit, or goodwill (friendly groups let them go). Left too long they may escape, change sides, or (with zealots) die.
- **Infestation hives (`src/sim/hives.js`, numbers in `hiveconst.js`):** the hives on the map at the start (5–6 on a Normal map: four around the outbreak origin, one around each other hot spot; Large and Huge maps add outbreaks of 3 at landmarks, see *World sizes*) begin with 5 resources each and gain more every day by cluster: a hive with n hives (itself included) within 5 tiles gets 1, 3, 6, 8, 10 or 12 a day (n(n+1)/2 up to three, then +2 per hive, capped at 12; stores up to 80). They spend them on hordes: small 10–25 of the dead for 3, medium 50–80 for 8, large 100–200 for 15; and for 40 (only a hive that has grown a **Wallbreaker den**, from day 200, one at a time per hive and at most one every 90 days) on a **Wallbreaker**, a hive boss that leads a horde against a base; and on growths around them (*Hive tech tree* below). No hordes exist at the start. Each hive first keeps a guard horde at home (rebuilt below 40). Every 120–180 days a site for a new hive is picked on open ground 4–9 tiles from the nearest hive, away from people (one at a time; on a bigger map more of them, and the days between sites and between border hordes divided by its area: a Huge map picks a site every 49–73 days, up to two at once, and gets a border horde every 2–4 days); hives within 10 tiles of it are *supporters* and send up to two hordes each until enough of the dead are gathered or on the way, the rest are *defenders* that send hordes out to roam near home (1 + half their cluster size at a time, and only while the state holds fewer free dead than 1,000 + 250 per hive and their surroundings fewer than 600; medium ones only before day 60, then large ones too) and, more often in the first two months (20% of new hordes at first, 40% on day 30, 65% from about day 70, always with a Wallbreaker), to attack a base: yours by your numbers and distance (weighed 2.5× a group's, from up to 45 tiles), or a group's by its size (within 22). While the hives near a build site feed it, they send out fewer hordes: a lull you can use. Hordes reaching a site gather into one mass; once the hordes delivered add up to a **score of 30** the mass starts turning into a hive (a quarter of it, at least 25 and at most 40, a day), and the hive stands when **1,000 of the dead** have gone into it (up to 150 left over stay as its guard): about four weeks from the first gathering, the window to strike the hives that feed it or thin the mass. Wandering hordes within 6 tiles of a site that still needs the dead are drawn to it. Every 5–9 days a horde of random size (half small, a third medium, the rest large, a little bigger as the year goes on) comes in from off the map on a border land tile at least 6 tiles from your bases, so destroying every hive makes the state safer but never safe. A hive holds the land within 4 tiles: sites there show at least 3 danger skulls, red on the ring 1 tile around the hive, where every one of the dead met (encounters, raids on its sites, culls, horde cards) is much tougher (+3 power; +1 elsewhere in its land). It radiates infection (1 on its tile, 0.1 less per tile out to 6); tiles move toward that or their own level by 0.02 a day up and 0.01 down, and when a hive is destroyed the land within 7 tiles settles toward a low level over the following weeks (the overlay and the hotzone counts follow). **Destroy hive** (mission on a hive tile, also right-click): a card with the hive's guard and its depth, then **Enter the hive**: the hive crawl (`src/sim/hivecrawl.js`, below) from its mouth to the core room, where the **Broodmother** (300 health) guards the **Hivemind**; kill the Hivemind and the hive is destroyed. Nothing from outside kills or burns a hive (the card's fires and firebombing are gone, 2026-10): only its Hivemind's death in the core does, for your teams and the groups' war parties alike.
- **Volunteers (`src/sim/volunteers.js`, `src/ui/volunteers.js`; Help → *Volunteers*; `node tools/volunteertest.js`):** people lent between groups, both ways. **Asking:** *Ask for volunteers…* on a group's card (Groups tab) or in its radio panel, *Ask allies for volunteers…* in a team's panel, or the Volunteers row of the trip planner (asked as the team leaves) opens a window: the group, the team, 1–4 people, 3–30 days, an offer (goods from the team's home base, or a favour owed) and their answer worked out before you ask. Points (no dice): goodwill (every 10 over 50 a point), the coalition +2, a trade pact +1 (non-aggression +0.5), +0.5 a job done for them (at most 1.5), the mission's danger (a hive crawl −2 and −0.5 a tier, an assault −2, a rescue −1.5, a cull −1, scavenging −0.5), −0.75 a person after the first, −0.1 a day past 7, their trouble (at war −1.5, a horde of 30+ within 6 tiles of a base −2, under 14 people −1.5), −1 for each volunteer of theirs who fell with your teams in the last 60 days (−2 each who died any other way), +1 every 40 of trade value offered, +1.5 a favour owed. 0 or more: yes (−2 goodwill with nothing offered); a no is remembered 3 days; goodwill under 30 or hostile: not asked at all; they keep 8 at home. **Volunteers** are real people (`state.vols.people`: kept apart from yours, found by `survivorById`), with skills by their type (military +4 Shooting) and kit from their group's arsenal (it leaves the arsenal and goes back with them); they walk from their nearest base (1.2 h a tile) and join the team wherever it is, bringing a day's food and water a day of the span (up to 8) and 30 rounds of their gun's calibre. They carry, eat the team's food, count in fights by the numbers and go into the hive crawl (volunteers for a hive bring a flashlight when they have no light; in the cave's dark each sees by their own light, 9 tiles without one; the core's hoard is the team's, they keep only what they carry; a coalition member that sent volunteers to the team does not also thin the brood or the defenders: `alliance.js hiveAllies / assaultAllies`). Home when the span is over (never mid-fight or mid-crawl), when the team comes home, settles or is disbanded, when none of your people is left with them, or when their goodwill falls under 20; their group's people away meanwhile. A death in a fight −3 goodwill (the group mourns), any other −10; home safe after fighting beside you +1 each. **In battle** (`combat.js` `volunteerTurn`, `volunteersFollow`): side "player" with `u.vol`, a violet ring and a VOL tag, a list of their own under your team; your orders are refused (*their own group's orders*); at the end of your turn their AI moves them: near your people and holding fire while nobody has noticed you, then the battle AI (cover, shots, reloads, meds, dragging the downed); the Evac and the retreat take them along, they leave when your people are all out, fight on when your people are down; *AI plays my side* moves your people and leaves them to their own turn. **Their asks:** a group at war, with a horde near its base or a hive crawl coming calls on the radio (5% a day, 15% when you owe them a favour; one ask at a time, 25 days apart) for 1–3 people for 3–7 days: send the fittest, *Choose who…* (a window), or decline (−3 goodwill, ×3 when you owe them a favour, half when they are under 50; the default when the card is left in the tray). Your people lent are *With <group> (back ~day N)* on the roster and out of your orders; their power adds to the group's strength (`factions.js strength`, its hive war party); each day of the span their fight may come (hive 35%, defence 30%, war 25%: each one 35% to be hurt 10–40, which can kill), and they come home with 4 food, 6 ammo and 2 scrap a person a fight from the group's stores, +3 goodwill and +1 a fight. **Groups lend each other** the same way, coarse: a group in need, 8% a day, gets 1–3 fighters from its best friend (relations 30+ or its bloc, 14+ people, not in need itself) for 3–7 days, their power on its strength, a few lost, relations +4. **The governor** asks allies when a hive crawl (squad over the hive's strength under 1.5 by style, 1.8 for the Turtle, 1.25 Aggressive) or an assault (odds under 0.8 by style) looks marginal (two people, else one, the best answer; food offered by the careful styles), and answers the asks by style (`volunteers.js STYLES`: people kept at the hub before lending 8–16, at most 1–3 lent: the Diplomat sends the most, the Turtle declines). **Follow-ups:** *Watch the fight* (`volunteers.js watchCard / watchSetup`, `combat.js` `setup.lentIds` / `setup.watch`): when a fight comes for your people lent out (in the browser, not on autopilot) a card offers to watch it on the battle map (your lent people moved by the AI as volunteers are, the group's own fighters as a friendly side, 4–7 of the enemy group's fighters for a war or 8–14 of the dead; you only watch, the battle plays itself, the field's spoils stay with the group) or to let it play out by the numbers (the default); either way it is one of the loan's fights. *Old hands* (`noteVet`, `vetCard`, `welcomeVet`): volunteers home alive are kept by their group (`f.volVets`, up to 6) and go first next time; after 3 trips or 30 days with you (their group's goodwill 60+, their health 60+) one may ask to join you for good (35% a trip home): welcomed, they move to your hub (the group lets them go with a weapon from its arsenal at goodwill 80+, else −5 goodwill and they come with a knife); a fighter a group lends another stays with it 10% of the time. *Orders* (`combat.js volOrder`): Free / Hold here / Follow (one of yours, the one selected) from the Volunteers list in battle; they act within it (hold: shoot from there, reload, else watch; follow: a fair shot from where they stand, else up to their lead); none in a fight you only watch. *The share of the haul*: the request window's 0 / 10 / 25% (the share × 6 points); what the team's cargo gained while they were with it × the share goes from the cargo (or the home base when the team is home) to their group when the last of them leaves, +1 goodwill and +1 per 60 of value (at most 3); the governor offers 10% when nothing less gets a yes. *Pulled out hurt*: under 50 health a volunteer's group pulls them out (never mid-fight; between a hive crawl's chambers, yes). *The council* (`alliance.js` council, `volunteers.js councilVolCard / councilVolVote`): when a member is at war with a group outside the coalition the council may vote fighters for it; each other member votes yes with relations 10+ with it and under 10 with its enemy; you vote on the card (yes and send 1–2 of yours, yes, no: −2 goodwill with the member); a majority (your vote counted) carries it: each member that voted yes sends 1–2 fighters for 4–7 days (coarse), yours go as people lent out, +2 goodwill with the member. `node tools/volunteers2test.js` checks all six.
- **The hive crawl (`src/sim/hivecrawl.js`, window `src/ui/hivecrawl.js`, sound `src/ui/hivecrawlsfx.js`; Help → *The hive crawl*; `node tools/hivecrawltest.js`):** every hive is a den of chambers (`hive.den`, from the world's seed and the hive: the same every visit, regrown deeper when the hive's tier rises). The shortest way from the mouth to the core is 2, 3, 5, 7 or 9 chambers by the hive's tier 0–4 (`HCRAWL.DEPTH`; a screamer spire adds one): the crawl is the mid and late game's work. Each layer holds 2–4 chambers; at least two routes that share no chamber run to the core, with side branches, dead ends, pockets and loops. Kinds: tunnels, galleries, nurseries (many, weak), egg chambers (burn them with 3 fuel or a Molotov: the hive loses 8 stores and 5% of its guard, its chambers fill more slowly), bone pits (the worn guns, armour and packs of earlier teams, ammunition and meds), worm pools (the worm's exposure, `worm.js`), the roots of the hive's growths (burning one hurts the growth above, 70), collapses. **Warmth**: every chamber's from its distance to the core (the core 100); the window's gauge, the air in words (*cool and damp* → *the walls sweat*), its tint and vignette going from slate to ember red, and a draft on every passage (*warmer*, *colder*); a team misreads an unexplored passage's draft 40% of the time less 4.5 points a point of its best Scouting (the visit's own dice). **The brood**: (3 + 0.9 × the layer) × the kind's share × √(guard / 80), 2–14 a chamber, power the hive's brood × (1 + 0.06 a layer), the special dead its growths breed (8% + 6% a layer), elite deep in a hive with a mutation vat, × 1.3 next to the core. The hive's guard horde is the brood: each one killed inside takes its share of the guard. Cleared chambers stay clear 14 days (× 2 burned, × 1.5 everywhere after its eggs burned), then fill again. Choices: fight on a cave map (layouts `hivechamber`, `hivecore`) or by the numbers (`crawlByNumbers`: the odds as `byNumbersOdds`, the blood fitted to the same fights played on the cave map, 135 × (their strength / yours)^1.9 health spread over the team), creep past (Stealth + d10 against 7 + 0.7 a layer + Alert / 20), burn, search, tend the wounded (field treatment with the team's meds), lie still (2 h, Alert −15), move (1 h), fall back, leave the way you came. **The hive's Alert**: noise raises it, 3 an hour fades it; from 60 the brood converges (a swarm into the team's chamber, cleared chambers behind it filling again), from 85 it packs the mouth. **The Hivemind** (`defences.js BOSSES.hivemind`, its turn and death through `combat.js registerBossAI`): rooted, 480 health + 60 a tier, takes 55% of each hit; every turn a pulse (everyone within 3 tiles 4–10, 35% stunned an action) and a lash at someone next to it (16–30); every other turn 2–3 brood out of the walls (8 + 2 a tier in all); its death kills every one of its brood. The battle AI (and *AI plays my side*) aims for it first (`shotPri`) and judges the fight with a rooted boss at a quarter of its blows and half its flesh (`fightOutlook`). **The autopilot** (`hiveCrawlAutoStep`): follows the warmer drafts (unexplored first, never through the core or a chamber full of brood), fights by the numbers at 80%+ (60%+ when it cannot creep past), creeps past at 70%+, burns eggs, searches bone pits, tends the wounded under 70, lies still at Alert 70+, turns back when two are under 45 or half the team is unfit, fights the core on the map headless when the whole team is fit and the core's odds by the numbers are 75%+, else turns back to come again. Its campaign (`autopilot.js`) goes straight for the core (a storm trip) when the core's odds by the numbers for the squad (`coreGate`: at the guard it last saw, × the wear of the fights on the shortest way in, with the lights, Molotovs and bangs the squad carries or the armory would give it) reach its style's `stormNeed` (0.5 + 0.25 × hiveOdds, 0.7–0.9: Aggressive 75%, Balanced 80%), packs 60 + 15 ammo and 3 + depth / 2 meds a chamber of depth and the hive kit (`kit.js packHiveKit`: a light each, 2 Molotovs, 2 flashbangs, a frag grenade, a pipe bomb and 2 road flares from the armory), plans the hours inside, and when the core is beyond the squad sends **crawl trips** that clear the way and thin the brood (the next trip finds the chambers clear and the guard thinner). **The AI groups** (`factions.js groupHiveWar` → `groupHiveCrawl`) crawl the same den: their party's strength (as before) is spent chamber by chamber on the shortest way (the whole way costs the guard × (depth + 1) / 3, the core's bosses 6 + 6 a tier on top), they clear what they pay for (shared with your teams), are driven off where it runs out and burn the hive when they pay for the core. **Lights, flares, listening** (`HCRAWL.LIGHT`, `FLARE`, `LISTEN`): the team's lights are on by default when anyone carries one (douse them in the window): the drafts out of its chamber are misread half as often, but a move costs +1 Alert and creeping past is 1 harder; both cave maps are dark (`b.dark`, `caveDark`): someone without a light sees 9 tiles, with one its light's sight + 4 (a headlamp 12, a spotlight 16), night vision as by day, the brood its own; by the numbers the brood in a chamber counts up to 15% more for the share of the team in the dark (`darkMult`; not the core, whose numbers are fitted to the map: `HIVEMIND.numbersK`). A road flare (now craftable: 1 scrap, 1 fuel, 4 rounds, in the field too) thrown down a passage lights the chamber beyond: its kind, its brood exactly and the true draft (Alert +5, no time). Listening at a passage (1 h): the best listener's (Scouting + Stealth) / 2 + d10 (the visit's own dice) against 9 + 0.4 a layer: by 3+ the kind and brood exactly, by 0+ the kind and roughly how many, less a guess (half the time the wrong kind), by −4 or worse also Alert +6 and the brood beyond waits (spotted). The autopilot, before choosing between two or more ways on it knows nothing of, listens with a listener of 5+, else throws a flare while the Alert is under 40, and takes the warm way with the fewest brood it heard of (the core first; never a dead end it heard). **The Hivemind's second phase** (`HIVEMIND.P2`): below half its health it tears open (its own sprite, a battle-log line, a quake rumble in the hive pulse group and a faster, louder beat): 3–5 pillars of flesh come down in the open (never closing a passage; those of yours near one may be hurt 3–8), its pulse reaches 4 tiles for 6–12 (45% stunned), the walls give up 3–4 brood at a time and 1 more in all. Retuned so the rifle squad with headlamps wins the core as before (tier 2 99%, tier 4 72% over 100 battles; before 98% and 73%; without lights 84% and 51%). **The Hivemind's weak spots** (`HIVEMIND.WEAK`; `throwables.js` notes where each throw lands, `b.landed`): fire on it or next to it (a Molotov, flames spreading to it) burns it 30–45 a turn (before its thick flesh) for 2 of its turns after the last flames touch it, and while it burns the walls give up no brood; a flashbang within 3 tiles stuns it for 3 of its turns, a frag grenade or a pipe bomb within 2 for 2: no pulse, no lash, no brood called, and its flesh lies open (it takes all of a hit, not 55%) until its next turn. The battle AI and *AI plays my side* (`combat.js bossThrowTarget` → `stealth.js botAttackThrow`) step into reach (outside its pulse) and throw them at it first, never with one of theirs in the fire or the flash; their retreat (`combat.js fightOutlook`, a boss's own `outlook`) counts the Hivemind burning (half its flesh and blows) or reeling (70% of its flesh, none of its blows) and each Molotov or bang still in hand (+12% to your side, up to 4), so they hold on in a core they are winning with fire. A bang next to the Broodmother dazes her (a flashbang 2 actions, a grenade 1: `combat.js bossOnLand`), and fire panics the brood the Hivemind called: within 3 tiles of a Molotov bursting, 2 of the flames or 4 of it while it burns, each loses an action on its next turn. The autopilot makes Molotovs (a bottle and fuel) and road flares at the hub for its campaign while the armory holds fewer than the hive kit (from 10 days before its first storm day; `planCrafts`), and an AI group about to send a war party makes up to 2 Molotovs from its fuel (`groupMakeKit`). The rifle squad with headlamps at tier 4 against a guard of 120: 38% bare, 94% with 2 Molotovs and 2 flashbangs, five prepared 88% (16 battles each); 40 battles each: guard 80 75% bare, 100% prepared, 83% with 2 flashbangs only; the shotgun squad (Shooting 7, vests) at tier 2 10% bare, 80% prepared. Fire does most of it (the flames also catch and panic the brood called around it), a bang alone a few points. By the numbers (`coreSetup`, `coreGate`) the bosses count × (1 − 0.325 × the share of the team lit) × (1 − 0.4 × its Molotovs − 0.15 × its bangs, up to 2 of each), and a fight by the numbers spends them; `numbersK` refitted to 2 (`tools/hivecorecalib.js --fit`: four squads × tiers 1/2/4 × guards 60/120 × lit or not × prepared or not, 12 battles each; best fit 1.7 at RMS 0.180, 2 at 0.188 leans careful where the numbers overrate weak teams; the old 2.5 alone 0.430). The AI groups' war parties bring the Molotovs and bangs in their arsenal (up to 4): the core costs them up to 25% less of its bosses, and they are used. **The hoard** (`hiveHoard`, `HCRAWL.HOARD`): the core holds what the hive took from the dead, from its own dice (the world's untouched): 1 + 0.8 a tier + its stores / 30 guns (better kinds the higher the tier), 0.5 + 0.5 a tier + stores / 60 armour pieces, a rare piece (night vision, thermal, a military radio, a surgical kit, an M79…) at 15% + 12% a tier (two rolls from tier 3), flares, 20 + 18 a tier + 0.8 × stores rounds by calibre, meds, gun parts and scrap, each worn as the dead left it (`itemcond.js`, survivors' and now and then soldiers' kit). Once the Hivemind is dead the team takes the meds, then the best of the gear, then the rest as far as its cargo holds; what is left stays at the hive as cargo left behind (on the map: Recover it). An AI group that burns a hive takes it into its stores and arsenal (`groupTakeHoard`). **The crawl's record** (`hive.crawlRec`: deepest, chambers mapped, the core found or located from a passage, visits; reset when the den grows deeper; none in old saves until a team goes in): the State Map's hive tile (*Crawled*, with the known groups' war parties' depth, `hive.groupCrawl`), a ⛏ 4/7 label under the hive (★ the core found), the hive card, the Goals card. The groups' war parties go 8% further for their torches and listening, and the second phase costs them 2 more at the core. **Destroy every hive with none being built and you win** (from year 10, once the outbreak beyond the border has burned out and the state has stayed clear for a season: see *Eras and winning*): a victory screen with *Watch the timelapse*, *Keep playing* and *New campaign* (`world.js checkGameOver`).
- **Hive birth you can see (`src/sim/hivebirth.js`, every number in `BIRTH`):** a new hive is no longer born out of sight. The day the hives pick a build site you may hear a rumour (log and diary: "Travellers talk of the dead drifting toward …") when the site is inside your radio coverage, within 12 tiles of a base of yours or within 6 of a team in the field. Once the gathering is known by the usual rules (in view of a base or a team in radio contact, or in a report that reached the hub) a card **"The dead are massing near X"**: *Break it up* (the planner opens aimed at the gathering with the Cull horde mission; also marks it), *Mark it and watch it* (the default: an alert that tracks the days left, red under a week, and a dashed red ring on the map) or *Ignore it*. The build-site mound on the map grows with the dead massed there and has a line under it, the days until the hive stands as far as you know (from what is in view, or the last report less the days since, with a "?" once it is over 3 days old); the tile panel says the same. A Cull horde team at the gathering gets **Break up the gathering**: a tactical battle against 6 of the dead at the edge of the mass (+1 per 80 massed, at most 12) at power 2 (+0.6 per point of the ground's toughness), with no Broodmother: far smaller than storming a hive (10–18 of the brood at power 5+ and the Broodmother). Win and the gathering scatters: 30% of it falls and the rest run 5 tiles off for 6 days, and the hordes still on their way are called off. Before the mound is begun (a score under 30 delivered) the site is given up and no new site is picked for at least 30 days; once it is under way the mound loses 40% of the dead gone into it (at least 150), has to gather a score of 30 again, and the hives stop feeding it for 10 days. Fall back and what the team cut down is gone from the mass. If a hive you knew of rises anyway, a card says so (plan an attack with the planner aimed at it, or not now). The autopilot marks every gathering and sends a Cull horde squad of 4 (at least 3) when a known gathering is within its range (×1.15, Aggressive ×1.5) and its best fighters have the odds (its hive odds ×1.2, Aggressive ×0.9), one such trip at a time. The hive pace itself (sites every 120–180 days, the 30-score / 1,000-dead rule) is unchanged.
- **Hive tech tree (`src/sim/hivetech.js`, the special dead in `zombietypes.js`; every number in `hiveconst.js` `TECH`, `GROWTHS`, `ZTYPES`):** a hive spends resources on **growths**, structures it grows on land within 2 tiles of itself (not water or a bridge, a site, a ruin, a base, a hive or build site, another growth), one at a time, at least 12 days apart, up to 10, each taking days to grow:

  | Growth | Tier | Cost | Days | Gives its hive |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Biomass pit (up to 2) | 0 | 12 | 10 | +1 resource a day |
  | Brood chamber | 0 | 20 | 15 | guard kept at +40; from tier 2 one more horde out |
  | Worm cyst | 0 | 18 | 15 | Carriers in its hordes (the first special dead: a young hive grows its first pit, then this) |
  | Feeding grounds | 1 | 25 | 20 | income ×1.5 |
  | Runner warren | 1 | 25 | 20 | runners in its hordes |
  | Brute pit | 2 | 35 | 30 | brutes |
  | Spitter sac | 2 | 35 | 30 | spitters |
  | Screamer spire | 3 | 40 | 40 | screamers; attacks bases 10 tiles farther; calls wandering dead within 6 into its guard |
  | Crawler burrow | 3 | 40 | 40 | crawlers |
  | Wallbreaker den | 3 | 50 | 45 | Wallbreakers (with `HIVE.BOSS_DEN` they come from nowhere else) |
  | Mutation vat | 4 | 60 | 60 | its special dead are elite (×1.3 hp, ×1.2 damage) |

  **Tiers** open by the calendar and the hive's age (`TECH.TIER_DAY` 0 / 120 / 450 / 995 / 1331, each up to 35% later per hive (tier 3 with the late game; 1100 / 1500 before the progression census of 2026-10-09); `TIER_AGE` 0 / 20 / 90 / 200 / 400 days old; in the game's 112-day years, see *Eras and winning*): Carriers from the first months (the worm cyst is tier 0; a young hive grows it after its first pit, so about the second month), runners from year 2 (the early game), brutes and spitters from year 5–6 (the mid game), screamers, crawlers and Wallbreakers from year 10–13 (the late game; no Wallbreakers before), elite dead from year 13–17, so the threat grows over a 10–15 year game; a hive that rises late starts again at tier 0. **The hive's mind** (`growthWish`, daily after its income): its guard first; income while young or poor; a brood chamber when it was attacked within 60 days (a crawl, a growth burned); a kind of dead its cluster-mates do not breed yet over one they do; the den and the vat high once open; a growth it cannot afford it saves for (no new roaming hordes) for up to 20 days. **The special dead** (`ZTYPES`): a hive that breeds a kind puts about its share into every horde it buys (runners 15%, brutes and spitters 8%, Carriers and crawlers 6%, screamers 3%, × 0.6–1.4, together at most 45%: `h.mix`, kept through merges by numbers and through splits); border hordes carry the era's kinds at half that from day 900 (year 9). In battle (`combat.js` hooks: `applyZType`, `zombieTurn`, `zTick`, `zRevealed`, the `acid` weapon) each is a unit with its own sprite, ring color and letter: **runner** (6 tiles an action, 0.85× hp; counter: walls, overwatch), **brute** (2.4× hp, takes ¾ of every hit, 1.5× damage, slow; smashes the barricade, wall section, fence or crates in its way when it cannot reach anyone; counter: armor, heavy guns, pits and trenches), **spitter** (acid at 4–6 tiles every other turn, cover a step less against it, keeps its distance in between; when it dies, by any hand in any battle, its sac bursts (`acid.js`, `ACID`): its tile and the 8 round it (open floor at its height, not through walls) pool with acid for 3 rounds, everyone living there is splashed for 10–16 at once, and the living who step in (once a round) or start their side's turn in it take 6–10, armour stopping only half as much; the dead walk through it unharmed, people's AI paths round it when it can (dearer, never closed), the move preview turns green and the tile hint warns; worth ×1.6 in a fight by the numbers; `tools/acidtest.js`; counter: smoke, shoot it first, kill it away from your people), **screamer** (hangs back ~5 tiles and shrieks every other turn, calling 1–2 more of the dead from the enemy's edge, 6 a battle; counter: kill it first: the AI's shooters do), **crawler** (unseen and unshootable until within 2.5 tiles of one of yours, lit or it has struck; its first bite ×1.6; counter: floodlights, flares, staying together). Fights resolved by the numbers count each by its weight (`typeStrength`). On the State Map: the walls hold a horde as its size × the mix's `wall` weight (brutes 2.5, spitters 1.4, screamers 1.2, runners 0.9, crawlers 0.5); every 12 brutes smash a defence like a Wallbreaker (2 at most); crawlers slip past held walls (60% of them, half less with a watchtower, half with lit floodlights, a quarter if manned) and 4+ are a small ambush battle inside (lost: 15% of the food and meds); a horde with 10%+ runners moves a quarter faster; screamers in a horde pull wandering hordes within 3 tiles along; warnings, cull and horde cards and the tile panel name the special dead. **The player:** growths show on the map beside a hive you know of (live or last reported: faint while growing, grey husks after the hive), in the tile panel (what it does, its flesh) and the hive's row (its tier and growths); one finished in sight is logged, one out of sight at a hive you know of reaches you as a rumour 40% of the time and goes on the map; a team that comes upon a hive sees them too. **Burn the growth** (mission on its tile, right-click): fight its guard (6 + 1 per 40 in the hive's guard, at most 12, power 2 + 0.6 × the ground's toughness, the hive's special dead plus a quarter of the growth's own kind; the "growth" battle layout), or burn it: 4 fuel burns 60 of its 100 flesh, 2 Molotovs 100; it regrows 5 a day. Gone, the hive loses what it gave and marks itself attacked. Destroying the hive withers its growths (no effect; gone after 40 days). **The autopilot** burns or fights the most dangerous growth it knows of (a spawner, the den or the vat, never pits or brood chambers; den, burrow, vat first; near its bases ahead) when no hive can be stormed yet, with a crew of 4 and fuel for two fires (`growthCandidate`, `growthChoice`). The hives' choices (what and where to grow, a horde's mix, the spire, rumours) roll their own dice (`hivetech.js roll`: a hash of the seed, the hour and what is rolled for), so they never shift the world's random numbers. Levers for the long-game balance: `TECH.TIER_DAY` / `TIER_AGE` / `JITTER` (the pace), `TECH.MIX_MAX` and each `ZTYPES[k].share` (how many special dead), `ZTYPES[k].wall` / `weight` (their worth at the walls and by the numbers), the battle stats in `ZTYPES` (`hp`, `dmg`, `mob`, `armor`, `cooldown`, `callMax`, `reveal`, `ambush`, `slip`, `smashEvery`), `GROWTHS[k].cost` / `days` / `income` / `incomeMult` / `guard` / `out` / `outTier`, `HIVE.BOSS_DEN`, `TECH.GAP` / `SAVE_DAYS` / `THREAT_DAYS`, `TECH.BORDER_DAY` / `BORDER_SHARE`, the growth fight and fires (`GUARD_*`, `FIRE_*`, `BOMB*`, `REGROW`), `SPIRE_*`, `ELITE_*`.
- **The armoured dead (`src/sim/deadsoldiers.js`, every number in `MILDEAD`; their stats as special dead in `hiveconst.js` `ZTYPES.soldier` / `ZTYPES.riot`):** soldiers and riot police who turned in their gear. No hive breeds them (no growth unlocks them, `share` 0); they are where they fell. **Dead soldier** (`soldier`): 1.3× an ordinary zombie's health, the same bite (a walker's damage, hit chance and critical chance exactly: no bonus for being armoured; the 1.7 they weigh in a fight by the numbers is how hard they are to put down, not how hard they hit), never faster than a walker (the kit weighs them down), a plate carrier and a combat helmet: each hit is × the armour for the weapon that struck (`MILDEAD.ARMOR.soldier`: pistol 0.5, revolver 0.6, shotgun 0.6, bows 0.45–0.55, lever-action 0.7, carbine, assault rifle and hunting rifle 0.75 (no rifle a full counter); knife 0.4, spear 0.45, machetes 0.5, fire axe 0.75, baton 0.75, bat 0.85, nail and spiked bats 0.8, crowbar 0.85, sledgehammer 1; a gun butt 0.8). Through it: a **critical hit** (a round under the helmet's rim, a blade in a gap: the armour is ignored, so Deadeye and the Assassin perk shine), a **blade from behind** or on one that has not noticed you (knife, machetes, pipe spear, fire axe: a knife to the base of the skull, `stabsGap`), **fire** (Molotovs and burning ground ×1) and **pipe bombs** (×0.85); spike pits and bear traps ×0.8. **Dead riot cop** (`riot`): 1.25× health, hard-shell riot gear the other way round: blows and blades glance off (bat 0.6, baton 0.5, knife 0.45, sledge 0.8), pistols 0.7, buckshot 0.75, but rifle rounds go straight through (×1). By the numbers they weigh 1.7 and 1.5 (`weight`), at the walls 1.1. `combat.js damage(b, t, raw, by, nonLethal, hit)` asks `armorVs` with the hit (`{ how: shot | melee | bash | fire | blast, crit, sneak }`); the AI's shot choice and the battle tooltip (how much of the selected unit's weapon gets through) use it too. **Where** (`siteDeadMix`, deterministic: no world dice): the share of them among a site's dead, as a horde-style mix placed by share (`zombietypes.js battleTypes` / `mixList`) in raids on the street map (`createRaid`), inside buildings (`buildingmaps.js placeInterior`), in road encounters (`conflicts.js enemyGroup`) and among the dead drawn in by noise later (`nextOfMix`, `b.zmix`): National Guard bases 45%, army helicopter wrecks 50%, the tiles next to a military site (the old cordon) 15%, within 2 on a road (the checkpoints) 8%, a fallen military base in hardcore 60% (30% next to it; read from `state.hardcore.groups.military`); police stations 30% riot police, prisons 20%, city centres 5%; anywhere else a lone soldier 3% of the time (a site, a week). The city crawl (`citycrawl.js`): a police station among a block's places has its riot police (30%), a barricaded checkpoint's street map half dead soldiers (`MILDEAD.CHECKPOINT`), and the street hordes the city's share; the hardcore military arc's horde of the fallen (`HC_MILITARY.FALLEN_SOLDIER`) and the strike's dead merged into a hive's guard come in as dead soldiers. A horde crossing a military site nobody holds picks some up (+6% a pass, up to 15%: `hordeMilitaryPickup`), and they come with it to your walls; `makeHorde(state, { ..., mix: { soldier: 0.6 } })` is the horde of the fallen. The hour-by-hour search at such a site is harder (threat + 4 × the mix's extra weight) and a won fight there may leave one to strip (share × 1.2). **Their bodies** (`bodyLoot`; `inventory.js enemyBody` after a battle, `deadBodyFinds` in a raid, and by the numbers a body or two after a road fight): a soldier a plate carrier (15%) or a Kevlar vest (30%) worn to 30–60%, a **combat helmet** (`milhelmet`, new: head armour 1.4; 35%), 3–10 rounds (60%) and a rifle magazine's 10–20 (15%), now and then an assault rifle, a carbine, a military radio, a smoke grenade, a flare, a bandage kit; a riot cop the riot helmet (35%), arm and leg guards, a baton, cuffs, a few rounds. Levers: `MILDEAD.ARMOR`, `ZTYPES.soldier.hp` / `weight`, `MILDEAD.SITE` / `NEAR` / `ROAD` / `FALLEN` / `RARE`, `HORDE_ADD` / `HORDE_MAX`, `SEARCH_THREAT` / `SEARCH_BODY`, `MILDEAD.BODY`. Sprites: standing `sprites.js paintArmoredDead(kind, seed, look)` (`armoredCanvas`): rotting skin in the zombie palette, camo with a sleeve torn off, a plate carrier with pouches, a helmet with its strap hanging; the riot cop in black with a hard shell, shoulder pads, guards and a cracked visor pushed up. Tested by `tools/milzedtest.js` (the balance tables too).
- **The Carrier, the worm swarm and the human dead (`src/sim/carriers.js`, every number in `hiveconst.js` `CARRIER`; their stats as special dead in `ZTYPES.carrier` / `ZTYPES.swarm`; `tools/carriertest.js`):** the dead carry the worm, and a **Carrier** (`Ca`, a pale ring) is one of them so full of it that it is swollen: 1.5× an ordinary zombie's health, 0.8× its bite, slow (2.5 tiles an action), shot first by the AI. The **worm cyst** (tier 0, 18 resources, 15 days) is the first special dead a hive breeds: a young hive grows its first biomass pit, then the cyst; its hordes carry about 6% Carriers (× 0.6–1.4), and border hordes some from day 900. **Raising the dead, at once:** a human body (anyone killed: your people, raiders, soldiers, neutrals; never one of the dead, never someone only down or knocked out) within 2 tiles of a Carrier (diagonals count as 1) gets up with no warning: on the Carrier's own turn as it ends its move (and acts at once), or at the start of the enemy turn for a body killed near one during yours, as an ordinary zombie ("Raider 3 (turned)", or for your own people and the third side the first name: "Dana (turned)") with 1 action, going for every living thing. The map marks a body in danger with a pale "!" while a Carrier is within 8 tiles or the worm is in it (red: within 2, it gets up at the end of this turn); the hover text names what can be done. An aware Carrier with none of the living next to it walks to the nearest body within 8 tiles. **Answering a body** (any human body, any time, Carrier or no Carrier): **Crush skull** (U, 1 action, silent, from next to it; the loot stays; the battle AI and the raid bot do it to a body in danger next to them), **Double tap** (J, 1 action, 1 round, the noise of a shot, line of sight: the same from range), **fire** on its tile (as it would rise and at each turn's change: the body and everything on it are gone), or **Pick up body** (P, 1 action: half the move distance and no shooting while carrying, a carried body cannot get up; P again puts it down in front of the carrier, Z lays it in the team's car at a raid's vehicle zone or on a field battle's evac strip when the team came by car, if the car has room: a body weighs `CARRIER.BODY_KG` 70 kg plus its things in the team's load and capacity (`parties.js bodyLoad`) until it is buried or burned). A body in the car cannot rise and comes home with the team (`p.cargo.bodies`, `base.bodies`): the base's Armory tab lists it with **Bury** (its things to the armory) and **Burn** (nothing kept); one with the worm in it left unburied gets up in the night after a day, hurts someone and is put down. **The risen keep what the person wore:** worn armour takes its share of every hit (your people: what they have on, not the Tough trait; others: their vest or plate), no guns. **Their things are never duplicated or lost:** put down and the battle won, one of yours is their own body to loot under "Dana (turned)", anyone else one body with the person's kit (their weapon, armour, pockets); in a raid the body's container goes with the risen and lies where they are put down. **Walking loot:** a risen still walking when a battle ends (any side, any outcome) takes its things off the death site (or out of the armory) into `state.walkingLoot` with its tile and the person's name; the death site stays and says the body walked. Each day an entry drifts a tile, half the days (toward the nearest hive 70% of the time, else at random), so the dead seem to wander. A battle or raid made within 8 tiles of an entry (60% on the tile, less with distance, at most 2 a battle) has one of its ordinary dead marked as that person ("Dana (turned)", in their armour), the entry leaves the pool, and the Diary notes the meeting; put down and the battle won its body has the things on it (a raid: a body container with them in it); still walking at the end it goes back in the pool; put down in a lost fight the things lie at a death site on the tile. **The worm swarm** (`W`; a Carrier that dies bursts, a Sleeper splits, a Flinger throws one): 0.7× health, 0.6× bite but +15 to hit, quick (4 tiles); a single bullet or bolt (pistols, rifles, carbines, bows, crossbows) does 15% of its damage (`CARRIER.SHOT`), a shotgun's spread all of it (`SHOTGUN` 1, by the weapon's `data.js` category), blades 0.9, blows 1.25, blasts 1.5, fire 2.5× as hot. **Its bite infests:** each hit on someone living leaves the worm in them 35% of the time (`INFEST`; a kill always does): an enemy so bitten gets up when killed; one of yours who lives through the battle has caught it (`worm.js catchWorm`, cause "swarm": the normal incubation, but spotted at once since everyone saw the bite), and whoever has the worm and dies, anywhere and any time later, gets up: in a battle where they fell; on the State Map or at a base they tear at someone before they are put down, and alone on the road they walk off with their things into the walking loot (`riseOnMap`, from `killSurvivor`; the worm module's own turning and a person put down by their own people clear the worm first, so nobody rises twice). With no one to go to the swarm sinks into the ground after 6 enemy turns and leaves no body. By the numbers a Carrier weighs 1.5 and presses the walls at 1.1. Battles without Carriers, swarms or walking loot nearby play as before (no dice are drawn for them).
- **The mid game's special dead and the Sleepers (`src/sim/middead.js`, `sleepers.js`; every number in `hiveconst.js` `MIDDEAD`, `SLEEPER`; `tools/specialtest.js`):** five tier-2 growths (year 5 on) each breed a kind: the **Ossuary** Bonebacks (`Bb`: plates in front, a hit from the half it faces does 30%, from the side or behind all of it; hit, it turns on its attacker), the **Sinew scaffold** Climbers (`Cl`: over walls, fences, barricades, a closed gate, cars and crates and up onto walkways without a ladder, never stopping on one; at a held wall half of them get over, fewer with a watchtower and manned defences, and fight inside), the **Worm pit** Flingers (`Fl`: wind up a turn at someone within 7 tiles, then throw a worm swarm next to them; a hit of 10+ while winding drops it at its own feet; 3 throws a battle), the **Egg cistern** Sowers (`Se`: every tile their horde crosses on the State Map gets Infection, 1.5 points a Sower, at most 20 a pass, fading as usual; in battle the infection rises while one stands) and the **Dread hollow** Nightmares (`Nm`: a scream every 3 turns once it sees you: Heat +15 in a raid, noise, the dead within 12 tiles wake, and your people within 6 who fail their nerve at 30% (brave +30, cowardly −20, a friend beside +10) spend their next turn running for the way out or away from the dead). **Sleepers** (`Sl`, from day 1): bodies lie about every fight in the open and every raid map, some of them Sleepers (15% + 25% × Infection) that split into a worm swarm, with a free bite, when anyone living steps next to them; Scouting spots one within 3 tiles (then it can be shot from afar), fire cooks it. **Where the special dead come from:** a hive's new horde rolls for each kind it breeds by its size, the hive's tier and a brood chamber (`HORDEMIX`), a hive assault fields at least one of every kind, and away from the hordes they turn up exploring: more in towns and cities every year, more on a hive's land, the hive's own kinds on its red-skull ring (`AMBIENT`, `hivetech.js siteSpecials`).
- **The fallen (`src/ui/downsprites.js`):** every unit that is down or dead lies on the battle map as its own picture, not its standing sprite turned on its side: a body seen from above on a 26 × 14 grid, built from the same look as the standing sprite (the same seed's colours and clothes, the person's appearance, armour and weapon). The dead (`paintDownedZombie(variant, seed, look)` for walker / runner / bloated / brute: sprawled in a pool of blood, torn clothes and wounds, the runner mid-stride, the bloated one burst open, the brute a slab; `paintDownedZType(kind, seed, look)` for spitter (its acid sac burst, an acid puddle), screamer (mouth still wide, arms over its head), crawler (no legs, arms reaching on), shepherd (its shroud spread out, the bone crook beside it), soldier (camo, plate carrier, helmet), riot (black shell, visored helmet, shin guards); `paintDownedBoss(kind)` for the Wallbreaker (its concrete slab beside it) and the Broodmother (the mound collapsed, egg sacs burst, the crown of bone)). People (`paintDownedSurvivor(app, weapon, armour, pose, { ko, searched })`, `paintDownedEnemy(kit, colour, weapon, armour, seed, pose, opts)`): pose `"down"` (wounded or knocked out: curled on their side, an arm over the stomach, no pool; eyes shut) or `"dead"` (sprawled on their back in a pool, the weapon dropped by the hand). Anyone drawn by `paintSurvivor` (your people, or a unit carrying an appearance `t.app`) or `paintEnemyHuman` (any hostile kit, whatever its side) gets them through `unitDownCanvas(state, b, t, salt)`. A searched body (its raid container searched) has its pockets turned out and its weapon gone. Drawn flat by `drawDowned(ctx, cv, X, Y, ax, ay, { facing, scale })`: laid onto the projected floor axes so it turns with the view, in the painter's order with everything else; the tied-up rope still goes over a knocked-out prisoner. **Every body is searchable in a raid**: the dead too (combat.js `damage`: a body container with `zombie`, `ztype` and the unit's id): what the person had on them (`ZBODY`: a phone, lighters, smokes, a snack, a bandage kit, a ring or a watch 30%; a few rounds 8%; food 12%; a pistol with 3–8 rounds 2%; a bag 3%; a key to a nearby place 4%, `locks.js`), a dead soldier's or riot cop's kit as above; a spitter's acid still burns the hands that search it (4–8). One action like any container (two in the dark); the raid bot walks over only for the armoured dead and searches the others when it passes. Battles with a loot step (the bodies after a won fight on the map) already list the dead with their pockets; a dead soldier's body there holds his kit.
- **Jobs for other groups (`src/sim/jobs.js`, Groups tab and the Diplomacy window in `src/ui/diplomacy.js`):** known groups that are not hostile post jobs over the radio (in reach: 6% a day, traders and communes 8%) or at their gates (your team there: 60%, once every 3 days per group); at most one open offer and two jobs under way per group, six in all. An offer is a card (Accept / Maybe later / Decline) and stays on the Jobs list for 4 days (declining or leaving it to expire costs 1 goodwill). Types, each checked by the sim every hour: *clear a horde* within 6 tiles of them (gone or under 40% with your team having come within 1 tile; 12 days), *escort their caravan* to another group (it sets out when your team is at their base, raiders leave it alone while your team is within 2 tiles, done if your team was within 2 tiles for half its road hours; 10 days), *deliver* N of what they want (meds after sickness, food, ammo at war): a team at their base hands it over, or *Ask them to fetch it* and their caravan takes it from your trade base (10 days), *rescue their people* held by a war enemy or raiders (a card at the holders' gates: storm 60% of their guards, talk them out, or pay 40 a head; 14 days), *storm a hive* within 8 tiles of them with their fighters beside you (20 days), *scout a region* (70% of the tiles within 2 of a spot reported after the job; 12 days), *retrieve a package* from a site, a crash site if there is one (picked up by a team on the site, carried to their base or brought home for their caravan; a team lost with it drops it; 14 days). Rewards: 6 + 4 × size goodwill and one or two of supplies from their surplus (about 40 × size), gear you cannot craft, a manual or blueprint you lack (`blueprints.js blueprintReward`), 1–2 recruits (a card), intel (their region, hives and hordes near them) or, for a big job from a group with two or more bases, one of their outposts with two of their people as staff. Missing a deadline: −2 goodwill (small jobs) or −4; a job that can no longer be done through nobody's fault is called off at no cost. Accepted jobs show on the map (a gold *Job* tag, also in the tile tooltip) and in the planner (job targets first in *Move to a site*, a note when the trip's target is a job; the Jobs list's *Plan a trip…* opens the planner on it).
- **Groups and each other (`src/sim/diplomacy.js`, Diplomacy window → World affairs):** a relations matrix between the groups (−100..100: from their types and how close their bases are, drifting 2% a day back toward that; raids outside a war −12; a shared enemy +0.4 a day). Pacts at 28+ (5% a day, broken below 10): pact partners never raid each other and may join each other's wars. Wars at −42 or worse between groups within 18 tiles (1% a day × temper: raiders 1.5 … traders 0.1; three wars at most; 90 days of quiet after a peace) over land, resources or hive ground. Each side raids the other (6% a day, aggressive 9%): sieges as usual (you can answer their call for help), odds att / (att + defence), a win kills 5–10% (never below 3), loots 10%, may take a secondary base (35%) and a captive. Peace when a side is worn out, down to 6 people or after a long war: a lead of 5+ cedes a base (land), 2+ pays 25% tribute, else a white peace; captives go home. News comes over the radio (📻 in the log; the Affairs tab keeps it). A group at war pays 35% more for ammo and meds and 30% more for weapons and armor (both ways) and keeps twice its reserve of food, ammo, meds and fuel; arming one side costs 1 goodwill a deal with the other (3 a day at most). You can stay out, *broker a ceasefire* (both in radio reach, neither hostile, once a week: 20% + 3.5% a Speech point + their goodwill / 400 + weariness / 200; success +8 with both and a thank-you, failure −3), or *join a war* (from the Affairs tab or the card a friend sends when its war starts): the enemy turns hostile (−60) and its war raids come for your bases too, your side +10 and 25% stronger; if your side wins you get half the tribute, or the land it cedes is left free for your outpost for 60 days; afterwards the enemy settles at −35. *Leave the war* costs 8 goodwill with your side.
- **Blocs and wars with you (`src/sim/blocs.js`, `playerwars.js`; Groups tab "Blocs and wars", each group's card, Diplomacy window → World affairs; Help: Blocs and wars; `tools/diplo2test.js`):** AI groups ally into **blocs** by the same rules for every type (`groupcare.js` `GROUP_PRIORITIES` now has `ally`, `warlike`, `peaceful`; the AI-AI war temper reads `warlike`, the old TEMPER numbers): two groups outside any bloc and your coalition, with good relations (by type: communes and traders 18, fortress 22, military 25, expansionists 28, zealots 12, raiders −26), bases within 22 tiles and a **shared threat read from what they know** (a common war enemy; you, when both are hostile and know a base of yours, or your coalition when it holds 30% of the state; a third group both hate and have scouted; prey for raider gangs; a hive near order-keeping types) form one at 2.5% a day × type fit × √(ally × ally). Type lines: traders, communes and fortresses with each other and the military; the military only with order-keeping types (expansionists 0.3); raiders only with raiders (loose gangs: a third of the cohesion, half the help, a hungry raider may turn on a weaker gang-mate); zealots only with zealots. Up to 4 members (others join at 3% × ally). Members **defend each other** (a raid on one draws every member with a base within 16 tiles, each sending what it can spare: 30% of its strength, −10% a war it is in, less its wounded, × ally; at most the member's own strength + 4), **raid jointly** (a war raid, a raid on the bloc's foe, a war on you: members within 18 tiles at 35% × raid weight add 80% of their spare), **go to war together** (60% × ally on the defender's side, 35% × raid on the attacker's; one that stays out snubs it, −8), **share intel** (`f.knows` merged daily, the freshest look wins, and the latest scouting of you), **trade first** with each other (food bought from a member without the markup; convoys 60% to members) and grow closer (+0.4 relations a day). They **break** when a pair falls 25 under its line (the one that likes the others least leaves), on a **betrayal** (a vehicle theft found out between members, a raider turning on its gang: −40 with the victim, −15 with the others, out), when **interests diverge** (no shared threat for 45 days, bases drifted apart) or when a member joins your coalition alone. **An invitation to a bloc member goes to the bloc** (your invite, "Invite the whole bloc" on a member's card and in the coalition tab, a member's ask, the governor's): each member **votes agree, decline or break off** by a fixed score (goodwill to you around 45, its type's lean, a pact, its ties to the invited member and to the coalition's members, a foe shared with you or sitting in your coalition, a small hashed nudge; one that cannot sit in the coalition, hostile, at war with you or a member or under goodwill 25, breaks off). **Most agrees: the bloc joins together** (decliners bow to the majority and sign a pact if they had none; those that broke off leave the bloc and stay out, two or more keeping it); otherwise the invited group joins alone only if it agreed. The log names each member's vote, and the invite buttons preview today's vote. AI blocs facing the same foe **merge** by the same vote (the smaller bloc votes, 2% a day × ally). A bloc against a group hostile to you (or a hive) may **invite you** (all members know you, average goodwill 35+, in radio reach or next door; 3% a day, every 60 days at most): stand with it and its members send fighters against raids on your bases within 20 tiles (12% of their strength each, up to half your defence); striking at a member ends that (−15 each), and any big blow against a member costs a third of it with the others. Your assault on a member's base meets up to 4 extra fighters from its bloc (`conquest.js` `assaultForce`). Each bloc has a colour: a dashed ring of it round its members' bases on the map. **Wars with you:** every loss of goodwill you cause is filed as a grievance by its reason (raids and assaults, theft, executions of their people, holding their people, your bases spreading toward them, arming their enemies; fading 1% a day; their prisoners in your jails and your bases they know nearby count live). From day 40, one at a time and at least 80 days apart, a hostile group (a peaceful one also needs 25 grievance) with 10 fit people, no pact, no ceasefire and 120 days since its last war with you, that knows a base of yours within 30 tiles and thinks itself strong enough for it (its strength and its bloc's spare against the era's raid preparation × the defence it saw), **declares war** at 1.5% a day × warlike × (1 + grievance / 40) × 1.4 (spies saw rich stores) × 1.3 (hungry). Its **war goal** comes from its history: revenge, loot (a spy's report of rich stores, hunger), territory (an outpost of yours within 9 tiles: that base is its target), conversion (zealots) or breaking a rival (expansionists and the military, by your coalition's share of the land); the **casus belli** is its biggest grievance. Its bloc comes in with it. During the war: war raids every 9–15 days (one out at a time, on bases they know, joined by bloc members, outside the eras' pace but pushing it back for everyone else), a **siege** at the base they want (12 hours at the walls, siege kit), your convoys near their land robbed twice as often (peaceful types too) and your teams met 1.6× as often within 7 tiles. Score and **weariness**: their losses, hunger and deserters, raids you hold and bases you take wear them (× 0.6 + 0.4 × peaceful); your dead, lost bases and break-ins wear you. Every 10 days they send word **by radio or an envoy**: worn out (45+) they sue for peace (a ceasefire, your captives back, tribute when you lead by 5); ahead, goal met or you worn out, they offer **their terms** by goal (tribute 25%; the base they want; their prisoners back and 10%; a tithe and a convert; your nearest outpost and 15%). You can **offer terms** from their card every 5 days (ceasefire, tribute, their prisoners back, an outpost, their terms, or demand tribute or a base of theirs): the % shown is the chance they agree (type, weariness, score, how the terms answer their goal; zealots at war for the faith take nothing but conversion). Past 85 weariness they pull back; after 100 days 4% a day ends it in a white peace. A peace brings a 60-day ceasefire (40 for a white one: no raids from them), goodwill at least −30 (aggressive types drift back to hostile behind the ceasefire) and 60% of the grievances settled. **You can declare war** too (their card: for tribute, for land, or to break them): they turn hostile (−60), the pact and route go, their bloc joins them, their friends −4, infamy +8 unless they were a hostile gang. The autopilot braces (Cautious gives in when it is cheap), takes a peace offered and accepts terms that cost little against the hub's worth, more readily when the war goes badly.
- **The Calder Coalition (`src/sim/alliance.js`, Diplomacy window → Coalition & goals):** invite a group (Groups tab, radio panel or the Coalition tab) when it is in radio reach, your team is at its gates, one of your bases is within 8 tiles of one of its bases or a member has a base within 12 tiles of it, its goodwill is 50+ (more for a peaceful group once you have conquered others: 50 + infamy / 2), it has a pact with you and you did a job for it (or the council votes it in: members with relations 10+ toward it, a majority). Qualifying groups also ask to join (a card). Members share every horde within 12 tiles and every hive and hive site within 15 of their bases (and the land within 3) each day, send fighters against raids on your bases within 20 tiles (strength × 0.15 each, up to half your own defence), harry a horde heading for your base once (15% each, 30% at most), and send fighters to your hive assaults within 15 tiles (1 per 10 people, 1–3 each; each keeps one of the brood off your squad, 40% at most); they never raid you or each other and do not go to war with each other. A member leaves below goodwill 20, without a pact, or when it attacks another member. The council meets at the start of every season (days 15, 43, 71, …) and asks for supplies for a member (send: +6 / +3 to the rest; refuse: −4 with all), a job (refuse: −3 with all) or a ruling between two members (side with one: +6 / −8 and they fall out; or a compromise, a Speech check).
- **Alliance victory and goals:** the coalition is its own way to win, next to destroying every hive: it wins when it has at least one member and holds more than half the land (hives standing or not), and keeps that up for a year (the Calder Accord, see *Eras and winning*). Groups outside the coalition no longer block it: conquest counts (break them, claim the land up to their gates, or leave them be); the Goals panel still lists them with what to do about them. Land: your claims (below) plus land tiles within 9 of your hub and of every base you took from a group, 7 of an outpost (8 with walls standing) and of a member's base (`alliance.js controlRadius`; a member's land counts only once it has been in the coalition a year, `ALLIANCE.LAND_AFTER` 112 days, and while it is at war with nobody, you or any group: `alliance.js memberLand`, the join day kept in `alliance.joined`, a save without one counts the member as joining on the load day; the Goals card and the Coalition tab show each member's land as counting, "counts from day N" or "not counted: at war", and the share once every member at peace has its year; the bandwagon goodwill, the closed state (no new gangs or bands), the blocs' fear of the coalition and the rival war goal read the same counted share, only the governor's land planner counts the members that wait for their year, so it does not found outposts over their land; × the square root of the world's area on other sizes: Small 7 / 5, Large 11 / 9, Huge 14 / 11, so it takes about as many bases on any map). On Normal at the start your hub holds about 10% of the land; every group's bases at 7 tiles plus the hub come to about 50–60%, so a coalition of everyone left plus two or three outposts or captured bases gets there. A member's radio carries an invitation: a group within 12 tiles of a member's base is in reach. The first victory ends the run with its screen; after *Keep playing* the other one can still come, once, with its own (both at once: the coalition's is named). The Goals card at the top of the Groups tab (and in full on the Coalition tab) shows both: hives destroyed and left (and being built); land held against 50%, members of groups still standing, and every group outside with what to do about it (invite them now, win them over and why not yet, or defeat them: hostile, their bases and the fewest defenders at one), your infamy, and the bases of yours other groups hold (to retake). The timelapse captions the coalition's victory.
- **Land claims and wells (`src/sim/claims.js`, `orders.js`, `economy.js`, `alliance.js`, `ui/render.js`, `ui/expedition.js`, `main.js`; or `node tools/claimstest.js [seed]`, a few seconds):** a team on a land tile bordering land you hold (a base, the ground right around it or another claim) gets the order **Claim this tile** (team panel, right-click menu, orders card): 2 hours on the spot and 3 scrap carried for the marker post. The tile counts as land the coalition holds (`controlledLand`), toward the 50% the alliance victory needs, so claims chain outward from your bases to the contested ground; the Territory layer draws them in a lighter blue and the hover text says *Claimed by you (day N)*. Not on water, a base, a hive or the ground a group's base holds; another group's claim bordering yours can be claimed over. A claim is lost when a group founds or takes a base on or within 2 tiles of it (the group gets the claim), a hive rises there, or a hostile team or band (a hostile group's party, raiders from outside, a hardcore band) stands on it at the day tick (`claimsDaily`). **Dig a well** (24 hours, 15 scrap, 1 part carried) on a claim of yours next to one of your bases: beside a river or lake 10 water a day, farmland 6, forest 4, hills 2, suburbs and city 2, mountains none; it holds 1,500–4,000 (the dice and the ground), gives half under 20% left and runs dry at 0 (a log line). The base's people haul it into `waterProduction.hauled` (at most one well per 4 people at the base, the best first); the base card's water line lists the wells. `state.claims = { [tile]: { owner, since, well? } }`, plain data in a save; older saves have none.
- **Field works (`src/sim/fieldworks.js`, the base window's **Field works** tab and the map in `src/ui/fieldworks.js`; or `node tools/fieldworkstest.js [seed]`, a few seconds):** structures a base builds on the land it holds outside its walls (its ground or a claim of yours, within 3 tiles; hub 6, outpost 4, shelter 2, camp none). Pick a kind on the tab (cost chips, green when the base has it) and click a green tile on the map (Esc cancels; the clock waits), or right-click a tile of yours: *Plan … here*. The materials leave the stores at once; the base's builders walk out and put it up (the work-hours plus 1 hour a tile there and back for each 8 hours of work), every other hour while the base's own queue has work, every hour when it has none; enemies on the site stop the work. Kinds: **Roadblock** (road tiles; 10 scrap, 150 firewood, 10 h, 60 condition): a hostile party or a horde entering the tile is held while it forces a lane through (60% of its condition at 4 + 0.4 × the party's strength an hour, at most 10 h; a horde 3 + 0.05 a head, at most 12 h), and the roadblock takes that damage; your teams and friends pass, and a group's roadblock stops your teams (hostile 3 h and a beating, otherwise a 1-hour checkpoint). **Field well** (12 scrap, 1 part, 20 h, 50): a claim well's yield by the ground and its 1,500–4,000 water, hauled home by the base's people (`waterProduction.field`, one well per 4 people after the claim wells); deep ground water carries half the ground's worm eggs (`worm.js rawWaterEggs`), still un-purified: boil it. **Watch tower** (15 scrap, 120 firewood, 18 h, 70): post 1–2 lookouts (the tab's *Post…*): they live there (location `post`: fed, healed and counted by their base through `residents`, out of the base for its work and its battles: `atBase` leaves them out), one alone sleeps 22:00–06:00, two keep watch round the clock; manned it shows the map 3 tiles round it by day, 1 at night (`liveTiles`), and the lookouts warn the base of raids and hordes in its sight (`watch.js lookoutsSpot`, the warning card names the tower); unmanned it sees nothing; a lit, manned tower draws a roaming horde within 3 tiles now and then. **Relay mast** (15 scrap, 3 parts, 24 h, 40, Mechanics 3 at the base): a relay node (`radio.js radioNodes`, 6 tiles, links like a base's relay), 1 part every 10 days. **Stockpile** (8 scrap, 90 firewood, 8 h, 50): a drop point of 200 kg; teams leave their finds there (*Leave finds*, keeping their food, water, meds and ammo) and *Load* it; *Send a haul team* (two people and the base's best running vehicle) loads it and brings it home; raiders passing over it on their way to you empty it. **Threats:** a horde entering the tile batters it (10 + 5 × its wall power; a tower's lookouts shoot first); a site going up loses half its work; a falling tower's lookouts run for it (half hurt, 1 in 10 killed). A hostile group within 10 tiles may send a small raid at one (`fwraid` parties: 2% a day, more for a full stockpile, half for a group only aggressive): the lookouts fight it by the numbers (`combat.js winOdds`, a tower ×1.5); beaten, the raiders take it over when it is near their land (half the time, never a relay) or wreck it, and empty a stockpile. Alerts (the news lines) when one is damaged badly, taken or destroyed. **Repairs** (the tab's *Repair* and *Repair all*, the 🔧 on the map: amber under 70%, red under 35%): 60% of its cost × the share missing, half its build hours × that share plus the walk, by the builders. *Demolish*: half its materials back. **Your teams at a group's structure** (right-click, the team menu, the orders card): *Wreck* (3 h), *Take* (4 h: it becomes yours, held from your nearest base) or *Take their stockpile's goods* (1 h), a fight by the numbers first with its guards; goodwill −8 with a group not hostile. **The groups (AI parity):** every 5 days a group may put one up on its land near its main base (`groupFieldDaily`: a tower when threatened, a well, a stockpile with 15% of its stores, a roadblock on a road for aggressive types; 1 + people / 12, at most 4), standing in 3 days, mended from their scrap; their manned towers add 10% each (2 at most) to their base's defence (`groupworks.js groupBaseDefence`), their wells count as water works, their masts extend their radio network. **The governor and a base leader** (`autopilot.js governBase`, 08:00): repairs below 70%, a well when the water runs short, a tower when the base is threatened (the dead within 6, a hive within 10, a hostile group within 12, a raid coming), one lookout at 10 people, two at 20. Hooks for the outdoor job trips: `waterSourcesFor(state, base)`, `dropPointsFor(state, base)` and `depositAt(state, fw, key, qty)`, `outOfBase(s)`. `state.fieldworks = { list, jobs, news }`, plain data in a save; older saves have none.
- **Trouble on job trips, and the trips' follow-ups (`src/sim/tripevents.js`, `tripgear.js`, `tripland.js` PACES, `jobtrips.js` drop legs, `grouptrips.js` groupTripLosses, `hunting.js` cabinGain, `ui/workermap.js`; or `node tools/tripeventstest.js [seed] [trials=600]`, a minute or two):** a worker out alone on a job trip meets trouble now and then (TRIP_EV.rate 0.4% an hour out, × the pace, × fog, snow and storms: about 2.4% of a six-hour wood trip, one meeting every 2–3 days at a base with six outdoor workers): 1–3 of the wandering dead (more on infected ground, near a horde, at dusk), a wild animal by the ground and the season (a wolf, a wild boar, feral dogs, a bear: none in winter), the job's own accident (an axe slip, a falling branch, broken glass, a fall through a rotten floor, a slip into cold water, a turned ankle on the way), getting lost (1–3 hours; Scouting and a light help), a lone stranger (a trade, or an armed one who wants the load) or a small find (scrap, tins, pills, a knife or a light). Rolled once a trip on the trip's own dice (the seed, the person, the day and the trip: never the world's rng). The one person deals with it with what they are and carry: their fighting worth (combatPower; a gun with no rounds at the base is a club), Scouting and Stealth to get away, a tourniquet or kit to stop a bleed (used up), Speech with a robber. They put the trouble down or drive it off (a boar is 16 kg of meat home), run (dropping the load), get hurt (real wounds, a deep cut, a sprain, a chest cold from cold water) and, rarely, die: an ungeared worker about 0.4% of meetings with three of the dead or a bear, a geared one about never. Shots spend the base's rounds and make noise (base.tripNoise into the base's Heat; roaming hordes within 3 tiles may turn toward them). A bear or a stranger at a base you run yourself brings a card (Back away slowly / Keep still / Stand and fight; Hand it over / Stand firm / Run for it; Trade / Wave them on) and the worker holds where they are ("Facing a bear") until it is answered, at most TRIP_EV.hold (3) hours, then does what seems best; under the governor or a base leader it is decided at once. One log line each; the map shows a "!" over the walker ("?" a small thing, "+" a find, ✝ where someone fell).
  Gear (`tripgear.js`): the governor's and the leaders' hourly gear pass, Assign all idle and the Work now panel's **Gear outdoor workers** hand the outdoor workers what the armory can spare: a weapon for the job (a hunter a gun or bow with rounds or arrows, the others a quiet melee weapon, a woodcutter's axe first), a light for the dusk jobs, light body armour (≤ 6 kg), a tourniquet (one kept for a team) or a med kit (one kept for the medics). The Work now panel's chips warn **No weapon**, **No rounds** and **No light**. The groups (`grouptrips.js` groupTripLosses): their workers out (15% of their people) each have 1.2e-5 / g² a day of being killed and 0.004 / g of a wound (g: their arms × kit, a commune 0.6, a military group 1.6–2): a group of 30 loses one person in 20–50 years and has 5–9 wounded a year.
  The outdoor work pace (Settings → Options → Play, saved with the game): Relaxed (1.3× the walking, 0.7× the trouble), Normal, Brisk (0.75× the walking, 1.35× the trouble). A camp fishes and fetches water from water right next to it that nobody holds (a camp holds only its own tile).
