The governor builds a hunting cabin where its wider grounds pay (cabinGain ≥ 1.3: the best hunt 2 tiles out against the tiles next to the base, walking counted), not by the game land within 2. Stockpiles as drop points: a trip that is not its window's last leaves the load at a stockpile of the base nearer the work than the base is (the walk back that much shorter), the next sets out again from there, and only the window's last trip (or one when the stockpile is full) walks home ("Dropping 140 firewood at stockpile (x,y)"); the tile choice counts it (tripland.js tileFactor), so far ground near a stockpile is worth working (a woodcutter 2 tiles out: 162 → 182 firewood a day with a stockpile beside the ground); a haul team brings the stockpile in. Hover a worker on the map: their route, faintly (out dashed, back dotted, to the drop).
- **Eras and winning (`src/sim/eras.js`, every number in `ERAS`; `outbreak.js`):** years are the game's calendar years (four seasons of 28 days = 112 days; year 1 is days 1–98, year 5 starts on day 435, year 6 on 547, year 10 on 995, year 15 on 1555). *Early game* (years 1–4, the first winters and the first expansion): nothing below grows in years 1–2. *Mid game* (years 5–9, the long war, as the hives evolve to tier 2; retimed from years 3–7 by the progression census of 2026-10-09, `review/progression-census-2026-10-09.md`): from year 3 the threats grow every year to a cap: new roaming, attacking and border hordes +3% a year (at most +30%; the hives multiplying and their growths are the main growth: about 6 standing in year 1, 9 in year 5, 11 from year 10), the aggressive groups' strength +2% a year (better arms; raids, wars, the bases they defend; at most +20%), they recruit up to 45 + 1 a year (at most 55), new gangs from beyond the border arrive with 12–18 + 1 a year people (at most +10), outsider raider bands go past their first-year ceiling of 14 people and power 6.0 by 1 person every four years and +0.05 power a year (at most +3 / +0.5). *Late game* (year 10 on, the reckoning, as the hives reach tier 3 and the victories open; year 8 before 2026-10-09): the growth nears its caps, new gangs keep coming whenever fewer than 2 hostile aggressive groups are left (`ERAS.LATE_MIN_AGG` can raise that), and every coalition member sends two more fighters to your hive and base assaults (the coalition has grown into an army); a coalition that holds over half the land wins the peaceful groups over with time (+0.25 goodwill a day, up to 60: `ERAS.BANDWAGON`) and closes the state to new gangs and raider bands digging in. **Fewer, bigger threats** (`ERAS.RAIDS`, `ERAS.HORDES`): raids on your bases (a group's raid, a war raid, a grudge raid, an outsider band coming for a base, together) come at a pace of about 3 a game year in years 1–2, 5 from year 3, 7 from year 5, 10 from year 8 and 14 from year 10 (not a cap: the next one may start 112 / pace × 0.75–1.25 days after the last began, and only if someone is ready then; in practice about half of that); a group raids a base only if its whole strength reaches 0.8 × the base's defence with everyone at a post (0.95 from year 3, 1.05 from year 5, 1.15 from year 8, 1.25 from year 10) and sizes the raid to 0.6–1.0 × that defence early, up to 0.85–1.15 × from year 10; an outsider band goes for a base only at 0.6–1.05 × its defence by the same years. Hordes a hive sends at your bases come at a pace of 6 a year early, 10 from year 3, 14 from year 5, 18 from year 8, 22 from year 10, 26 from year 13 (in between the hives' hordes go for the groups or roam; wandering and border hordes that stumble on a base are not paced: empty outposts in hive land are overrun often). **The hives late** (`ERAS.BROOD`, `HIVE_CAP`): the brood inside a hive fights 3.5% harder a year from year 4 (at most +30%: `hives.js broodForce`), and from year 8 no new build site is picked while 11 hives stand or are being built. **New gangs and raider bands do not dig in on the coalition's land** (`alliance.js coalitionMask`): holding the land keeps the gangs out. **The Balanced autopilot pushes to win in the late game** (`PERSONALITIES.balanced.lateConquer`): from year 10 it storms any hostile group's base, and any group that will not join (goodwill below zero), at 70% odds. **The outbreak beyond the border** (`outbreak.js`): until it burns out in a year between 11 and 14 (fixed per world from its seed) the state never runs out of hives: when fewer than 2 stand or are being built (1 from year 8), a mass of about 1,100 of the dead gathers on open ground near the border 20–40 days later (8+ tiles from any base) and starts building a hive at once: about four weeks to strike it, as any build site. Once it has burned out, no new hive site is picked at all and the hives left starve (their daily resources × 0.4, `ERAS.OUTBREAK.STARVE`), so the last hives can be crawled out (progression census 2026-10-09). **Winning:** no victory before year 10 (day 995). The hive victory needs every hive destroyed and none being built after the outbreak beyond the border has burned out, and then the state kept clear for 28 days (a hive or a started site sets it back to zero). The coalition victory (the Calder Accord) needs you and at least one member holding over half the land (groups outside do not block it) kept for 112 days of the late game (the clock runs from year 10: a coalition that held the land by year 3 used to win on the first day of year 8; each day it holds counts one, a day it fails takes two back; a gang that has just come over the border or a raider band that dug in does not count against it for its first 56 days: break it or win it over), with a coalition at least 12 seasons old. Coalition members also fight beside you when you storm a group's base: each member with a base within 15 tiles that is not on good terms with the defenders keeps 1 per 10 of its people (1–3, +2 in the late game) of the defenders busy, at most 40% of them (`alliance.js assaultAllies`, `conquest.js assaultForce`). The Goals card shows the year and era, both clocks and how far the coalition's age has got; Help → *Eras and winning* says the same in the game. `tools/eratest.js` checks it; `tools/balancesim.js` with a long run (over 730 days, e.g. `node tools/balancesim.js 1666 --runs 50`) adds a year-by-year table.
- **Fights by the numbers, calibrated on the battle map (`combat.js winOdds` / `ODDS`, `breakoff.js BREAKOFF`, `tools/calibrate.js`):** a fight nobody plays on the map (the Auto-resolve button, the autopilot, crews, the headless tools) is as likely to be won and as deadly as the same fight played out by the AI on the tactical map. The odds are a logistic of the power ratio (squad over enemy, × 1.4 for striking first) with the numbers on each side for people (3 against 5 weak raiders is a coin flip), the dead about half as strong per point of power as people, a mixed crowd in between and a little for a hive's brood (the cards, the planner, conflicts, assaults and the autopilot all use it). The blood: each member of a team that loses a fight fought out goes down 80% of the time (92% against the dead), in a won fight 5% + 42% × the chance it had of going the other way (whoever falls in a won fight falls in its last round: nobody breaks off a fight they are winning because of it), and of the rest about half are wounded. Over 1,100 grid fights (`tools/calibrate.js`): the map wins 70% with 1.44 dead a fight, by the numbers 66% with 1.58 (before, 52% and 0.10). The retreat policies still save most of those lives (`tools/retreattest.js`). The autopilot's fight choices count the blood a won fight costs.
- **Conquest (`src/sim/conquest.js`, mission *Assault their base*, right-click a group's base):** a tactical battle against the base's defenders behind their walls (the "baseAssault" map: their wall line with a gate and breaches, barricades behind it; headless and on the autopilot the usual statistical resolver with the walls as a multiplier). Defenders: the group's people shared over its bases (the main base counts double), 60% of them fighting (3–16), each as tough as a rescue's guards (2.6 + arms × 1.6), × (1 + 0.05 a wall point: a fortress or military base 7, a group's main base 4, any other 1). The planner and the card warn hard against a group that is not hostile: attacking costs 30 goodwill at once, ends any pact, and a coalition member you attack leaves (−5 with every other member). Win: the base is your outpost (battered walls, their bunkhouse, half of that base's share of their stores; it reaches 9 tiles of land); one of the team (the weakest fighter, if the team has 3+; the autopilot keeps its squad together) stays to hold it, the rest wait for orders; a base with nobody in it is land you hold: raiders loot it and the dead wreck it, but it is not lost; of the defenders who live 40% get away to their other bases, of those who give up a quarter ask to join you (a recruit card, at most 3) and a fifth are taken prisoner (at most 3), your people held there walk free. Their main base: the leaders fall with it and half of those left scatter. A group down to 3 people breaks up. A group with no base left (or 2 people) is broken (`factions.js collapseFaction`). Fallout: pact partners of the victim −8 goodwill (−15 when you finish it), its friends (relations 28+) −4 (−8), its enemies +2 (+4). Infamy: +2 a base taken from a hostile raider-type group (breaking bandits worries nobody much), +15 from anyone else (+10 more for finishing it); −0.1 a day; above 20 peaceful groups you know lose (infamy − 20) / 200 goodwill a day and want goodwill 50 + infamy / 2 (at most 90) before they join the coalition. A failed assault: the defenders lose 60% of those you killed, a beaten team may lose one to capture.
- **Losing bases, retreat and the hub:** a raid that overruns a base of yours may **raze** it (aggressive gangs, `razeChance` above; the warning card says "<gang> burn what they cannot hold" when it is 15%+): every building and parked vehicle goes, the stock is carried off or burned, the people walk to your nearest base, and the tile is a ruin (a little to scavenge; a new outpost there needs only 5 scrap). Your hub burns 0.4× as often, a base with nobody in it 0.5×. The hub razed is the hub lost (the nearest base becomes the hub, else the exiles). Otherwise a raid that overruns a base of yours (or breaks into an outpost, a third as likely) may keep it (`keepChance` above; the hub × 0.35): it goes on their bases list with its land, half of what is left in it goes into their stores, the raid damage stays on its buildings, and a garrison of 60% of the raid's head count (at least 2) holds it. Your people there walk to your nearest other base as a team, carrying what they can (water and food for 4 days, 15 scrap, ammo, meds; one vehicle; the others are left on the tile, marked). The raid warning card offers *Retreat to <base>* (or *Abandon <base> and take to the road* from your last one): everyone leaves before the raid arrives and the raiders take the base without a fight. A base battle (raiders, outsiders, a horde breaking in, a rescue raid) has a way out on the side away from the attack: pulling out of a group's raid abandons the base to them as above; outsiders loot it as an overrun and move on; the dead tear through it (half the food, water and meds spoiled, the walls battered) and move on, and everyone who got out comes back; a rescue raid frees its people. Raiders from outside the state never keep a base (they are passing through): an overrun is looted and wrecked but stays yours. Lose the hub with another base left and the nearest one becomes the hub (the buildings stay where they are); with no base left your people are one team on the road (exiles) that founds a new base (*Establish base*: a staging camp as hardcore's first hub, a little cheaper: **10 scrap, 1 part, 8 food, 8 water** and 40 person-hours of work, about a day for three; it becomes a shelter, built up into the hub later like hardcore's) or takes one back (a camp still standing then is struck, its stores left as a cache); the run ends only when nobody is left. The holders patch a base up (each broken building 5% a day, wear −1 a day; the garrison grows back 0.2 a day), so it is easiest to take back right after it fell; the tile panel says who holds it since when, what is still broken and the garrison, a card and the Goals card name it to retake, and *Retake the base* on it is the same assault: won, it is yours again with its buildings as they stand and the stock they left. If its holders are broken and nobody takes the tile, it comes back to you empty.
- **Wants and surplus, the market board (`src/sim/trade.js` marketLists, Diplomacy window → Market board):** every group's lists are made again every 7 days from its real stock, kind and state (`demand`: a group at war wants ammo, a sick one meds): *wants* (demand 1.25+: the quantity back to comfortable, at least half a usual lot, and what they pay per unit for a usual lot, before Speech) and *surplus* (demand 0.9 or less with 5+ to spare: the quantity and what they charge; never what their kind refuses). Shown on each group's card in the Groups tab and in its radio panel (with arrows against last week). The market board lists every group you know in radio reach (not hostile) for a good you pick, best to sell to or best to buy from first, with arrows, what they want and have spare, and a shortcut to their radio panel. Shortages hit at random (1% a day per group: a lost harvest, sickness, a fuel fire, a lost ammo store; news on the radio) and the lists are made again at once. Their radio offers and requests and the caravans you ask for use these lists.
- **Autopilot and diplomacy:** the governor accepts jobs it can do (deliveries the hub can spare, fetched by their caravan; scouting and packages within 12 tiles with 4+ people at home, sent as recon trips; Aggressive also culls a small horde), keeps in touch with every group it knows in radio reach (a greeting whenever one is due, a scan of the airwaves once a week) and signs the pact each will sign (non-aggression, then trade), builds a radio station from a dozen people (once it has the parts) and a radio relay at any base where one would link and bring a known group (not hostile) into reach (saved up for when radio reach is what keeps that group out of the coalition; a supply run carries the parts an outpost lacks for it), sends envoys to groups out of reach (two people with the hub's surplus as gifts while goodwill is short, a pact, and the invitation at their gates once they qualify; one per group every 20 days), welcomes groups that ask to join and invites every group that qualifies, stays out of wars on Cautious (Balanced and Aggressive join only beside a much stronger friend against a group that is no friend of yours), and on Aggressive sells ammo over the radio to groups at war (once every 8 days).
- **Hordes (`src/sim/hordes.js`):** each has a job (guard, build, mass, roam, attack, border) and a **score** from its size (size^0.8 / 3.4: about 3 small, 8 medium, 16 large, 33 at 400) that sets how hard it is to hide from and how a fight with it goes (horde cards, culls) and what it adds to a new hive. A horde moves at most **2 tiles a day** (12 hours a tile, slower in deep cold and snow: weather.js `hordeSpeedFactor`; a quarter faster with 10%+ runners, `hivetech.js`), along a land path (rivers at bridges). Hordes on the same errand that meet merge (up to 600; the rest stays a horde of its own), very big wandering ones split now and then, and a split always leaves at least 10 in each part. Wandering hordes rot slowly (1.2% a day) and drift toward people (more often as the year goes on). You see hordes only near your bases (further with a watchtower), with teams in radio range, through a team's report (recon, encounters) or a trader's sightings; where you saw one last stays as a faded "N?" ghost. Cull or lure them away; a horde cut below 5 is gone from the map.
- **Defences against hordes (`src/sim/defences.js`):** a base holds back a horde up to its **hold limit**: 15 + 18 × wall strength (hub 69 / 123 / 177 / 231 for wall tiers 0–3, outposts 33 / 87 / 141 / 195), plus 2 per point of combat power on guard duty (everyone at 0.8 for two days after *Man the defences*), times the defence buildings: **barricades** (8 scrap, 10 h, up to 3: +12% each, full cover on the battle map; one is wrecked in each break-in fight), **spike pits** (6 scrap, 8 h, up to 2, one use: the next horde loses 7% + 4 per pit before the walls, and the pits hurt the dead on the battle map), **trench and stakes** (12 scrap, 30 h: +25%, and the dead that reach it lose their turn), **reinforced gate** (14 scrap + 1 part, 20 h: +20% and two more barricades in battle), **floodlights** (8 scrap + 2 parts, 12 h: burns 2 fuel per attack for +10%, and defenders aim +10 at night, +4 by day). A **Wallbreaker** cuts the limit to 60% (two: 35%) and smashes a defence each attack (a barricade, then the gate, else a wall section 40% of the time). A horde at or under the limit is beaten off at the walls (it falls back 4 tiles and is not drawn back to that base for 10 days); a bigger one **breaks in**: the walls still hold back half the limit and the rest (up to 20 on the map, power 1.5 + their number / (5 × units), up to 6, tougher on a hive's land, plus the Wallbreaker) is fought on the base's battle map by the best 8 there, with the barricades, pits and trench on it. In the browser that is the tactical battle; headless and on autopilot the same battle is played out by the AI on both sides, so the odds match. Lose it and the base is **overrun**: the downed have a better chance at home (40%), everyone else there has a 40% chance of a 20–60 wound, a third of the food, water and meds is spoiled, an outpost is lost; a hub with nobody left alive ends the game. The base panel shows "Holds a horde of up to ~N (M with a Wallbreaker)". When lookouts see a horde heading for a base within 6 tiles you get a **warning card** with the same figure: *Man the defences*, *Burn a decoy fire* (6 fuel; 25–80% by its size, 15% with a Wallbreaker) or hold.
- **One shared clock** for the map and every scavenging run.
- **Seasons and weather** (`src/sim/weather.js`): a year is 4 seasons of 28 days (112 days) and a run starts on day 15 of spring. Every day has one regional weather record (clear, overcast, rain, storm, fog, heat wave, dry spell, snow, blizzard, hard frost) drawn by season from the seed on its own random stream, with warm and cold spells that last a few days, plus ground that carries over: mud after rain and thaw, snow cover that builds up and melts, soil moisture. Averages: spring 13 / 3 °C, summer 26 / 15 °C, autumn 13 / 4 °C, winter 1 / -8 °C. *Regional weather (fronts):* the season, the temperatures and the ground are statewide, but a day's storms, blizzards and fog come as **weather fronts** (`state.weather.fronts`, `FRONT_KINDS`): thunderstorms (radius 5–8 tiles on a Normal map, 0.9–1.5 tiles an hour), rain belts (7–12, 0.7–1.2), blizzards (7–11, 0.6–1.0) and snow squalls (6–10, 0.7–1.2) come in at a map edge (mostly the west; more from the north in winter, the south in summer), head for a point inside the map and cross it over hours to a couple of days (72 hours at most, growing over 3 hours and fading over the last 6); fog banks (6–10) form inland before dawn and burn off in 8–14 hours. A stormy, blizzard or foggy day brings 1–2 of its fronts (45% a second) and the background sky between them is rain, snow or overcast; each season adds its own (spring: rain belts 30% a day, thunderstorms 6%; summer: thunderstorms 14%, rain 12%; autumn: rain 30%, storms 5%, fog 10%; winter: snow squalls 30%, blizzards 4%, fog 5%), rain falling as snow below freezing. Bigger maps have bigger fronts (up to ×1.4). Fronts are rolled a day at a time from their own seeded stream. `weather(state)` / `wxNow` is the weather at the hub (`weatherAt(state, tile)`, `wxAt(state, x, y)` for any tile): a team's pace, sight, ambushes, road injuries, travel events (the Storm card only comes under rain or snow, mostly under a storm or blizzard front), boat crossings, hunting, fishing, foraging, woodcutting and surveys go by the weather where it is, a horde's pace by the weather where it is; the hub's crops, rain water, wells, thirst, exposure and heating by the hub's own day (a front that sat over it 3 hours for a storm or blizzard, 6 for the rest). The top bar shows the hub's weather (and ⛈️ in Nh when a storm or blizzard front will reach it within 12 hours); its tooltip lists the fronts on the map with distance, direction and when they reach the hub ("Thunderstorm 6 tiles west, here by evening (5 h)"). Alerts: a storm or blizzard heading for the hub within 12 hours. *Frozen rivers:* a freezing index (degree-days below 0 °C, minus 3× the warmth above, at most 45) on each day freezes the rivers' northern reaches at 30 and the far south at 45, so they freeze from the north down in deep winter (about 3 weeks a year up north, 2 down south, none in the south in a mild winter) and thaw the other way. Frozen river tiles (drawn as pale ice) can be walked, 1.75 hours a tile, never driven (bikes neither): by your teams, other groups and hordes, so a river-protected base is open until the thaw (a log line and an alert: "River by X frozen: the dead can cross"). Within 5 of the freezing point the ice is thin: a team on foot goes through on 6% of the tiles it enters (3–8 damage, 2 hours lost). When the ice melts, anyone on it gets ashore and every way over it is planned again (`src/sim/rivers.js`). *Travel:* every step's cost is multiplied by the weather of the hour it is taken in: mud slows off-road travel (vehicles about twice as much as walkers), snow cover slows everything (off-road most), storms and blizzards slow everyone a lot (up to ×3) and can hurt someone on the road (1.5% / 2.5% an hour on foot, half in a vehicle), fog and blizzards cut sight by a tile and make it easier to be ambushed, storms bring more road events, and boat mishaps are 1.5–2.5× as likely in wind and storms. The whole year's weather is fixed ahead (rolled day by day from the seed as far as anything asks), and estimates (the planner, panels, queue, the map's estimated position) step through the same hours with the ground and the fronts already on the map when the estimate is made (they keep their course and speed): a front that forms later can still slow the team. The planner's ETA says how many hours the weather adds. *Forecast:* hover the season and weather next to the clock for the effects and the next 3 days (4 with a watchtower, 5 with a radio station), less sure the further out. *Farming hooks:* growth factor (0–1.3) per crop and day, death chances (frost, hard freeze, blizzard, drought, heat, storm) and rot (wet or snow), growing seasons per crop, rain water per collector and a well factor; also cold / heat exposure risk, a horde speed factor in deep cold and a food-need factor and the thirst factor (`thirstFactor`: water a person drinks that day by season and heat; used by the farming, sickness, horde and water systems). The *Weather* map layer washes the land white with snow, dark with rain and warm in heat (one fill, in the cached map) and draws the fronts as faint drifting clouds (a few cached puff images a front, on the moving layer; they glide while the clock runs, and with Map animations off move only when the map draws anyway); the *Weather fronts* layer (off by default; Shift+W, rebindable) shows them strongly with their edge, name and a 6-hour heading arrow, and a tile's tooltip names the front over it and frozen river. With Reduce motion the fronts move an hour at a time; their colours (blue-grey, white, grey) carry no red/green meaning, so the colour-blind palette leaves them as they are. The minimap shows frozen river as pale ice; the sound follows the weather where the camera is (ui/audio.js weatherAt); the Codex has a Weather page for each kind of front and the frozen river, opened the first time one is on the map. The timelapse shows the season by the date and the statewide wash (not the fronts). Season changes and stormy days in the region are logged. The autopilot launches no trip while a storm or blizzard is over the hub, and no trip longer than a day with one forecast in the region in the next two.
- **Autopilot (dev tool):** the dev panel's *Autopilot* checkbox (or P) hands the holdout to an AI governor (`src/sim/autopilot.js`) so you can watch it play itself; turn it off and you are back in control with everything as it stands. It runs from the hourly tick (`autopilotHour`, daily planning at 07:00 in `autopilotDaily`) and only uses the same calls as the UI and what you know of the map (knowledge, markers, known groups). While it is on, battles resolve by the numbers (auto-combat; restored when you switch off, and a battle already open is played out by the AI), scavenging uses the hour-by-hour search, and cards, searches and trades never stop the clock. Every move is in the log with 🤖 (role changes as one line a day). What it does:
  - *Roles* every hour, per base: patients and the worn-out rest (up to a third), farmers for the plots in use (more when food is short of the food planner's target; the plots get their tenders first while the winter stores fall short, but one hand stays free for a queued build; extra plots only while there are hands to tend them), an operator for the preserving kitchen when fresh food would otherwise spoil (stored outside, or the summer and autumn surplus), whoever is left farms too while food is short (base scavenging is a last resort), guards against a spotted raid or a horde at the door (and a standing watch against known raiders or hordes in sight), medics while anyone is hurt, builders while something is queued, operators for stations worth running (ammo, meds, parts, by stock), one crafter while the craft queue has work, the rest scavenge.
  - *Buildings* through `canBuild` / `queueBuild`, saving scrap for what matters: rain catchers and wells when water runs short (cisterns when it overflows), beds when they run short (`nextHousing`: a bunkhouse while building plots are plenty, a barracks once a quarter of them or fewer are free or the bunkhouses are at their max, while a part is left after paying for it, else a bunkhouse; with no plot free a bunkhouse expanded into a barracks), farm plots, food storage before it is needed (root cellars, then a cold store or a storehouse, as soon as the fresh food plus what the fields still bring would fill the room; urgent once food is stored outside), a drying rack and a preserving kitchen for the summer surplus, a greenhouse before winter (saved for when the winter stores fall short), walls when threatened (and when a horde in sight would break in), then barricades, spike pits, a trench, the reinforced gate and floodlights once hordes are a known danger, the hospital tier by tier, watchtower, med station, storehouse, machine shop, radio, garage; outposts get a farm plot, beds and walls (and a root cellar once four or more live there and the food fills most of the pantry). *Growing outwards* (`hubCramped`): when the hub has outgrown its plots (no building plot free, nothing more it can build or expand for beds, and its people within two of its beds) it founds one outpost more than its style would once no outpost it founded has a bed free, its outposts build their beds ahead (three free) with the scrap the supply runs bring, and up to three people at a time (farmers first, the medics, builders and station hands last) move to an outpost it founded (not a base taken from a group: those stand out in the wild) with beds free, summer water for them and a supply line of strength 0.5 or better while the hub has 10 days of food a head (`movers`; a supply run that disbands there with 4 food a head). *Crafting*: weapons for anyone without a usable one (crossbow, saw-blade machete, spiked bat, pipe spear), armor, dressings when meds are short, a water filter, a crowbar for scavengers, backpacks, pads; armory gear goes to whoever it helps most. *Medicine*: infected and severe wounds treated with base meds right away, others below 50 when stocks allow. Since the progression picks (2026-10-09; never built in the 200 census games before): the **chapel** at 8+ people while the mood is under 70 with a common room up, or two or more grieve (saved for from three, or under 50), at a big hub (14+) once the scrap is there; the **Master workbench** once crafting is in use (a crafter, a craft job, or an ammo or reloading bench) with a Mechanics 5 builder, 10+ people and its 3 parts plus 3 spare; **storage crates** when the food overflows and no building plot or bigger store is left (and one as the stopgap while food spoils outside); the **radio station** saved for from 10 people and day 60 while two or more known groups that are not hostile are out of radio reach, so the **radio relays** at the outposts (wished for where one would bring a group into reach) can chain it; an **animal pen** at a walled hub from 8 people while the winter stores fall short or the farmers lack plots. The **Turtle** style (`homeFood`) lives on its own land: walls from 5 people (saved for), then an animal pen (a second from 9), greenhouses saved for from 6 people (the second at 10), beds 6 ahead (a bunkhouse fire must not crowd it), the chapel from 6 people, half as much winter margin again before the farm crew is capped, and at least 2 out on a trip from a hub of 5 (`minOut`: a lone scavenger left most of the scrap behind). Every style: when the beds are lost (3+ people over them) and there is no scrap for the next bunkhouse, two go to a known site with scrap whatever the style's team limit (`bedsLost`, an emergency like the water and food ones).
  - *Food and the winter* (the food planner): the governor plans the year with `winterOutlook` (farming.js): day by day from today to the first spring harvest after the coming winter (`hungryGap`: winter starts on day 71 of each year, the first spring harvest is the quickest crop sown on the first day of spring, about four weeks in), everyone at the hub, the outposts and the teams out eats (the usual food need for the time of year, more in the cold), fresh food first, spoiling by the storage there is at the usual temperature; the crops in the ground come in when they should ripen (times the chance they survive) and every plot then gets the crops the farmers would sow on it (`plannedCrop`, at 85%, tended by today's crew spread over every plot in use, not over the few planted today), and an outdoor crop due to ripen inside the hungry gap counts 75% (`GAP_RIPEN`: from mid-autumn a big hub's outlook was mostly such crops, and in a sixth of the autumns measured its fields brought in under 70% of the promise; progression picks, 2026-10-09); seed for the spring and a 15% margin for bad luck are kept back. From mid-summer to the first spring harvest its *food target* (days of food the hub should hold) is that need less what the fields and outposts bring, so a short winter plan means more farmers, a higher food need for trips, trades and radio deals, storage and preserving built in time, and **no newcomers** (strangers, refugees, lone survivors on the road) while the plan with them would fall short. Every week in autumn and on the first day of winter it logs a 🤖 *Winter stores* line: food in store (fresh, preserved, cellar room, outside), what the fields should still bring, what the people need to reach the first spring harvest (eating, spoilage, seed, margin) and the verdict. Every figure is in days of food (`foods.js foodIn`: a stock's kcal ÷ 2000, one person's day), the unit people eat in: a headless Turtle game eats 0.92–0.96 days a head a day against the planner's 1.0–1.1 (lean days and the cold), so there is no item-count or 12× factor anywhere; the one leak was the trade cap: the spare table counts food under one key (`food`, in days) while a deal is made by food item, so merchants and caravans at the gates were paid in grain and beans with no cap at all (seed 6005's Turtle hub sold 113 days of grain to merchants on the eve of winter, twice what its rule allowed, and went into the winter short); now each item is capped by the days to spare (`foods.js spareOf` / `spendSpare`), none while the winter plan is short. Getting food (ap-food 2026-10-12, user request "make them want to farm and get food more"; every style, it is the same code): the **hub's food run** (`hubFoodRun`, `FOOD_RUN`) sends a hunting or fishing trip every morning the food is short (the winter plan short, the food under its target, or autumn's stocking up) and none is out, outside the style's trip cooldown and team limit (half the hub at most goes, the style's share if smaller); no hunting or fishing trip goes while the raw game and fish in store pass 3 days of everyone's eating (plus 8 a drying rack), the drying rack is wished for then; the hands left over forage up to the food target (not only under 12 days a head); a shelter's woodcutters keep its fire, its only cooking, in firewood, so the game is cooked and eaten before it rots. **Newcomers**: a hub of up to 8 people with the newcomers (`GROW`) takes them on 10 days of food a head and the beds it has or has queued, whatever the winter plan says (they farm, hunt and fish: the colonies that turned everyone away never grew and starved small); Cautious and Turtle (`strictWinter`) refuse newcomers while the winter plan with the people there now falls short only from mid-summer, not in spring and early summer. `node tools/turtlefoodtest.js [days]` (about a minute) checks the units, the per-item trade cap, the newcomer rule and the food run.
  - *Trips* (one a day at most, style sets how many teams, how often, how far, how many stay home and how healthy the crew must be): scavenge the best known site for what the hub needs (loot left as it last knew it, danger, infection, hostile land, distance; when fewer than 3 sites within its range are believed to hold 30%+ loot it looks 8 tiles farther out and allows trips 1.6× as long, logged as "the sites nearer home are picked over"), check spotted caches, camps and wrecked cars (a wreck restored when the hub can pay its car parts and fuel and has no car, else stripped; the same find not tried again for 20 days), recover the dead's gear or a lost vehicle (only with the fuel to start it), a trade visit (sells surplus, buys what is short, proposes pacts; it asks only for what the team can carry home, and every amount is priced as a whole, the way the trade window totals it), a rescue for captives, or found an outpost when the population allows. Teams take the truck when it can make the round trip (worn under 50%, it is mended first, up to 4 repairs until it is back to 50%: car parts first, 2 machine parts for one only for a car under 35% and only past the machines' reorder level; a trip called off gives back exactly what that took; `autopilot.js` VEH_REPAIR, tripRepair, tripUndo; a car under 20% that nobody at the base can mend, not its last running car, and every burnt-out wreck are stripped for parts once a day: stripDeadCars); searches stop at the style's Heat or hour limit, when someone is hurt, the cargo is full or the site is dry. Done cards: treat the badly hurt with carried meds before a long way home, sometimes search one more site nearby, else go home. Only one search runs at a time: a second team arriving holds at the site until it is free.
  - *Supply runs* to outposts that are short of hands (a volunteer disbands there), to take people from a hub that has outgrown its plots (see *Buildings*), or of scrap for a building (crew sized to the load).
  - *Cards*: conflicts are scored from the odds they show (talk, toll, pact, run, hide, fight, ambush, slip away, steal; style weighs fights and raids), travel events by fixed rules (detour, shelter, spare parts, mark hives on the map, take in loners if the food lasts), raid warnings judged against defense on alert (prepare, pay tribute, or fight in person), horde warnings (a decoy fire when the defences would not hold and no Wallbreaker leads it, else man the defences), calls for help, newcomers (every recruit card: strangers, refugees, loners, people met on the road, a lost teenager, a returning deserter) only with food and beds for them (built, queued or the next bunkhouse or barracks the hub can still get, only built or queued at a hub of up to 8, which asks 10 days of food a head and no winter plan: see *Food and the winter*; Aggressive allows some crowding; at an outpost, or sent to one, its own beds and water and the hub's food also do), else only the best one, else sent to a base with free beds, else supplies and a handshake when the stores are full, else turned away; hub events and warnings before desertion by a score on each choice (`ai`); the Overcrowded card (moveout.js) *Leave it for now* (its own outposts and bunkhouses deal with crowding; it raises none itself), the *Enter …?* card yes for a team sent there to move in or when the base has a bed for each of them, else no; a new leader by Leadership; anything else falls back to simtest's rule ("Return home", else the first choice).
  - *Reserves* (Cautious; `reserveStatus(state, base)` → per supply `{ have, target, days, short, low, why }`): a target for every supply that the hub should not drop below, from what you can see. Food is the food planner's (fresh + preserved: `foodTarget` and the winter outlook); water 7 days of drinking (10 in summer, at most 90% of the tanks; from three weeks before summer the summer plan's heat-wave stock if that is more; short while the wells fall behind on a dry day or under 15 days are left); meds a treatment for everyone sick now (+1 per infected wound) plus a third of the people + 2 (a quarter more in autumn and winter); ammo 2–4 break-in battles (about 6 a defender, up to 8 defenders, plus the 3 a head every horde attack burns; one battle more for each threat: a horde in sight, one the defences would not hold, a horde warning, a hive or hive build site known within 12 tiles, two or more attacks so far); fuel a decoy fire, the floodlights and the truck's next trip; scrap 20 + 1.5 a head + 10 per threat level (up to 70) for repairs and defences; parts 3 (+2 with a vehicle). Below target Cautious acts at once, and safely: a trip every 2 days instead of 4, up to twice as far (trade visits too) with a crew of three and a quarter more food and water, aimed at sites whose loot has what is short (low danger first, never risk above 0.8), two more search hours under the same Heat limit, and a recon first when no known site has it (a look around, then the search if it is worth it); a radio station once there are parts for it, a daily scan of the airwaves and one radio deal a day for the supply furthest below target, paid with what is well above its own target (parts, surplus food, ammo); trade visits that also sell surplus parts and food; the ammo bench and the med station staffed before the plots take every hand (from the scrap there is, down to a day's worth when the stock is low), a med station as soon as meds run short, dressings and a water filter; nothing optional built or crafted from scrap, meds, parts or fuel below target, and trips leave the ammo reserve at home. Risk it cannot afford: no trip while a horde in sight would break in, 5 people stay home while one is in sight or the defences are manned, standing guards before extra farmers, no trip of more than half a day with a storm or blizzard forecast while it is out, a second wall tier once hordes are a known danger. The dev panel's 🤖 line says what is short; hover it for the whole table with the reason for each target.
  - *Styles*: **Cautious** (one stealthy team close to home, short searches, more guards, pays tribute, avoids fights; keeps a reserve of every supply and goes for more as soon as one runs short: see *Reserves*), **Balanced** (default), **Aggressive** (more and longer trips, loud searches, raids hostile land, ambushes camps and robbers, takes in more newcomers, expands fast).
  - *The war on the hives* (every style, from day 30 Aggressive / 90 Balanced / 150 Cautious): a squad of six of the best fighters (health 80+, 12 ammo each) goes for a known hive when it can beat the brood it expects there: the squad's storm value (per fighter: hit chance × damage of the gun they can use, as the battle plays it, more with armor, less when hurt; about 2 for a shotgun and a vest at Shooting 6, 1 for a pistol at Shooting 4) over the brood (`hives.js broodForce` from the guard last seen at the mound, 100 if never seen up close, less one for every fighter coalition members and job givers send: `alliance.js hiveAllies`) must reach the style's margin (1.05 / 1.2 / 1.35: at 1.3 a storm costs about one fighter in three, at 1.1 about one, at 0.9 two or three). Fires around the mound (6 fuel each, up to 3, never the fuel for the drive home) thin the guard first when that is what it takes; the team storms on its next orders. Targets: hives near your bases, then those a group asked you to storm (the governor takes a hive job when it would storm that hive anyway), then the nearest. In the endgame (a coalition member, at most 3 hives left, the land short) it founds up to 3 more outposts where they add the most uncovered land.
  - *Conquest* (`autopilot.js conquestCandidate`): a squad of five or six of the best fighters (health 75+) storms a base when its combat power against the defenders behind their walls gives the style's odds (Aggressive 65%, Balanced 75%, Cautious 80%, the odds the card shows), at most one assault or hive squad out and 12 days between assaults: every style takes back a base of yours a group holds (10% lower odds accepted); Aggressive goes for any hostile group from day 60; Balanced for hostile groups that raided it in the last 120 days, and for any hostile group once every other group still standing is in the coalition or the coalition holds 35% of the land (from day 150); Cautious only then (from day 240). It sticks with one group until it breaks (its main base first, then the group it went for last, the one with the fewest bases). The old hub in their hands: whoever is fit (health 50+, at least 3, two stay home) goes at once at 35%+ odds. With no base left it takes one back when the odds are fair (45%+, 60%+ once a staging camp is 30% built; any odds from 20% while it lacks the camp's scrap), else it runs the staging camp as hardcore's road does (roadAct; exiles keep what they carried out of their base). On the raid warning it retreats from an outpost that would be overrun (defence below 0.9× the raid for Cautious, 0.8× Balanced, 0.6× Aggressive) when the tribute is not worth it, from the hub only below 0.25× with another base to go to, never from its last base.
  - *Water through the summer* (`waterPlan`): from three weeks before summer (and all year for newcomers) the governor plans by the summer outlook for the people at the base, the teams out and two more: wells, rain catchers and cisterns go up before summer (saved for), water filters (ahead of anything else in the craft queue, kept up as they clog; a clogged one is re-packed for 1 scrap before a new one is made for 4) close the heat-wave gap or today's dry-day gap (up to 18), and a summer stock (a heat-wave stretch, at most 90% of the tanks) becomes the water reserve and a trip need. Scrap and parts are held back for water: a broken well or the hub's pump is repaired before any other repair (`breakdowns.js`: water fixtures first, nothing else takes the materials while one waits), and builds and crafts leave the scrap for it and for two filters. No newcomers the summer water could not carry (an average summer day and a heat-wave stretch that fits the tanks, filters at three quarters); water carried to an outpost about to run dry or short of its summer stock; water runs while the hub's water is tight and a river or lake shore is within 16 hours on foot (two to four people, Fetch water, at most a Heavy load each, one run at a time); while water is tight (under 8 days left, or summer near and under half the summer stock) no water goes into the still or the preserving kitchen and no guests are sheltered.
- **Saving and loading** (`src/sim/save.js`, storage in `src/ui/saves.js`, the menu in `src/ui/settings.js`): ⚙ Menu in the top bar (or Esc with nothing open, or ⚙ Menu in a battle's panel). A save is the whole world, written as a graph: plain objects and lists as JSON; Maps, Sets, typed arrays (the timelapse frames, base64), the seeded generators (their exact position) and the hex grid as tagged objects; an object reachable from two places (an item worn by a survivor and listed at a death site, the chronicle's shared frame lists, a battle's supply pools that are the team's own supplies, the battle's rng) is written once and referenced after, so the links survive; the chronicle's WeakMaps keep the entries for the hordes and teams still in the world; undefined, NaN, Infinity and −0 keep their values; the id counter (`world.js` `newId`) is saved, so a loaded world hands out the same ids and plays on exactly as the original (`tools/savetest.js`). Battles, raids, the deploy step, searches and trade windows are saved as they are, mid-turn included. **An open event card cannot be saved** (its choices are code, closures over the moment the card was made): Save is disabled with the card's name until it is answered, and the autosave waits for that too. Storage: up to 12 named saves plus the autosave in the browser's storage (the saves themselves in IndexedDB, where a long campaign's saves fit, and the index with the day, hour, hub, survivors, seed and a small picture of the map in `localStorage`; saves made before, an Ironman game's save as the page closes, and a browser without IndexedDB keep the save in `localStorage` too, and reading finds it in either), gzip-compressed through the browser's `CompressionStream` and packed 15 bits a character (a 365-day Normal world: about 3 MB of JSON, about 490 KB stored (250,000 characters; the diary and the timelapse recording are the biggest parts)); without `CompressionStream` the JSON is stored as it is. *Export* and *Save to file* download a `.bsave` file (the save's JSON); *Load from file* adds one to the list. The autosave runs at dawn every day (or every 2, 3, 5 or 7 days), when a hive assault starts (the battle is saved before the first shot) and when the run ends (that save leaves any open card out); at dev fast-forward speeds at most once a minute. *Save and exit* saves to the game's slot and goes back to the new-run screen; loading pauses the game. Saves carry a format version (`SAVE_VERSION`): a save from a newer version, or with no version, is refused with a reason; older versions are upgraded by the migrations in `save.js` (none yet: this is format 1). **Rotating autosaves** (`src/ui/saves.js` writeAutosave): each campaign keeps its last 3 autosaves (`auto-<campaign>-1…3`, the oldest written over), each named by a one-line summary (`src/sim/savesummary.js`: "Day 143 · 31 people · winter · at war with Iron Jackals": the day, people alive, the season, then the run's end, a war you joined, a hostile group or a Hardcore band on the road); manual saves show the same line under their name. A campaign is a run's own id (given as it begins); saves from before that are grouped by their seed. The menu's save list is grouped by campaign (`src/ui/savelist.js`: a head line with the hub, mode, seed and count; the game being played first) and warns when the saves fill 70% of the browser's storage; a full storage makes the autosave clear the slot it writes over, then the campaign's older autosaves (its newest always stays). Sizes (seed 42, Balanced autopilot): a Normal map's save at day 365 is about 3 MB of JSON and 250,000 stored characters (gzip, 15 bits a character), at day 730 about 5.2 MB and 430,000; a Huge map's at day 365 about 4.5 MB and 360,000 (so about 650,000 at two years). Browser storage holds about 5 million characters a site, so three two-year autosaves take a quarter of it on a Normal map and two fifths on a Huge one. **The error screen** (`src/ui/crash.js`): an exception nobody caught (the frame loop: the clock, the autopilot, drawing; a click, key or timer; a promise) stops the clock and writes the world to the crash save (its own slot, `crash`, open cards left out; never a manual save, the autosaves or an Ironman slot), then a screen says what happened: *Copy report* (the error and its stack, the time, day, seed, map, mode, Ironman, autopilot, speed, the build's commit (`build.json`: the dev server and the desktop app run from source answer it from git, `npm run dist` writes it), the browser, the crash save and the last 20 log lines), *Try to keep playing* (paused), *Load the crash save*, *Back to title*. While it is up no frames run and further errors are only counted; a crash within 5 s of Try to keep playing shows it again with a warning and keeps the first crash save. Ironman: the crash copy sits beside the game's slot (which keeps its last save, and is not written as the page closes while the screen is up) and is removed once the game saves itself again. `window.game.crashTest()` opens it from the console.
- **Timelapse:** the world is recorded from day 1 (`src/sim/chronicle.js`): one frame a day (every 6 hours for the first 3 days, plus a frame a few hours after a death or a base gained or lost) with tile owners and infection (stored as changes against the previous frame, a full keyframe every 60 frames), your bases, the groups' bases, hordes (with their Wallbreakers), hives and hive build sites, parties, your population and hub stocks, and captions: your log's deaths, raids, horde attacks, bases founded and lost, plus what the fog hid (groups taking or losing sites, sieges and how they ended, hordes striking a group, big hordes, hives rising and destroyed, collapses). About 0.25 ms per frame and roughly 300 KB for a year. When every survivor is dead (or every hive is destroyed) the game-over or victory screen offers *Watch the timelapse*; the dev panel has *Timelapse so far* (the game pauses, Close returns to it). The viewer (`src/ui/timelapse.js`) fits the whole map with the fog off and draws each frame with the normal map renderer: land growing and shrinking, infection spreading, hordes and teams stepping toward their next position, bases appearing and disappearing, and graves where your bases stood at the end. Overlay: day and time, your people over time (chart with a cursor), hub stocks, the groups' land or population chart with a table, horde / hive / hotzone counts, the latest captions (a ring pings on the map where each happened), and a bar with play / pause, step, speed (1 / 2 / 5 / 10 days per second), infection and territory toggles, fit, close, and a scrubber with day labels and ticks for major events.
- **Alerts, log and diary** (`src/ui/alerts.js`, `src/ui/diary.js`, `src/sim/diary.js`): a stack of alerts on the right edge of the map lists what needs you now, worst first: a team waiting for orders (or its plan finished), a base out of or low on food or water (under 5 days of food, 3 of water), stations idle (no operator, operator sick, no input), a horde you can see within 6 tiles of a base, a watchtower's alarm (🗼: a horde near a base with a watchtower, and who moved Guard up for it; in place of the plain horde alert), a likely break-in (a horde heading for a base bigger than its defences hold), a gathering of the dead you marked (the days left until its hive stands, red under a week), people sick or hurt and untreated for two days, someone close to leaving, and for a day after it happens a building finished, a caravan arrived or a vehicle repaired on the road running again (🔧 with its own chime, `src/sim/repairwait.js`; `node tools/repairalerttest.js`); and cards still waiting in the pile. Click an alert to go there (the team panel, the base on the right tab, the horde on the map, the survivor screen); 💤 hides it for a day, ✕ until it clears. Options → *Good news alerts* (on by default; off: no row, flash or chime, the log keeps the lines) covers the news that asks nothing of you: a repair done, a building finished, a caravan or merchants arrived, a planner trip home. The log under the map has filter chips (deaths, combat, hordes and hives, trade and groups, teams, base and people, medical, food and water, weather and world), a 🤖 toggle for the autopilot's notes and a base filter, and every person, base, group, team and place in a line is a link. The **Diary** (J) keeps every log line of the run, day by day (up to 150 a day, the rest counted): a day list with the season, the weather and a one-line summary (deaths, departures, arrivals, fights, harvest, buildings, trades), the chosen day's lines grouped by kind with the leader's short note for the day, the same filters, a search over the whole run, and the same links. A year of an autopilot run keeps 2,000–3,000 lines in about 0.3–0.5 MB (`tools/uitest.js` measures it).
- **Threat forecast** (2026-10-12, docs/suggestions-5.md #19; `src/sim/forecast.js` threatForecast, `src/ui/threatbar.js`; `node tools/forecasttest.js [seed]`, seconds): a meter for the coming week joined to the Needs button in the top bar: seven small bars, today first, each as tall and as hot as the worst thing coming that day (calm, watch, danger, severe: colour and height, so it reads without colour; the colour-blind palette has its own), the week's word over them, the days past the sky's reach with a dashed edge. Hover it, click it (a touch screen, a TV) or tab to it for the causes day by day (the quiet days folded): what, how sure (sure, likely, possible, a guess, with the percentage), whom it hits (a base, a team, a person) and where it was learned (seen on the map, a watchtower, the lookouts, a sighting, the radio, the stores, the weather forecast, the team's last report), with the rule behind it in the tooltip; a click shows it (the base's window at its tab, the team's panel, the horde or the raiders on the map, the person). Every cause comes from the game's own forecasts, never a rule of the meter's: a horde marching on a base on the day it gets there (its path, `hordes.js` HOURS_PER_TILE and hordePace, the pace of the days ahead from `weather.js` hordeSpeedFactor), severe when it is bigger than the walls hold (`defences.js` holdLimit), a watch when they hold it easily; a wandering horde of 15 or more within 8 tiles that may drift onto the base it would turn to (`hordes.js` driftTarget and driftPull: the share of days the dead turn to the noise, over the days left); a horde seen near a base in the last two days and out of sight since (a guess); a group's war party the lookouts saw, you can see, or you heard on its channel (`radiolisten.js` arrivalDay), danger when the defence holds it (`factions.js` baseDefense, RAIDS.HOLD) and severe when it would break in, today at the walls; raiders from outside you can see or hear; a war with you (all week, a guess); storms and blizzards from the fronts on the map (`weather.js` frontsApproaching) and the forecast (`forecast`, its own confidence; a blizzard is danger with teams out), heat waves, frost or a freeze that may kill an outdoor crop (`cropDamage`); the day a base's food and water run out at today's rates (`economy.js` foodOutlook, waterOutlook; less sure with a harvest due before it) or already have, the day the firewood and fuel run out on the forecast's freezing days (`upkeep.js` heatUse and heatRateAt, the stove and the boiler from `woodcutting.js` woodNeed), the day the generators' fuel runs out (`power.js` powerStatus; danger with a freezer); the worm reaching someone's brain (day `WORM.BRAIN_FROM`), two or more contagious at a base, the sick with no meds; a team in the field whose people go hungry or thirsty before it can be home (the supplies at the rate it eats on the move, `plans.js` foodDays, then the gauges running down, `needs.js` NEEDS, against `plans.js` hoursHome or the way back; less sure out of radio contact); a team marching through the night; a hive due within 10 tiles of a base (`hivebirth.js` siteView). Fog of war: a horde counts only in sight, tracked by a watchtower, warned of by the lookouts or seen within 2 tiles in the last day; a raid only once seen or heard. The result is a plain structure ({ days: [{ day, level, items }], week, items, level, label, range }; an item { day, kind, level, text, confidence, sure, source, target, why, go }) for the UI, the tests and later the autopilot (not wired in); it is cached per in-game hour (and per state; a base, a team, a horde or a card more or less refreshes it), about a millisecond fresh and under a microsecond cached. The top bar's gaps were tightened a little (0.85 to 0.65 rem) so that at 1920 × 1080 it stays on one row with the meter.
- **Ledger, run summary and stock trends** (`src/sim/history.js`, `src/ui/ledger.js`): at midnight a row is written for the colony and for each base (lost bases keep theirs): stocks, and per resource (food, water, fuel, firewood, meds, ammo, parts, scrap) what came in and went out that day, measured by marking every base's stock at the end of each hour and between the steps of the daily economy (so every way stock moves counts, without hooks in each module), plus what the outposts' daily surplus run moved between your bases; people, average mood and Heat; for the colony also everyone alive, deaths, arrivals and births, hives known and destroyed, known groups (allied, hostile) and the coalition's share of the land. The last 120 days are kept daily, then weekly averages for two years, then 28-day averages (measured on a Balanced autopilot run: 21 KB of JSON at day 90 with one base, 38 KB at a year, 134 KB at two years with 8 bases, about 20 KB gzipped: under 3% of the save). The **Ledger** (📈 Ledger in the top bar, or E) draws one row per resource (in against out a day, and the stock) and rows for population with deaths and arrivals, mood and Heat (for all bases also hives, groups and land, with the 50% line), for all bases or one, over 30 / 90 / 365 days or the whole run; hovering a chart shows that day's numbers in its row. Its **Run summary** tab is also the body of the game-over and victory screens: the timeline (population, food, water, Heat, hives, land), the best people (most kills, longest with the group, top skill, most trips, most treated, most built), the worst day (most deaths, else the biggest loss) with its diary lines, deaths by cause (from the memorial wall), what happened (the timelapse's weightiest captions; news from beyond your sight only once the run is over) and *Watch the timelapse*. The top bar's stock chips show ▲ / ▼ / – for the last full day against the day before (green up, red down), and their tooltip adds the change and a 14-day sparkline. A save from before the Ledger starts its history on the day it is loaded.
- **Alerts, log and diary** (`src/ui/alerts.js`, `src/ui/diary.js`, `src/sim/diary.js`): a stack of alerts on the right edge of the map lists what needs you now, worst first: a team waiting for orders (or its plan finished), a base out of or low on food or water (under 5 days of food, 3 of water), stations idle (no operator, operator sick, no input), a horde you can see within 6 tiles of a base, a likely break-in (a horde heading for a base bigger than its defences hold), a gathering of the dead you marked (the days left until its hive stands, red under a week), people sick or hurt and untreated for two days, someone close to leaving, and for a day after it happens a building finished, a caravan arrived or a vehicle repaired on the road running again (🔧 with its own chime, `src/sim/repairwait.js`; `node tools/repairalerttest.js`); and cards still waiting in the pile. Click an alert to go there (the team panel, the base on the right tab, the horde on the map, the survivor screen); 💤 hides it for a day, ✕ until it clears. Options → *Good news alerts* (on by default; off: no row, flash or chime, the log keeps the lines) covers the news that asks nothing of you: a repair done, a building finished, a caravan or merchants arrived, a planner trip home. The log under the map has filter chips (deaths, combat, hordes and hives, trade and groups, teams, base and people, medical, food and water, weather and world), a 🤖 toggle for the autopilot's notes and a base filter, and every person, base, group, team and place in a line is a link. The **Diary** (J) keeps every log line of the run, day by day (up to 150 a day, the rest counted): a day list with the season, the weather and a one-line summary (deaths, departures, arrivals, fights, harvest, buildings, trades), the chosen day's lines grouped by kind with the leader's short note for the day, the same filters, a search over the whole run, and the same links. A year of an autopilot run keeps 2,000–3,000 lines in about 0.3–0.5 MB (`tools/uitest.js` measures it).
- **First-week guide and Start here** (`src/ui/guide.js`, `src/ui/help.js`): an opt-in checklist pinned under the alerts for the first days, with a line on why each step matters and a **Show me** that opens the right screen and pulses the button to press (a gold outline that throbs for about 10 seconds). A normal start: check your food and water (the Farming tab), put your people on work priorities, queue a building, send a team to scavenge (Show me picks a site near the hub and points at Scavenge / raid), start the clock, get through the first night, bring a team home, read the diary. A Hardcore start (no base yet): meet your team, start the clock, find water, pitch a staging camp (Establish base), bring in what the camp needs, found your shelter, the first night, the diary. Steps tick off by themselves from the state of the game, in any order (the Farming tab opened, someone on priorities, a building queued, a team that left a base and is back, the clock past the first dawn, more water held than before…), each with a small toast; the next one is marked ▶ and any one can be picked to see its why line. It folds to one line (▾), ✕ hides it, and it steps aside by itself once every step is done or after day 7. ⚙ Menu → Game shows it again or hides it (brought back by hand it stays past day 7; a finished list starts over, and an older save gets one). The new-run screen's **First-week guide** box is on for the first campaign on this machine (no saves and never started one; remembered in the options as `guideSeen`) and off after that. It never pauses the clock or adds cards, and the autopilot and the tools never start it (`state.guide`, saved with the world). Help opens on a new **Start here** topic: the game in a few plain lines (your people, bases, teams, the State Map, cards and alerts, the clock, winning and losing) with links to the deeper topics.
- **"Why?" on deaths and lost bases** (`src/sim/causes.js`, `src/ui/causes.js`): when someone dies or a base is lost, a short chain of causes is written down at that moment: the immediate cause, then up to four things that led to it, taken from the base, the team and the last days of the diary, with day references. Per kind of death: thirst and hunger (days without, broken wells and the scrap missing to repair them, a dry spell, the food that came in against what was eaten, the diary's lines about water or food there), on the road (when the team left, how overdue it was, what it ran out of, what held it up), cold (since when the firewood was gone), illness and infected wounds (when it started and why, never treated or not enough doses, no meds since day N, nobody on the Medic role), the worm (caught when and how, never spotted, never treated), hordes and raids at a base (the line about the attack, the walls' state, who was away on a trip and on guard, short ammo, not on alert, when the group turned hostile and why, their earlier raids), fights and scavenging (your people against theirs and the odds of a fight by the numbers, an ambush, short ammo, already hurt, a small team, the team's lines), their own people (mood, breaks, quarrels). A lost base (taken, abandoned before a raid, burned, overrun by the dead or lost to raiders) gets the same: the attack, the walls, the defenders, broken walls left unrepaired, the group and earlier hits. A **Why?** link sits on the death or loss line in the log and the diary, on the memorial wall, on the survivor screen of the dead and on the lost-base card; it opens a small panel where a day opens the diary there and a name or place goes to it. The chain lives on the memorial entry (`why`) and in `state.losses` (the last 30), a few hundred bytes each; `base.lows` keeps the day each base's water, food, scrap, meds, ammo or firewood ran out ("no scrap since day 38"). Deaths in older saves are pieced together from the diary. For other screens: `deathCause(state, survivorOrEntryOrId)`, `deathsByCause(state)`, `lossesOf(state)`. `node tools/causetest.js` checks it.
- **Skills that grow, teaching, books and perks** (`src/sim/skills.js`, survivor screen in `src/ui/skills.js`): people gain XP in the skills they use (a day on Farm, Build, Craft or Operate 11; Medic 4 plus 3 a wound treatment, 6 a surgery, 2 an illness, 4 a field treatment; Guard 3 Shooting; Scavenge 6; in battle 1 a shot, +2 a hit, +2 a kill, 3 a patch-up, 1 Stealth a move while the enemy is unaware, 2 Scavenging a container; a fight by the numbers 6, +3 for a win; a search hour 3, +2 Stealth sneaking; an hour on the move 0.5 Scouting; looking around 4; a road talk 5, +5 if it works; hiding 5 Stealth; a trade 6, a radio deal 4 Speech; a vehicle repair 5 Mechanics; the leader 3 and each team's best leader 4 Leadership a day). XP to the next level is 40 + 0.32 × level³ (49 at 3, 80 at 5, 360 at 10, 1,120 at 15): a 3 becomes a 7 in a season of daily work, 15 → 16 takes about a year. Use XP is capped at 15 a day per skill and teaching plus books at 25. Skills above 10 unused for 14 days slip 2 XP a day, never below 10 nor the level the person joined with. *Teach* role: one skill to up to 2 learners at the same base whose skill is 4+ below; each learner gains 4 + 0.4 × the teacher's skill + 0.15 × (Leadership + Speech) a day and keeps their job; the teacher gains Leadership and Speech and does no other work. *Read* role: 6 hours a day with a book from the base's armory (one reader per copy, then the next), 4 XP an hour up to the book's level: Field Medicine Handbook (Medicine to 8), Emergency Surgery (Medicine 14), Small Engine Repair (Mechanics 10), Gardening Through the Seasons (Farming 8), Marksmanship Manual (Shooting 9), Army Field Manual (Shooting 13), Wilderness Survival (Scouting 8), Negotiation 101 (Speech 8), Lockpicking for Locksmiths (Stealth 8), Leading Small Teams (Leadership 8), Salvage Yard Secrets (Scavenging 8); comics and novels give mood. Books are pack or cargo items found in schools, universities, police stations, the military, homes and other fitting sites (their own dice, so no other result changes) and sold by traders, communes, the military and fortresses. **Perks:** reaching 5, 10 and 15 in a skill gives a point for that milestone, spent on one of two perks (54 in all) from a card or the skill tree on the survivor screen (nodes taken / available / locked / passed over, the exact effect on hover); survivors join with points for the milestones of their two best skills; the autopilot picks by its style.
- **Locks, keys and locked rooms** (`src/sim/locks.js`, raid panel in `src/ui/locks.js`): some raid containers are locked, by area (house cabinets 12% lockboxes, locker rooms and barracks 30–35% lockers, pharmacy and clinic cabinets 25–35% drug cabinets (the only big meds find in a lock), police evidence lockers 30%, armory and gun-counter gun cages 40–45%, office and control-room safes 8–12%, warehouse, hangar and motor-pool crates 12–20%, crash-site cargo cases 25–45%), and a site may have a locked room: every door of one building with containers is a locked door cell (no walking or seeing through) — police and military 60%, pharmacy, gun store and prison 50%, hospital 40%, university and dam 35%, clinic, warehouse, factory, office, airport and a cargo-plane wreck 30%, elsewhere 15–25%. Difficulty: lockbox and locker 1; drug cabinet, evidence locker, sealed crate, cargo case and locked door 2; gun cage and steel security door 3; safe 4. Open from next to it: **key** (a key for the site on the key ring: sure, 1 action, silent; it opens every lock there and every door of the room); **pick** (needs the Crowbar and lockpicks item on that person: 55% + 4% a Stealth point − 15% a difficulty step above 1, +10% Quiet Hands, 5–95%; 1 action a try, Heat +1, the picks lose a use and snap when worn out; Stealth XP 6 for a success, 2 for a miss); **force** (with the crowbar: 85% + 1.5% a Mechanics point − 20% a step, 1 action, Heat +6 and noise that wakes everything within 8 tiles; a safe cannot be forced) or **smash** without one (difficulty 1–2 only: 50% − 20% a step, both actions, Heat +10, wakes everything within 11). Locked things hold more: supplies ×1.5 (×1.3 in a locked room) plus extras by kind (a gun cage 20–40 ammo and pistols, shotguns, rifles, sometimes an assault rifle; a drug cabinet 3–6 meds and kits; an evidence locker ammo, guns, keys, the Lockpicking book; a safe meds, ammo, keys, blueprints), and blueprints 4× as often (2× in a locked room, half as often in a plain box). **Keys** are named for a site ("Easton North Precinct evidence room key") near where they turn up: on human bodies 12%, in plain containers 3%, in locked ones by kind (lockbox 20%, evidence locker and safe 30%), and now and then in an hour-by-hour search. They hang on the hub's key ring (Armory tab, map tooltip "You hold a key"), never in cargo; keys found in a raid join it only if someone gets out. The hour-by-hour search meets locks too: a search hour at a police station (35%), military base (40%), gun store, pharmacy, hospital or prison (30%), and less elsewhere, turns up a locked thing that the team opens with the key, picks (two tries in the hour, Heat +2, unless the approach is Loud), forces with a crowbar (Heat +8) or smashes (Heat +12). The raid bot (raidtest, autopilot) keys, picks (30%+ while Heat is under 50) or forces (40%+, Heat under 40) and gives up on the rest.
- **Manuals and blueprints** (`src/sim/blueprints.js`; Buildings tab list, "Needs" lines in Construction, Medical and Crafting): the upper tiers are locked until the matching item is found **and** studied at a base: Field Surgery Manual → Clinic (Medicine 4+, 24 h; hospitals, universities, clinics, the military, fire stations), Hospital Operations Manual → Trauma hospital (Medicine 8+, 36 h; hospitals, universities), Machinist's Handbook → machine shop (Mechanics 3+, 24 h; factories, universities, hardware stores, the dam, the airport, auto shops), Generator Schematics → generator (Mechanics 3+, 24 h; the dam, factories, the military, the airport, hospitals, radio towers), Army Engineering Manual → reinforced gate and trench (Mechanics 2+, 30 h; the military, the prison, police), Water Treatment Schematics → cistern (Mechanics 2+, 20 h; the dam, universities, factories, fire stations), Refrigeration Schematics → cold store and walk-in freezer (Mechanics 3+, 20 h; supermarkets, warehouses, factories, truck stops, universities, groceries), Commercial Canning Guide → a second preserving kitchen (Farming 3+, 16 h; farms, feed stores, schools, groceries, supermarkets, churches, ranches), Radio Engineering Handbook → radio relay (Mechanics 3+, 24 h; radio towers, the airport, police, the military, universities), Improvised Weapons Plans → scrap crossbow and pipe shotgun (Mechanics 4+, 20 h; gun stores, police, the prison, the military, hardware stores, hunting lodges), Armor Smithing Notes → scrap plate vest (Mechanics 3+, 16 h; the military, police, factories, the prison, hardware stores). Everything else (plots, walls, beds, wells, the infirmary and field hospital, benches, the radio station, the first kitchen…) is open from day 1. Finds: per search hour (or per raid container, half that) 1.8% at a city site, 1.2% in a town, 0.8% in the countryside, 3% at a landmark, × the loot left; 4× in a locked container, 2× in a locked room; 25% at a crash site (its own list); traders sell one about 30% of weeks, the military 30%, communes 20%, fortresses 15%; `blueprintReward` hands one out for jobs. Only blueprints you neither know nor hold turn up. Studying: anyone on the **Read** role at the base holding it, with the skill at the minimum, studies it before any book (6 hours a day, fewer when ill, 2 study XP an hour in that skill); progress is kept per blueprint, so someone else can carry on; once done it is known for good and the copy stays in the armory (sell it or keep it). The autopilot (and stewards with Roles) put the best qualified free person on it when food is not short. A locked building's card says "Needs: Field Surgery Manual (where it may be found)", with "a copy is here" or the study hours when that is so.
- **Crash sites and supply drops** (`src/sim/crashes.js`, the circle and the wreck drawn in `src/ui/render.js`): the first between days 8 and 14, then every 10–20 days, something comes down: an Army helicopter (33%), a supply plane (22%), a medevac helicopter (20%), a news helicopter (13%) or a parachute supply drop (12%), 5–13 tiles from whoever saw it (the hub, an outpost or a team in the field; the hub's radio hears one up to 20 tiles out). A card says so, and the map shows a dashed orange **search circle** of radius 4 (3 seen from a base with a watchtower, 5 heard on the radio) with a smoke column and the days left; the wreck is somewhere inside, at most radius − 1 from the centre, and is on no map until found. A team that passes within 1 tile sees it; a look around (or a recon's survey) finds it within 2 tiles (3 with Scouting 8+); a look in the circle that misses clears what it covered and narrows the circle by a tile (it still holds the wreck). The card's *Send a search team* sends up to 3 of the fittest free people (3 stay home) on foot with food and water for the plan (recon the centre, **Search the area**, home); the planner has the same *Search the area* step (shown while a crash is on): it looks around at the spots that clear the most unsearched ground, up to 8 looks, then raids the wreck. Found, the wreck is a temporary site (Army helicopter wreck: ammo, military gear; supply plane: food, preserved food, meds; medevac: meds, surgical kits; news chopper: parts; supply drop: food, water, meds, ammo) whose raid map has wreckage and cargo cases (many locked) and its own blueprints. It burns out and is gone 7–10 days after it came down. Groups with a base within 22 tiles race you (raiders 75%, the military 85%, expansionists 65%, zealots 50%, fortresses 40%, traders 30%, communes 20%): a salvage party leaves 12–36 hours after the crash and walks there; one that arrives strips it to 30% of what is left, and if your team is on the tile it challenges you (a conflict card). The noise draws up to two hordes within 8 tiles toward it for 3 days. Alerts and the tile tooltip show the circle and the days left.
- **Machines, machine parts and the walk-in freezer** (freezer-parts 2026-10-10; `src/sim/breakdowns.js` header, `power.js`, `buildings.js`, `groupworks.js` MACH; Help: *Machines and machine parts*): every building with a motor, pump, compressor or press is a machine: it costs machine parts to build, its proper repair takes parts and a Mechanics hand, it wears with use (a station on the days it runs, the generator on the days it runs, a powered building on the days it has power, the radio on the air, floodlights in each horde attack, the garage with age), and a running one uses up a spare part every so many days (generator and radio 20, freezer 12, machine shop and sealer 30, still 40) from its base or else the hub; with none it wears 3 more a day until one comes in. The ammo and reloading benches' repairs take a part now. The **Walk-in freezer** (a building plot, 30 scrap, 5 parts, 2 electronics, 40 h, Mechanics 3, up to 2) adds 300 food room and draws 2 power every day (machines-2: locked behind the Refrigeration Schematics with the cold store, for the groups too); while on (`base.freezerOn`, set by `refreshPower` every hour) its food keeps ~20× longer than in a cellar, off (no generator, its tank dry, no fuel above the reserve, too little power, switched off, broken) it warms up and keeps as a pantry; the log (one line when an outage has lasted into a second hour, one when it ends), the alerts and the Buildings tab say why. The **Vacuum sealer** (a utility plot, 12 scrap, 2 parts, 2 electronics, 16 h, Mechanics 3) draws 1 power while switched on; `hasSealer(state, base)` (power.js) is the hook for the cooking wave's packaged survival meals. Crafting with parts: a fishing rod with a reel and a bear trap join the older mechanism recipes. The **Pressure canner** (machines-2) is fitted to a preserving room where it stands (Construction, the preserving room's *Fit a canner*: 6 scrap, 2 parts, 12 h, Mechanics 3; a canner kept in the armory from an old save fits one for nothing): that kitchen +1 preserved a day, and it wears like a machine (repairs take a part, a spare every 30 days it runs); the governor fits one to a working kitchen when the parts are there. The governor builds a freezer at a hub of 10+ with a generator, a still or 30 fuel, a big fresh stock and its parts and electronics; the groups build a still, a generator, a freezer and a sealer (machines-2: behind your manuals and Mechanics rules, see *The groups' manuals*; coarse, daily: a day's fuel while there is any, wear, breakdowns, repairs and spares from their one count of parts, which their workshop makes once they know the Machinist's Handbook, 0.25 a day for 16 people with a mechanic 4, from 6 scrap a part, kept for their machines (the earmark: guns, kit, trade and their other works use only the parts above it, a vehicle repair takes it at two for one), and they buy from merchants).
- **The groups' manuals** (machines-2 2026-10-10, the user's rule "AI groups need to have the same restrictions and gameplay features"; `src/sim/groupworks.js` groupTech): the AI groups find manuals while scavenging (`aiscav.js`, your odds by site and loot), buy them from a traders group that is not hostile to them (a weekly 30% chance, its value × 1.25 in scrap), and study one at a time, 6 hours a day, with someone at its skill (their mechanic, their doctor). They start as you do, with none, but traders know every manual they sell and communes theirs (refrigeration, water, canning); the military and the fortress hold theirs unstudied.
