Their generator waits for the Generator Schematics (and the Refrigeration Schematics: they build it for a freezer), the freezer for the Refrigeration Schematics, the clinic and trauma hospital tiers of their sick bay for the Field Surgery and Hospital Operations manuals, their workshop's parts for the Machinist's Handbook; the generator, freezer and sealer need a group mechanic of 3, a machine's repair its proper repair's Mechanics. ai-locks (2026-10-11; `node tools/ailockstest.js`) carries the same rules through the rest of what they build and make: a machine shop of their own for the parts (the manual, Mechanics 4, half the output below it as yours), a radio station (Mechanics 4) for their network, a garage for vehicle repairs (or a Mechanics 4 hand at 12 of 25), a reloading bench (Mechanics 3), a reinforced gate (the Army Engineering Manual) on an assault's map, the trauma hospital's Mechanics 4; their crafting is your recipes at your skill, bench and manuals (no gun of their kit rebuilt from scrap and a part: factory guns come from finds, newcomers, purchases and raids), their battering ram wants a workshop and Mechanics 3, vehicle fitting each upgrade's Mechanics (the turret's 6), their field works pay your firewood and the relay mast's Mechanics 3, gear repairs your skill gates, and a wreck brought home is restored at your 2 car parts and 6 fuel.
- **Things break** (`src/sim/breakdowns.js`, the base's **Repairs** tab): the hub's well pump, wells, the generator, the still, the machine shop, the ammo bench, the med station, the preserving kitchen, the radio station, walls, the reinforced gate and the storehouse wear out. Wear a day: a running station 1.6 (generator), 1.2 (still, machine shop), 0.8 (ammo bench, kitchen), 0.6 (med station), idle 0.05–0.15; the pump and wells 0.9, +2.5 on a frost day, +4 in a storm; the radio mast 0.7, +6 in a storm; the storehouse roof 0.1, +8 in a storm, +1 in rain; walls and gate 0.05 a day and 18 / 22 (±30%) per horde attack (raids do their own damage, below); all × (1 + days since built / 240), × (1.15 − 2.5% a Mechanics point of the operator, at least 0.65), × 0.8 with a Grease Monkey or 0.85 with a Handy survivor living there, × 1.6 while jury-rigged. What the holdout starts with is not new (the hub's pump 30–50 wear, the rest 5–25). Builders with no construction job take 1.5 wear a day off the most worn. A fixture breaks at 100, and from 60 up with a daily chance of ((wear − 60) / 40)² × 30% (most break around 80). Broken: a station stops (the Workshops tab says so), a well gives no water, the pump costs the hub 6 water a day, the radio station is off the air, walls lose their +3 wall strength, the gate its horde bonus, the storehouse its room. A card (an alert, and the Repairs list) offers **Repair** (the best free mechanic with at least the job's Mechanics: generator 10 h, 4 scrap, 2 parts, Mechanics 3; radio 10 h, 4 scrap, 2 parts, Mechanics 4; machine shop 8 h, 3 scrap, 1 part; still, pump and wells 6 h, 3 scrap, 1 part; walls 12 h, 10 scrap; gate 8 h, 6 scrap, 1 part; storehouse 6 h, 4 scrap; benches and the kitchen 4 h, 2 scrap; hours × (1.2 − 3% a Mechanics point, at least 40%), faster with Handy or Master Builder; wear back to 8), **Jury-rig** (about a third of the hours, about half the scrap, no parts; wear left at 65 and 60% faster wear until repaired properly) or **Leave it**. Scrap that is short can be made up with parts (1 for 4). Materials are paid when the job starts (cancel: all back); the repairer keeps their role and works it 08:00–20:00 while at the base and well enough; Mechanics XP when done. The autopilot and stewards with Building on start repairs themselves (repair if they can pay, else jury-rig) and replace a repairer who left. The storm "broken well pump" hub event is this system now (the same card). **Raid damage** (`raidDamage`): when a raid on your base is settled (by the numbers, in person, or raiders from outside) it smashes buildings with a budget of wear = the raid's strength × 3 × (the hours the siege lasted / 8, 0.15–1.5) × the outcome (driven off 1, beaten in person 0.5, a break-in 2.5, overrun 4.5), in hits of 30–70 wear: the outer line first (walls, gate, barricades, floodlights, watchtower: 85% of the hits when driven off, 50% in a break-in, 35% when overrun), else the buildings inside; 40% of the hits fall on the building hit last. Buildings that only wear from raids: barricades, floodlights, the watchtower, bunkhouses, cisterns, cellars, the cold store, the garage, cells, the common room, the chapel, drying racks, rain catchers and the relay (repairs 2–8 h, 2–8 scrap, a part for the floodlights, garage, cells and relay). At 100 a building breaks (no card of its own: the raid's result card lists what was wrecked, what is damaged at 60+ and the bill, with *Start the repairs* / *Later*; the autopilot and stewards start them themselves); an overrun base loses one wrecked building for good (60%, never the walls). A damaged wall section (60+) gives 2 wall strength instead of 3; anything at 60+ can be *patched* with a proper repair before it gives way (the autopilot patches defences while it has 20 scrap to spare). No building or repair work goes on at a base on alert or under siege. The Repairs tab shows the last raid's damage and a *Repair all* button with the bill. Parked vehicles lose 1% condition a frost day (2% in a blizzard); the Repairs tab lists them with the usual *Repair (1 part)*.
- **Codex** (`src/ui/codex.js`, C or 📚 Codex in the top bar): every kind of the dead (the ordinary dead, the special dead, the armoured dead, the Wallbreaker and Broodmother, with their battle sprite), the hive and its growths, every item and supply, every building, the illnesses and injuries (the worm, infected wounds), the seven kinds of group and the three kinds of outsider, in seven categories with a "found / total" count. Each entry has its numbers from the game's tables (health, damage, speed, armor, what it follows, its share of a horde; an item's slot, power, skill needed, noise, weight; a building's cost, hours, plot and needs; an illness's course, harm, work and dose; a group's attitude, arms and raids) and a line on how to deal with it. An entry opens the first time your people meet the thing: a kind of the dead seen in a battle or in a horde in sight, a hive or growth you know of, an item someone holds or a base keeps, a building built or queued, an illness someone has (the worm once spotted), a group you know; until then it shows as ??? with its category. New entries flash on the map ("New in the Codex") and are marked new until you look at them. Saved with the campaign (`state.codex`); an older save opens what its world already shows.
- **Search** (`src/ui/cmdpalette.js`, Ctrl+K or /, 🔍 in the top bar): a centered box that matches a few letters of a name (the start of a word first, then anywhere, then the letters in order: "wtr brl" finds Water barrel) across your people, bases, teams, the groups you know, the sites and towns you know, the buildings you can build at the selected base (the base screen open, else the selected tile's base, else the hub), the menu (save, load, options, controls, key sheet, sound on / off, the first-week guide, order templates), the diary, roster, codex, Ledger and run summary, Undo when the screen under the box has something to undo, the Help topics and the codex entries you have opened. Each result shows its kind and a hint (role and base, mission, attitude, why it cannot be built yet). Enter jumps: a person's screen, a base centered and opened, a team's panel, a group's radio panel (or its base on the map), a site centered, the Construction tab with the build button lit up. Empty, the box lists your last picks, then the menu and your bases. Builds its list in about 15 ms with 50 people and 50 groups.
- **Drop-downs you can type in** (`src/ui/selectsearch.js`): a drop-down with 8 or more options (the start-gear and background item pickers, presets, long slot lists, trade and caravan base and stop lists once they grow, the roster's role lists) and the gun builder's Ammo list open a box with a filter instead of the browser's list: typing narrows it to the options with every typed word anywhere in their name or their group's heading ("ak 74", "rifle", "medical"), the typed part marked. ↑ ↓ (Page Up / Down) move over the options that can be picked, Enter or a click picks, Esc, Tab or a click outside closes (Esc only the box, never the window behind it). A letter, Space or Enter on a focused long drop-down opens it with that letter typed; ↑ ↓ on a closed one still step through it. Typing in the box never fires the map's keys. The drop-down stays the real thing (its value set, the same change event), and nothing behind it redraws while the box is open. `data-search="0"` on a select keeps the browser's list, `data-search="1"` gives a short one the box.
- **Keys** (`src/ui/keymap.js`, `src/ui/controls.js`): one table of every key (about 140 actions: the map (the Ledger's E included), the team queue, undo (Ctrl+Z in the planner, build queue and Work tab), battle, the battle view, the timelapse, the title menu), each with its group, context and default keys; the handlers ask the table which action a key press is, so a key changed in Menu → Controls changes everywhere. Adding a key is one line in the table (see the comment at the top of `keymap.js`).
- **Roster** (`src/ui/roster.js`, R): every survivor in one table: where they are, role, health, mood and the risk of leaving, conditions, the nine skills (with a bar for the progress to the next point), weapon, armor and traits. Click a column to sort (again to reverse), filter by place (a base, on teams, away), role and status (hurt, sick, unhappy, fit, no contact), change a role in its row, or tick rows (or everyone shown) and set one role for all of them (people away keep theirs). A name opens the survivor screen over the table; health and mood stay hidden for people you have no contact with.
- **Status icons** (`src/sim/status.js` `STATUS` holds every threshold, `statusesOf`; drawn by `src/ui/status.js` `statusIcons`): small stacking icons next to a name wherever people are listed (the roster, the People tab, the survivor screen, a base's people and equipment tables, the Medical tab, the work grid, the trip and supply-run crew pickers, the team panel, the battle roster without the health one), one per state that is on: 😞 low mood (below 30; red below 15 or when they may break), 🍽 hungry (hunger below 25; red and "Starving: day 3, mood −6 a day" when their base is out of food, or below 15 away from a base), 💧 thirsty (the same for thirst), 🩸 low health (below 40; red below 20), 🦠 infected (an infected wound, or the worm once spotted), 🤒 sick (🩹 when only injured; red at severity 50+ untreated). Each has a tooltip with the detail; only for people you are in touch with.
- **Roster bulk actions** (`src/sim/bulk.js`, `src/ui/rosterbulk.js`): for the ticked people, **🎒 Equip best** hands each one at a base the best weapon, armor (body, head, arms, legs), pack and off-hand tool in their own base's armory (the autopilot's gear choice, `autopilot.js itemScore`: best fighters first, only real upgrades, the old piece back to the armory, nothing taken from anyone else, the worn kit kept within a Medium load or no heavier, a new pack takes the old one's contents) and lists every change under the bar; **🏥 To the hospital** walks the hurt and the ill at a base without a running hospital (built and staffed) to the nearest one; **Move to base…** walks them to any base. Both moves are the supply run's transfer (a one-off convoy on foot that disbands at the far end: road food and water from the base they leave, the road's usual time and risks, never instant). Greyed-out buttons say why on hover (out on a team, captive, on a convoy, an empty armory, no road, no hospital).
- **Watching panel** (`src/sim/watchlist.js`, `src/ui/watchlist.js`): 📌 a person (survivor screen), team (team panel), group (Groups tab), base (Bases tab, tile panel) or a horde in sight (tile panel), or right-click a tile, and it shows in a small panel at the top left of the map, under the Running orders and above the Layers box, with its live status: a person's place, health, mood and task; a team's order, ETA, place, food, water and Heat; a horde's size, heading and distance to your nearest base; a group's attitude, size and strength against yours; a base's days of food and water and the threats in sight. It keeps to what you know (no contact, out of sight: last seen). Click a row to go there; the dead and gone grey out with ✕. Up to 16, folds with ▾ (and with Map only), saved with the campaign.
- **Map notes** (`src/sim/mapnotes.js`, `src/ui/mapnotes.js`, the editor in `src/ui/watchlist.js`): right-click a tile → Add note: one of 8 icons (flag, cache, danger, bridge, target, star, question, skull) and a short text. A small pin on the map (drawn on the dynamic layer), the text in the tile's hover description and beside the pin when zoomed in; listed under Notes in the Watching panel (go, ✎ edit, ✕ twice to delete, 👁 hide them all). Saved with the campaign.
- **Work priorities** (`src/sim/work.js`, the base's **Work** tab in `src/ui/work.js`; RimWorld's work tab): instead of one fixed job, put people in a named role (Generalist, Farmer, Builder, Guard, Medic, Crafter, Night watch, a Scavengers crew, or your own) with a priority 1–5 per work type (blank = never): Doctor, Guard, Farm, Preserve (the kitchen), Workshop (the other stations), Craft, Build, Repair, Teach, Study and Scavenge. Every hour each person does the first type, priority 1 first, that has work and room for more hands (one operator a station, the hospital's medics, a lookout or two, a lesson's learners, a book's copy; Manual people already on it count), the most urgent work and the best skilled first at the same priority; nothing at 1, then 2 …; nothing at all, rest. A person's own cell overrides the role's (drawn ringed); the role can ask for a skill minimum per type. Before priorities: the badly hurt, infected and too ill rest; a raid warning or manned defences send everyone with Guard set to the walls, a horde as many of them as it takes to hold it; the **watchtower alarm** (`defences.js` towerWatch: a base with a watchtower sees a horde coming for it within its view, or one within 3 tiles; a tile of view at night; a log line once and a 🗼 alert while it lasts, no card of its own as a coming horde already gets the lookouts' card) moves Guard up to priority 1 for everyone there who has it set lower than their first priority (a backup job; `work.js` watchPromotions keeps their old cell, the Work grid shows the 1 in red, the log names who moved up and who went back) and the walls take as many more lookouts as it takes to hold that horde with a quarter to spare (at least one), before any other work at priority 1, until the horde has gone (beyond 4 tiles, or a tile past the view while still coming, beaten off or lured away); setting the Guard cell by hand during the alarm ends it for that person, and leaving the base ends it too. The governor and stewards post their guards in step 3 the same way (as many as a horde at the door would want, up to half the people); a patient in danger with nobody on duty and a broken well pump or wall pull the first hands with Doctor / Repair set, and a manual or blueprint in the armory nobody studies pulls the best qualified person with Study set (as the governor: the cisterns, the trench and the clinic wait for them); a queued build always keeps one hand with Build set even when the farm would take everyone (the governor's rule against the builder deadlock), a craft queue with work keeps one hand with Craft set, taken from scavenging or from farmers beyond the governor's crew for the plots (0.6 a plot with food comfortable, 1.2 below; the governor's one crafter; and since that queue never waits for spare hands, the governor or a steward keeps the scrap the next building needs from crafts that can wait: only a filter while the water runs short, a weapon for the unarmed and dressings for short meds dig into it), a queued water filter pulls the first hand with Craft set while the water runs short or while the water carries the worm's eggs and nothing boils it (as the governor; such a filter is never held for full tanks), from autumn the woodcutters it takes to have the winter's firewood in by the cold go before the farm and the builders, up to a quarter of the people (the governor's rule: Cut wood sits under Farm in every default role, so with the plots short of hands nobody cut wood and the hub stocked its winter by woodcutting trips alone), and a queued well, rain catcher or cistern comes before a hungry farm; then the role's schedule (an hour strip: work, guard watch, free time, sleep; the hours off give the Rest role's mood by the hour, and at midnight the daily systems count people at their last work of the day); then free time below 25 mood until 40. Each person's *Now* column says what they do and why ("Building: priority 2 (before it: Farm: full (4 of 4))"). Manual keeps today's fixed job (picking a job by hand, from any role drop-down, the medics list, a station or the study buttons, puts that person on Manual); pinned people keep theirs; stewards only move Manual people. Stations follow their standing orders (without one, only while the base is short of what they make); unless the order is your own they leave the scrap the next building needs (the ammo bench stops under 50 scrap, the med station under 40, the machine shop under 60: higher than the governor's floors, because priorities staff a station as soon as it has work, where the governor only uses hands left over). A water filter the governor or a steward queued waits while the tanks are three-quarters full and something is queued to build and the water is not running short (the crafter works the jobs behind it): crafted into full tanks its water spills over, its scrap went before the build's, and it hides the dry-day shortfall the governor builds rain catchers for. *Everyone here on priorities* gives people a role by what they do now; newcomers can join a role. Repair set at a base makes it start its own repairs. Adding a work type (hunting, fishing) is one entry in `WORK_TYPES`.
- **Lunch** (2026-10-12, TIME-03; `src/sim/meals.js` `lunchHour`, `LUNCH_HOURS`, state.lunchHour; the Work tab's *Lunch* select): a third mealtime, an hour, 12:00 unless the Work tab moves it to 11:00–15:00 (every base; undoable; the role schedules underline it). Everyone eats until they are 80–100% full (their own target, `eating.js`; a lean or short ration level keeps its own target) and gets a small lift to mood (`EAT.lunchMood` 0.25, an hour of free time's). All work stops for it: construction, hand crafting, gear and building repairs (as at breakfast and dinner: `MEAL_WORK_SHARE` 21 of 24 hours) and the garage's vehicle upgrades (lunch only); nobody sets out on a job trip in it, and someone out on one stops where they are and eats from their pack ("Lunch break at forest (3,7) (from their pack)"): a job trip sets out with at least a day of food in the pack (`PACK.trip`, topped up from the stores as they leave), and the lunch hour inside a trip, at the work or on the walk, does not count toward its load (`jobtrips.js` `tripHours`: a woodcutter's or forager's day is about an hour of work shorter; the hunters' and fishers' dawn and dusk trips are outside it). The guards stay on the walls; farms, stations, teaching and reading are counted at midnight as a day's work and are unchanged. The AI groups stop too: their crews build `MEAL_WORK_SHARE` of a day and their woodcutters lose the hour (`grouptrips.js` `GROUP_LUNCH_SHARE`, 11 of 12 outdoor hours); their food (mostly fields) is unchanged. The Kitchen's cooks serve it. Old saves get lunch at 12:00.
- **Crews** (`src/sim/crews.js`, the Work tab's Crews panel): a role of kind *crew* forms a team that runs trips from the hub by itself, by the role's field priorities (scavenge the best known sites for what the hub needs, recover your dead's gear, bring vehicles home and strip wrecks, trade visits, supply runs to outposts; more are one entry in `FIELD_TYPES`) within its limits (range, hours out, team size, stance, vehicle or on foot, ammo, meds, rest days at home) and come-home rules (hurt, food, cargo: the plan's own conditions). It uses the governor's judgement through `autopilot.js crewAI` (site scores, the hub's needs, how to search and when to leave, the answers to road cards, trades) in the role's style, searches sites hour by hour on its own, may search one more site nearby, answers its small road cards and leaves you the big ones (fights with people, hives, recruits, raids, jobs) unless the role says *Handle everything*. Its team carries the role's name on the map; the log and the panel keep its trips; *Pause* and *Send now* in the panel. At home its members work by the role's priorities.

## Type checking

The code stays plain JavaScript (no build step, nothing to install to play), and TypeScript checks it from JSDoc comments, one module at a time: the first step of the move to TypeScript, so nothing has to be rewritten.

```bash
npm run typecheck        # or: node tools/typechecktest.js [--list]
```

It runs the TypeScript checker (`tsc`, pinned to 5.6.3) with `jsconfig.json`: a local install if there is one, else `npx` fetches it once into its cache (offline with neither, the check says SKIPPED and passes; `npm install --no-save typescript` in `prototype/` makes it work offline). It reports each opted-in module as passed or failed with its errors, fails if a core module drops out or a module imports the types file at run time, and takes a few seconds (the first run longer).

- Checked: the modules whose first line is `// @ts-check` (`checkJs` is off, so the others are read for their types but not checked). The sim's core is typed: `world.js`, `parties.js`, `save.js`, `rng.js`, `hex.js`, `data.js`, `economy.js`, `weather.js` (their functions have `@param` types); `buildings`, `health`, `hiveconst`, `hives`, `hordes`, `inventory`, `markers`, `supply`, `vehicles`, `water`, `worldsize` and `zombietypes` are opted in as they are (what they call in the typed modules is checked). `strict` is off for now.
- The shapes: `src/sim/types.d.ts` (State, Tile, Survivor, Item, Base, Building, Vehicle, Party, Order, Faction, Horde, Hive, HiveSite, Card, Choice, the stocks…), read like C# records (`field?: T` is optional). Never loaded by the game. The big records (State, Survivor, Base, Party, Faction, Card, Tile, Item…) also allow fields that are not listed, since many modules keep a few of their own there; the small ones (Horde, Hive, HiveSite, Vehicle, DeathSite, TileSnapshot) do not, so a misspelt field is an error.
- To opt a module in: put `// @ts-check` on its first line and `/** @import { State, Party } from "./types" */` under the header comment, give its functions `/** @param {State} state @param {Party} p */`, run the check and fix what it finds with JSDoc (`@type` on an object literal, a `@typedef` for an options object). Where a fix would change what the code does, leave `// @ts-expect-error <why>` and look at it as a possible bug. Never `import` the types file in code (the browser has no `types.js`).

## Balance sheet

`docs/balance-sheet/index.html` is one self-contained page (open it by double-click; no server, no network) with every gun, melee weapon, magazine, internal magazine, calibre, ammo type, round, box size and bulk container, their numbers and what follows from them (hits on no armour / a vest / a plate, damage per kg, points, box sizes and prices, find weights). Sort by any column, filter, hide columns, edit the blue values (rarity tiers by dropdown, the order of rarity and its find weights in the side panel), then **Export changes**: a JSON patch (downloaded and shown to copy). Edits stay in the browser between visits; **Import changes** loads a patch back.

```bash
node tools/balancesheet.js                  # regenerate the page from the game's data (after any change to it)
node tools/applybalance.js patch.json --dry # what a patch would change
node tools/applybalance.js patch.json       # write it into the data files, then check the game loads the new values
node tools/balancesheettest.js              # the sheet, its formulas against the game, and a patch round trip
```

`applybalance` edits only the literal each value comes from (combat.js WEAPONS, data-guns.js, data.js GEAR, data-ammo.js, data-kit.js, data-stacks.js, mags.js, custompoints.js, startgear.js), refuses a change whose `from` is no longer the source's value (regenerate the sheet and redo it), and exits 1 if anything was refused. Afterwards regenerate the sheet and run `tools/arsenalbalancetest.js` (the damage ladder's rules).

## Code layout

```
src/sim/   pure simulation, no DOM (world, mapgen, parties, supply, factions, hordes, economy, buildings, vehicles,
           markers, events, conflicts, combat, scavenge, trade, radio, caravans, medical, crafting, weather = seasons and weather,
           valuation = what things are worth to whom (hidden base values, kinds of group, their state, worth tiers),
           farming = crop plots and the winter outlook, storage = food storage, spoilage and preserving, health = illness and injuries, needs = hunger, thirst, treats and heating, woodcutting = firewood (the land, the Cut wood job and order, fires), chronicle = timelapse recording, history = the Ledger's daily rows (stocks, in and out, people, the colony's state),
           autopilot = AI governor for the dev tools, groupworks = what the AI groups build at their bases (barestart), plans = trip plans and the radio rule, production = standing
           station and craft orders, power = generators, the still and powered buildings, steward = per-base stewards,
           fieldheat = Heat for teams that stand still (and the camp kit against it), raidplan = choices before a raid
           (the side, a scout first, the night), raidloot = raid loot by hand
           (each survivor's load, the vehicle's trunk, the search window's moves, splitting stacks), search = the full
           search (1 action) and the quick rummage (part of it, loud) in raids, loot = small finds per site and
           raid area and the theme tags (tools/loottest.js), comforts = mood items and treats, throwables = grenades, pipe bombs,
           Molotovs and fire, flares and smoke, throws over walls, siege = ladders, the ram, rocks from the walls, raiders' siege kit
           and the overlay data the battle renderers draw, decoys = what the dead sense, decoys, fire's pull and the Shepherd's aura,
           kit = what a trip takes from the armory (and an Assault's siege kit), recruits = newcomers' cards, morale = crowding, warnings
           and desertion, hubevents = decisions for the days at home, diary = every log line by day with categories and links,
           causes = "Why?": the chain of causes of a death or a lost base, and the day each stock ran out,
           relations = friends, rivals, grief, the service record, the memorial wall and the daily mood factors,
           family = couples, expecting, births and children growing up into survivors (state.children),
           skills = skill growth, decay, teaching, books and perks, locks = locked containers, rooms and keys,
           blueprints = manuals and tech locks, crashes = crash sites and their search circles, breakdowns = wear and repairs,
           jobs = jobs for other groups, diplomacy = relations, pacts and wars between groups, alliance = the coalition and its victory,
           blocs = alliances between groups (forming by type and shared threat, help, joint raids, shared intel, betrayal), playerwars = wars declared on you or by you (grievances, goals, war raids and sieges, weariness, terms),
           conquest = assaulting groups' bases, infamy, bases lost to raids, retreat, the exiles and retaking a base,
           aiscav = other groups' scavenging parties, loot that never grows back and the loot bands,
           visitors = outsiders from beyond the state (raiders, merchants, explorers),
           work = work priorities: roles, the WORK_TYPES table and the hourly scheduler, crews = crew roles' own trips,
           hunting = hunting, snares, fishing, the game on the land, fresh game and the hunting cabin,
           worm = the worm (catching it from water, raw meat and camping; its stages, spotting, cures and turning; the water
           boiler and cook fire; infested herds),
           survey = the Survey mission (routes, odds, the planner's word) and rumours, optics = binoculars, scopes and the telescope,
           breakoff = fights by the numbers in rounds and the "Retreat if it goes badly" policies (escape by the numbers),
           deploy = a base battle's wall map and the deploy step, interior = its yard and buildings map (the second and third tiers),
           buildingmaps = a raided building's own map (archetype floor plans, doors, windows, exits, locks, who is inside),
           citycrawl = the city crawl (blocks, places, street events, the Alert, encounters, the way out),
           battlesize = battle map sizes by context (the size table), cover (densify), where the sides start, the "clear enough" end,
           save = the world to a JSON-safe save and back, versions and migrations, savesummary = a save's one-line summary and campaign,
           save = the world to a JSON-safe save and back, versions and migrations,
           cardrules = pause rules for cards: each card's kind, its default answer, staleness, holding its team,
           hardcore = Hardcore mode's dispatch, hardcore-start = its start on the road and the first hub,
           staging = the staging camp with no base (Establish base, the stores, the work, neglect, its water and fire; done: the shelter, tier 0, built up into the hub),
           hardcore-outbreak = its outbreak, hardcore-groups = its groups, hardcore-loot = its half-picked-over sites,
           wanderers = people on the move (survivors, armed strangers, military squads), thirdside = the third side in battle,
           bulk = the roster's bulk actions (equip best, to the hospital, move to base), watchlist = the Watching panel's list, mapnotes = map notes,
           carriers = the Carrier (bodies that get up at once wearing what they wore), its worm swarm (bullets pass through, its bite infests), crushing, shooting, burning and carrying bodies, the walking loot, bodies brought home)
src/ui/    map renderer, DOM panels/modals, combat screen, timelapse viewer, diary + log panel, alerts + map keys, roster,
           ledger = the Ledger, the run summary (also the end screens' body) and the top bar's stock trends,
           explore = Repairs tab, blueprints and key ring, locks = the raid panel's Open buttons and padlocks,
           diplomacy = Goals card, Jobs list and the Diplomacy window (jobs, market board, world affairs, coalition),
           settings = the menu (save, load, options) and the autosave, saves = save slots in browser storage, compression, files,
           the rotating autosaves and the crash slot, savelist = the save list grouped by campaign, crash = the error screen,
           visitors = the Groups tab's Outsiders section, their alerts and the trade window's merchant buttons,
           work = the Work tab (priority grid, role editor, schedule, crews) and the role drop-down,
           trips = order templates, Recent trips and Repeat trip (with sim/trips.js), undo = undo in the planner, build queue and Work tab,
           layout = folded panels, panel widths, window sizes and auto-open (rules), windows = their bars, grips and drags,
           causes = the "Why?" links and panel,
           hardcore = the panels with no base (top bar, the first-hub / staging camp card, alerts, Goals line, tile row and camp card),
           citycrawl = the city crawl's window (block card, encounters, HUD),
           keymap = every key in one table (rebindable), controls = Menu → Controls and the key sheet,
           codex = the Codex (entries, what has been seen), cmdpalette = the search box (Ctrl+K),
           audio = sound (WebAudio voices made on the spot, ambience, the Sound options), soundcues = what to play when
           guide = the first-week guide under the alerts (steps, Show me and its pulse, the menu's row)
           rosterbulk = the roster's bulk buttons and report, watchlist = the Watching panel, its 📌 pins, the right-click extras and
           the map-note editor, mapnotes = notes drawn on the map
           pacing = the pause rules, the card tray, the stops with no card, the speeds (keys 1–5) and the drop to 1×
src/sim/types.d.ts  the core shapes for the type checker (never loaded; Type checking above); jsconfig.json = its settings
src/main.js  game loop, input, action dispatcher
tools/     headless balance scripts; tools/saves/ = the save corpus (old builds' saves, tools/savecorpustest.js)
```

The `sim` folder is kept separate from rendering, so the rules could be ported to another engine (for example Godot with C#) almost one-to-one.

`node tools/raidtest.js 10 7` plays raids with an auto-player per site type and reports extraction rate, deaths, loot and vehicles kept (`node tools/raidtest.js 3 7 all` covers every site type; add `kit` to give everyone bottles, a noise maker, a flashlight and the team a suppressor, and `night` to start the raids at 22:00: `node tools/raidtest.js 10 7 x kit night`). The auto-player ties up the knocked out, takes down an unaware guard next to it, stalks a lone guard from behind and throws things to pull guards away; the line under the table counts throws, takedowns, ties and muffled volleys.

### Building interiors

Scavenging a building on the map (`src/sim/buildingmaps.js`) opens the building's own map instead of the street raid: a footprint split into rooms by an archetype's floor plan (house, farmhouse, cabin, lodge, ranger station; shops: grocery, corner store, pharmacy, gun store, hardware, feed store, diner, gas station, truck stop; supermarket and department store; corridor buildings: clinic, infirmary, hospital, police station, school, offices, lab, barracks, armory, prison cell block, mess hall, dam control building, transmitter hut, airport terminal, fire station; open floors: warehouse, factory, hangar, motor pool, auto shop, garage, workshop, machine shed, barn; church, motel, apartment block), with a little street, parking or yard around it. Maps run from about 17 × 16 (a cabin) to 38 × 30 (a supermarket). Each room is a raid area with its own loot type (`combat.js AREAS`) and furniture: aisles of tall shelving (they block sight), counters, tables, beds, racks, cell bars, cars in the bays. The same site always has the same floor plan.
- **Doors and windows**: a closed door blocks sight. Your people open it by walking through; other people stop in the doorway to open it (the rest of their move); the dead cannot open doors and batter them down (hit points: an inner door 30, an outer one 45, a cell door 60, a locked door 55, a steel security door 90, a roller door 70; a claw blow does about 10–16, so one of the dead takes about 3 turns on an inner door, two together fewer). Every blow is noisy (Heat, and the dead and guards within 7 tiles come to look), and a battered door stays broken. A roller door (bays, loading docks) goes up whole and loudly (Heat +3, wakes what is within 8 tiles). *Close door* (Shift+E, 1 action, quiet; a roller door is hauled down loudly) shuts an open door next to the unit, *Brace door* (Y, the rest of the turn) holds a closed one: people cannot open it (they must kick or shoot it in) and it takes half of each blow, until the unit moves. Closing a door behind you when running really buys turns (in the tests the dead reached someone behind an open door in 2 turns, behind a shut one in 6). *Peek* (K, free, once a turn per unit) looks through the door or doorway next to the unit: the room beyond is seen (8 tiles from the far side of the door, tinted on the map) until the end of the turn, with who is in it, without stepping in; an unaware guard facing the door may catch it (35% − 2.5% a Stealth point, at least 5%), the dead right behind it 20%. Windows are seen through and half cover; *Climb window* (L, or click the window next to the unit; 1 action) smashes one and climbs through: Heat +3, wakes what is within 6 tiles, and the window stays open. A few windows are already broken.
- **Noise indoors**: thrown things (bottles, noise makers, firecrackers, flares, Molotovs...) go through open doorways but not through walls, closed doors, tall shelving or glass (they drop where they hit). Walls and closed doors muffle a noise (each counts as 3 tiles more). The dead already after you turn to a thrown noise too unless one of your people is within 3 tiles and in their sight: shut a door, step back, throw something into another room and slip out while they go to it (and batter any closed door on the way). The raid bot does the same on its way out: it shuts doors behind it and throws lures away from its exit.
- **Item condition by source** (`src/sim/itemcond.js` COND_SOURCES; `node tools/itemcondtest.js` prints the table): quality and uses left come from where an item was found. Shops (gun stores, hardware stores, malls, pharmacies) are picked over after months abandoned: good but used (quality 50–88%, uses 45–85%), 6% of finds still boxed and near new; their edge is how much they hold, not the condition. Homes are as picked over (50–90%, 40–90%). Both keep getting worse with the years since the fall (`COND_AGE`, by the game's day; a normal game's day 1 counts as 84 days after the fall, a hardcore run counts from the fall itself): as above through year 1, then sliding a little each year to a floor reached late in year 8 (shops 35–72% / 30–70%, homes 35–75% / 30–75%; a shop's gun averages 71% quality in year 1, 64% in year 5, 56% from year 9); sealed boxes keep. Military crates (75–100%, 60–100%) and gun safes and cages keep better and do not age; a gun store's own cages and safes roll in the military's range (no better than the Army's). Soldiers' and hired contractors' guns come off their bodies from 55% uses (typically 60–90%), guards' and caravan escorts' from 45%, everyone else's from 25%, on the battle map, by the numbers and in the groups' arsenals alike.
- **Risk pays**: what containers hold on the raid map (inside a building or on the street) scales with the site's danger: the countryside ×0.6, small towns ×0.9, city blocks ×1.3, hospitals, prisons and military bases ×1.5, open land ×0.55 (`buildingmaps.js REWARD`). With the dead indoors as they are, city buildings are the high-risk, high-reward raids. `node tools/riskreward.js [raids] [seed]` measures loot value, deaths and how often the bot gets out by kind of site (inside, the street map, the hour-by-hour search).
- **Exits**: the front entrance always; back doors, side doors, fire exits, loading docks, bay doors and a police station's sally port by archetype. Each has its green extraction zone at the map edge outside it; the team (and the vehicle) start in front. Some side and back exits are locked.
- **Locks** (`locks.js`): locked rooms where they belong (an armory, a gun cage, a pharmacy's dispensary, an evidence room, a manager's office, a records room...) with locked containers inside (gun cages, drug cabinets, evidence lockers, safes); open them by key, lockpicks or force from either side.
- **Upper floors**: some houses, farmhouses, lodges, apartment blocks and office buildings have rooms one level up the stairs (a barn's hayloft up a ladder): the usual verticality (sight, aim, cover).
- **Who is inside**: the site's dead (scaled down with the smaller map, a quarter of them outside, now and then one shut in a locked room) and guards in the rooms; people met there come from `wanderers.js interiorOccupants` (hostile ones are placed as guards; neutral survivors and military squads wait for a side of their own: `combat.js registerOccupantSide`).
- **Which building**: single-building sites (a pharmacy, a police station, a house...) open straight into it; towns, cities, farms, ranches, malls, military bases, prisons, universities, airports, factories, warehouse districts, the dam, suburbs and open farmland offer their buildings first (with what each likely holds; or the street map as before; the choice lapses once anyone acts). Campgrounds, crash sites and the wilds keep the street map. Headless the search stays hour by hour; the AI picks a building by its own dice when it plays a raid on the map.
- **Map size** (`src/sim/battlesize.js` fitMargins): the building is its archetype's floor plan; at a single-building site the outside around it grows until the map is at least the site's size class (a hospital 40 × 30, a gas station or pharmacy 28 × 20; the parking lot and street in front, the yard and alley behind), at a site with several at least Small (24 × 18), and the new ground is topped up with cover (cars, crates, trees by the ground). A supermarket's or school's plan alone is already Medium to Large.
- **The view**: in the isometric view a building keeps its roof until one of your people (or an enemy you see, or the mouse) is inside; then the roof comes off and only the walls within 6 tiles of your people drop to stubs, so the rest stand. Room names on the floor, a padlock on locked rooms. `node tools/interiortest.js [seeds] [raids]` checks every archetype (reachability, exits, locks, containers), the sites, the choice, doors, windows and plays raids on interiors.

### City crawl

A team you drive that reaches a city to scavenge (a city centre, or a city block with no site of its own) does not get one raid: it works its way in block by block, a roguelike extraction (`src/sim/citycrawl.js`, the window `src/ui/citycrawl.js`). Towns and every other site keep the normal raid; on autopilot and headless (the tools) a city is still searched hour by hour, so the census tools and a normal game take the old path.
- **Moving a team onto a city** (a Move order, the team you drive, no orders queued behind it) opens the *City crawl* card in place of the team's orders card: what a crawl is, the city's Alert now, how often you have been in; *Go in block by block* starts the crawl at block 1, *Not now* brings back the orders card (its Scavenge / raid still starts a crawl there). On autopilot, with battles resolved by the numbers and headless the orders card comes as before (`citycrawl.js` `offerCrawl`, from `parties.js` arrive).
- **A block** is a card: two to four places (buildings from the city's pool: grocery, corner store, pharmacy, apartments, offices, warehouse, hardware, auto shop, diner, motel, church; from block 2 a school, clinic, fire station, supermarket and the rare gun store; from block 3 the police station, hospital and department store), each with what it likely holds, its size and how dangerous it looks (Scouting 5+ reads it right; "movement inside" means people), and up to two street events: an abandoned car, a crashed ambulance, a barricaded checkpoint (block 2+), someone calling from a window, a horde passing through, smoke from a looted store, a raider lookout (2+), a stuck delivery truck, a body with a pack, a supply drop on a rooftop (4+), a collapsed street that blocks the way deeper (3+). Each event is a small choice resolved by the numbers; the ambulance and the checkpoint can also be searched on a small street map. Buttons: *Go in* / *Search quickly* (by the numbers, 2 h) on each place, *Lie low* (1 h, the Alert fades), *Go to the next block*, *Leave the city*.
- **The same city has the same blocks**: the places are seeded by the world, the tile and the block; a later visit has the same buildings, maybe one shut (fire, a collapse, chained doors) and other street events. The site remembers how picked over each place is (`tile.crawlMem`): less is left on the next crawl.
- **Going in** opens the building's interior map (the raid, `buildingmaps.js`; the vehicle waits at the city's edge). Extracting at any exit returns to the same block card, which now offers *Go back in*: the building as you left it (searched containers searched, doors open, windows broken, the dead you killed lying where they fell, the ones you lured where they went, unaware again). Wounds, ammo, what you carry and the noise carry over.
- **Depth** (`CRAWL` in citycrawl.js): the dead in a building × (0.7 + 0.25 a block deeper) and +0.4 power a block; raiders holed up in places from block 2 (8% + 6% a block); the special dead from block 3 (6% of them + 5% a block deeper + 10% a game year gone, at most 45%); more of the dead the Heat can call in (+2 a block). Rewards: container richness × (1 + 15% a block), rarer places deeper (gun store, police armory, hospital pharmacy).
- **The city's Alert** (0–100, Calm → Swarming): starts at 5 + 15 × infection, + 30% of the Heat a building gained, + 8 a street fight by the numbers (5 + the ammo spent on a map), + 3 a block walked, + 12 a car alarm; −2 an hour, −10 lying low. Each building starts at Heat 5 by day (0 at night) + 2 a block deeper + 35% of the Alert above the city's own.
- **Moving and trouble**: a block deeper is an hour on foot with 8% + 7% a block + 0.4% an Alert point of trouble (×1.25 at night): a horde (3 + 1.5 a block + Alert / 12 of the dead, the special dead deeper) or, from block 2, a raider patrol. Answer it: *Fight* on a street map or *by the numbers* (the odds shown), *Hide* (Stealth + d10 against 6 + 0.6 a block + Alert / 20, ±night, load and team size), *Throw* a noise maker, firecrackers or a bottle (90 / 85 / 70%), or *Fall back* / *Run* (each person hurt at 20% + 6% a block, more overloaded, more from raiders). Failing to hide or lure means they saw you.
- **Leaving** is the gamble: the way back to the edge is half an hour a block, each block from yours back to block 2 a roll (3% + 7% a block + 0.4% an Alert point + 25% × overload, ×1.2 at night); from block 1 you are out straight away. At Alert 70+ from block 3 or deeper the first block on the way is a breakout (a horde 1.5× the size between you and the edge). Dying in the city leaves what the team carried at a death site as usual. The card shows the risk (about 40–60% of trouble from block 4, 80%+ from block 6).
- **The HUD**: depth, the Alert, the clock (night), each person's health, ammo, meds, the load on foot (what the team carries against what its people can carry without the vehicle: overloaded is slower to hide and run and riskier on the way out) and what this run has found.
- **Saving** works on a block card and inside a building (the crawl is data on the team, `p.crawl`). A crawl left open when the autopilot comes on is played by its own simple policy (search blocks 1–2 by the numbers, leave hurt or loaded). `node tools/citycrawltest.js [seeds] [stats]` checks all of this (`stats`: the raid bot in a building at each depth).

### Stealth, prisoners and the deploy step

- **Takedowns and prisoners** (`src/sim/stealth.js`, `prisoners.js`): *Subdue* on an unaware human (or one at 30% health or less) is a takedown that knocks them out: 30% + 2.5 × Stealth, a spiked bat +20, a blade +10, fists or a gun butt +5, from behind (the side they do not face) +15, from cover +10, Brave or Tough +5 each (Coward or Frail −5), the Assassin perk +20, soldiers −15, zealots −5, an aware but staggering target −15 (5–95%). A failed takedown is a blunt blow (60% damage, never lethal) and the target cries out. On an aware enemy Subdue is a non-lethal blow. The knocked out wake in 4–6 turns unless tied up (*Tie up*, 1 action). After a won fight they are prisoners (the result screen: *Take them* or *Leave them*, goodwill +2); in a raid a tied prisoner dragged into an extraction zone leaves with whoever extracts there. On the road they walk with the team (on foot 10% slower each, at least 60% of its pace; in a vehicle they ride), eat 1 food and 1 water a day each and slip away 5% a day (2% in a vehicle). At home: a **jail** (below) and the base's **Prisoners** tab (a summary on top: food a day at their rations, labour by task, who is watched, the day's escape risk; then a row each with health, mood, opinion, days held, a rations and a work select, guard status, escape odds and the actions with their odds and costs): 1 water a day each and their **rations** of food (set each or all): *Full* 1 (heals +2 a day, escapes ×0.8, wins them over ×1.2, asking to join +5%), *Short* 0.6 (the default, and old saves: holds them near 60 health), *Starvation* 0.3 (−1.5 health a day, escapes ×1.5, winning over ×0.4 and just held they sour, asking −15%, questioning +10%, below 20 health a 3% chance a day of dying), *None* (unfed: −6 health a day, dead in six days; questioning +15%, asking −25%); the base's food line counts them at their rations (`upkeep.js`). While anyone there is on Starvation or None your people lose 1 mood a day (a Former Medic 2, the Brave, Tough and Iron Will nothing). Escapes by the jail's tier (below), +4% unfed, ×1.25 when their mood is under 20. **Labour** (set each or all): *Idle* in the cell, or out 07–19 at 50–70% of a survivor by the task's skill (2% a point) × rations (Full 1, Short 0.9, Starvation 0.6, None 0.3) × health (too weak under 25): *Farm* (adds to the farm labour that tends the crops), *Build* (builder-hours on the construction queue, 12 hours a day), *Fetch water* (+4 a day at full output), *Kitchen* (dries and salts surplus fresh food by hand, 4 → 3 preserved), *Clean and mend* (wear off the buildings as a builder's upkeep), *Cut wood* (firewood by hand from the best land within 2 tiles at the Cut wood job's rates, no axe), *Haul and sort scrap* (1.5 scrap a day at full output); a Shelter has no buildings to mend and water only beside a river or lake. Work is also × their **mood** (0.6 + 0.6 × mood / 100, 0.6–1.15; the tab says "works at 72%: mood low"), and a good day of work counts from 30% (it was 40% before mood counted). A guard watches up to 4 at work (3 from makeshift cells, all of them from a jailhouse's guard post; the Guard work type wants one for the cells and one per that many at work): watched they run 1% a day, unwatched 4%, × the jail (makeshift 1.5, a jailhouse 0.5), ×1.3 more under mood 25, when they also spoil a little of what they worked on 4% of days (sabotage); nobody goes out while the base is under attack. Work tires them (mood −0.5 to −2 a day, health −0.5 on Short, −1.5 on Starvation); each good day of work (Full or Short) adds 1% to the winning-over rate (up to +40%) and 0.4% to asking them to join (up to +10%). *Question* (every 3 days, Speech, +1% a point of the leader's Leadership) puts their group's bases, people, stock, parties out (raids too) and the hives near them on your map; *Roughly*: +20%, but they lose 10 health and 15 mood (opinion −15), everyone there 2 mood (a Former Medic 4) and their group 3 goodwill; *Ask to join* (every 7 days): (opinion − 30) / 140 × who they were + 0.5% a point of Leadership and Speech + rations + labour + mood, 2–95% (at opinion 80 on Short with a good leader and talker about 45%, Full ~50%, Starvation ~30%; with no Leadership or Speech 36%: winning someone over takes real effort); a no costs 8 opinion; *Ask a ransom* (goods worth 15 + 4 × power; paid in 2–4 days at 40% + goodwill / 200, −15% for aggressive groups; goodwill −4 paid, −3 refused); *Trade back* (goodwill +10); *Release* (+4); *Win them over* (1 a day × Speech, Leadership, the base's mood and who they were, zealots 0.35; at 100, about 2–4 months, a recruit card); *Execute* (mood −6 for everyone there, Brave or Tough −2, a Former Medic −8; goodwill −20 with them, −4 with every known group; infamy +3; the other prisoners there are cowed for 14 days: escapes halved, mood −10). `tools/prisonertest.js` covers rations, labour, guards, the actions, saves and the tab. Their group may come for them: 1.2% a day per prisoner (×1.5 aggressive, ×2 after a refused ransom, ×0.3 friendly; needs 6+ people within 25 tiles); a warning card 10 hours ahead: defend in person (the deploy battle), man the walls (defence ×1.3 by the numbers) or hand them over (goodwill +3). Lost, they free their people and carry off a tenth of the stores.
- **Jails** (`src/sim/buildings.js` family "jail", `prisoners.js` JAIL; `node tools/jailtest.js [seed]`, a few seconds): no jail, no prisoners. Three tiers, each upgrading the one below in place: *Makeshift cells* (6 scrap, 6 hours, no skill, a utility plot, a shelter can build them): 2 prisoners, escapes 2% a day guarded / 6% unguarded, a smuggled pick (12% of new prisoners carry a pick, key or blade), a loner's tricks or Stealth 6+ ×1.5 each, mood −1 a day and won over at 0.6×, a rescue meets the defence ×0.85; *Holding cells* (the old `cells` key, 12 scrap, a part, 16 hours, a building plot): 3, 0.4% / 3%, ×1.25; *Jailhouse* (30 scrap, 2 parts, 36 hours, Mechanics 2): 8, 0.2% / 1.5%, ×1.1, a guard on its post watches every prisoner at work, the work detail ×0.5, opinion ×1.1, a rescue ×1.25. Older saves keep their holding cells (two of them hold 6) and anyone in the old shed (8% a day) until a cell frees up, they are moved or let go. Room is a base's jail less those held and those on their way (`freeCells`); a team's prisoners are booked into a base with room (home first) and walked on by an escort when it gets home; those beyond the room of all your bases (a fight, a team home, surrendered defenders after an assault) each get a card: Release, Execute, Ask to join on the spot (12% + 1.2% a point of the best talker's Speech + 0.8% of the leader's Leadership, × who they were, 3–45%; no: let go), Hold at a base with room. The autopilot holds where there is room, asks those with a skill of 5+, releases the rest, and walks shed prisoners to free cells. The governor wants makeshift cells once anyone has been taken prisoner, holding cells when they fill, a jailhouse when those fill and more keep coming. The battle's Tie up tooltip and result screen show the room ("Room for 2 more prisoners at …"); the Prisoners tab the jail, its tier and next step, the room at every base, and a Move button.
- **The groups' jails** (`src/sim/groupjails.js`, `groupworks.js`; jailtest): the groups build makeshift cells, holding cells and a jailhouse at their main base as a work (after water, fields and quarters; raiders, the military, the fortress and groups at war early, the rest once they hold someone, communes after their fields; a full one is upgraded, a jailhouse from 15 people). Your people they hold (and other groups' people: `f.prisoners`, taken alive in raids they win between them by the winner's type, nonlethal.js NL_TAKE: traders 45%, communes 40%, the military and the fortress 35%, raiders 25%, expansionists 20%, zealots none, as many as their jail holds; and a war raid's captive; this is the one model of prisoners between groups, a save's older head counts are turned into it) count against it. No room: raiders, expansionists and traders sell to a group with room (unsold: raiders and expansionists kill, traders let go; your person: kept over their room for 6 days for you to ransom them through the ransom talks (ransom.js; a buyer is looked for every day), then raiders and expansionists kill them, traders let them go), the military, the fortress and communes let go, zealots kill; your captive's fate comes on a card. They work their prisoners (a worker's share each by type, raiders 0.9 to communes 0.3, × a mood stand-in): food, scrap and faster building. Your people held are worked too (health −1.2 a day by raiders, less elsewhere, down to 8, then a 4% chance a day of dying; mood −2 by raiders), escape by their jail, one formula with rescue's hidden tools (idle days at the cell rate, worked days at the work detail's × the tier's labour, raiders' prisoners ×1.4; Stealth 0.1% a point × the tier's bonus; a pin, lockpicks or keys kept hidden +0.5% / +1% / +1.5% × the tier's bonus over the cells' (×1.2 makeshift, ×0.88 a jailhouse); no jail there counts as makeshift: about 2.7% / 1.3% / 0.6% a day by tier at raiders), and turn ×1.5 once broken (mood under 20). Trades a day: ransom back (4%, not at war), exchange one for one (6%), sale (3%: raiders and the like sell to raiders and expansionists as labour, the military and the fortress buy only to free them), turning (1.2% after 30 days); your captives can be sold on (1.2% a day: the card says where, and a rescue follows their group and tile). Relations between groups move with it; trades you would hear of are radio news. `groupJailTier(f, tile, state)` gives the rescue raid's map (rescueraid.js `jailTier`) its tier: the lock, the ropes, the windows, and the same names as your jails (makeshift cells, a chain-link cage; holding cells, barred cells; a jailhouse, a jailhouse block). The Groups card shows "holding cells, holds 3 prisoners (1 of yours)" once a prisoner of theirs has talked or you have seen their base. Older saves: no limits on their side.
- **The stealth kit** (`src/sim/stealth.js`): bottles, noise makers and firecrackers (thrown 7–8 tiles, 1 action) pull every unaware guard and the dead within 6 / 8 / 13 tiles to look for 3 turns (a noise maker keeps ringing 3 turns; firecrackers add 6 Heat in a raid). A guard who sees a body or a tied-up comrade within 5 tiles (3 at night) raises the alarm for its group (+6 Heat); *Drag* moves a body, someone knocked out or a downed teammate 3 tiles for 1 action. People see the way they face: from behind a guard only hears you (half the detection range). An *Oil-filter suppressor* (workshop, Mechanics 4) carried by someone with a pistol, hunting rifle or assault rifle halves a shot's noise (Heat and earshot) and wears a shot at a time (12). Night raids (20:00–06:00): enemy sight 7 (the dead 5), detection ×0.6; your people see 7, 9 with a *Flashlight* in the off hand (seen from 3 tiles further), 13 with night vision; a full search is 1 action in the dark too, but a quick rummage without a light is heard 2 tiles further. Recipes: noise maker (2 scrap, field), firecrackers (1 scrap + 8 ammo, field), flashlight (2 scrap, 1 part), suppressor (4 scrap, 1 part, workshop); found in kitchens, houses, stores, garages, barracks, diners, gas stations and hardware stores.
- **Deploy before a break-in** (`src/sim/deploy.js`): a horde breaking in, raiders and outsiders defended in person and a rescue raid open on the base's map with what was built: with walls, the wall map (below) with the gate and the breaches already made (the dead +1 at 120 and 250 inside, +1 per Wallbreaker; people cut 1), barricades behind it, the trench and spike pits in front, and a watchtower's two towers (height 2: full cover, +10 aim down, the widest view). The map is turned so the attack comes from the side the horde (or the raiders, or their home) lies on the State Map. Place each defender on a green cell; whoever stands in the gate or a breach holds it (blocks it, and the battle AI keeps them there, as on a tower); floodlights (lit) are aimed at a third of the approach (+12 to hit at night, +5 by day in the light). *Default positions* (also headless and the autopilot): with 7+ defenders the toughest close fighter holds the gate (two with 9+), the best shots take the towers, the next best the walkway, close fighters wait a few tiles behind the openings, the rest stand well back so the dead cross open ground.
- **Flashbangs** (`src/sim/throwables.js` FLASH, `flashReach`, `stunUnit`, `flashSpot`; `stealth.js` THROWABLES.flashbang; Help → *Flashbangs*; `tools/flashtest.js`): thrown like the others (G, 7 tiles, 0.4 kg, value 20). A bang and a blinding flash: living people of either side within 2 tiles who see the burst cell (1 tile when the sight line crosses a doorway or a window; walls stop it) are *stunned* (`u.stun`): next turn they lose an action (2 within a tile), aim −30 until that turn ends (melee −15), and no overwatch (theirs drops at once). Facing away (`u.face`) and the thrower: only *dazed*, aim −15. A riot helmet's visor (GEAR `visor`) takes 30% off, ear protection `u.earCut` (a 0–1 share, when another branch adds it) its share (an action less at 0.25, two at 0.5). The dead are not blinded: the bang is a noise lure (12 tiles, a decoy for the enemy turn after it, so the aware dead turn to it too). 0–3 damage within a tile, no cover broken; it wakes the people within 12 tiles, gunfire noise, Heat +8 in a raid. A flashbang can be tossed through a doorway you see into a room nobody sees into. Soldiers (30%) and guards (20%) carry one (`FLASH_KIT`, their own dice; smoke grenades: soldiers 35%, contractors 30%, militia 12%, guards 10%, fighters 8%, raiders 4%, popped to cover a retreat: `siege.js` coverSmoke); they throw it at 3–7 tiles on two or more of your people bunched with one in cover and none of their own within 3 tiles, then rush; the autopilot and the raid bot (only at people who know the team is there) throw yours on two or more. Unthrown ones are on their bodies. Loot, all through `itemcond.js` and dice of their own: search hours at police stations, prisons, the National Guard, Army wrecks and convoys (`loot.js` SITE_GRENADES), raid containers (`AREA_GRENADES`: an armoury's crate 30%, 2–4 flashbangs or 1–3 smoke grenades; barracks and police locker rooms, cell blocks, Army helicopter wrecks now and then), evidence lockers (flashbangs 20%, smoke 15%), locked Army trucks and crash cases (`locks.js`), an armoury safe 0–2 (`gunsafes.js` STASH_GRENADES), Army trucks' crates (`miltrucks.js` TRUCK_FLASH), the armoured dead (`MILDEAD.BODY_EXTRA`: a soldier's flashbang 5%, a riot cop's smoke grenade 5% or flashbang 3%). On the map: the throw preview lights the cells its flash would reach (bright within a tile) and rings who it would catch; STN / DZ over the stunned / dazed, and the enemy lines and the hover line say so.
- **Siege, fire and decoys** (`src/sim/siege.js`, `throwables.js`, `decoys.js`; Help → *Siege, fire and decoys*; `tools/siegetest.js`): *grenades* (frag grenades, pipe bombs) are thrown over walls to any cell someone on the thrower's side sees (or, for your people, within a tile of where an enemy was last seen), landing a tile off by aim (45% − 0.8 a point of aim over 40, 8–45%, +15% blind and then 2 tiles past 5 tiles out, a pipe bomb +10%); 20–30 (pipe bomb 22–34) within a tile, light cover there blown to rubble, a door or the gate 30–45 (40–60), a wall section half. *Ladders* (crafted, 9 kg, cargo): the carrier moves a tile slower; one action from the foot of a wall section turns it into a "siegeladder" cell (walkable, height 1, a climb) up to the walkway; whoever is on it has no cover and is +10 to hit; a defender next to it on the walkway, a tower or behind the wall pushes it off (35% + a fifth of melee aim + Tough/Brave, −15 with a climber, 15–80%): the climber falls 10–18 and loses the turn, the ladder lies at its foot and one action raises it again. *Battering ram* (crafted at a workshop, 30 kg): a crew of 2–3 (the lead walks 3 tiles an action, the crew follows and spends its turn), no shooting, no cover, +10 to hit, shot first (×1.8); the lead swings at a barred gate or door right in front of it for 16–24 a carrier next to it; with one left it is dropped, and anyone with a helper next to them takes it up. *Rocks*: from the walkway or a tower on anyone lower within 2 tiles, 70% for 12–18, a ram's crew bunched (another of them 6–10). *Fire*: a Molotov burns a 3 × 3 patch (the middle 3 turns, the rest 2–3), 12–18 to everyone there; each round people in it burn 8–14 and the dead 16–24 (brutes 60%, bosses half); it spreads by fuel (hay 45%, crates, fences, barricades 25%, shelves 20%, a wooden door 15%, a field 20%, grass 12%, a wooden floor 8%; never stone, asphalt, dirt or the walls; 40 cells at most), wood and hay burn out to rubble, every burning cell's thick smoke blocks sight through it; people's AI (and the Shepherd) will not walk into it, your own orders can. *Decoys and senses*: the ordinary dead follow noise and light, runners and brutes noise, spitters noise and light, crawlers heat, screamers, Shepherds and the bosses nothing. Noise: bottle (a stealth lure only), noise maker (3 turns), firecrackers (2 turns), wind-up alarm clock (10 tiles, 5 turns), pocket radio (12 tiles, 6 turns); heat: hand warmers (5 tiles, 6 turns), chemical heat pack (7 tiles, 8 turns); light and heat: road flare and fire (7 tiles of a burning cell). A decoy pulls the dead that sense it even after they have seen you, unless one of your people is within 2.5 tiles; they walk to it (into the flames). People are never fooled: a guard who hears an alarm clock or a radio looks to where it was thrown from; heat packs are silent. *The Shepherd* (a hive's Shepherd's fold, tier 3: day 995, game year 10; 1.6× hit points, keeps 6 tiles from your people, steps round fire, shot first): the dead within 4 tiles of it ignore decoys and fire, do not shuffle about and wake with it; kill it and that ends. *Raiders' siege kit* (`raidSiegeKit`, its own dice): against a walled base, by the year's raid preparedness (eras.js PREP; outsider bands ×0.6), escalated or military / expansionist raids better prepared: ladders (2 with 8+ fighters), a ram (4+ fighters, likelier against a reinforced gate), 1–3 grenades; about 63% of raids in year 3, 86% in year 5, all by year 10; the raid warning card says what they carry and the battle hands it out. By the numbers the wall tier loses 6% a ladder, 10% a ram and 2% a grenade of its bonus (at most 25%); an Assault takes the hub's ram (3+ people), a ladder per 3 people (at most 2) and up to 4 grenades into its cargo, and the target's walls count 15% a ladder, 20% the ram and 4% a grenade less (at most 60%); their gate is barred (60 + 10 a wall point) and their wall sections have hit points (40 + 8). Decoys and Molotovs a team carries add 2% each (at most 8%) to its strength against the dead by the numbers, nothing against a Shepherd's pack. *State Map*: the horde warning offers *Set out decoys* (up to 3 noise decoys from the armory, heat ones only with crawlers) beside the decoy fire; a horde with a Shepherd says it is "led by something that isn't fooled" and turns for either 30% as often. *The AI*: your side pushes ladders (and walks over to do it), drops rocks on the ram's crew and climbers, shoots Shepherds, the crew and climbers first, throws a decoy away from your people when three of the dead are on them, a Molotov on two of the dead at a breach, the gate or a doorway, grenades on three bunched; raiders lead the ram to the gate (both actions when it is out of reach), set ladders and send three up them, throw grenades at two or more defenders bunched. *Drawn as data* (`battleOverlays`): fire cells, smoke, flares, decoys (rings by sense), ladders (against the wall or lying), rams at their lead, ladders on a carrier's back, the Shepherd's aura; both battle views draw them (isometric: flames and smoke rising, ladders leaning on the wall face, the ram at waist height).
- **Defending your base in three tiers** (`src/sim/deploy.js`, `src/sim/interior.js`, `combat.js`; Help → *Defending your base*): *tier 1, the walls* (18 deep, 13 rows on a small base, as long as its yard map less 3 on a big one): each wall section has 40 + 12 hit points a point of wall strength (a hub with one section 112, three 184; an outpost with one 88), blocks sight from the ground and is battered by the attackers (the dead claw, people bash or shoot at it; a Wallbreaker hits three times as hard); a horde that broke through has already worn it (0.2% a dead inside, at most 85%); the gate is barred (100, 180 reinforced) and whoever holds it is behind the bars until it gives; a walkway behind the wall (height 1, up ladders) is where the shooters stand; a section battered down is a breach and its walkway comes down. 3 attackers inside the wall (a quarter of those left, if more), or any with nobody on the wall line, at the start of your turn move the fight to *tier 2, the yard* (26 × 16 with no base built up; a hub 40 × 30 to 48 × 34 and an outpost 32 × 24 to 40 × 30 by their buildings: `battlesize.js`), or you fall back yourself (*Fall back to the yard*, Shift+R on the wall map; the attackers move next). The yard map is generated from the base's own buildings, the same every time for the same base: the main house and the bunkhouses (with an upper floor up the stairs), the infirmary, the workshop, common room, cells, radio shack, garage and sheds as enterable houses with barred doors (45 hit points) and windows (20), the storehouse and a cistern as flat roofs with a ladder, the watchtower inside the wall, barricades and armory sandbags across the yard, wells, rain barrels, crates and the garage's car as cover; the wall line as it stood, its gate and breaches where they were. Everyone carries over (health, the downed where they lay, the base's ammo, gear), the attackers inside the wall come along and the rest come through the openings (3 + 2 an open opening a turn); a short redeploy places your people within 12 tiles of where they were and 3 from any attacker (Options: skip it). *Tier 3, the last stand*: once 1.5× as many attackers as defenders still outside the buildings (at least 3) stand in the yard; the battle AI takes everyone indoors. Losing everyone overruns the base (as before); the evac strip is the back gate (abandoning the base). A base without walls opens on the yard map. **Verticality**: +2 sight a level, +5 aim a level at anything lower (at most +10), half cover against shooters below (not right next to them), raised ground is full cover seen from below; heights change only on a ladder, stairs or a tower's own ladder (1 move a level, the dead 2); a blow struck up is −10; sight lines run eye to eye, so a walkway or roof sees over a wall in front of it but not into the dead ground right behind one near the target. **Fog of war** (every battle): you see what your people see (13 tiles, at night in base battles and raids 7, 9 with a flashlight, 13 with night vision or a thermal; +2 a level; binoculars and a scope +3 by day, a monocular +2, a thermal +2 day and night); floodlit ground at night and a flare's light out to the daylight range; enemies out of sight are not drawn and cannot be shot, a faded ghost marks where one was last seen. **By the numbers** (`defences.js`): the wall tier ×(1 + 5% a wall section up to 3, a watchtower, the reinforced gate +5% each, the trench +3%, floodlights at night +3%), the yard ×(1 + 3% a barricade, 1.5% a sandbag bundle and a roll of wire, 2% a spike pit), the buildings ×(1 + 2% an enterable building, up to 5): an auto-resolved base battle is won at the first tier where the defenders match the attackers (held at the wall from 1.15×; fewer wounds at the wall, more in a last stand); raids by the numbers are held at the wall, pushed back from the yard or the buildings, or overrun; a horde warning says how many would break in and whether the yard or the buildings should hold them (holdCheck: the yard and buildings limits).

## Known gaps

- UI shell: the minimap draws the land as a block per tile (no hex outlines, rivers as water tiles) and shows no sites, markers, outsiders or other groups' parties; it does not zoom. Colour-blind-safe colours cover good / bad, the overlays and the battle's odds and threats, not each group's own colour or the zombie types' rings. Reduce motion does not change the battle screen's own animations. The confirmation box covers the map screen's orders and the menu; battles keep their own questions.
- Hives: the autopilot destroys hives only on Aggressive and rarely (its best six seldom reach the combat power it waits for, and it knows few hives); it culls the dead gathering at a build site only when one is in its reach and it has the odds (hivebirth.js `gatheringCands`). Base sprites do not show the defence buildings (they show on the battle map). Hives can rise on top of each other's land; a hive build site itself cannot be attacked: a Cull horde team breaks up the mass gathered there (*Hive birth you can see*). Hardcore's first-year sites and the military's hive get no rumour, massing card or Break up choice (they have their own calls). The days to a hive on the map are an estimate (the hives' feeding is not modelled), and a gathering seen only on the radio or in a team's report is never live. Hordes on the map do not march on your teams' camps; a camp's Heat draws a horde within 3 tiles in as a bigger surge card instead (the horde itself stays where it is).

- Raid balance is untested with real players; the auto-player loses people often.

- Field: the hub's estimated "?" position of an out-of-contact team on a plan follows the way out, waits there for the plan's time and walks back along the same road (it does not trace the nearby searches). The plan watches for a horde, a rich site and low supplies; survivors met on the road come through the usual event and recruit cards. The autopilot launches no plans (it keeps its own Done-card rules); it does not camp, and gives up waiting at a busy site past Heat 60. Orders queued from the team panel after a plan's Return home step are dropped when the team heads home (put them before it). The People tab's base Treat is outside the radio rule (it is a base action).

- Balance is first-pass.
- Saving: a save cannot be made while an event card waits for an answer (answer it first; the autosave waits); Esc does not open the menu in a battle (Esc cancels the battle action there; use ⚙ Menu in the panel). Saves live in the browser's storage for this site (or the desktop app's profile): clearing the site's data deletes them, so Export the ones to keep. The desktop app keeps saves in its browser storage too, not as files in its folder. A save does not keep the interface: open windows, the camera, camera spots, the selected unit and an open card's result screen; the game-over autosave leaves open cards out. Older save formats have no migrations yet (there is only format 1). Rotating autosaves: the last 3 per campaign whatever their age (no daily / weekly spread); deleting a campaign's saves is one at a time. The error screen catches errors in the game's own code; a crash inside the browser itself is the desktop app's dialog (below) or, in a browser tab, the browser's own page; the crash save of a world too broken to write says so and the report still copies. An Ironman game written as the page closes is saved uncompressed (`writeSaveSync`: the page cannot wait for compression), about 3 MB at day 365 and 5 MB at day 730 on a Normal map, which no longer fits the browser's storage after about a year and a half: the last dawn or battle save stands then. Browser storage (about 5 million characters) holds roughly 7–10 two-year Normal saves in all; IndexedDB would hold far more.
- Saving: a save cannot be made while an event card waits for an answer (answer it first; the autosave waits); Esc does not open the menu in a battle (Esc cancels the battle action there; use ⚙ Menu in the panel). Saves live in the browser's storage for this site (or the desktop app's profile): clearing the site's data deletes them, so Export the ones to keep. The desktop app keeps saves in its browser storage too, not as files in its folder. A save does not keep the interface: open windows, the camera, camera spots, the selected unit and an open card's result screen; the game-over autosave leaves open cards out. Older save formats have no migrations yet (there is only format 1).
- Roster bulk actions, watch list and map notes: Equip best only uses the armory of the base each person is at (it does not fetch gear from other bases or trade between people) and hands out off-hand tools only to empty hands; moves and hospital trips go on foot (no vehicle is offered) and a person's convoy follows supply-run rules (escorted by nobody else, the road's events can hit it); a finished transfer job stays in the Supply runs tab until the next transfer clears it; "Send to the hospital" does not force a bed (the base's admit line still decides). The Watching panel shrinks to fit between the Running orders and the Layers box (it scrolls when both are tall); hordes are tracked by id, so a horde that merged into another shows as gone once you see its old tile; there is no Layers-box entry for notes (the 👁 in the Notes list hides them). Notes are per campaign and cannot be moved (delete and add again).
- Saving: a save cannot be made while an event card waits for an answer, in the tray too (answer it first, or *✓ all* in the tray; the autosave waits); Esc does not open the menu in a battle (Esc cancels the battle action there; use ⚙ Menu in the panel). Saves live in the browser's storage for this site (or the desktop app's profile): clearing the site's data deletes them, so Export the ones to keep. The desktop app keeps saves in its browser storage too, not as files in its folder. A save does not keep the interface: open windows, the camera, camera spots, the selected unit and an open card's result screen; the game-over autosave leaves open cards out. Older save formats have no migrations yet (there is only format 1).
- Pause rules and the tray: a card's kind comes from its flags and title (`src/sim/cardrules.js`); a new kind of card whose title the rules do not know counts as "other" (it waits in the tray). The default answer is the safest, not the best (Stranded traders → Leave them, not Help them). Only a team on the move holds while its card waits; one camping, surveying or searching keeps at it. The stops with no card watch your people (a death, not a prisoner's), the map's groups (not Hardcore's bands or outsiders) and the horde and break-in alerts. Tray cards are not saved (saving waits for an empty tray).
- Alerts, diary and roster: the diary's categories come from the wording of each line (a few lines land in a neighbouring category); links are found by name when the line is written, so a team's link only works while the team is out, and a base renamed later keeps its old name in old lines; alerts do not cover teams short of food or water in the field, or vehicles out of fuel; the search box searches when you press Enter (not while typing); camera spots are kept for the run, not between sessions.
- First-week guide: the Show me pulse only finds what is on screen (a team out of radio contact has no Return home on its panel, so the Teams tab's is pulsed; a disabled button is not pulsed); "Check your food and water" ticks when a base's Farming or Overview tab is opened, "Read the diary" when the diary is opened; a team already out when the guide is brought back from the menu counts as sent; brought back with no base left (Hardcore before the hub, or the exiles of a normal game), it shows the road list.
- "Why?": a chain is written once, at the death or the loss, from what the state and the diary show then; it reads the diary for its lines, so a line over the diary's 150 a day is not seen, and decisions on cards (a tribute refused, a water run called off) count only when they wrote a log line or changed goodwill. The kind of death comes from the wording of its cause. People who walk out, desert or change sides are not deaths and get no chain; nor does the game-over screen.
- Sounding the retreat is not offered in raids (they keep their extraction zones) or in base-defence battles without an evac strip; there is no smoke on the battle map, and night counts only from the hour the battle started.
