The AI's retreat only runs in battles it plays on the map (autopilot hive assaults, battles played out headless); fights resolved by the numbers break off by the team's policy instead (`breakoff.js`), base defences by the numbers fall back through the three tiers instead (`interior.js baseTiers`), the battle screen's Auto-resolve button does not show the policy (the card does), and the hive burns keep their single "hurt getting away" roll. Prisoners are not on the map, cannot be moved between bases or handed to Diplomacy's jobs, and other groups never take your knocked-out people (your captured people are still conflicts.js captives). Enemy guards have no vision cones drawn. Base battles: two maps, not one continuous base (the yard map is generated from the building list, not from where things stand on the base screen); the dead do not batter building walls, only doors, windows, the gate and wall sections; night counts in base battles and raids only (other battles keep daylight sight); the autopilot's break-ins (fought headless on both maps) are won more often than before the tiers (hivetest calibration, the starting six at a tier-2 hub: 400 dead 63% → about 90%, 400 with a Wallbreaker 29% → about 80%, 600 with a Wallbreaker 4% → about 40%), at a cost in lives.
- Keys, codex and search: the tooltips, Help paragraphs and README still name the default keys (Menu → Controls and Help → Keys show the keys as set); the Work tab's number keys and Esc cannot be changed; the codex has no sprites for items or buildings, opens a building only when you build or queue one (not when you see it at another group's base) and the human dead (a battle against a group) do not open anything; the search box finds only the buildings of one base (the selected one) and does not search items in armories or the diary's text.
- Outposts cannot be abandoned by hand.
- Templates and Repeat trip: only trips sent from the planner are remembered (not crews', supply runs, the autopilot's or the map's quick orders); a team whose plan ends away from home offers *Repeat trip* once it is home, not on its *Done* card; a template is applied to one planner at a time (no "send these three templates"). Undo has no redo, and the build queue's undo does not cover demolishing (that is final).
- Sound: first pass, synthesized only (no recorded samples); no positional sound (battle sounds get a small random stereo spread, not their place on the map), no footsteps, doors, vehicles, digging or base work sounds, no sound on the State Map for fights resolved by the numbers or for your teams' road events beyond their cards; no key to mute (⚙ Menu → Options → Sound).
- Sites running dry: groups know the true loot of sites near them (they live there; they do not keep stale knowledge like you); their scavenging parties find no gear, books or keys, never search land held by you or another group, never go by vehicle and never return a second time in one trip; a party you drive off loses its haul but the site keeps what it already lost; radio news needs a radio station but not the group to be in radio reach; the base's own surroundings (Scavenge role) are a separate number from the site's loot (stripping a site next to a base does not lower it, and vice versa).
- Trade routes cannot swap escorts (end and recreate the route).
- Radio and trade: groups' caravans go to the hub when they can reach it (else your nearest reachable base); you cannot pick the base. Groups do not trade gear with each other. Your trade caravans start from the hub only, and the planner's estimate assumes 6 hours at each stop. A caravan of theirs caught by raiders or a horde on the way just never arrives (a log line only if you can see it). Maps you sell a group do not change what they know or do.
- Captives are not shown on the map (only in the People tab and tile tooltips); convoys on trade routes and supply runs do not get conflict cards (their road trouble is auto-resolved by the escort table).
- Held supply convoys do not help defend the base they are parked at.
- Timelapse: parties are only seen at frame times (a trip shorter than a day can be missed) and move in straight lines between frames, not along roads; death sites, markers and battles themselves are not drawn (deaths and fights show as captions and pings); the recording is part of the save (a loaded game's timelapse starts from day 1).
- Ledger: in and out are stock changes between the hourly marks, so something made and used within the same hour (or the same step of the daily economy) nets out; supply runs and trade convoys between your own bases count as out where they load and in where they unload (only the outposts' daily surplus run is "moved"); stock on teams, convoys and caravans is not counted; deaths by cause sort the memorial wall's words into kinds; births stay 0 until there are children; the hives known are those on your map (stale reports included).
- Crafting: no lethal throwables (Molotov, pipe bomb) or torches; makeshift gear is not traded.
- Production and power: station orders count only the base's own stock (not the other bases, teams or trade caravans); the drying racks share one order; a craft order counts the armory only (not gear people wear) and has one job in the queue at a time; the hospital, ammo bench, med station and radio relays take no power; a generator's refuelling is one daily chore, not a job on the Work tab; power is per base (no lines between bases). Stewards do not answer cards (horde or raid warnings, hub events, desertion warnings), plan trips or supply runs; their style is one of the governor's three.
- Weather: the temperature, snow cover and mud are statewide (fronts change the weather and a few degrees, not the ground), heat waves and dry spells are statewide too, and there is no north-south or height difference in temperature (rivers freeze by a fixed north-to-south gradient, all rivers on the same days); arrival estimates still know the day records ahead (mud, snow cover, the background sky) exactly, only fronts that have not formed yet are unknown; the forecast is the region's outlook, not where a front will go; a team's thirst and appetite go by the hub's day; there are no lakes to freeze (only rivers), boat crossings are still offered over a frozen river, and a team on the ice when it thaws is simply put ashore; AI groups move at the same weather-adjusted pace (fronts and ice included) but do not plan around storms; convoys on trade routes and supply runs are slowed but not hurt by storms.
- People: hub events come only at the hub (not at outposts); deserters never come back with a raider group, only alone at the gate; a lost teenager is an ordinary survivor with low skills (children are born at your bases: family.js); people walking to a base are not on the map and cannot be met or rescued on the way.
- Relationships and mood: opinion is one number per pair (the same both ways); hub events, crowding and the warning card change mood without a line in the breakdown's *Lately* list except the ones relations.js and morale.js note (grief, breaks, services, rough trips, warnings, stories by the fire); outposts get no hub events, so no memorial event there (the wall's service works at any base); books count as comfort whatever they teach; kills in fights resolved by the numbers are spread over the fighters by strength; there is no jealousy, and no mood effects from gear, clothes or temperature indoors.
- Children: other groups get no births of their own (their population already grows from their food, factions.js, which covers it); a child has no health, illness or gear and no survivor screen (only the People tab card and the roster row), cannot be moved to another base by hand (they follow a parent who lives elsewhere), and does not keep a run going: the run ends when the last adult is gone; children with the exiles neither eat nor grow until there is a base again; a hub event never brings an orphan; the autopilot does not plan for families (it plans food and beds by the head count, which includes children).
- Economy: crops have no pests, fertility or crop rotation; no salting (no salt source), and storage room is per base (outposts ship their surplus to the hub daily rather than keeping a winter store); the winter outlook plans by the usual weather and the current farmers, so a drought or a lost crop can still catch it out, and it does not count loot or trade; water is never rationed (food is); illness gives no immunity after recovery; vehicle fuel use per tile is unchanged (only fuel income was cut). The Balanced autopilot survives the first month and the food and water pressure of the first winter on most seeds, but the new hordes still break in on many of its year-long runs.
- Autopilot: fights and raids are always resolved by the numbers (never the tactical or raid maps); it does not set up trade routes, cull or lure hordes, build boats (a team cut off by water waits for you), or deliberately scout unknown land (unknown sites are scavenged on estimates). It re-sets roles every hour, so roles you set by hand while it is on are overwritten (except pinned people, and teaching or reading, which it leaves alone); it staffs stations through their standing orders (Until for ammo, meds and parts, so operators no longer flip at the threshold). Stewards run the same per-base code but never answer cards, plan trips or supply runs, or trade: those stay yours. Screens you open yourself (base, planner, survivor, inventory, help, timelapse) still pause the clock. The style resets to Balanced with a new campaign.
- Skills: no XP from hub events, morale cards or mediating quarrels (morale.js and the relations code give none yet); Pathfinder's +10% is not in the planner's estimate before the team leaves (it is once the team is out); Seed Saver is not counted in the autopilot's crop plan; the autopilot teaches and reads only with hands to spare after farming, building and guarding, so it seldom does early on; earned traits ("Bitten and Lived", "Road-Wise") are not in yet.
- Exploration: keys cannot be bought or sold (they live on the key ring, not in cargo), and other groups never use keys; no blowtorch or explosives for safes. Locks are only in raids on the map and the hour-by-hour search (not in conflicts or base fights). Blueprints are global once studied (any base can build), and studying happens on the Read role (no separate "Study" role in the roster's role list). Crash sites: AI salvage parties never fight hordes at the wreck, and a team already raiding the wreck when a salvage party arrives is not interrupted (the group strips what is left). Breakdowns: no greenhouse, rain catcher, cellar or vehicle-part failures; the repairer's role work is not reduced while they repair; the autopilot does not hold scrap back for repairs (short scrap is made up with parts).
- Inventory: sprites draw the weapon and body armor (or arm guards) but not helmets, leg guards or packs; enemies in battle do not wear helmets (soldier and raider bodies can carry one as spare kit); no picking things up from downed units during a battle (loot comes after a win, not after a retreat or defeat); raid maps keep their own body containers (Search); supplies are pooled per team, not carried per person; death sites store leftovers as a flat list, not per body.
- Outsiders: raids on other groups and robberies of caravans are resolved by the numbers (never a battle you watch); outsiders do not appear in conflict-type cards (conflicts.js) but in their own cards; your trade-route and supply convoys are hit by the numbers (no card); merchants never sign pacts or run trade routes; explorers who stay are ordinary recruits; a merchant's trade window says "reopen it from the Bases tab" though it reopens from the Groups tab; the radio call goes out once per caravan.
- Diplomacy: groups' wars are resolved by the numbers (their raids on each other are never a battle you watch unless you answer a call for help), and joining a war adds abstract patrols to your side's score rather than missions against the enemy's bases; a job's escort does not fight beside the caravan (it keeps raiders away; hordes still wear it down); a rescued group's people and a package are not items in your inventory; a job's horde that merges into another before your team arrives is called off; alliance land is a radius around bases (7–9 tiles), not the territory colors on the map; a captured or lost base keeps no people of the group that held it (their garrison is a number) and an AI group's own bases have no buildings or stock of their own (its people and stores are shared over its bases); there is no peace treaty between you and a group you fought except through their war's end.

## Desktop app

The game also runs as its own window (Electron), from `desktop/`. Double-click **`Play Broken State.cmd`** in the repo root (the first run installs Electron into `desktop/node_modules`, about 360 MB, once), or from `desktop/`: `npm install` then `npm start`. It always plays the current files in `prototype/`, so game changes need no rebuild (Ctrl+R reloads). The game is served on the fixed origin `app://brokenstate/`, so browser-saved settings (zombie look) stay between launches; the window remembers its size, position, maximized and fullscreen state. Keys: F11 fullscreen, Ctrl+R reload, Ctrl+Shift+I DevTools, Ctrl+= / Ctrl+- / Ctrl+0 zoom (zooming out shows more map and costs frame rate), Alt shows the menu.

- Saves and options live in the app's browser storage (`%APPDATA%\Broken State`), like the zombie look; the menu adds *Save and quit to desktop* and *Quit* (they close the window, which quits the app). *Export* opens a save dialog for the `.bsave` file.
- Errors: the game's own errors open its error screen (above). If the page's process dies (out of memory, a GPU fault) a dialog offers Reload or Quit; if it hangs, Wait or Reload (the question closes by itself if it comes back). Reload opens the title screen; the autosaves and a crash save are in Load game.
- `npm run dist`: a portable build in `desktop/dist/Broken State-win32-x64/` (`Broken State.exe`, about 330 MB, copy the folder anywhere) with a snapshot of the game (and `build.json`, its commit, for the error report); run it again after game changes.
- `npm run perf` (or `"Broken State.exe" --perf`): new game on seed 42, Reveal map + Autopilot at 200×, 60 s each at 1920×1080, maximized, and maximized at 100% scaling; prints frame-time and memory JSON and quits (`--perf-seconds=N`, `--perf-out=file.json`). `npm run perf:browser` runs the same test in the installed Chrome/Edge for comparison.
- `npm run dist`: a portable build in `desktop/dist/Broken State-win32-x64/` (`Broken State.exe`, about 330 MB, copy the folder anywhere) with a snapshot of the game; run it again after game changes.
- `npm run perf` (or `"Broken State.exe" --perf`): new game on seed 42, Reveal map + Autopilot at 300× (`--perf-speed=N` for another dev speed: 60, 300 or 1200; the old 200× is gone), 60 s each at 1920×1080, maximized, and maximized at 100% scaling; prints frame-time and memory JSON and quits (`--perf-seconds=N`, `--perf-out=file.json`). `npm run perf:browser` runs the same test in the installed Chrome/Edge for comparison.
